# Polar Ice Creamery — Original Recipes

Recipes distilled from [Polar Ice Creamery](https://www.youtube.com/@PolarIceCreamery) (Nick) YouTube videos.
Original ingredients, amounts, and methods — no swaps or adaptations.

---


### [100% Chocolate Gelato — Cocoa Mass Gelato](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AB5jAEjLgQ4)

Classic Italian recipe using pure 100% cacao (cocoa mass) — no added sugar chocolate, just fermented/dried/ground cacao beans. Based on a traditional cocoa mass gelato. Batch recipe (~982g, not single-pint).

**Ingredients:**
- **650g** whole milk
- **200g** sugar
- **128g** 100% chocolate / cocoa mass (e.g. Willie's Cacao)
- **3.5g** stabiliser

**Steps:**
1. Chop up the entire block of 100% cacao.
2. Heat milk in a pan slowly, adding sugar to completely dissolve.
3. Add all the 100% chocolate / cocoa mass to the warm milk. Melt completely until silky smooth.
4. Heat to 65-70°C, then take off heat.
5. Transfer to a blender or food processor. Blitz 30-60 sec to fully emulsify — this is critical, don't skip it.
6. Pour into a container and chill completely in the fridge.
7. Once fully chilled, remove any solid residue from the top (keep it, don't discard). Give a final stir.
8. Churn in ice cream machine (~10-12 min, varies by machine).
9. Load into tub and freeze.

**Notes:**
- Gelato is served at a warmer temperature than ice cream — expect it to need a few minutes out of the freezer.
- Use high-quality 100% cacao — the chocolate IS the flavour here, so quality matters enormously.
- Not bitter despite being 100% cacao — smooth, lingering chocolate flavour.
- Nick's suggested tweak: add 30g dextrose to soften slightly for eating straight from the tub.

**Scoopulator:** [gelato](https://scoopulator.app/calc?ingredients=usdaff-746782:650,usdaff-746784:200,usdasrlegacy-170273:128,stabilizer:3.5&target=gelato) *(100% cocoa mass approximated as 75% dark chocolate — no exact match in database)*

### [AMAZING Black Forest Ice Cream — My TOP 5 Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=t8gz8cvzkuQ)

Peruvian 100% cacao base churned with Amarena cherry syrup and 70% South African chocolate pieces — a bitter-sweet Black Forest ice cream. Batch/churn recipe (~1,388g base, not single-pint).

**Ingredients:**

*Ice cream base:*
- **705g** milk
- **400g** heavy cream (36%)
- **160g** sugar
- **120g** dextrose
- **2g** stabilizer blend (mixed into sugars before adding to dairy)
- **1g** pink Himalayan salt
- 100% cacao block (Peruvian — Willie's Cacao brand), amount not stated explicitly; used to flavour the full base

*Cherry syrup (thickened — layered in):*
- **100g** Fabbri Amarena wild cherry syrup
- **1 tsp** sugar
- **½ tsp** guar gum

*Mix-in (added in last minutes of churn):*
- 70% South African dark chocolate, chopped

**Steps:**

1. Weigh out sugars (160g sugar + 120g dextrose), salt, and 2g stabilizer blend into a bowl. Whisk the stabilizer through the dry sugars to break up any lumps.
2. Pour milk and cream into a pan. Add the sugar/stabilizer mixture and begin warming on the hob.
3. Chop the 100% cacao block into pieces and add to the warming dairy. Stir continuously — the mix will look bitty and grainy at first; once it passes ~60°C the cocoa fat melts and it comes together.
4. Bring to 85°C (185°F), then remove from heat.
5. Chill completely in the refrigerator — at least 4 hours (let the flavour develop fully).
6. To make the cherry syrup: mix 1 tsp sugar and ½ tsp guar gum together, then stir into 100g Fabbri Amarena wild cherry syrup with a small whisk. Allow to sit in the fridge for a couple of hours if possible (it will thicken further). The syrup works without refrigerating, but thickens better chilled.
7. Churn the chilled base in an ice cream machine. In the last couple of minutes of churning, add the chopped 70% South African chocolate so it gets fully incorporated.
8. When churning is complete, layer into tubs: one scoop of ice cream, then a generous spoonful of thickened cherry syrup; repeat until the tub is full.
9. Freeze to harden off completely — approximately 24 hours.
10. Serve directly from the freezer (no need to temper ahead of time).

**Notes:**
- The chocolate base must use a strong 100% cacao — the slight bitterness is intentional and essential to balance the ultra-sweet cherry syrup.
- Nick uses Willie's Cacao (Peruvian 100% cacao block, 185g, two tablets per pack) — excellent quality, UK-based, also available online. Any high-quality 100% cacao works.
- The 70% South African chocolate adds balanced chocolate chip texture throughout.
- Fabbri Amarena wild cherry syrup is a professional-grade culinary syrup (~80% sugar, 1.3kg bottle). Available on Amazon. Any cherry syrup works for the flavour, but it must be cherry.
- Thickening the cherry syrup with guar gum is key — it holds distinct layers in the tub rather than bleeding into the ice cream.
- Nick rates this one of his favourite chocolate ice creams ever — the contrast of bitter base, sweet cherry, and chocolate pieces is the whole point.
- This is a traditional churn recipe (not Ninja Creami).

### [AMAZING Haagen Dazs Chocolate Hazelnut ice cream but BETTER](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=vLtsd8IvPas).

**Ingredients — Ice Cream Base:**
- **700g** milk (2%)
- **327g** cream (33%)
- **257g** sugar
- **65g** skim milk powder (SMP)
- **45g** cocoa powder (20–24% fat; Nick uses 7 Hills — milk chocolate profile)
- **35g** dark chocolate (70%; 75% also fine)
- **60g** egg yolks (3 large)
- **5g** vanilla extract (add after cooling, just before churning)

**Ingredients — Hazelnut Ganache Swirl:**
- **150g** milk chocolate (40–45%)
- **15g** cocoa powder
- **60g** whole milk (any kind)
- **20g** refined coconut oil (refined = no coconut flavour)
- **10g** glucose syrup or honey (optional)
- **7g** hazelnut extract

**Steps:**
1. Combine milk, cream, sugar, SMP, cocoa powder, and chopped dark chocolate in a saucepan.
2. Heat on the hob, stirring, until all ingredients are dissolved and combined.
3. Add egg yolks and continue heating to 75°C (safe range: up to 85°C max — above 90°C they scramble). Stir constantly. This cooks the yolks and activates the lecithin for emulsification.
4. Remove from heat. Transfer to a container and refrigerate to chill completely.
5. While the base chills, pre-chill your ice cream machine.
6. Make the ganache: combine milk chocolate, cocoa powder, coconut oil, milk, and glucose syrup/honey in a small saucepan. Heat on the hob, stirring, until fully melted and smooth — it will look dry at first.
7. Once the ganache is hot and combined, stir in the hazelnut extract. Pour into a container and cool in the fridge for 5–10 minutes until the ice cream base is churned.
8. Remove the chilled base from the fridge, stir well, and add vanilla extract. Churn in your ice cream machine.
9. Layer churned ice cream and ganache into your container. Give one gentle mix to distribute — do not over-stir; you want large chunks of ganache throughout.
10. Freeze until solid.

**Notes:**
- This is a 1.5L batch. For a Ninja Creami Deluxe (24oz), use the full batch. For a standard Ninja Creami (16oz), divide by three.
- Stabilizer is optional here because egg yolk lecithin acts as the emulsifier/stabilizer. If adding one, use 1.2–1.5g per kilo of mix and choose a type with a lower activation temperature. Nick used locust bean gum.
- Nick uses 7 Hills cocoa powder (milk chocolate profile, 20–24% fat). If using a stronger dark-profile cocoa, reduce the quantity.
- Refined coconut oil is essential — unrefined will add coconut flavour.
- The ganache will look very dry and thick at first on the heat; keep stirring and it comes together once the milk and chocolate melt together.

### [Honey Pistachio Gelato](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LR6ZCUFZQI0)

Italian-style gelato made with real roasted pistachios blended into a paste, honey as the primary sweetener, and optional shaved dark chocolate mixed in.

**Note: This is a large batch recipe (multiple pints). Scale down as needed.**

**Ingredients:**

- **226g** heavy cream (33–35% fat)
- **128g** milk (2% or 3.25% / semi-skimmed or full fat)
- **205g** sugar
- **154g** honey (local/raw recommended; reduce by 20g for firmer result)
- **50g** skim milk powder
- **150g** roasted salted pistachios
- **50g** dark chocolate (60–80%), finely chopped/shaved (optional but recommended)
- **4g** almond extract (optional)
- **4g** stabilizer — Nick uses his "442 blend" (guar gum + xanthan gum based; substitute your own stabilizer blend or omit)

**Steps:**

1. Combine milk, cream, honey, sugar, and skim milk powder in a saucepan. Whisk in the stabilizer (pre-mixed with a small amount of the sugar to prevent clumping).
2. Heat on the hob, stirring constantly to prevent honey from burning on the bottom. Bring to 85°C (185°F) to activate the stabilizer, then remove from heat.
3. While the base heats, add the roasted salted pistachios to a small blender. Blend until as fine as possible — it will be chunky at this stage.
4. Once the base is hot, add a couple of spoonfuls of the hot milk mixture into the blender with the pistachios. Blend again until a smooth pistachio paste forms. Add more hot liquid if needed to loosen.
5. Add the pistachio paste into the hot base and stir to combine. If using almond extract, stir it in now.
6. Chill the base in the refrigerator for 4–5 hours to let the pistachio and honey flavors develop. (Ice bath optional for faster chilling.)
7. Before churning, whisk the chilled base well to re-incorporate any pistachio solids that have settled.
8. **Churned version:** Transfer to a pre-chilled ice cream machine and churn. In the last few minutes of churning, add the finely chopped/shaved dark chocolate. Dish into containers and freeze for at least 12 hours before serving.
9. **Ninja Creami version:** Pour base into a Ninja Creami Deluxe tub, freeze for at least 24 hours. Spin on Ice Cream setting. After the first spin, make a divot in the center, add the chopped chocolate, and run the Mix-In setting.

**Notes:**

- This is a large batch — feel free to halve the recipe or fill multiple Ninja Creami pints.
- Skim milk powder is important for milk solids and proper texture; technically optional but Nick recommends including it.
- Almond extract boosts pistachio flavor (pistachios and almonds share flavor compounds). Don't overdo it or it dominates. Nick uses 4g for this large batch.
- The natural color of real pistachio is a pale/muted green — no artificial coloring needed, but green food coloring can be added for a more vibrant look.
- Honey functions like corn syrup (fructose/glucose mix), depressing the freezing point and keeping the gelato soft/scoopable. If the result is too soft, reduce honey by ~20g.
- Nick uses Callebaut or Belcolade 80% cacao chocolate for the mix-in. The shards stay intact in the Creami Deluxe on Mix-In setting.
- Use a honey variety you enjoy — stronger-flavored honeys will be more pronounced in the final gelato.

---

### [AMAZING Ninja Creami Pistachio Gelato](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=WyurIaOK6Eo)

Real pistachio gelato using shelled salted roasted pistachios blended into a paste with hot milk — no cream, just nut fat for gelato-level richness.

**Ingredients:**

- **75g** shelled salted roasted pistachios (start with ~150g in-shell; yield is ~50%)
- **358g** 2% milk
- **90g** sugar
- **5g** invert sugar (corn syrup, golden syrup, or glucose syrup)
- **20g** skim milk powder (zero fat / lowfat milk powder)
- **¼ tsp** guar gum
- **1 tsp** sugar (used to disperse the guar gum — taken from the 90g total)

**Steps:**

1. Shell the pistachios and weigh out 75g of nut meat.
2. Add the pistachios to a small blender (e.g. KitchenAid desktop blender) along with half a tablespoon of sugar from your measured amount.
3. Blitz briefly for a couple of minutes into a rough paste. Do not use a food processor — it will overheat and still won't get smooth.
4. Take roughly a quarter of the total milk (~100ml) and warm it in the microwave until just hot enough to touch.
5. Spoon the warm milk into the blender with the pistachio paste a little at a time. Blitz between additions until the mixture loosens into a thin, smooth pistachio liquid.
6. Mix the guar gum with 1 scant teaspoon of sugar (taken from the measured amount) and blend dry to eliminate lumps.
7. In the Ninja Creami pint, combine the remaining milk, skim milk powder, remaining sugar, and the invert sugar. Microwave briefly to warm and dissolve the sugar and milk powder.
8. Stir the guar gum / sugar blend into the warm milk mixture until fully incorporated.
9. Add the pistachio paste liquid and stir everything together well, scraping it all out of the blender.
10. Pour the base into the Ninja Creami pint, filling to the max line.
11. Freeze for at least 24 hours (Nick froze for a couple of days).
12. During the first few hours of freezing, periodically stir the base (whisk, spoon, or fork) to prevent the nut solids from sinking and settling at the bottom.
13. Spin on the Gelato setting.
14. Serve immediately.

**Notes:**

- 150g of in-shell salted pistachios yields approximately 75g of usable nut meat — expect roughly 50% shell waste.
- Buy pistachios from a cheaper outlet (dollar store, etc.) since you'll be processing them — brand quality doesn't matter here.
- Don't worry about bits of papery purple skin on some nuts; it gets decimated during the spin.
- The fat from 75g of pistachios is what gives this recipe its gelato-level fat content — no cream needed. High-fat nuts like walnuts could work similarly.
- Nick chose not to use a hot-processed stabilizer — the guar gum is added cold/warm. He notes a stabilizer (even just guar gum) really helps texture.
- Pistachio is a light, delicate flavor that benefits from a thick, dense, low-overrun texture — exactly what the Ninja Creami delivers. Gelato setting is appropriate here.
- No-cook method: everything is done with a microwave. No stove required.

---

### [AMAZING Ninja Creami Piña Colada Ice Cream - Home made glacé cherries](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PhGy_aFeFM8)

Sugar-free dairy-free coconut ice cream base with a boozy rum-pineapple swirl and homemade glacé (candied) cherries. Batch recipe (~750g, fills one Ninja Creami Deluxe pint).

**Ingredients — Ice Cream Base:**
- **165g** coconut cream
- **439g** coconut milk
- **20g** water (to top up to volume)
- **37g** inulin
- **12g** erythritol (do not increase — inhibits freezing)
- **90g** allulose (or 55g xylitol)
- **2.5g** xanthan gum

**Ingredients — Pineapple Rum Sauce:**
- **540g** canned pineapple chunks in juice (1 × 540ml can — use both fruit and juice)
- **75g** rum (any rum you have on hand)
- **3g** xanthan gum

**Ingredients — Homemade Glacé Cherries (batch):**
- **400g** fresh sweet cherries (weight includes pits and stalks — before pitting)
- **200g** sugar
- Small splash of water (to help dissolve sugar)
- Invert sugar or corn syrup (optional — glucose syrup, golden syrup, or corn syrup all work)

**Steps — Pineapple Rum Sauce:**
1. Empty the entire 540ml can of pineapple chunks and juice into a small saucepan.
2. Heat on the hob over medium heat, bring to a boil and reduce down.
3. Remove from heat once reduced. Let cool for approximately 2 hours until almost at room temperature.
4. Once cooled, sprinkle in 3g xanthan gum (use xanthan — not guar, which doesn't work well in acidic foods).
5. Blitz with a hand blender or small blender. Leave some pineapple chunks for texture or strain through a fine mesh sieve for a smooth sauce — your choice.
6. Stir in 75g rum. Cool completely in the fridge overnight before using.

**Steps — Homemade Glacé Cherries:**
1. Pit 400g fresh sweet cherries (the 400g is the pre-pitting weight).
2. Place pitted cherries into a pan with a small splash of water and 200g sugar. Add invert sugar / glucose syrup / golden syrup if using.
3. Heat on the hob until the mixture reaches 235°F (113°C) — soft ball stage. This is when the sugar becomes invert sugar.
4. Remove from heat and let cool completely to room temperature. The cherries will look translucent when done.
5. Transfer to a jar or container and store in the fridge. They will keep for months due to the high sugar / invert sugar content.

**Steps — Ice Cream Base:**
1. Combine coconut cream, coconut milk, water, inulin, erythritol, allulose (or xylitol), and xanthan gum in a saucepan.
2. Heat on the hob for 5 minutes, stirring, until the mixture reaches 40–50°C. This is just enough to melt the coconut fat solids and disperse the xanthan gum (xanthan is a cold-process stabiliser — no need to boil).
3. Pour into a Ninja Creami Deluxe pint — exactly 750g.
4. Freeze overnight (minimum 24 hours).

**Steps — Spin and Serve:**
1. Process the frozen pint on Ice Cream.
2. Create a well in the centre and add a generous pour of the rum-pineapple sauce. Process on Mix-In.
3. Top with homemade glacé cherries to serve. Optionally garnish with a fresh pineapple wedge.

**Notes:**
- Batch recipe: the glacé cherries make more than needed for one pint — they keep for months in the fridge and can be used in baking, cakes, and future ice cream batches.
- If using xylitol instead of allulose, reduce from 90g to 55g.
- The erythritol amount (12g) is intentionally small — its role here is solely to adjust the freezing point, not sweetness. Do not increase it or the base will struggle to freeze.
- Nick notes that coconut ice cream with a small amount of dairy added is better than fully plant-based due to what milk solids contribute — but this plant-based version performs very well.
- Cherry recipe sourced from Bigger Bolder Baking (Gemma, Irish chef). The invert sugar prevents recrystallisation so the cherries stay shelf-stable for months.
- Nick had to develop the cherry recipe three times before getting it right — this version is the one that worked.
- The glacé cherries are called "candied cherries" in the Americas and "glacé cherries" in Europe.

### [AMAZING White Chocolate Blondie Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V0EDfRAC6KA) loaded with homemade white chocolate blondies and a Werther's Soft caramel sauce swirl. Inspired by Gelato Messina and Scientifically Sweet.

> **Batch recipe** — 1.5L / ~1500g base. Yields 3x standard Ninja Creami pints, 2x Ninja Creami Deluxe pints, or 1x 1L churn + 1x standard pint.

---

**Ingredients — Ice Cream Base:**
- **680g** milk
- **440g** 33% whipping cream
- **100g** dextrose
- **55g** skim milk powder
- **110g** white chocolate (high quality — Nick uses mostly Valrhona + some white chocolate chips)
- **~11g** vanilla bean paste (~3/4 tablespoon)
- **1.8g** stabilizer (Nick's own blend)

**Ingredients — White Chocolate Blondies** (Scientifically Sweet recipe):
- **85g** white chocolate (high quality — Nick uses Valrhona)
- **100g** butter
- **135g** white sugar
- **55g** light brown sugar (Nick used demerara)
- **2** eggs, room temperature
- **2.5g** pink salt (~1/2 tsp)
- **7ml** vanilla bean paste (~1.5 tsp; Nick used ~3 tsp because he likes vanilla)
- **160g** all-purpose flour
- White chocolate chips (extra, folded in at end — amount to taste)

**Ingredients — Werther's Caramel Sauce:**
- **20** Werther's Soft caramels (unwrapped)
- Splash of cream (enough to loosen into a pourable sauce)

---

**Steps — Blondies (make first):**
1. Preheat oven to 175°C (350°F). Line an 8x8 pan with baking parchment.
2. Melt butter and white chocolate together. Microwave in 30-second blasts, stirring between each, until smooth. Be careful not to burn the white chocolate — stir it into the hot butter rather than microwaving hard.
3. In a large bowl, add white sugar, brown sugar, eggs, and salt. Mix by hand until pale and thick.
4. Add vanilla bean paste and mix through.
5. Pour in the melted butter and white chocolate mixture. Stir to combine.
6. Fold in the flour until just incorporated — do not whisk or overmix.
7. Fold in extra white chocolate chips.
8. Pour into the lined pan. Bake 25–30 minutes until golden around the edges with a smooth top.
9. Remove from oven and cool completely. Cut into pieces. Keep some for mix-in; vacuum-seal and freeze extras.

**Steps — Ice Cream Base:**
1. Combine milk powder, dextrose, and stabilizer in a pot. Whisk dry.
2. Add milk and cream. Add white chocolate (broken into pieces).
3. Heat on the hob, stirring, until the white chocolate melts fully and the base reaches 85°C (185°F). Remove from heat.
4. Add vanilla bean paste and stir through.
5. Note: white chocolate can cause cocoa butter to solidify on top as it cools — if this happens, re-incorporate with a whisk or immersion blender.
6. Chill in the fridge until cold (rapid chill first, then finish in fridge).
7. Churn in ice cream machine until thick and aerated.

**Steps — Caramel Sauce:**
1. Unwrap all 20 Werther's Soft caramels into a small jug.
2. Add a splash of cream.
3. Microwave in 30-second blasts (then shorter 20–25 second blasts) until melted. Stir together until smooth.
4. Refrigerate until ready to use (stays pourable for hours; does not set hard).

**Assembly:**
1. During the last few minutes of churning, add a few center-cut blondie pieces (softer pieces without hard edges) into the churning ice cream so they break up and distribute.
2. When loading into containers, drizzle in the Werther's caramel sauce between layers as a swirl.
3. Freeze until hardened.

---

**Notes:**
- Use high-quality white chocolate (Valrhona, Guittard, etc.) — it makes a significant difference to the final flavour.
- White chocolate ice cream is notoriously cold/flat when made with lower-quality chocolate. The stabilizer helps with this.
- Nick uses 1.8g stabilizer here because white chocolate benefits from slightly more stabilizer than other flavours.
- Heating the base to 85°C with skim milk powder mimics commercial pasteurisation and reduces water content, giving a more luxurious result.
- Vanilla bean paste is preferred over vanilla extract here to avoid alcohol in the base.
- The outer edges of the blondies stay crispy even when frozen in the ice cream; the centre pieces stay chewy and decadent.
- Werther's Soft caramel sauce is easy: cream + caramels + microwave. Stays perfectly pourable after hours in the fridge.
- This is a churned ice cream recipe, not a Ninja Creami no-churn recipe.
---

### [Alcohol in ice cream - Peanut Butter Baileys](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gqMO7Hb4yeM)

Peanut butter Baileys gelato with no added sugar — uses homemade peanut butter Baileys as the base liquid, churned traditionally. Episode 1 of Nick's Alcoholic Ice Cream series.

**Ingredients:**

- **357g** milk (Nick uses 2%)
- **330g** homemade peanut butter Baileys (Baileys Irish Cream blended with peanut butter — pre-made)
- **58g** skim milk powder
- **1.5g** guar gum
- **3g** stevia drops

**Steps:**

1. Prepare your homemade peanut butter Baileys in advance by blending Baileys Irish Cream with peanut butter.
2. Combine the skim milk powder and guar gum in a small pot and whisk them together dry.
3. Add the milk and the peanut butter Baileys to the pot. Heat on the hob over high heat, stirring continuously, until the fats melt and the mixture becomes homogeneous (target ~50°C). Do not bring to a full boil.
4. Remove from heat. Add the stevia drops and stir through.
5. Chill in the fridge for 1–2 hours until cold.
6. Churn in an ice cream machine until thick and aerated.
7. Transfer to a container and harden in the freezer.

**Notes:**

- Baileys is 17% ABV — a lower-alcohol liqueur than spirits (40% ABV). Lower ABV means less freezing-point depression, making it more tractable in ice cream than straight spirits.
- Alcohol affects freezing point significantly — you can't simply guess the impact. Different alcoholic drinks vary widely; always calculate rather than estimate.
- If you substitute plain Baileys for the homemade peanut butter Baileys, you will miss the fat contribution from the peanut butter. In that case, add some cream to compensate.
- Guar gum is the only stabilizer used here. Nick notes it has both stabilizing and mild emulsifying properties, making it well-suited for low-temperature bases without added sugar.
- Stevia drops (250–300x sweeter than sugar) are added off the heat. Weigh carefully on a precision scale — 3g is the target. Nick warns that over-use gives a funky off-taste.
- This recipe is intentionally low in total solids (~31%) — the Baileys contributes alcohol but not much solid mass. Nick acknowledges this and uses guar gum to compensate for mouthfeel.
- Result: smooth, creamy peanut butter up front, with Irish whiskey warmth on the finish after swallowing.
- This is a churned gelato recipe, not a Ninja Creami recipe.
---

### [Amazing Almond Joy Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BCof8Vq1kD8)
Coconut ice cream base with desiccated coconut, dark chocolate stracciatella, and a crackle top — inspired by the Almond Joy / Bounty candy bar. Batch recipe made in a traditional churn, with a Ninja Creami comparison.

**Note: Batch recipe — large yield (~2000g+), scale down as needed. Also demonstrated in Ninja Creami.**

**Base ingredients:**
- **900g** coconut milk (~17% fat)
- **561g** coconut cream (~21% fat)
- **225g** plain sugar
- **112g** whipping cream (~33% fat)
- **50g** glucose syrup (GL glucose 40 DE) or corn syrup
- **56g** allulose + monk fruit extract blend (sweetener)
- **95g** skim milk powder
- **2g** cold process stabiliser (gum blend)

**Add-ins:**
- Desiccated coconut / shredded coconut (generous amount — key to texture)
- Toasted whole almonds (for the Almond Joy garnish on top)

**Crackle topping:**
- 80% dark chocolate (or milk chocolate — to taste)
- Small amount of coconut oil (deodorised or neutral) — just enough to slow the freeze slightly

**Steps:**
1. Combine coconut milk and coconut cream in a pan. Warm gently to 40–50°C to emulsify the fats together into a homogeneous liquid.
2. Warm the glucose syrup briefly in the microwave to loosen it (it is very thick/sticky), then add to the warm coconut mixture and stir in.
3. Add the allulose/monk fruit blend and plain sugar to the warm liquid — the residual heat helps dissolve the solids.
4. Add the whipping cream and stir to combine.
5. Add the skim milk powder. Stir well.
6. Sprinkle in 2g of cold process stabiliser (gum blend) over the powder on top, then stir everything together until fully dissolved and smooth.
7. Refrigerate for several hours (minimum 4h, or overnight) until thoroughly chilled before churning.
8. While the base chills: melt the dark chocolate with a small amount of coconut oil in the microwave until smooth. Set aside for use during churn and as crackle topping.
9. Toast whole almonds if desired and set aside.
10. Churn the cold base in a traditional ice cream machine. In the final minutes of churning, drizzle in the melted chocolate mixture — it will form stracciatella shards as it hits the cold ice cream.
11. Load generous amounts of desiccated coconut in with the churned ice cream and fold through.

**For Ninja Creami version:**
1. Fill the Creami pint with the prepared (unchurned) cold base — no add-ins yet.
2. Freeze solid (24h+).
3. Process on Ice Cream mode.
4. After processing, fold in desiccated coconut using a spoon, pressing down to close the top back over.
5. Top with melted chocolate crackle (chocolate + small amount of coconut oil).

**Serving (both versions):**
- Scoop ice cream into bowl.
- Top with a whole toasted almond.
- Pour over melted chocolate crackle and allow to set on top.

**Notes:**
- This recipe took Nick over 2.5 years to perfect — the fat balance and coconut texture are critical, not just a garnish.
- Coconut ice cream is texturally different to dairy-based ice cream; the dairy (whipping cream, skim milk powder) is essential to get the right consistency — pure coconut alone gives poor texture.
- Make sure to match the fat percentages of your coconut milk and cream. If your brand is lower fat, the texture will suffer.
- Glucose syrup (GL40 DE) is used specifically to avoid increasing sweetness while adding anti-freeze body. Corn syrup works as a substitute and is much easier to pour.
- Cold process stabiliser is used intentionally to avoid heating the base to high temperatures (which would cook off coconut aroma).
- For the Ninja Creami version, the desiccated coconut will be slightly firmer/harder (less time to absorb moisture from the dairy) — Nick considers this a different but acceptable result.
- The Ninja Creami blade decimates large chocolate shards — they become tiny fragments, not the large stracciatella pieces possible in a traditional churn. Still tastes excellent.
- Crackle top tip: more coconut oil in the chocolate = slower freeze on the ice cream. Chocolate alone (no oil) freezes very fast. Don't overdo the oil.
- Any neutral oil works for the crackle (coconut oil, olive oil, etc.). Since this is coconut ice cream, coconut flavour in the oil is irrelevant.
- Both Almond Joy (US) and Bounty (Europe) are essentially the same bar — coconut filling in chocolate. Almond Joy adds an almond on top. Nick used 80% dark chocolate; substitute milk chocolate if preferred.

---

### [Amazing Biscoff Ice Cream in Ninja Creami](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Sn76CZ2Xks4)

Simple no-stabilizer Biscoff ice cream using condensed milk, cream, and Biscoff spread — balanced with extra milk to bring fat% down from an initial 24%.

**Ingredients:**
- **286g** whipping cream (30–35% fat)
- **218g** milk (any fat %)
- **350g** condensed milk (1 × 300ml can)
- **156g** Biscoff spread (Lotus)

**Steps:**
1. Weigh cream, milk, and condensed milk directly into the Ninja Creami pint.
2. Add 156g Biscoff spread.
3. Stir thoroughly until the spread is fully incorporated — it will resist at first when hit by cold liquid, keep stirring until smooth.
4. Lid on, freeze until solid (Nick notes this one took a while due to high fat content and warm ambient temp — allow extra time in summer).
5. Spin on Ice Cream program.

**Notes:**
- No stabilizer needed — high fat content provides structure.
- The caramel notes from condensed milk complement Biscoff well; Nick chose condensed milk specifically for this.
- Recipe started at ~24% fat; the extra milk brings it down to a workable range.
- Spinning removes all crunchiness from the spread — result is ultra-smooth. Add Biscoff cookie crumble as mix-in after spinning if you want texture back.
- Biscoff contains cinnamon — expect a subtle cinnamony undertone in the finished ice cream.
- Works in any ice cream machine as a standard churn recipe too.

---

### [Amazing Chocolate Ice Cream using Lindor Balls](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4QBwWj3UE38)

Lindor milk chocolate balls melted into a Guittard cocoa powder base — silky, milk-chocolate-forward, no add-ins. Batch recipe (~1.5L, not single-pint).

**Ingredients:**
- **586g** milk (2%)
- **400g** heavy/double cream
- **40g** cocoa powder (Guittard recommended)
- **135g** Lindt Lindor balls, original milk chocolate (12 balls)
- **100g** sugar
- **151g** dextrose
- **55g** skimmed milk powder
- **1.3g** stabilizer (optional)

**Steps:**
1. Add milk to a pan over heat. Add sugar and dextrose, warming until dissolved.
2. Add cocoa powder and stir in well until fully incorporated with no lumps.
3. Add skimmed milk powder and stir until completely dissolved.
4. Turn off the heat. Add the Lindor balls and stir slowly until fully melted and the mixture looks silky smooth.
5. Transfer to a container. Add the cream and give a final mix.
6. Chill in the fridge for several hours until fully cold.
7. Once chilled, give one final stir, then churn in an ice cream machine for 12–15 minutes (longer for a large batch).
8. Transfer to a freezer container and freeze for 3–4 hours before serving.

**Notes:**
- Batch recipe — description amounts are for a 1–1.5L base. Scale up or down as needed.
- Chocolate ice cream freezes harder than most — the high dextrose load compensates for this and gives a better scoop from the freezer.
- Guittard cocoa powder gives a silkier, milk-chocolate-leaning flavour rather than a dark, punchy result. Nick rates it just behind Valrhona, but the style is different — pair it with Lindor for best results.
- Stabilizer is listed as optional — 1.3g is a small dose for this volume.
- No add-ins in this recipe; Lindor is fully melted into the base rather than used as a mix-in.
- Scoops directly from the freezer without waiting — described as "just the right density."

---

### [Amazing Häagen-Dazs Coffee Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qGqM09J_Jdc)

A cold-process (no-cook) high-fat coffee ice cream made to match — and beat — Häagen-Dazs' iconic coffee flavour. Batch recipe (~1000g, traditional ice cream machine).

**Ingredients:**
- **135g** whole milk
- **450g** double cream
- **175g** espresso (approx. 4 espresso pods, strong)
- **130g** sugar
- **40g** dextrose
- **65g** skim milk powder
- **1g** guar gum
- **1g** pink himalayan salt
- Chocolate-covered coffee beans (espresso beans) — for topping

**Steps:**
1. Combine guar gum with 1 tablespoon of the skim milk powder in a small container and mix together — this prevents the gum from clumping when added to the liquid.
2. Add milk, cream, sugar, dextrose, remaining skim milk powder, and the guar gum/milk powder mix into your main container. Stir to begin dissolving the sugars.
3. Add the brewed espresso and stir well to combine everything.
4. Check sugars are dissolved by running a spoon along the bottom — if you feel crystals, keep stirring.
5. Lid on, place in the fridge for 1 hour to allow the skim milk powder to fully hydrate.
6. After 1 hour, remove from the fridge and give a final thorough stir to fully homogenise — reincorporate any skin or layer on top before pouring.
7. Churn in ice cream machine for approximately 15–20 minutes until ready.
8. Scoop into container and top with chocolate-covered coffee beans.
9. Freeze for 4–5 hours to harden.

**Notes:**
- Cold process — only the espresso is hot. Guar gum works without heat, which is why it's chosen here.
- Use strong espresso for pronounced coffee flavour. Filter coffee works too — just make it strong.
- Always stir your base thoroughly before pouring into the machine; lumps from undissolved skin ruin the final texture.
- Guar gum is optional — if skipping it, still include the salt.
- Erythritol ≤ 8% of mix weight keeps crystallisation at bay; xylitol or glycerin help prevent it.
- Decoration: chocolate-covered espresso beans add visual contrast and a flavour hit.
---

### [Amazing Häagen-Dazs Belgian Chocolate Using Condensed Milk](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_unavailable_see_info_json)

Simplified third take on the Häagen-Dazs Belgian Chocolate dupe — uses sweetened condensed milk for ease, churned in a traditional ice cream machine with two types of Callebaut milk chocolate folded in at the end.

**Note: Batch recipe — traditional ice cream machine, not Ninja Creami.**

**Ingredients:**

- **325g** milk (2% fat)
- **400g** cream (33% fat)
- **380g** sweetened condensed milk
- **40g** dextrose (or corn syrup — same amount)
- **40g** Seven Hills organic cocoa powder
- **~50–80g** Callebaut milk chocolate block (~45% cocoa solids) — for mix-ins (grated shavings + chopped chunks)

**Steps:**

1. Combine cream and milk in a pan over low heat. Add dextrose and cocoa powder. Whisk to combine.
2. Open the condensed milk and pour the entire can in. Whisk continuously for 4–5 minutes until everything is fully emulsified — make sure the condensed milk has no streaks and is completely incorporated.
3. Remove from heat. Pour into a container and chill overnight in the fridge.
4. Prepare chocolate mix-ins: using a Callebaut milk chocolate block, grate half into fine shavings and chop the other half into slightly larger chunks. Place both in separate containers in the freezer.
5. Churn the chilled base in an ice cream machine.
6. About 2 minutes before churning is complete, add all the frozen chocolate shavings and chunks. Let the machine combine for the remaining time.
7. Load into tubs. Freeze 2–3 hours to harden completely before serving.

**Notes:**

- Seven Hills organic cocoa powder is essential — Nick has tried many cocoa powders and none replicate the Häagen-Dazs flavor profile as closely. Worth tracking down specifically for this recipe.
- Dextrose and corn syrup are interchangeable at the same weight — use whichever is easier to find.
- No cooking to a specific temperature needed — just warm enough to fully dissolve and emulsify the condensed milk with the other ingredients.
- Two chocolate textures (fine shavings + larger chunks) give contrast in every bite. Both must be frozen before adding to the churner.
- This is the third version of Nick's Häagen-Dazs Belgian Chocolate series. Earlier versions: [v1](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qIbplhjsvRs) | [v2](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qIbplhjsvRs) | [Plant-based](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qIbplhjsvRs).
---

### [Amazing Ninja Creami Baked Alaska](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ZouDhyTZYFs)

Eggnog-flavored Ninja Creami ice cream layered on store-bought cake, covered in Swiss meringue, and baked at high heat until golden — a Christmas-themed showstopper dessert.

**Ingredients:**

*Ice Cream Base (single Ninja Creami pint):*
- **380g** milk
- **430g** whipping cream (33% fat)
- **55g** skim milk powder
- **90g** white sugar
- **40g** dark brown sugar
- **3g** nutmeg
- **3g** cinnamon
- **1g** guar gum
- **2 tsp** homemade vanilla bean paste (or vanilla extract)

*Meringue:*
- **3** egg whites
- **100g** white sugar
- **¼ tsp** cream of tartar
- **1 tsp** vanilla extract

*Assembly:*
- Store-bought marble cake (e.g. Safeway), 2 slices as the base

**Steps:**

1. Combine nutmeg, cinnamon, and guar gum with the sugars and stir together.
2. Add the spice/sugar/gum mix and skim milk powder to the milk. Microwave in two stages — 1 minute, stir, then up to 30 more seconds — until sugars are fully dissolved with no crystals remaining.
3. Add the whipping cream and vanilla bean paste. Stir to combine.
4. Pour into the Ninja Creami pint, freeze overnight (minimum 24 hours).
5. Process the frozen pint on the Ice Cream setting.
6. While the pint is spinning, preheat oven to 450°F (232°C) with the shelf on the lowest rack position.
7. Make the meringue: whip egg whites with a balloon whisk attachment. Mix cream of tartar into the 100g white sugar, then add the sugar mixture to the egg whites gradually, bit by bit, until fully dissolved and glossy. Add vanilla extract and whip to stiff, glossy peaks.
8. Line a baking pan with parchment paper. Place two slices of store-bought cake on the pan side by side, joined with a dab of meringue.
9. Scoop the spun Ninja Creami ice cream on top of the cake base (double-bowl style).
10. Pipe or spread meringue over the entire surface — top, sides, and all gaps — ensuring no ice cream is exposed. Use a piping bag with a round nozzle.
11. Bake at 450°F (232°C) for approximately 5 minutes, watching closely. Remove when the meringue is browned nicely.
12. Serve immediately.

**Notes:**
- Nick kept sugar slightly lower in the ice cream base specifically so it wouldn't go too soft during the brief oven blast — important for structural integrity in a Baked Alaska.
- The meringue acts as an insulating layer — it must completely seal the ice cream with no gaps or holes or the heat will melt it.
- Nick skipped the traditional step of re-molding the ice cream and freezing it a second time in a bowl shape, and it still worked.
- The Ice Cream setting is equivalent to the Gelato setting on this machine.
- Nick used Safeway marble cake as a convenient store-bought base — any pound cake or sponge cake works.
- This is a Christmas/holiday recipe — the cinnamon and nutmeg give an eggnog flavor profile.
---

### [Strawberry Gelato with Dark Chocolate Stracciatella](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OLfIEGMkXpM)

Strawberry gelato made with store-bought jam for consistent flavour and balanced solids, finished with dark chocolate stracciatella drizzled in during churning.

**Ingredients:**

- **299g** strawberry jam (pure, no sugar substitutes — check label)
- **64.7g** skim milk powder
- **777.9g** whole milk (3.25%)
- **74.9g** sugar
- **278.4g** cream (33–36%)
- **4g** stabilizer (warm-process type)
- Red gel food coloring (optional, fat-soluble)

*Stracciatella:*
- **100g** dark chocolate (60–70%)
- **15–20g** extra virgin olive oil

**Steps:**

1. Combine milk, cream, strawberry jam, and sugar in a saucepan.
2. Pre-mix stabilizer with the sugar before adding to the base to prevent clumping.
3. Heat the base to 80–85°C and hold for 2–5 minutes to fully hydrate the stabilizer.
4. Remove from heat. Add red gel food coloring if desired.
5. Add skim milk powder off-heat and stir until fully dissolved — Nick found this gave better results than adding it before heating.
6. Strain the base through a fine sieve to remove any jam solids. Transfer to a clean container.
7. Chill in the fridge overnight (or cool rapidly in an ice bath, stirring as it cools).
8. Churn in a pre-chilled ice cream machine until near-done.
9. For stracciatella: chop chocolate, combine with olive oil in a microwave-safe container, and melt in 30-second bursts (approx. 1–1.5 min total). Do not burn. Set aside to cool slightly but keep fluid.
10. Towards the end of the churn, drizzle the melted chocolate in a thin stream into the churning gelato — it freezes on impact, shattering into fine flakes.
11. Transfer to a container, smooth the top, and harden in the freezer for 4–6 hours before serving.

**Notes:**

- This is a traditional (non-Ninja Creami) churned gelato recipe — uses a standard ice cream machine.
- Always check the jam's nutrition label for sugar content — it directly affects freezing point. Avoid jams sweetened with sugar substitutes (sorbitol, maltitol, etc.) as they throw off the balance.
- Use fat-soluble gel food coloring; water-based coloring doesn't distribute evenly in a fat-containing base.
- Pour chocolate in a thin, steady stream for delicate flakes. A thick pour creates chunks. Add generously — Nick says more is better.
- Magic shell can substitute for the chocolate/olive oil mixture but the flavour is inferior.
- Let the finished gelato harden 4–6 hours before serving for best texture and scoop.
- Gelato uses more stabilizer than ice cream (4g here) because the lower fat content requires more structural support.
- Nick tested three variations and found that adding skim milk powder after the heating phase produced the best result.
- Calculator used: icecreamcalc.com (Chrome-based web app).

---

### [Amazing Snickers Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NHNbBdaC1Dc)
Deconstructed Snickers candy bar in ice cream form: nougat base, peanut caramel swirl, and milk chocolate stracciatella. Traditional churned recipe.

**Note: Batch recipe — uses a traditional ice cream churn, not a Ninja Creami.**

---

**Ingredients:**

*Nougat base:*
- **550g** milk (2% or whole)
- **550g** cream (33%; use 450g if using 50% double cream)
- **66g** skim milk powder (SMP)
- **110g** nougat scraped from Snickers bars (approx. 8 x 52g bars)
- **220g** sugar
- **1.2g** stabilizer blend (optional)

*Milk chocolate stracciatella:*
- **100g** milk chocolate
- **7g** refined coconut oil (refined = no coconut flavour)

*Peanut caramel swirl:*
- **150g** salted peanuts (pulsed fine in food processor)
- **250g** sugar
- **100g** water
- **25g** butter
- **250g** cream (33%)
- **50g** invert sugar (glucose syrup, corn syrup, or golden syrup)

---

**Steps:**

1. Slice open Snickers bars with a knife and scrape out the nougat layer only — discard the chocolate coating. Collect 110g of nougat.
2. Combine milk, cream, SMP, sugar, and optional stabilizer in a saucepan. Whisk together, then add the nougat. Heat on the hob to 85°C (185°F), stirring until the nougat dissolves fully into the base.
3. Transfer base to a container and refrigerate overnight to age and develop nougat flavour.
4. For the caramel: combine sugar, water, and invert sugar in a saucepan. Cook over medium-high heat without stirring until the mixture turns a deep golden brown colour.
5. Remove caramel from heat and carefully add the butter (it will spit), then slowly pour in the cream, stirring continuously. If the mixture seizes, return briefly to heat until smooth.
6. Stir the crushed peanuts into the finished caramel. Pour into a container and leave to cool to room temperature.
7. Melt milk chocolate and refined coconut oil together in a microwave until fully liquid. Set aside.
8. Churn the aged base in your ice cream machine according to the manufacturer's instructions.
9. In the final minute of churning, stream in the melted chocolate to create a stracciatella effect.
10. Layer scoops of churned ice cream into a container, alternating with spoonfuls of peanut caramel swirl as you build up the layers.
11. Freeze for several hours until firm before serving.

---

**Notes:**
- Use refined (not cold-pressed) coconut oil for the stracciatella — refined has no coconut flavour.
- The invert sugar (glucose/corn/golden syrup) in the caramel is essential — it prevents the caramel from setting rock-hard in the freezer. More cream also keeps the swirl softer.
- Nougat from the Snickers bars acts as a natural stabilizer for the base; the additional 1.2g stabilizer blend is optional but recommended.
- Peanuts should be pulsed to a cornmeal-like texture — fine but not a paste — so they distribute evenly through the caramel.
- Age the base overnight in the fridge to maximise nougat flavour infusion.
- The caramel swirl is layered in during assembly, not churned in; this preserves distinct pockets of caramel.

---

### [An Intro to Sous Vide Ice Cream + Boozy Maple Gelato](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mGVwBgd52Fk)

Maple liqueur gelato made using a sous vide for pasteurization, sponsored by Vevor chamber vacuum sealer.

**Ingredients:**

- **660g** 2% milk
- **120g** 33% whipping cream
- **40g** skim milk powder (SMP)
- **126g** sugar
- **4.5g** Special Ingredients stabilizer blend (or 3.5g if using guar/xanthan alone)
- **100g** Wayne Gretzky Maple Canadian Cream liqueur (17% ABV)

**Steps:**

1. Set sous vide to 175°F (79°C) and allow water bath to heat up.
2. Combine milk and cream in a bowl or jug.
3. Add SMP to the liquid.
4. In a separate small bowl, mix sugar and stabilizer blend together dry to prevent clumping.
5. Add the sugar/stabilizer mix into the liquid and stir until dissolved.
6. Add 100g maple liqueur and stir to combine.
7. Transfer base to a vacuum bag. Add a sous vide weight to prevent floating. Vacuum seal.
8. Submerge sealed bag in the sous vide bath at 175°F for 40–50 minutes.
9. After cooking, transfer bag to an ice bath to cool rapidly.
10. Age in the fridge for at least 24 hours (longer is better — 3 days was used here).
11. Cut corner off the bag, pour base into ice cream machine, churn as normal.
12. Dish into container and freeze.

**Notes:**

- Gelato style: uses higher stabilizer levels than American ice cream due to lower fat content and more free water. For gelato, 0.2–0.4% stabilizer per kg of base is appropriate (vs. 0.1–0.2% for ice cream).
- Solids are deliberately low (~low 30s%) due to the alcohol's PAC contribution — this is as low as you want to go.
- Sugar substitution: allulose may work (reduce amount slightly). Xylitol is not recommended — it would require nearly 50% reduction and pushes solids too low (~24%), which is unacceptable.
- Alcohol: use any liqueur at approximately 14–20% ABV. Baileys is a suggested alternative. Do not exceed ~20% ABV or the freezing point will be too disrupted.
- Sous vide advantage: because the mix is sealed in a bag, no alcohol evaporates during cooking (unlike stovetop), so the PAC from alcohol stays fully intact and consistent in your recipe calculations.
- Vacuum sealer tip: do not overfill bags with liquid — the machine struggled with a full 1L; ~750–800g in a 10-inch bag worked reliably. Reduce vacuum time for liquids to minimize "boiling" during the vacuum phase.
- Place your sous vide container on a trivet or towel — sustained heat near 80°C can crack some quartz countertops.
- Stabilizer brand used: Special Ingredients (UK). If substituting with just guar or xanthan gum, reduce to 3.5g to avoid over-stabilization.

---

### [Avengers Hunka Hulka Burning Fudge Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gb6HHMrvgCg)

A fictional Marvel Avengers ice cream brought to life: green marshmallow base with grated dark chocolate–condensed milk fudge flecks, a pourable chocolate fudge core, and a smashable green white chocolate crack top.

**Ingredients:**

*Ice Cream Base:*
- **400ml** milk
- **500ml** double cream
- **150g** sugar
- **100g** marshmallows (good quality, heavy on vanilla flavour), chopped
- Green food colouring

*Dark Chocolate Fudge (for flecks):*
- **400ml** condensed milk
- **340g** dark chocolate

*Fudge Core:*
- **125g** Dairy Milk chocolate
- **125g** butter
- **1** egg
- **30g** caster sugar
- **15g** glucose syrup

*Green Crack Top:*
- **75g** white chocolate
- **6ml** water (just over 1 tsp)
- Green food colouring

**Steps:**

1. Make the dark chocolate fudge: melt condensed milk and dark chocolate together in a bain-marie over low heat until fully combined. Pour into a mould and refrigerate until set. Once set, transfer a portion to the freezer so it's firm enough to grate.
2. Make the ice cream base: combine milk, cream, sugar, and chopped marshmallows in a pan. Heat slowly, stirring, until the marshmallows and sugar are fully dissolved and incorporated. Add green food colouring to desired hue. Chill completely in the fridge.
3. Make the fudge core mixture: melt Dairy Milk chocolate and butter together (microwave or bain-marie). In a separate bowl, whisk the egg and caster sugar, then combine with the melted chocolate mixture. Whisk together quickly. Add glucose syrup and mix in — this keeps the core from freezing too hard.
4. Churn the chilled base in your ice cream machine. Five minutes before the end of the churn, remove the frozen fudge block from the freezer and grate it. Add the grated fudge flecks into the machine to distribute throughout the ice cream.
5. Transfer the churned ice cream to a tub or container. Insert a plastic tube (sealed at the bottom) down through the centre to form a well for the core. Freeze for 10–15 minutes to firm up around the mould.
6. Remove the plastic tube. Pour the liquid fudge core mix into the cavity.
7. Make the crack top: gently melt white chocolate with the water to make a water ganache. Stir in green food colouring.
8. Pour the green chocolate ganache over the top of the ice cream. Freeze for at least 4 hours until the crack top is fully hardened.
9. Smash through the crack top to serve.

**Notes:**
- Use good-quality marshmallows with a strong vanilla flavour — cheaper marshmallows don't melt down properly and won't give the same result.
- The dark chocolate fudge melts very quickly at room temperature — work fast when grating it and return it to the freezer immediately if needed.
- Glucose syrup in the fudge core is key: it lowers the freezing point so the core stays soft and scoopable rather than rock-hard.
- The water ganache (white chocolate + a small amount of water) creates the crackable top — the small water addition is deliberate to adjust the set texture.
- Nick noted in hindsight he should have called it a "Smash Top" rather than a "Crack Top" — a nod to the Hulk.
- This is a creative interpretation; there is no official recipe for Hunka Hulka Burning Fudge — it's a fictional ice cream from the Avengers films.
---

### [Baileys Ice Cream — Häagen Dazs Baileys Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=a5oA0oePmis)
- **500g** cream or Baileys-flavored extra thick cream
- **150g** sugar (if using Baileys cream, which contains ~50g sugar; use 190g sugar if using plain cream)
- **65g** skimmed milk powder
- **40g** Baileys Irish Cream liqueur (or store-own Irish cream liqueur)
- **1g** xanthan gum

**Steps:**

1. Mix the sugar and skimmed milk powder together dry, combining them thoroughly so they are well incorporated.
2. Pour the milk into a saucepan and begin heating over medium heat.
3. Add the sugar-powder mixture into the warming milk — it dissolves almost instantly when pre-mixed this way.
4. Spoon in the thick Baileys-flavored cream and stir continuously until fully emulsified and dissolved.
5. Remove from heat, transfer to a container, and allow to cool slightly before refrigerating for 3–4 hours until completely chilled.
6. Stir in the Baileys Irish Cream liqueur and give the base a final mix.
7. Churn in an ice cream machine — took approximately 12 minutes (alcohol slows freezing; yours may take slightly longer).
8. Transfer churned ice cream to a tub and freeze overnight to harden fully.
9. Scoop and serve.

**Notes:**

- The Baileys-flavored extra thick cream has higher butterfat than standard UK double cream; the fat layer forming on top after chilling confirms this.
- Nick used a store-own Irish cream liqueur rather than branded Baileys — cheaper and, in his opinion, better flavour.
- Alcohol lowers the freezing point: the ice cream won't go as hard as a non-alcoholic batch, making it easier to scoop straight from the freezer.
- Safe alcohol threshold for churnable ice cream is roughly 75ml per litre of mix. Adding more requires reducing sugar to compensate for the extra PAC (anti-freezing power), which affects solids balance.
- If not using the Baileys-flavored cream, increase sugar from 150g to 190g to compensate for the missing sugar content in the cream.

---

### [Banoffee Ice Cream LOVERS Rejoice!](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mRASaquHb7Q)

Banoffee pie ice cream made without actual banana — roasted banana flesh is steeped in the base overnight to extract deep flavor, then removed before churning. Made for both traditional ice cream machine and Ninja Creami.

**Note: Batch recipe** — makes enough for one traditional churn + one Ninja Creami pint (roughly 2 pints total).

**Ingredients — Ice Cream Base:**
- **537g** milk (2%)
- **550g** whipping cream (33% fat)
- **230g** sugar
- **25g** corn syrup (or golden syrup / inverted sugar)
- **55g** skim milk powder
- **2g** ice cream stabilizer (Special Ingredients brand used; any cold- or hot-process stabilizer works)
- **3 medium bananas** (for steeping only — removed before churning)

**Ingredients — Graham Cracker Crust:**
- **200g** graham cracker crumbs (or digestive biscuits / hobnobs)
- **80g** butter, melted
- **20g** sugar

**Ingredients — Assembly:**
- Dulce de leche (real South American-style preferred — not Eagle Brand canned)

**Steps:**

1. Preheat oven to 350F (175C). Place whole unpeeled bananas on a lined baking sheet. Roast 10–15 minutes until skins are basically black. This draws sugars to the surface and develops deep banana flavor via the Maillard reaction.
2. While bananas roast, combine milk, cream, sugar (reserving 10g to mix with stabilizer), corn syrup, and skim milk powder in a saucepan. Mix the stabilizer with the reserved 10g sugar, then add to the pan. Heat on the hob to 85C (185F), stirring continuously.
3. Pour the hot ice cream base into a large bowl. Squeeze the roasted banana flesh out of the skins (discard skins) and add the flesh directly to the hot base. Steep overnight in the refrigerator — do not blend.
4. Mix graham cracker crumbs, melted butter, and sugar together until it resembles wet sand. Press firmly into a small lined pan to form a compact block. Bake at 350F for 10–12 minutes (watch carefully — it burns quickly). Cool, then refrigerate overnight.
5. Next day: remove the banana flesh pieces from the base (they hold together well — just lift out). Reserve base liquid only.
6. Fill one Ninja Creami pint with the strained base. Pour the rest into the traditional ice cream machine and churn.
7. Cut the chilled graham cracker slab into ~1cm cubes.
8. Warm the dulce de leche briefly in the microwave to make it pourable.
9. **For the churned version:** Add graham cracker cubes during the last few minutes of churning. Layer ice cream, dulce de leche, and cookie crumb pieces when filling tubs. Freeze to harden.
10. **For the Ninja Creami:** Freeze the filled pint for at least 24 hours. Spin on Ice Cream mode. Add cookie crumb cubes and drizzle dulce de leche on top after spinning — do not run Mix-In (the buttery crumb and dulce de leche will disintegrate completely in the machine).

**Notes:**
- The banana is used purely for flavor infusion — no banana texture in the final ice cream. Banana extract can substitute if you prefer, but roasting gives a more natural, rounded result.
- Isoamyl acetate is the compound responsible for banana flavor; roasting encourages it to release along with surface sugars.
- Use real South American dulce de leche (thick, pale, creamy). Eagle Brand canned dulce de leche is thin, tastes burnt, and is not recommended.
- Golden syrup or golden corn syrup is preferred over plain corn syrup for an additional flavor note.
- The graham cracker crust sugar melts during baking and creates a more cohesive slab that cuts into clean cubes without crumbling too fine.
- The Ninja Creami version melts faster than the churned version due to lack of air overrun — serve quickly once scooped.
- Nick notes the churned version has better finesse and melt behavior; the Creami version is denser and fudgier but still tastes great.

---

### [Basil Ice Cream Recipe from 'The Perfect Scoop'](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=UiLIo41cbLo)

Basil ice cream adapted from David Lebovitz's *The Perfect Scoop* — made with stabilizer instead of egg yolks, using a traditional ice cream machine.

**Ingredients:**
- **25g** fresh basil
- **150g** sugar
- **250g** whipping cream (blended with basil)
- **250g** heavy cream (combined separately)
- **250g** 2% milk
- **1 pinch** salt
- **ice cream stabilizer** (amount not stated — pre-mixed with a little sugar before adding)
- **zest of 1 lemon**

**Steps:**
1. Combine the basil, sugar, and 250g whipping cream. Blend to as smooth a puree as possible.
2. Divide the blended basil mixture in half. Pour one half into a bowl.
3. Add the 250g heavy cream to the bowl and stir to combine. Set aside in the fridge.
4. Pour the remaining blended half into a pan.
5. Pre-mix the stabilizer with a small amount of sugar (a teaspoon or two) to prevent clumping.
6. Add the 250g milk, a pinch of salt, and the pre-mixed stabilizer to the pan. Stir to combine.
7. Heat the pan on the hob, whisking, until the mixture reaches 80°C (to hydrate the stabilizer).
8. Remove from heat. Add the lemon zest and stir to combine.
9. Combine the heated pan mixture with the cold cream bowl mixture. Stir well.
10. Chill completely in the fridge.
11. Once fully chilled, stir again to recombine, then churn in an ice cream machine (~12 minutes).
12. Transfer to a container and freeze.

**Notes:**
- Nick substituted stabilizer for egg yolks — stabilizer gives a cleaner basil flavor since egg yolks add their own taste that competes with delicate herbs.
- The lemon zest is important: it cuts through and brightens the basil without making it taste lemon-flavored — just a subtle lift at the finish.
- Basil flavor is naturally strong; the dairy fat tones it down to a pleasant, lingering level rather than overpowering.
- Nick made two versions (with and without egg yolks) and preferred the stabilizer version for complexity.
- The heated basil intensifies its green color.
- Pre-mixing any powdered stabilizer with sugar (or milk powder) before adding to liquid is essential — adding it straight to liquid causes clumping and it won't function.
- Classic 2:1 cream-to-milk ratio from the original book recipe.
- Recipe source: David Lebovitz, *The Perfect Scoop*.

---

### [Beautiful Costco Salted Caramel Gelato](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-ZVcWs7Xsh0)

Rich, creamy gelato built around Costco's Salted Butter Caramel sauce — just four ingredients, no extra stabilizers needed.

**Ingredients:**

- **225g** Costco Salted Butter Caramel sauce (French, from Costco — contains sugar, glucose syrup, condensed milk, cream, carrageenan, butter, salt)
- **600g** milk (2% semi-skimmed or whole)
- **250g** cream, 33–36% fat (double cream also works — slightly higher fat)
- **30g** skim milk powder (SMP)

**Mix-in / garnish (optional):**

- Valrhona Dulcey 35% blonde chocolate (caramelised white/milk hybrid), melted and drizzled — or substitute Caramac or any caramel-flavoured chocolate

**Steps:**

1. Combine the milk, cream, and skim milk powder in a bowl. Whisk together until the powder is dissolved.
2. Warm the Costco caramel sauce briefly in the microwave (~1 minute on high, repeat if needed) until it is fluid enough to blend into the dairy.
3. Add the warmed caramel to the dairy mixture and stir/whisk until fully incorporated and uniform caramel colour throughout.
4. Pour the base into Ninja Creami pints and freeze overnight (minimum).
5. Spin on Gelato or Ice Cream setting (Nick notes the speed is identical — either works).
6. Optional mix-in: use a spoon to hollow a well in the centre of the spun pint, pour in melted Dulcey/caramel chocolate, then re-spin or fold in.
7. Dish up and garnish with extra chocolate drizzle or flakes if desired.

**Notes:**

- No additional stabilizer required — the caramel sauce already contains carrageenan, and the cream also has carrageenan. Adding more is unnecessary.
- Serving temperature: -14°C / 6.8°F (warmer than typical ice cream — allow a couple of minutes out of the freezer).
- Nick's reported metrics: 7.5% fat, 20% sugars, 9.1% MSNF, 37% solids, 64% water.
- If Costco caramel is unavailable, a Caramac bar or any caramel-flavoured chocolate melted into the base would be a good substitute.
- Nick used the Ninja Creami OG (not Deluxe) in this video — behaviour is the same on Deluxe.

---

### [Beautiful Custard Apple Gelato - Chirimoya, Anone, Sitafal, Srikaya](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6-7oHRYljU8)

Cold-process custard apple (atamoya) gelato — all ingredients blended raw and spun on the Ice Cream setting.

**Ingredients:**

- **375g** custard apple flesh (atamoya/cherimoya), strained and deseeded (~3 ripe fruit)
- **321g** whole milk
- **254g** whipping cream / heavy cream (33%+ fat)
- **128g** sugar
- **45g** glucose syrup (corn syrup or golden syrup work)
- **55g** skim milk powder
- **2g** guar gum

**Steps:**

1. Allow fruit to ripen at room temperature until soft to the touch (like a ripe avocado) — 3–4 days from firm.
2. Halve the fruit, remove all seeds, and scoop out only the soft creamy white flesh. Discard the outer skin/shell.
3. Pre-mix the guar gum with the sugar to break up any clumps before blending.
4. Add all ingredients (fruit flesh, milk, cream, sugar mixture, glucose syrup, skim milk powder) into a blender.
5. Blend on low for 1 minute, then on high for 1–2 minutes until completely smooth.
6. Strain through a fine-mesh sieve to remove any remaining seed fragments or fibrous particles.
7. Pour into the Ninja Creami pint container and freeze for at least 24 hours.
8. Process on the Ice Cream setting.

**Notes:**

- Atamoya is a hybrid of cherimoya and sugar apple — sweeter and creamier than either parent. Cherimoya, sitaphal (sitafal), anon, and srikaya are all related varieties and can be used interchangeably.
- Sugar content of the fruit is ~14g per 100g (similar to mango) — factor this in if adjusting sweetener levels.
- Always wait for full ripeness before using — underripe fruit will lack sweetness and the characteristic custard texture.
- Glucose syrup prevents ice crystal formation and improves scoopability.
- Mixing guar gum into the sugar before adding to the blender prevents clumping.
- Flavor profile: tropical, creamy, with notes of pineapple, green apple, banana, and a hint of papaya.
- Result was described as incredibly smooth texture with a very clean, delicate flavor.
---

### [Ferrero Raffaello Ice Cream with Raspberry White Chocolate Ganache Swirl](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nl9XgEgL4x0).

**Ingredients:**

Ice Cream Base:
- **90g** Raffaello chocolate bar (from a 190g bar)
- **387g** whole milk (split: ~200g for melting chocolate, ~187g added after)
- **140g** heavy cream / whipping cream (33%+)
- **97g** sugar
- **11g** corn syrup or glucose syrup
- **20g** skim milk powder
- **3g** coconut flavoring (glycerin-based; Nick uses Frontier Co-op)
- **1g** salt
- Guar gum (a pinch, mixed with sugar — amount not specified)

Raspberry White Chocolate Ganache (separate video — full recipe at https://youtu.be/VmFSDXQJE9s):
- **300g** white chocolate couverture
- **130g** 35% cream
- **40g** glucose syrup
- **40g** unsalted butter (soft, room temperature)
- **20–25g** dehydrated raspberry powder
- **15g** honey
- **1g** citric acid
- **0.5g** salt

**Steps:**

1. Measure ~200g of milk into a jug. Break up the Raffaello bar and add it to the milk.
2. Microwave in 30-second blasts (about 3 rounds / 90 seconds total) until the chocolate is soft enough to stir.
3. Stir until the chocolate is fully melted into the warm milk.
4. Add the remaining ~187g milk and the 140g cream. Stir to combine.
5. In a separate small bowl, mix the sugar with the guar gum, then stir that into the base.
6. Add the skim milk powder and stir until dissolved (residual heat is sufficient; will fully dissolve in about 30 minutes even cold).
7. Add the coconut flavoring (3g) and salt (1g). Stir to combine.
8. Add the corn syrup / glucose syrup and stir through.
9. Pour into a Ninja Creami Deluxe pint tub. Freeze overnight (minimum).
10. Spin on the Ice Cream setting.
11. Dig a tunnel down the center of the spun ice cream with a spoon.
12. Spoon or pipe the raspberry white chocolate ganache into the tunnel.
13. Swirl the ganache through the ice cream by hand using a spoon — do NOT use the Mix-In setting.

**Notes:**

- The coconut flavoring is a glycerin-based extract (not a water-based extract); Nick uses Frontier Co-op brand.
- The coconut extract boosts the coconut flavor already present in the Raffaello coating.
- The recipe makes approximately 750g / 750ml — sized for the Ninja Creami Deluxe. For the standard (non-Deluxe) Ninja Creami, remove one third of all ingredients.
- Do not use the Mix-In setting to incorporate the ganache — it will completely blend it into the base, eliminating the swirl. Hand-swirl only.
- If you don't want to make the full ganache, substitute: fresh raspberries boiled down with sugar, thickened with guar gum, xanthan, or tapioca starch (not corn starch — it makes the sauce semi-opaque). Pipe into the tunnel and hand-swirl.
- Skim milk powder dissolves at fridge temperature; it will fully dissolve in about 30 minutes even in cold liquid.
- Guar gum shelf life: Nick notes his was over 3 years old and still effective.

---

### [Raspberry & Cream Gelato with Homemade Chocolate Fudge Pieces](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=i2OmigGCEd4)
- **460g** whole milk (can substitute 2% / semi-skimmed)
- **70g** skim milk powder
- **172g** sugar
- **10g** dextrose, glucose syrup, corn syrup, or honey (one of these)
- **2–3g** vanilla extract
- **100g** homemade chocolate fudge (see fudge recipe: https://youtu.be/4gs4oOrHYbA)
- A couple of tablespoons of water (for cooking the raspberries)

**Steps:**

1. Place raspberries in a pan with the sugar and a couple of tablespoons of water. Heat over medium, stirring and breaking the berries down, until the mixture comes to a light boil and the sugar is fully dissolved — about 5–10 minutes.
2. Remove from heat. Transfer the hot raspberry syrup to a food processor or blender. Pulse on low for 10–20 seconds, then run on high for a further 10–15 seconds until smooth.
3. In a separate pan, combine the milk, cream, skim milk powder, and dextrose/glucose/corn syrup/honey. Place on the hob over gentle heat.
4. Pour the hot raspberry syrup into the milk and cream mixture. Stir well to combine — the mixture will turn a soft pink.
5. Return the combined base to the hob and heat gently to 50–60°C (until steaming), ensuring all fats, sugars, and solids are fully emulsified. This takes only a couple of minutes.
6. Stir in the vanilla extract (2–3g). Transfer to a container.
7. Cool, then refrigerate for at least 4 hours to fully chill and allow flavours to bond.
8. Once fully chilled, strain the base through a fine-mesh strainer to remove raspberry seeds and any solids.
9. Churn in a pre-chilled ice cream machine.
10. While the base is churning, cut 100g of chocolate fudge — use a cheese grater (fine side) for smaller shards and a knife for larger chunks. Work quickly as the fudge melts in your hands. Refrigerate the chopped fudge for 5–10 minutes to firm back up.
11. At the end of the churning phase, add all the fudge pieces into the machine. Churn briefly to fold in.
12. Dish up and serve, or pack into a container and freeze.

**Notes:**

- Raspberries are acidic and relatively low in sugar — not much balancing needed, but the sugar quantity is still important for texture and sweetness.
- Can be made sugar-free: substitute sugar with allulose 1:1, or xylitol at ~70% of the sugar weight (reduce by ~30%).
- Adding a splash of water to the raspberries at the start helps draw out the juices and dissolve the sugar faster.
- No stabiliser is used in this recipe — no need to heat to any specific pasteurisation temperature; just bring to steaming (50–60°C) to emulsify.
- Adding the raspberry syrup to the warm (not yet fully hot) base is fine — the hot syrup brings the whole mixture up to temperature together.
- Optional: add a small amount of red food colouring to boost the colour — only a drop or two needed. Nick keeps it natural here.
- The fudge is specifically formulated to stay soft (not rock-hard) when frozen — it melts quickly at room temperature, so work fast and chill before adding.
- Chop fudge using both a fine cheese grater (for smaller pieces) and a knife (for larger chunks) to get a mix of textures throughout.
- Fudge recipe (separate video): https://youtu.be/4gs4oOrHYbA — Nick makes large batches (~1.5kg) and keeps it in the freezer.
---

### [Ben & Jerry's Cherry Garcia - Ice Cream Perfection](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=37S4AS70O_Y)
- **300ml** milk (1.8% fat)
- **80g** sugar
- **60g** cherry concentrate (homemade — see Steps)
- **2 tsp** cherry liqueur (optional)
- **2 tbsp** skimmed milk powder
- **50g** 70% dark chocolate, chopped
- **50g** Opie's black cherries in Luxardo kirsch, chopped (Ben & Jerry's use Bing Cherries; UK version uses maraschino/Luxardo)

**Steps:**

1. Make the cherry concentrate: remove stalks from fresh cherries, place in a pan with a little water and a little sugar. Bring to a rolling boil for 5–10 minutes to soften and release the juice.
2. Strain out the cherry flesh. Return the syrup to the pan and reduce at a rolling boil for a further 5–10 minutes. Optional: add a small amount of pectin during this second boil for more body. Decant into a small container and set aside.
3. Combine milk and sugar in a pan. Heat to dissolve the sugar. Stir in the skimmed milk powder and cherry concentrate. Adjust quantity of concentrate until you reach the desired deep pink colour.
4. Remove from heat and stir in the cream — this cools the mix quickly so it can go straight into the fridge without temperature concerns.
5. Chill the base completely in the fridge (2–4 hours).
6. Churn in an ice cream machine for 15–30 minutes depending on your machine.
7. Once churned, fold in the chopped Opie's black cherries and chopped chocolate.
8. Layer into a container, seal, and freeze for several hours (or overnight/multiple days) until fully set.

**Notes:**

- The cherry concentrate is critical — the original Ben & Jerry's base is quite mild in cherry flavour, so a deeply reduced, punchy syrup is needed to get real cherry taste into the base.
- Cherry variety makes a big difference to flavour intensity. UK cherries can be bland; reduce harder and longer if needed. Bing cherries (used by B&J originally) are preferred but hard to find in the UK.
- Opie's black cherries soaked in Luxardo kirsch are used here instead of plain maraschino cherries — they don't freeze as hard as plain cherries soaked in sugar syrup (a lesson from the first failed attempt).
- The US version of Cherry Garcia uses fudge flecks; the UK version uses chocolate chunks — this recipe goes with chopped 70% chocolate.
- Adding the cream cold into the hot base (rather than heating it) lets you skip a long cooling wait before refrigerating.

---

### [Ben & Jerry's Chocolate Fudge Brownie - make at home](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Cd9Pvt5btPQ)

A full recreation of B&J's Chocolate Fudge Brownie — homemade gooey malt brownies folded into a medium-dark chocolate ice cream base.

---

**Gooey Brownies:**

- **120g** all-purpose flour
- **60g** diastatic malt powder
- **50g** dark chocolate cocoa powder
- **200g** semisweet chocolate
- **57g** dark chocolate
- **250g** granulated sugar
- **50g** brown sugar
- **4** eggs
- **260g** butter

**Ice Cream Base:**

- **60g** dark cocoa powder @ 24% fat (Calibo brand used; 55g primary + 5g dark/black cocoa — Van Gaal 100, 12% fat)
- **300g** double cream @ 49% fat (UK; US/Canada substitute: 500g whipping cream + reduce milk to 450g)
- **165g** sugar
- **550g** whole milk @ 1.8% fat (UK; US/Canada: 450g)
- **50g** skimmed milk powder
- **50g** glucose syrup
- **2** egg yolks
- **1g** stabilizer (optional)

---

**Steps:**

**Brownies:**
1. Melt the semisweet chocolate, dark chocolate, and butter together in a microwave (or bain-marie) until fully melted.
2. Add in the eggs and sugar; combine well.
3. Add all dry ingredients (flour, malt powder, cocoa powder) and mix until fully incorporated.
4. Pour into a lined 8×8 inch (20×20 cm) baking pan.
5. Bake at 200°C (385°F) for 25 minutes. Do not exceed this temperature.
6. Remove from oven, cool slightly, then cut into ~1–1.5 cm cubes. Cool completely before use.

**Ice Cream Base:**
1. Add glucose syrup to a saucepan, then pour milk in on top.
2. Whisk egg yolks until smooth and add them to the pan.
3. Add the cocoa powder (both types if using the two-cocoa approach) and whisk to combine.
4. Add skimmed milk powder and mix thoroughly.
5. Heat gently, stirring, until the mixture reaches 65°C.
6. Remove from heat and pour into a container. Add the double cream and stir to combine.
7. Add sugar and stabilizer (if using); mix well.
8. Chill in the fridge for at least 4 hours.
9. Churn in an ice cream machine until soft-serve consistency.
10. Layer churned ice cream and brownie cubes into a tub and freeze for several hours before serving.

---

**Notes:**

- This is a batch recipe — makes more brownies than needed for one batch of ice cream. Leftovers keep well.
- The diastatic malt powder is key to replicating the subtle maltiness in the original B&J brownie. Nick discovered this note in the real product and reverse-engineered it from the ingredients list.
- B&J's brownies are made by Greyston Bakery in Yonkers, NY — Nick adapted a Greyston-style recipe with the malt addition.
- Two cocoa powders used for the ice cream: a high-fat primary cocoa (Calibo 24%) for bulk flavor, plus a small amount of intense black cocoa (Van Gaal 100, 12% fat) to deepen color and achieve a medium-dark chocolate profile.
- B&J's chocolate ice creams are formulated to ~13–15% fat, ~16–18% sugar. The recipe is built to approximate these ratios.
- For US/Canada: swap 300g double cream + 550g milk for 500g whipping cream + 450g milk — approximate fat equivalence.
- Do not exceed 200°C when baking the brownies — they should be gooey, not fully set.
- Pan size matters: recipe is calibrated for an 8×8 pan. Larger pans will need reduced bake time.

---

### [Ben & Jerry's Cookie Dough Twist: A Decadent Delight](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VJBAtzcF8f4)

A churned chocolate ice cream base inspired by Ben & Jerry's Dough-ble Chocolate Cookie Dough Twist, paired with a no-bake chocolate cookie dough core.

**Ingredients:**

Ice Cream Base:
- **250g** double/heavy cream
- **300g** water
- **40g** sugar
- **65g** dextrose (or use 110g total sugar if no dextrose)
- **50g** cocoa powder, 12% fat (De Zaan)
- **100g** condensed milk
- **2** egg yolks
- **50g** chocolate chips, 56% cocoa (Barima), added at end of churn
- **0.7g** locust bean gum (optional)
- **0.45g** guar gum (optional — blended with the sugars as a 70/30 guar/carrageenan mix)

Cookie Dough Core:
- **60g** unsalted butter, room temperature
- **15g** sugar (white)
- **55g** brown sugar
- **5g** salt
- **30g** milk
- **15g** golden syrup (or regular corn syrup — not high-fructose)
- **5g** vanilla extract
- **70–90g** all-purpose/plain flour (heat-treated: bake at 180°C for 10 min, cool completely)
- **50g** cocoa powder, 12% fat
- **60g** semi-sweet chocolate chips

**Steps:**

Ice Cream Base:
1. Add water to a pan. Add dextrose, sugar, and stabilizers (mixed dry into the sugars). Heat and dissolve.
2. Add condensed milk and egg yolks. Stir continuously.
3. Add cocoa powder as the mix heats up. Cook to 65–70°C to pasteurise the egg yolks.
4. Add cream and stir to combine. Remove from heat.
5. Transfer to a container, refrigerate until fully chilled (3–4 hours).
6. Churn in ice cream machine as normal. Add chocolate chips just before the end of the churn.

Cookie Dough Core:
1. Beat room-temperature butter with white sugar and brown sugar until well combined.
2. Mix in vanilla extract and golden syrup.
3. Add cocoa powder and heat-treated flour. Mix well.
4. Fold in chocolate chips.
5. Transfer mixture to a bag and refrigerate until cooled and firm.
6. Roll into a cylinder shape. Freeze until solid.

Assembly:
1. Fill the tub with churned ice cream, leaving about 2.5cm (1 inch) of space at the top.
2. Press the frozen cookie dough cylinder straight down into the centre of the tub.
3. Top up any gaps with remaining ice cream mix.
4. Freeze for several hours until fully hardened.

**Notes:**
- The base uses no milk — condensed milk and water replace it, following Ben & Jerry's actual ingredient list.
- The base will be noticeably thick and viscous before churning; this is expected and typically yields a creamier, less icy result.
- Heat-treat flour at 180°C for 10 minutes and cool completely before use — do not sieve it while still hot.
- Golden syrup can be substituted with light corn syrup; avoid high-fructose corn syrup.
- Barima 56% chocolate chips and De Zaan cocoa powder were used in the original and praised for flavour.
- Nick rated this significantly better than the actual Ben & Jerry's product.
---

### [Ben & Jerry's Rain-Dough Ice Cream Recipe](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0-b6eL6aNaU)

A churned vanilla ice cream base inspired by Ben & Jerry's Rain-Dough Cookie Dough Twist, with a sprinkle-loaded no-bake cookie dough core and multi-coloured Deco Melt chocolate drops. Batch recipe (not single-pint).

**Ingredients:**

Ice Cream Base:
- **500ml** double (heavy) cream
- **250ml** whole milk
- **200g** granulated sugar
- **1–2 tsp** vanilla bean paste
- **2 tbsp** non-fat powdered milk / skimmed milk powder

Cookie Dough Core:
- **136g** all-purpose flour, heat-treated (microwave or oven at 180°C) to kill bacteria
- **113g** unsalted butter, softened
- **136g** granulated sugar
- **1/4 tsp** salt
- **1.5 tbsp** milk, plus more as needed
- **1/2 tsp** vanilla extract
- **36g** rainbow sprinkles

Coloured Chocolate Drops:
- Deco Melts (baking candy melts), assorted colours — make plenty, more than you think you need

**Steps:**

Ice Cream Base:
1. Combine cream, milk, and sugar in a pan. Heat gently until sugar dissolves.
