### [Chocolate Orange Cheesecake Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=onCyCQJldH4)

A churned chocolate ice cream base layered with blended leftover chocolate orange cheesecake (mixed with crème de cacao and double cream) and digestive biscuit crumb. Inspired by a homemade Christmas cheesecake — presented as an improvised use of leftovers rather than a repeatable cheesecake-from-scratch recipe.

**Note: This is a large batch recipe (multiple pints). Scale down as needed.**

**Ice Cream Base Ingredients:**
- **550g** whole milk
- **400g** heavy/double cream
- **180g** sugar
- **50g** dextrose
- **60g** SMP (skim milk powder)
- **75g** cocoa powder (Nick uses Cacao Barry Brute)

**Cheesecake Core Ingredients:**
- Leftover chocolate orange cheesecake (baked, with chocolate cookie crumb base and orange zest filling)
- **30–50ml** crème de cacao (Mozart chocolate liqueur used; can substitute vodka or any spirit, or omit)
- Double cream, as needed to loosen

**Mix-In:**
- Digestive biscuits, broken into crumbs (US/Canada: graham crackers)

**Steps:**

1. Add milk to a saucepan over heat. As it warms, whisk in the dextrose and sugar until dissolved.
2. When the milk reaches ~50°C, whisk in the cocoa powder until fully combined.
3. Add the SMP and continue stirring until fully incorporated (it takes time to dissolve).
4. Remove from heat and transfer to a container. Add the cold double cream — this brings the temperature down quickly.
5. Refrigerate overnight.
6. The next day, take frozen/cold leftover cheesecake and break into chunks. Add to a small blender with 30–50ml crème de cacao and a splash of double cream. Blend until very smooth, adding more cream if needed to loosen. Set aside.
7. Remove the chilled ice cream base from the fridge, give it a final stir to ensure it's fully emulsified.
8. Churn in an ice cream machine until done (Nick's batch took ~9 minutes).
9. Layer into containers: scoop of churned ice cream → dollop of blended cheesecake mixture → sprinkling of broken digestive biscuit crumb. Repeat until the tub is full.
10. Freeze overnight before serving.

**Notes:**

- This recipe uses leftover cheesecake as the core — Nick acknowledges you may not want to make an entire cheesecake just for this. The video is meant as inspiration for using up leftovers or adapting with your own cheesecake base.
- If repeating, Nick would reduce the milk and increase the alcohol (crème de cacao) in the cheesecake blend for better flavour and softer texture.
- Chocolate ice cream churns faster than vanilla-based recipes — Nick's batch set in ~9 minutes.
- Digestive biscuits are the UK equivalent of graham crackers. Use whichever is local to you.
- The blended cheesecake mix should be thick but pourable — add cream in small amounts to reach that consistency.
- Result was described as "pretty good but could be improved," mainly around the cheesecake core proportions and alcohol level.

---

### [Chocolate Orange Cinnamon Rolls + Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=uiKE7zYouXI)

Chocolate orange cinnamon buns with cream cheese frosting, served with or churned into a vanilla cinnamon ice cream. Batch recipe — bun dough yields multiple rolls.

---

#### Cinnamon Bun — Chocolate Dough

**Ingredients:**
- **60g** unsalted butter
- **220g** whole milk
- **11g** fast-action dried yeast (1 packet)
- **50g** caster sugar
- **1** medium egg, room temperature
- **1** medium egg yolk, room temperature
- **460g** strong white bread flour, plus more for dusting
- **50g** cocoa powder (Menier 100% Cocoa Powder)
- **5g** salt

**Steps:**
1. Melt the butter, then warm the milk to 50–60°C. Whisk in the yeast and leave a few minutes to hydrate.
2. Add flour, sugar, salt, cocoa powder, whole egg, and egg yolk to the stand mixer bowl with the dough hook. Pour in the butter, then the milk-yeast mixture.
3. Mix on medium speed for about 7 minutes until the dough comes away from the sides of the bowl and is smooth and springy.
4. Lightly oil a bowl, add the dough, cover, and leave to prove for 90 minutes to 2 hours until doubled in size.
5. Roll dough out to roughly 14 in × 9 in rectangle on a lightly floured surface.

---

#### Cinnamon Bun — Orange Cinnamon Sugar Filling

**Ingredients:**
- **60g** unsalted butter, room temperature
- **120g** light brown sugar / muscovado sugar
- **20g** ground cinnamon
- **1** large orange, zest only

**Steps:**
1. Mix cinnamon into the brown sugar until evenly coated (cinnamon coats the molasses, making it less sticky and easier to spread).
2. Smear softened butter over the rolled-out dough, then sprinkle the cinnamon sugar evenly across the surface.
3. Grate the orange zest over the top.
4. Roll the dough tightly along the long edge into a cylinder. Trim the ends and cut into 1½-inch slices.
5. Place rolls in a baking pan. Leave to prove again for another hour.
6. Bake until cooked through. Cool before frosting.

**Notes:**
- If making a larger batch, freeze rolls after cooling but before frosting — they keep for months.

---

#### Cinnamon Bun — Chocolate Orange Cream Cheese Frosting

**Ingredients:**
- **50g** milk chocolate (Menier Swiss Milk Chocolate)
- **75g** full-fat cream cheese, room temperature
- **50g** icing sugar (powdered sugar)
- **1** large orange, juice only (use the orange zested for the filling)

**Steps:**
1. Warm the cream cheese in the microwave for about 20 seconds until malleable.
2. Beat together cream cheese and icing sugar until combined.
3. Melt the milk chocolate. Pour into the cream cheese mixture and stir until fully combined.
4. Add orange juice and mix through. Spread or drizzle over cooled cinnamon buns.

---

#### Vanilla Cinnamon Ice Cream (Ninja Creami / churned)

Condensed milk base — super simple.

**Ingredients:**
- **350g** whole milk
- **350g** whipping cream
- **300ml** sweetened condensed milk
- **1½ tsp** vanilla bean paste
- **½ tsp** ground cinnamon (adjust to taste)

**Steps:**
1. Whisk all ingredients together until completely combined.
2. **For Ninja Creami:** Pour into a pint tub, freeze solid (24h+), then spin on Ice Cream mode.
3. **For churned:** Pre-chill machine, pour in the base, churn until set.
4. To assemble: Chop cooled cinnamon buns into small cubes. Load chunks between layers of spun/churned ice cream. Do not add bun pieces before spinning — add after the spin is complete to keep them chunky.

**Notes:**
- Cinnamon bun pieces should be refrigerator-cold (not frozen) when added as mix-in after spinning.
- Do not use the Mix-In program on the Ninja Creami for the bun pieces — it will decimate them. Fold in manually after spinning.
- The vanilla bean paste boosts the cinnamon flavour.
- Nick uses a traditional ice cream machine for the video demo, but explicitly confirms the Ninja Creami method works: pour, freeze, spin as normal.

---

### [Chunky Monkey Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sEjTGBYrgRY)
Ben & Jerry's Chunky Monkey dupe: banana ice cream base steeped with roasted bananas, loaded with chopped walnuts and chocolate banana shapes. Batch recipe using a traditional ice cream churn.

**Note: Batch recipe — uses a traditional ice cream churn, not a Ninja Creami.**

**Ingredients:**
- **500g** whole milk
- **300g** cream (heavy or whipping)
- **155g** sugar
- **20g** glucose syrup
- **40g** skimmed milk powder
- **2** egg yolks (free-range, organic or corn-fed — must be orange/deep yellow)
- **4** large ripe bananas (ideally very ripe; can roast to intensify flavour)
- **50g** walnuts, chopped
- **50g** chocolate banana shapes (or any chocolate fruit shapes from a silicone mold)

**Steps:**
1. If bananas are not very ripe, roast them in their skins at 175°C for 10–15 minutes to concentrate sugars and strengthen flavour. Remove from oven and allow to cool slightly.
2. Combine milk, cream, sugar, glucose, skimmed milk powder, and egg yolks in a saucepan. Heat, bringing just to the point of boiling, then hold for a couple of minutes. Remove from heat and transfer to a container.
3. Scoop the flesh from the roasted bananas and add it to the warm base. Steep for a minimum of 24 hours in the fridge.
4. After 24 hours, strain out the banana solids. This infuses pure banana flavour without pulp chunks or extra ice crystals.
5. Churn the strained base in an ice cream machine as normal.
6. While the base churns, make the chocolate shapes: melt chocolate in a microwave or bain-marie, fill a silicone mold (banana shapes or any fruit shapes), and freeze for ~10 minutes until hard. Pop out and store in the fridge until needed.
7. Chop walnuts to roughly 1cm pieces — smaller and they disappear, larger and they're overwhelming.
8. Once churned, layer ice cream into the tub with walnuts and chocolate shapes, stirring them in evenly. Fill the tub completely.
9. Freeze for a few hours before serving.

**Notes:**
- Egg yolks are added for colour and texture — use corn-fed or organic eggs with deep orange yolks. Heat base to 82°C if concerned about food safety with raw eggs.
- The steep-and-strain banana method (rather than blending banana into the base) is Nick's preferred technique: delivers true banana flavour with a clean, smooth texture and no ice crystals.
- Glucose syrup can be substituted with glucose powder, dextrose, corn syrup, or golden syrup — result is essentially the same.
- Chocolate shapes are optional; use whatever chocolate mix-ins you can source or make. Any silicone fruit mold works.
- Walnuts: buy pre-chopped or chop whole walnuts yourself to ~1cm.

---

### [Coffee & Donut Ice Cream DUO TUB — James Hoffmann / Dana Cree style](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=EhFEbFXiRqU)

A duo tub with one half coffee ice cream and one half donut-infused ice cream, inspired by James Hoffmann's coffee-and-donut drink and Dana Cree's donut ice cream from "Hello, My Name Is Ice Cream." Made with a traditional ice cream machine (churned).

---

**Coffee Ice Cream Ingredients:**
- **400ml** whole milk
- **200ml** double (heavy) cream
- **130g** sugar
- **25g** skimmed milk powder
- **50g** ground coffee beans (coarse) OR 2–3 tsp instant coffee granules

**Donut Ice Cream Ingredients:**
- **400ml** whole milk (plus extra — need at least 400ml after steeping/straining)
- **200ml** double (heavy) cream
- **120g** sugar
- **25g** skimmed milk powder
- **6** Krispy Kreme Original Glazed donuts (4 will work at a push)

---

**Steps:**

*Coffee Ice Cream — Bean method:*
1. Combine milk, sugar, and 50g coarse ground coffee beans in a saucepan. Heat briefly, then turn off the heat and leave to steep for 30 minutes.
2. Strain through a cheesecloth. Refrigerate until ready to use.

*Coffee Ice Cream — Instant method (alternative):*
1. Warm a small portion of the milk in the microwave. Add the sugar and instant coffee granules and stir until fully dissolved.
2. Pour the warm mix back into the remaining cold milk to bring the temperature down. Refrigerate until ready to use.

*Donut Ice Cream:*
1. Steep the donuts in the milk in the fridge for 24 hours, letting the glaze and dough flavour fully infuse.
2. Strain well, squeezing all excess milk out of the donuts. Discard the spent dough. You need at least 400ml of donut-infused milk after straining.
3. Refrigerate until ready to churn.

*Assembly (Duo Tub):*
1. Churn the coffee ice cream first. When done, load it into one half of the tub. Lay the tub on its side, cover the open face with cling film, and freeze for approximately 30 minutes.
2. While the coffee half is firming up, mix the donut milk with sugar, milk powder, and cream until the powder is fully hydrated, then churn in the ice cream machine. Stop the churn slightly earlier than fully done so the mix is still soft enough to pour.
3. Remove the tub from the freezer, take off the cling film, and fill the empty half with the donut ice cream. Press it right down to the bottom to avoid air pockets.
4. Freeze overnight before serving.

---

**Notes:**
- This is a batch/churned recipe — requires a traditional ice cream machine, not a Ninja Creami.
- The duo tub technique works with any two flavour combinations — just churn one half, freeze it firm, then churn and add the second.
- Krispy Kreme Original Glazed are Nick's preferred donut; use whatever glazed donuts you can get.
- Stopping the donut churn a few minutes early makes it easier to press into the void left by the coffee half without overfilling.
- Inspired by Dana Cree's donut ice cream ("Hello, My Name Is Ice Cream") and James Hoffmann's coffee-and-donut drink concept.

---

### [Cookies and Cream Ice Cream - Häagen Dazs](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CW5cMUVDAPo)

Churned vanilla custard base with Oreo cream center melted in, finished with chopped and powdered Oreo biscuits — a recreation of Häagen Dazs Cookies & Cream.

**Ingredients:**

- **500g** whipping cream
- **500g** milk
- **180g** sugar
- **2** egg yolks
- **50g** Oreo vanilla cream (scraped from 1 pack of Double Stuf Oreos)
- Remaining Oreo biscuits from the same pack (chopped and powdered)
- **10g** vanilla extract
- **50g** SMP (skim milk powder)

**Steps:**

1. Combine milk, sugar, egg yolks, and SMP in a saucepan. Mix well.
2. Heat to 75–85°C, stirring frequently to prevent the eggs from catching.
3. Stir in the 50g of Oreo cream centers until fully melted and incorporated.
4. Remove from heat and transfer to a container. Add the whipping cream and vanilla extract. Stir to combine.
5. Refrigerate until completely chilled.
6. Churn in an ice cream machine until soft-serve consistency.
7. While churning, chop the leftover Oreo biscuits (without cream) into small pieces. Set a small portion aside and grind to a fine powder.
8. Once at soft-serve consistency, fold or machine-add the Oreo powder and cookie pieces. (If the machine is not powerful enough, stir in by hand after churning.)
9. Transfer to a tub and freeze.

**Notes:**

- The cream is scraped from a full pack of Double Stuf Oreos — this equals roughly 50g of filling; the remaining biscuit wafers are used as mix-ins.
- Nick powders 2–3 cookie halves for the dust and chops the rest into small pieces — powder tints the base darker than Häagen Dazs' white base.
- Häagen Dazs' commercial version uses only small cookie flecks with no powder, giving a noticeably whiter appearance.
- Nick's verdict: the original Häagen Dazs tasted more like plain vanilla with sparse cookie pieces; this homemade version is more heavily speckled.
- Recipe is for a standard batch (ice cream machine, not Ninja Creami).

---

### [Cookies n Cream No Churn Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tCtGv4qJ0-4)
- **600ml** double/heavy cream
- vanilla extract (to taste)
- **1 pack** cream-filled cookies (e.g. Oreos), chopped/crushed

**Steps:**

1. Combine condensed milk and double cream in a large bowl.
2. Whisk together until the mixture is thick.
3. Add vanilla extract and mix in.
4. Fold in the chopped/crushed cookies.
5. Transfer to a freezer-safe container and freeze until solid.

**Notes:**

- No ice cream machine required — technique relies on whipping the cream and condensed milk together for structure.
- Use any cream-filled cookie of your choice.
- Nick plans to upload more no-churn flavour variations in future videos.

---

### [Creamy Pistachio Ice Cream using Monin Syrup](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Eypu322sstA)

Cooked custard base using Monin pistachio coffee syrup — a template for turning any flavored coffee syrup into ice cream.

**Ingredients:**

- **344g** 2% milk
- **317g** 33% whipping cream
- **61g** sugar
- **72g** invert sugar (corn syrup, golden syrup, or glucose syrup)
- **52g** skim milk powder
- **95g** Monin pistachio syrup
- **1** whole egg
- **1.5g** stabilizer (off-the-shelf; e.g. guar gum or similar — optional)

**Steps:**

1. Combine milk and cream in a pan on the hob.
2. Mix stabilizer with sugar, then add both to the pan.
3. Add skim milk powder and stir in.
4. Add invert sugar.
5. Crack the whole egg directly into the pan and stir to combine.
6. Add Monin pistachio syrup.
7. Heat while stirring constantly until the mixture reaches 80°C — do not stop stirring or the egg will scramble.
8. Remove from heat and pour into a container. Check the pan bottom — no cooked egg bits should remain.
9. Refrigerate until fully chilled.
10. Churn in an ice cream machine (or use Ninja Creami). Load into containers.

**Notes:**

- The Monin syrup contributes a large portion of sugar (syrups are ~66–75% sugar), so the recipe is balanced around that — account for the syrup's sugar content when adapting to other flavors.
- Some Monin syrup flavors will cause the base to split/curdle — Nick mentions a separate video on how to handle that.
- The base works as a template for any coffee/flavored syrup flavor (hazelnut, Irish cream, raspberry, butterscotch, vanilla, etc.).
- Adding the whole egg directly to the cold/warm mix and heating slowly is Nick's preferred alternative to traditional tempering — works fine at home where you have time to stir constantly.
- Sugar-free Monin syrup versions can be used but still require balancing the recipe for PAC/sweetness.
- 1L of Monin syrup yields roughly 10 batches — economical per serve.

---

### [Creamy Vegan Pistachio Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mGG6_r29I8c)

Vegan pistachio ice cream using Elmlea plant-based double cream and oat cream, with homemade pistachio paste and a hint of almond extract. Traditional churned recipe.

**Ingredients:**
- **250ml** Elmlea plant-based double cream
- **250ml** oat cream
- **200g** water
- **180g** sugar
- **5g** corn syrup
- **80g** pistachio paste (homemade — see steps below)
- **2ml** almond extract (≈ ¼ tsp)
- **20g** chopped pistachios (to fold in at end of churn)

**Steps:**
1. Toast pistachios in the oven at 150°C for 6–8 minutes. Watch carefully — do not burn. Remove and let cool for 5–10 minutes.
2. Add 80g of the toasted pistachios to a small blender. Pulse first, then blend on high into a super smooth paste. This can take up to 15 minutes. If the pistachios won't come together, add a small amount of simple syrup (or water/oil) a little at a time and keep blending until smooth.
3. In a pan, combine the water, sugar, and corn syrup. Heat gently just to dissolve the sugars.
4. Add 250ml oat cream and 80g pistachio paste to the pan. Gently warm through. The mix will not be smooth at this stage — that's normal.
5. Remove from heat. Use an immersion blender (or a balloon whisk) to emulsify the mixture until smooth and homogeneous.
6. Once properly emulsified, add the 250ml Elmlea vegan cream and 2ml almond extract. Stir together well.
7. Refrigerate the base for several hours until fully chilled.
8. Churn in an ice cream machine. Right at the end of the churn (soft-serve stage), fold in the 20g chopped pistachios.
9. Transfer to a tub and freeze.

**Notes:**
- Elmlea plant-based double cream is Nick's top recommendation for vegan cream. If unavailable, try another plant-based double cream alternative and report back.
- You can add liquid (water, simple syrup, or oil) to nut paste while blending — ignore advice saying otherwise. Add slowly and keep blending until it comes together.
- Batch recipe (not single-pint) — yields approximately 700–750ml depending on churn overrun.
- Almond extract complements pistachio strongly — don't over-do it.
---

### [Create Ben & Jerry's Tiramisu Ice Cream at Home](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KaosgB5kA70)

B&J "Tiramisu Topped" recreation — mascarpone ice cream layered with fudge swirl, shortbread pieces, espresso fudge, and a chocolate ganache top. Batch recipe (uses a traditional ice cream machine, not Ninja Creami).

---

#### Ice Cream Base

**Ingredients:**
- **500ml** whole milk *(note: description says 400ml, VTT says 500ml — VTT used here)*
- **120g** sugar (pre-mixed with stabilizer)
- **400g** mascarpone cheese (split: 200g hot, 200g cold)
- **40g** corn syrup / golden syrup
- **40g** dextrose
- **15g** skimmed milk powder
- **10g** butter
- **1.1g** stabilizer blend
- **0.5 tsp** silk gel (or another emulsifier)
- **1/4 tsp** vanilla extract
- Pinch of salt

**Steps:**
1. Add milk to a pan. Add sugar (with stabilizer already mixed in), dextrose, and corn syrup.
2. Start heating. Add skimmed milk powder, butter, and silk gel emulsifier.
3. Add 200g of mascarpone to the warm pan and stir in well.
4. Turn off the heat, then stir in the remaining 200g of cold mascarpone.
5. Transfer to a container and chill in the fridge overnight.
6. Once chilled, churn in your ice cream machine until done.

---

#### Fudge Swirl

**Ingredients:**
- **110g** sugar
- **25g** cocoa powder
- **57g** butter
- **65ml** milk
- **1.5 tbsp** corn syrup / golden syrup
- **0.5 tbsp** vanilla extract

**Steps:**
1. Add all ingredients to a pan and heat while stirring.
2. Bring to a rolling boil and let it bubble for at least 1 minute.
3. Transfer to a container and store in the fridge.

---

#### Espresso Fudge

**Ingredients:**
- **340g** 50% chocolate
- **400g** sweetened condensed milk (1 can)
- **2 tbsp** espresso powder

**Steps:**
1. Add condensed milk and chocolate to a pan. Heat until chocolate is fully melted and combined.
2. Stir in espresso powder.
3. Press into a lined 8x8 baking pan. Cool to room temperature then freeze overnight.
4. When needed, chop into small pieces and keep frozen until assembly.

---

#### Ganache Top

**Ingredients:**
- **50g** 50% chocolate
- **1 tsp** deodorized coconut oil

**Steps:**
1. Melt chocolate with coconut oil together until smooth.

---

#### Shortbread

- Shop-bought shortbread used in the video. Nick links BBC Good Food recipe (https://www.bbcgoodfood.com/recipes/shortbread-biscuits) as a homemade alternative.

---

#### Assembly

1. When ice cream is freshly churned, layer shortbread pieces and fudge swirl into the container alternating with ice cream.
2. Place chopped espresso fudge pieces on top of the ice cream.
3. Pour over the chocolate ganache.
4. Freeze for at least 4 hours before serving.

---

**Notes:**
- Adding the second half of the mascarpone cold (off the heat) helps keep the texture light and creamy.
- Silk gel is an emulsifier — helps blend the butter and fat properly. Any emulsifier works as a substitute.
- Golden syrup is a direct substitute for corn syrup (UK / no corn syrup available).
- The stabilizer is pre-mixed into the sugar before adding to the pan.
- The espresso fudge makes a large batch (8x8 pan) — only a portion goes into one tub.
- Nick notes the shortbread crunch combined with the fudge swirl is a standout element.

---

### [Black Forest Frozen Yogurt – Tart Cherries, Chocolate & Creamy Greek Yogurt](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hYa7hXhbhds)

Churned Black Forest frozen yogurt with a Greek yogurt base, cherry syrup swirl, and grated 70% dark chocolate mixed in at the end of the churn.

**Ingredients:**

Base:
- **215g** whole milk (3.5% MF)
- **474g** Greek yogurt (5% MF) — full-fat strongly recommended; lower fat makes balancing very difficult
- **85g** whipping cream (33%+ MF)
- **138g** sugar
- **17g** invert sugar (corn syrup, glucose syrup, or golden syrup)
- **5g** vanilla extract (up to 10g acceptable)
- **3g** stabilizer (Nick uses his "442 blend"; any stabilizer works — cold-process types need only ~50°C, heat-activated need 85°C / 185°F)
- **60g** skim milk powder (SMP) — intentionally high to boost MSNF (milk solids-non-fat)

Cherry syrup:
- **~100g** Fabri cherry syrup (or homemade cherry syrup — see Notes)
- **~3g** xanthan gum (pre-mixed with a small amount of sugar to prevent clumping)

Mix-in:
- **~50g** 70% dark chocolate (Callebaut or any 70%), grated and/or roughly sliced into mixed-size pieces

**Steps:**

1. Combine milk, yogurt, cream, sugar (reserve ~30g to premix stabilizer), invert sugar, stabilizer, and skim milk powder in a saucepan. Stir gently — the yogurt will make it prone to splashing.
2. Heat to 85°C (185°F) if using a heat-activated stabilizer. Cold-process stabilizer: heat only to ~50°C, enough to dissolve solids and combine everything.
3. Allow to cool for ~5 minutes off the heat — the base will thicken noticeably. Add vanilla extract once slightly cooled.
4. Transfer to a container (base may appear to separate in the fridge — this is normal for frozen yogurt). Refrigerate 3–4 hours until fully chilled.
5. Prepare cherry syrup: mix xanthan gum with a small pinch of sugar, whisk into ~100g cherry syrup. If it clumps, microwave 5–10 seconds and whisk again, or warm briefly on the hob. Refrigerate until needed.
6. Prepare chocolate: grate and roughly slice 70% dark chocolate into a mix of fine and larger pieces.
7. Once base is fully chilled, whisk it back together (it thickens in the fridge). Pour into ice cream churn machine and churn. Frozen yogurt churns faster with less overrun than standard ice cream due to high solids content.
8. Towards the end of the churn, add the dark chocolate pieces and incorporate fully.
9. Layer into serving containers: alternate scoops of frozen yogurt with small spoonfuls of cherry syrup until containers are full.
10. Freeze 3–4 hours to harden. Serve.

**Notes:**

- **Recipe was edited three times** after early versions went too hard from day 2 onward. The ingredient amounts above (published 31 May 2025) are the final corrected version and are described as "wonderful — doesn't go too hard."
- **Description also lists an alternate/earlier set of amounts** (240g milk, 385g yogurt, 85g cream, 185g sugar, 10g invert sugar, 10g vanilla, 3g stabilizer, 20g SMP) — use the VTT/final version above.
- Use 5% MF Greek yogurt. Zero to 3% MF yogurt makes balancing very difficult and is not recommended.
- Invert sugar (corn syrup, glucose syrup, or golden syrup) is key — it prevents the graininess and iciness that frozen yogurt is prone to and improves mouthfeel.
- Cherry syrup: Nick uses Fabri brand (recommended as best available). Homemade is easy — flavored sugar syrup thickened with xanthan gum.
- Xanthan gum is preferred over other stabilizers for the cherry syrup because cherries are highly acidic and some stabilizers fail in acidic environments.
- The base will look like it is separating in the fridge — this is completely normal for frozen yogurt; just whisk before churning.
- Works in a Ninja Creami or traditional churn-style machine. Overrun will be lower than ice cream due to high solids.
- Chocolate: mix of grated (fine) and sliced (larger) pieces gives textural contrast.

---

### [DELICIOUS S'mores Ice Cream for 2024](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nd2yAk8eWnw)

Toasted-marshmallow ice cream base with buttery graham cracker crumb and milk chocolate stracciatella. Batch recipe (2L / ~4 Ninja Creami Deluxe pints) — halve for 2 pints, quarter for 1 pint.

**Ingredients:**

*Ice cream base:*
- **562g** whole milk
- **857g** 33% whipping cream
- **327g** marshmallows (standard, ~60% sugar — do not substitute low/zero-sugar marshmallows)
- **110g** sugar
- **38g** dextrose (sub: glucose syrup or corn syrup)
- **105g** skimmed milk powder
- No stabilizer (gelatin in marshmallows stabilizes the base)

*Graham cracker crumb:*
- **150g** graham crackers, crushed
- **60g** butter
- **30–40g** golden syrup (sub: honey, corn syrup, or invert sugar)

*Stracciatella:*
- Milk chocolate (Nick used Valrhona + Cacao Barry milk chocolate), quantity to taste
- Small amount of coconut oil (to thin chocolate for drizzling)

**Steps:**

1. Preheat oven to 420°F / 215°C.
2. Spread marshmallows on a baking sheet. Place on the middle shelf and bake until expanded and softened (~8–10 min). Watch closely — every oven differs. Then torch or broil the tops until deeply toasted/charred (this burnt-sugar char is the key s'mores flavor).
3. Meanwhile, combine milk, cream, sugar, dextrose, and skimmed milk powder in a pan over medium heat. Warm to ~50–60°C (until steam rises and all dry ingredients are dissolved — no need to reach 85°C since there is no stabilizer).
4. Working in batches if needed, transfer some warm base into a blender, add the toasted marshmallows (in batches), and blend for ~30 seconds each time until fully emulsified. Combine all blended base together.
5. Chill base completely in the fridge.
6. **Graham cracker crumb:** Melt butter in the microwave. Stir in crushed graham crackers and golden syrup until a buttery, cohesive mix forms. Press into a baking pan and bake at the same oven temp for ~10–12 minutes until the butter and syrup just start to bubble. Do not over-bake. Cool, then chop into small (~1 cm) cubes.
7. Once base is fully chilled, stir well and churn in an ice cream machine.
8. Near the end of churning, drizzle in melted milk chocolate (thinned with a little coconut oil) to form stracciatella flakes.
9. Layer into tubs: ice cream → graham cracker crumb → chocolate drizzle → ice cream → graham cracker crumb → chocolate drizzle. Freeze.

**Notes:**

- Marshmallow sugar content matters: standard marshmallows are ~60% sugar (28g marshmallow = 17g sugar). Low-sugar or zero-sugar marshmallows will result in a rock-hard base — account for this if substituting.
- The char on the marshmallows is intentional and essential. Do not skip toasting to darkness — that is what delivers the s'mores flavor.
- The golden syrup in the crumb creates a chewy (not crumbly) texture in the frozen ice cream. Honey or corn syrup work equally well.
- For Ninja Creami use, make a half or quarter batch. The full marshmallow blend can be done in one blender pass at smaller volumes.
- Nick notes this recipe works equally well in a traditional churn machine.

---

### [Gingerbread Cookie Dough Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=WKFcEm3BQ3E)
A 12% fat custard base flavored with molasses and warm spices, folded with soft edible gingerbread cookie dough.

**Ingredients — Ice Cream Base:**
- **458g** whole milk (3.25%)
- **264g** heavy cream (33–35%)
- **65g** egg yolks
- **115g** sugar
- **17g** glucose syrup or corn syrup (golden syrup also works)
- **45g** skim milk powder (SMP)
- **26g** molasses (not blackstrap — too bitter)
- **6g** ground ginger
- **4g** cinnamon
- **1g** nutmeg
- **0.5g** ground cloves
- **1g** salt
- **10g** vanilla extract

**Ingredients — Gingerbread Cookie Dough:**
- **100g** heat-treated all-purpose flour (plain flour)
- **40g** soft butter
- **50g** brown sugar
- **25g** molasses
- **10g** milk (or gingerbread syrup, e.g. Monin/Fabri, to boost flavor)
- **2g** ground ginger
- **1g** cinnamon
- **0.3g** nutmeg
- **0.1g** ground cloves (optional)
- **0.5g** salt
- **2g** vanilla extract

**Steps:**
1. Heat-treat the flour: spread on a baking sheet and bake at 70°C (175°F) for about 10 minutes until the internal temperature reaches at least 70°C. Stir occasionally. Let it cool slightly.
2. Make the cookie dough: cream together butter and brown sugar by hand until combined (sieve the brown sugar first if lumpy). Add molasses, salt, and vanilla extract. Mix in the spices. Add the heat-treated flour and mix. Add milk (or gingerbread syrup) a little at a time until the dough comes together with a soft, chewable texture — not too dry, not too wet.
3. Turn the dough out onto a silicone mat, flatten/roll to even thickness, and shape as desired. Chill in the freezer or refrigerator until firm enough to handle, then cut into chunks.
4. Make the custard base: combine milk, corn syrup, egg yolks, sugar, skim milk powder, molasses, spices, salt, and vanilla extract in a saucepan. Heat over medium, stirring constantly to prevent the egg yolks from sticking and scrambling on the bottom.
5. Cook the custard until it thickens (nappe consistency). Add cream. Transfer to a container and refrigerate for about 1 hour to chill.
6. Churn the base in an ice cream maker until frozen.
7. Fold in gingerbread cookie dough chunks during or after churning. Pack into a container and freeze.

**Notes:**
- Nick films a double batch in the video — all amounts above are the single (standard) batch from the description.
- Do not use blackstrap molasses in the base — it is too bitter; save it for savory dishes.
- The cookie dough is formulated to stay soft and chewy straight from the freezer, not rock-hard.
- Spice levels are flexible — increase ginger for more heat, increase cinnamon for a pumpkin-spice-adjacent profile.
- Vanilla bean powder (optional) can be added to the base alongside vanilla extract for a deeper vanilla note without affecting the recipe balance.
- Nutritional breakdown available to Patreon supporters and YouTube channel members.
---

### [DIY Toffifee Ice Cream Recipe](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Pix2I3t9FoA)
Hazelnut milk chocolate and dulce de leche ice cream inspired by Toffifee sweets — simple off-the-shelf ingredients, churned base with swirl layering.

**Ingredients:**
- **300ml** milk
- **400ml** double (heavy) cream
- **180g** sugar
- **1-2 tbsp** dulce de leche (plus more reserved for the swirl)
- **100g** hazelnut milk chocolate (brand used: Lindt; any supermarket variety works)
- **2 tbsp** milk powder

**Steps:**
1. Combine milk, cream, and sugar in a saucepan. Heat gently and stir in the dulce de leche until fully incorporated.
2. Transfer the base to a larger vessel to avoid overflow during churning. Chill in an ice bath or the fridge.
3. Once chilled, add the milk powder, leave for 20 minutes, then mix again before churning.
4. Chop the hazelnut milk chocolate and keep it chilled in the fridge or freezer until needed.
5. Churn the base. As this is a high-sugar recipe, allow extra churn time and avoid overfilling the machine.
6. Once churned, layer the ice cream with the remaining dulce de leche and the chopped hazelnut chocolate.
7. Freeze overnight before serving.

**Notes:**
- High sugar content means the mix takes longer to freeze and churn — don't overfill the machine.
- Use a larger pan than you think you need when heating the base; the mixture is prone to spilling.
- Lindt hazelnut milk chocolate is recommended but any hazelnut milk chocolate works.
- Reserve most of the dulce de leche for the swirl layer — only 1-2 tbsp goes into the base.
- This recipe was originally created for a community member (Munira @ I Scream SG) who popularised it in Singapore.
---

### [Gelato Messina Chocolate Sorbet](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ROFSwywUT_8)

Classic sorbet-style recipe using dextrose and maltodextrin for solids, with a stabilizer blend — heavy base that needs agitation before churning.

**Ingredients:**
- **650g** water
- **200g** sugar
- **60g** dextrose
- **25g** maltodextrin
- **5g** stabilizer (unspecified blend)
- **60g** cocoa powder (Valrhona used in video)

**Steps:**
1. Heat water to 40°C.
2. Add all dry ingredients (sugar, dextrose, maltodextrin, stabilizer, cocoa powder) to the 40°C water and stir to combine. Cocoa disperses better above 40°C.
3. Return to heat and bring up to 85°C, stirring until fully combined.
4. Base will be very thick (pudding-like) at this stage — whisk or blend before churning if needed.
5. Chill fully in the fridge, then churn in an ice cream machine.
6. Freeze for approx. 12 hours before scooping.

**Notes:**
- Base becomes extremely thick after heating — described as "pudding-like." Must be re-agitated (whisked or blended) before putting in the machine.
- 5g stabilizer per ~1kg batch is very high — host notes this is "insane" and attributes textural issues to over-stabilization.
- The maltodextrin is present to boost solids content and improve mouthfeel, compensating for the low-fat, water-heavy base.
- Outcome: dry, crumbly scoop with strange texture — broke apart rather than scooping cleanly. Flavor was good (Valrhona cocoa), but texture was a fail.

---

### [David Lebovitz Chocolate Sorbet](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ROFSwywUT_8)

Famous two-stage water method with dark chocolate added after boiling — no stabilizer, rich rounded flavor, traditional scooping texture.

**Ingredients:**
- **555g** water (split: 375g + 180g)
- **200g** sugar
- **75g** cocoa powder (Valrhona used in video)
- **2g** salt (pink Himalayan)
- **170g** dark chocolate (60%)
- **3g** vanilla extract

**Steps:**
1. Combine 375g water with sugar, cocoa powder, and salt in a saucepan. Whisk together.
2. Bring to a boil and boil whilst whisking for 45 seconds.
3. Remove from heat and add the 170g dark chocolate. Stir until melted (less than 1 minute).
4. Add vanilla extract (3g) and the remaining 180g cold water to cool the mixture down.
5. Chill fully in the fridge, then churn in an ice cream machine.
6. Freeze for approx. 12 hours before scooping.

**Notes:**
- No stabilizer — relies on the chocolate itself for body and texture.
- The cold water addition at the end is a deliberate technique to rapidly cool the base; can also use cold cream in other recipes.
- Boiling the cocoa-water mixture pushes cocoa flavors forward (same principle as the host's chocolate fudge base).
- Outcome: won the showdown. Traditional glossy scoop, held together well, smaller ice crystals, more rounded flavor (cocoa + chocolate both present).
- Host verdict: "Hands down David Lebovitz chocolate sorbet is the winner."
- Both recipes work well in a Ninja Creami (sorbet program).

---

### [Clive's Luxury Chocolate Hazelnut Gelato](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sqwAyAP4fZE)

Diabetic-friendly chocolate hazelnut gelato using allulose and stevia, churned in an ice cream machine with a 1 kg base yielding ~1.3 L at 30% overrun.

**Ingredients (allulose version — primary):**
- **540g** unsweetened cashew milk (any brand, check for no added sugar)
- **200g** 33% cream (30–36% fat)
- **160g** allulose
- **50g** inulin
- **75g** unsweetened cocoa powder (strong/dark — not drinking chocolate; Nick uses Extra Brute / Cacao Barry Brute)
- **1g** salt
- **5g** ice cream stabilizer (Nick's own heated blend; 4–5g range)
- **1g** liquid stevia (added after cooking, not heated)

**Add-ins (churned in at end):**
- **~50g** 100% chocolate (chopped fine; no added sugar — use 100% cacao bar, not 85%/90% which still has sugar)
- **~handful** hazelnuts, roasted and chopped (raw or pre-roasted; Nick toasts 10 min at ~120°C / 250°F and rubs off skins)

**Xylitol version (described as "not quite excellent but still good"):**
- **535g** cashew milk
- **210g** 33% cream
- **140g** xylitol
- **55g** inulin
- **75g** cocoa powder
- **1g** salt
- **5g** ice cream stabilizer
- **1g** liquid stevia

**Steps:**
1. Combine cashew milk, cream, allulose, inulin, cocoa powder, salt, and stabilizer in a saucepan.
2. Heat to 80–85°C, stirring continuously, and hold for 2–3 minutes to activate the stabilizer.
3. Pour into container. Let cool, then stir in liquid stevia (do not cook the stevia — heat increases bitterness).
4. Refrigerate overnight to develop flavors (ice bath + immediate churn is acceptable but overnight is preferred).
5. Toast hazelnuts in oven ~10 minutes at ~120°C (250°F); rub off skins, let cool, then chop roughly.
6. Chop 100% chocolate bar into fine pieces.
7. Churn cold base in ice cream machine until nearly done.
8. Add chopped chocolate and chopped toasted hazelnuts in the last moments of the churn.
9. Transfer to containers (~1.3 L yield from this 1 kg base, ~30% overrun). Freeze.

**Notes:**
- Allulose version is significantly better than xylitol — xylitol dose is capped lower, which reduces solids and hurts texture.
- Stevia must go in after cooking; heating stevia amplifies bitterness.
- Use 100% cacao chocolate for the add-in — even 85%–95% bars contain added sugar.
- If pre-roasted hazelnuts taste weak, re-toast them; it makes a notable difference.
- The amount of 100% chocolate add-in is approximate (~50g for the 1 kg base); keep it restrained — it is intensely bitter.
- Nick mentions citrus fiber as an upcoming substitute for inulin in plant-based recipes if it becomes available.
- This is a traditional churned gelato recipe (not a Ninja Creami recipe).

---

### [Malted Vanilla Ice Cream (Whoppers/Maltesers)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=A4LRnEkuFXs)

Classic malt shop-style vanilla ice cream with dry malt extract and a stracciatella-style chocolate swirl, topped with Whoppers or Maltesers.

**Ingredients:**
- **250g** whole milk
- **500g** heavy cream
- **3** large egg yolks (OR 1.2g stabilizer)
- **150g** sugar
- **50g** dry malt extract (or liquid malt extract; malted milk powder works 1:1 but weaker flavor)
- **5g** vanilla extract (can use up to 10g)
- Pinch of salt
- **~7g** (1.5 tsp) deodorized/refined coconut oil (for stracciatella)
- Dark chocolate (55%, e.g. Belcolade) — amount not specified; enough to drizzle as stracciatella

**Steps:**
1. Combine milk, cream, sugar, malt extract, salt, and stabilizer (or egg yolks) in a saucepan.
2. Heat over medium, stirring, to 75°C if using egg yolks, or 85°C if using stabilizer.
3. Remove from heat. Cool 5–10 minutes, then stir in vanilla extract.
4. Refrigerate at least 4 hours until fully chilled.
5. Churn in a pre-chilled ice cream machine.
6. Melt chocolate with refined coconut oil until fully liquid.
7. Near the end of the churn, drizzle in the chocolate mixture to create stracciatella shards.
8. Dish into containers and freeze overnight.
9. Serve with a Whopper or Malteser on top.

**Notes:**
- Dry malt extract can clump if stored; lumps dissolve when heated — not a problem.
- Malted milk powder (popular in the US) can substitute 1:1 for dry malt extract but delivers a milder malt flavor.
- If in Europe, liquid malt extract from supermarkets is a fine substitute for dry malt extract.
- Do not boil the dairy base — dairy denatures at 80–85°C; boiling bitters the flavor.
- Whoppers have a stronger malt flavor and lighter texture; Maltesers are denser with European-style chocolate.
- Whoppers/Maltesers cannot be mixed into the base — the malt center dissolves in moisture. Use them as garnish only.
- The chocolate stracciatella is added right at the end of churning (stretchyella technique).
- Recipe yields good overrun; scoops straight from the freezer without tempering.
---

### [Delicious Rice Gelato — Gelato di Riso](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9naNqC0I6g0) — enough to submerge the rice for cooking (approx. 200–250ml)
- Whole milk — for the base (quantity not precisely stated, approx. 400–500ml)
- **180g** sugar
- **60g** glucose syrup (40 DE)
- **5g** stabilizer blend (proprietary; Nick notes: if using guar or xanthan gum, reduce to 2.5–3g)
- Skim milk powder (optional — increases smoothness and creaminess; quantity not stated, small amount)
- **15g** vanilla bean paste (or high-quality vanilla extract)

**Steps:**

1. Wash the Arborio rice thoroughly in a fine mesh strainer to remove surface starch.
2. Combine the washed rice with the cream in a pan. Stir to ensure no rice sticks to the bottom. Simmer on a gentle heat for approximately 20–30 minutes, stirring periodically, until the rice is fully cooked and soft (not al dente). Turn off the heat and leave to rest for 10 minutes.
3. In a separate pan, warm the milk. Whisk in the skim milk powder (if using).
4. In a small bowl, combine the sugar and stabilizer blend. Whisk into the milk mixture.
5. Add the glucose syrup to the milk pan.
6. Heat the milk mixture to 85°C, stirring throughout.
7. Once the milk base reaches temperature, add the cooked rice and cream mixture. Stir well to combine.
8. Leave to settle off the heat for 15 minutes.
9. Transfer the entire mixture (including all rice) to a bowl. Chill down quickly in an ice bath or refrigerate overnight until completely cold.
10. Once chilled, stir in the vanilla bean paste.
11. Churn in an ice cream machine until done.
12. Transfer to a freezer-safe container and freeze for several hours until hardened.

**Notes:**

- This is a traditional batch recipe for a conventional ice cream machine — not a Ninja Creami recipe.
- Arborio rice (Italian short-grain) is essential; long-grain rice behaves very differently.
- The rice must be fully cooked through — al dente rice will be too hard once frozen.
- As the rice simmers in cream, water evaporates and the cream reduces, concentrating fat — this is intentional.
- Glucose syrup (40 DE) can be substituted with plain corn syrup (not identical but close), or replaced weight-for-weight with sugar as a last resort.
- Vanilla bean paste is preferred over extract for the subtle flavor; use a good quality one.
- Nick deliberately omits eggs, using stabilizers instead for the same structural effect.
- Nick's stabilizer blend is proprietary — if using individual gums (guar, xanthan), halve the quantity to ~2.5g to avoid over-stabilizing.
- The finished gelato has whole soft rice pieces throughout and a clean, subtle vanilla flavor — described as "a Frankenstein between ice cream and rice pudding."

---

### [Delicious Strawberries n Cream Gelato](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=vKJCBh83LS0)

Fresh strawberry gelato cooked down with sugar and corn syrup into a rich invert syrup base, churned in a traditional ice cream machine or Ninja Creami.

**Ingredients:**
- **200g** fresh strawberries
- **115g** sugar
- **55g** corn syrup (or glucose syrup)
- **195g** 2% milk
- **386g** 33% whipping cream
- Skim milk powder (amount adjusted — original had too much; see Notes)
- **1.2g** stabilizer (optional — off-the-shelf generic blend; guar gum, xanthan gum, or a combination blend works)

**Steps:**
1. Hull and quarter the strawberries (smaller pieces cook faster).
2. Combine strawberries, sugar, and corn syrup in a pan over high heat. Stir and cook 10–15 minutes until strawberries fully break down into a thick, foaming syrup.
3. Remove from heat and allow to cool somewhat.
4. In a separate bowl, combine milk, cream, and milk powder (with stabilizer mixed into the powder if using a hot-process stabilizer).
5. Whisk the dairy mixture, then slowly pour in the warm strawberry syrup, scraping out all the syrup from the pan.
6. If using a hot-process stabilizer, heat the combined mixture back up to 85°C, stirring continuously. Remove from heat.
7. Transfer to a bowl and chill in the fridge for 3–4 hours until fully cold.
8. Blend briefly (30–60 seconds) to break up any remaining fruit chunks (optional but recommended — large fruit pieces go icy).
9. Churn in a pre-chilled ice cream machine for approximately 12–15 minutes.
10. Pack into tubs and freeze.

**Notes:**
- Corn syrup / glucose creates invert sugar during cooking, which inhibits recrystallization and gives a smooth, creamy texture — this largely replaces the need for a stabilizer in this recipe.
- Strawberries are ~95% water, so milk quantity is kept low to compensate.
- Stabilizer is optional here; the invert sugar does significant stabilizing work on its own.
- Nick adjusted the milk powder quantity down from the original (original video had too much) — use a conservative amount.
- Take out of the freezer a few minutes before serving to soften enough to scoop — sugar level was intentionally reduced slightly to let strawberry flavor shine, so the gelato sets firmer.
- Works in both a traditional ice cream machine and Ninja Creami.
---

### [Delicious Tim Tam Ice Cream Ideas](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=InG_HUJpYYY), Dimbulah Coffee, and Murray River Salted Caramel — each with its own swirl or fudge add-in. Batch recipe (3 pints).

---

**Recipe 1: Chewy Caramel Tim Tam**

**Ingredients — Ice Cream Base:**
- **400ml** milk
- **300ml** cream (49% fat)
- **100g** sugar
- **60g** dextrose
- **30g** dulce de leche powder (or 30g wet dulce de leche)
- **35g** skimmed milk powder (SMP)
- **1.75g** xanthan gum

**Ingredients — Tim Tam Fudge:**
- **3** Chewy Caramel Tim Tam biscuits, crushed/finely chopped
- **40g** sugar
- **50g** chocolate
- **10g** cocoa powder
- **60g** milk
- **10g** cream
- **20g** corn syrup (or golden syrup)

**Ingredients — Caramel Swirl:**
- Use the shared caramel sauce (see Recipe 3 below)

---

**Recipe 2: Dimbulah Coffee**

Coffee ice cream with chocolate and caramel notes, finished with a milk chocolate swirl.

**Ingredients — Ice Cream Base:**
- **300ml** milk
- **300ml** cream (49% fat)
- **85g** sugar, slightly caramelised in the oven
- **30g** dextrose
- **30g** golden syrup
- **35g** skimmed milk powder (SMP)
- **7g** dark brown sugar
- **50g** Dimbulah ground coffee beans (soaked in warm milk for 30 minutes)

**Ingredients — Milk Chocolate Swirl:**
- **50g** sugar
- **50g** milk chocolate
- **5g** cocoa powder
- **50g** milk
- **15g** corn syrup (or golden syrup)
- **7g** coconut oil
- **1 tsp** vanilla extract
- Pinch of salt

---

**Recipe 3: Murray River Salted Caramel**

Salted caramel ice cream with a caramel swirl.

**Ingredients — Ice Cream Base:**
- **335g** milk
- **340g** cream
- **85g** sugar
- **40g** dextrose
- **1** egg yolk
- **60g** caramel sauce (from recipe below)
- **40g** skimmed milk powder (SMP)
- **5g** kosher salt (Nick used a touch too much — recipe adjusted down)

**Ingredients — Caramel Sauce (shared with Chewy Caramel swirl):**
- **200g** sugar
- **80g** butter
- **150g** cream
- Touch of vanilla extract

---

**Steps:**

**Ice Cream Bases (all three follow the same method):**

1. Add milk to a pan with most of the sugar; stir until dissolved.
2. Add skimmed milk powder and mix until lump-free.
3. Add remaining sugars and stabilizer (xanthan — for the Chewy Caramel base); heat to the temperature required for your stabilizer.
4. Remove from heat, transfer to a container, then add cream to rapidly bring the temperature down.
5. For the Coffee base: steep chopped Dimbulah beans in warm milk for 30 minutes, then strain through cheesecloth or a fine sieve before proceeding. Add golden syrup and dark brown sugar with the other sugars.
6. For the Salted Caramel base: add the pre-made caramel sauce and stir until fully dissolved before adding milk powder.
7. Refrigerate all bases until cold.

**Caramel Sauce:**

1. Add sugar to a pan; swirl gently over medium heat — do not stir. Keep swirling until fully melted and a deep caramel colour forms.
2. Turn off the heat and add butter carefully (it will bubble).
3. Stir until combined, then add cream (warm cream reduces splatter). Mix until smooth.
4. Store in a container; use as a swirl for both the Chewy Caramel and Murray River pints.

**Tim Tam Fudge:**

1. In a pan, combine milk, cream, sugar, and corn syrup; stir together.
2. Add cocoa powder; bring to a rolling boil, then turn off the heat.
3. Allow to calm for 1–2 minutes, then add chopped Tim Tams and stir through.
4. Store in a container until ready to use.

**Milk Chocolate Swirl (for Coffee base):**

1. Combine milk, sugar, corn syrup, coconut oil, and cocoa powder in a pan; stir together.
2. Bring to a rolling boil; turn off the heat.
3. Add chopped milk chocolate and stir until fully melted.
4. Store in a container until assembly.

**Assembly:**

1. Freeze all bases for at least 24 hours.
2. Churn/process each base according to your machine.
3. Layer or swirl add-ins as you fill each container:
   - Chewy Caramel: Tim Tam fudge swirl + caramel swirl
   - Coffee: milk chocolate swirl
   - Salted Caramel: caramel swirl

**Notes:**
- The Chewy Caramel was the fan-voted favourite from a Facebook poll — Nick made all three because he couldn't stop at one.
- Xanthan gum is used deliberately in the Chewy Caramel base to create a chewy texture mimicking the biscuit.
- Dulce de leche powder works best but wet dulce de leche (same weight) is a valid substitute — just stir it directly into the base.
- Corn syrup is not available in the UK; Nick uses golden syrup as a 1:1 substitute.
- For the caramel sauce, warm the cream before adding it to reduce violent splatter from the hot caramel.
- Boiling the fudge/swirl mixtures is essential — it changes the sugar structure so the swirl stays soft and gooey in the frozen ice cream rather than turning icy.
- Salt level in the Murray River base has been adjusted down from what Nick used in filming — the recipe as listed gives a strong but not overpowering salt punch.
- Tim Tams (Chewy Caramel flavour) cost around £8/pack in the UK — a premium ingredient worth seeking out for this recipe.

---

### [Devon Cream Tea Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=W3dY0p2rQBA)
- **400g** clotted cream (63–67.5% fat — Nick uses Rodda's)
- **170g** sugar
- **50g** dextrose
- **10g** glucose syrup
- **50g** SMP (skim milk powder)
- **1.3g** stabilizer (optional)
- Jam to taste (raspberry, strawberry, or black cherry — Nick uses raspberry)
- Scones, broken into pieces (store-bought or homemade)

**Steps:**

1. Add milk, sugar, dextrose, glucose syrup, and SMP to a pan. Whisk until everything is really well combined before moving on.
2. Add the clotted cream and heat on the hob, stirring, until the cream is fully melted and homogenized into the base.
3. Transfer to a container and chill in the fridge. Note: the high butterfat will solidify on top as it cools — this is normal.
4. Once fully chilled, blend for 10–15 seconds to re-emulsify the fat back into the base before churning.
5. Churn in your ice cream machine. The base will solidify quickly due to the fat content — keep a close eye on it.
6. To tub up: spread scone pieces with jam. Layer churned ice cream, jam-covered scone chunks, and extra jam swirls into your container.
7. Freeze for several hours until fully set.

**Notes:**

- Devon cream tea tradition: cream goes on the scone first, jam on top. Cornish style is jam first.
- Clotted cream cannot truly be replicated at home — the commercial crust is unique. Nick links a homemade recipe in the description for those outside the UK.
- The recipe yields a very fatty, rich base — this is by design, not a mistake.
- Use whatever jam you prefer; strawberry is traditional but raspberry or black cherry work well.
- Nick uses Rodda's clotted cream (67.5% fat).
- The stabilizer is optional — Nick lists 1.3g but notes you don't have to use it.
---

### [Oreo Space Dunk Mint Cookies 'n' Cream Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=maijkLNh2Ek)

Double-color (pink + blue) mint cookies and cream ice cream made with limited-edition Oreo Space Dunk cookies (cosmic cream with popping candy).

**Ingredients (per batch — recipe is made as 2 batches):**
- **750g** coffee cream (18% fat)
- **160g** sugar
- **30g** dextrose
- **50g** skimmed milk powder
- **2g** stabilizer blend (optional)
- Mint extract (to taste — added after heating)
- Pink gel food coloring (batch 1) / Blue gel food coloring (batch 2)
- Oreo Space Dunk cookies — cookie parts only, crushed to powder (mix-in)
- Marshmallow — for topping (optional, blowtorch-toasted)

**Steps:**
1. Combine cream and milk in one vessel; combine sugar, dextrose, and stabilizer blend in another; add skimmed milk powder to the dry mix.
2. Combine dry ingredients into the cream/milk mixture and stir well.
3. Heat on the hob, stirring, to 80–85°C (180°F) if using a stabilizer. If not, just warm enough to dissolve the sugars.
4. Remove from heat and allow to cool.
5. Add mint extract after the heating phase (or once fully cooled for strongest flavor). Add gel food coloring — pink to one batch, blue to the other.
6. Chill both bases in the fridge until cold.
7. Crush the cookie parts (no cream) of the Oreo Space Dunk cookies into a coarse powder.
8. Churn each base separately in your ice cream machine. Add crushed cookie pieces during or after churning.
9. Layer scoops of pink and blue ice cream in serving bowls for the double-color effect.
10. Top with marshmallow (store-bought or homemade) and blowtorch to toast.

**Notes:**
- This is a 2-batch recipe — both batches use identical bases, differentiated only by food coloring color.
- Use gel food coloring designed to work in fat if possible; water-based colorings will also work.
- Always add extracts after the heating phase to preserve flavor and alcohol; adding before heating loses both.
- The Space Dunk cookies are marketed as marshmallow-flavored but taste more like standard vanilla — the mint base and toasted marshmallow topping compensate.
- The popping candy in the cookies comes through noticeably in the finished ice cream.
- Use cookie parts only (scrape/discard the cream filling) for the mix-in to keep texture clean.
- Fat-based gel food colorings give more vivid results in a cream-heavy base.

---

### [Discovering the Perfect Bounty Ice Cream Recipe](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Xc7RcXgh89s)

Two failed attempts at a Bounty (Almond Joy) ice cream — iterating toward the right coconut flavor and texture using available UK supermarket ingredients.

---

#### Attempt 1 — Infusion Method (failed)

Original recipe called for frozen grated coconut (unavailable), so creamed coconut was substituted. The high fat content (~65%) of creamed coconut flooded the base with fat, leaving only ~15g of coconut solids after straining — resulting in a powdery, crumbly texture. Flavor was good; texture was not.

**Ingredients:**
- Coconut cream (infused, then strained)
- Creamed coconut (substituted for frozen grated coconut — 100% coconut, ~65% fat)
- Whole milk
- Whipping cream
- Sugar
- Corn syrup
- Proprietary stabilizer and emulsifier mix
- Pinch of salt
- Shredded coconut (added after heating)

**Mix-in / Finish:**
- Milk chocolate stracciatella (milk chocolate + small amount of coconut oil, drizzled at end of churn)

**Steps:**
1. Combine coconut cream, creamed coconut, whole milk, and whipping cream in a saucepan. Heat on the stove until combined.
2. Chill overnight in the fridge.
3. Strain the mix. (Note: most solids were lost — creamed coconut fat fully dissolved into the base.)
4. Add sugar, corn syrup, stabilizer/emulsifier mix, and a pinch of salt. Heat to dissolve sugars.
5. Stir in shredded coconut. Chill the base again until cold.
6. Churn in an ice cream machine.
7. Near the end of churning, drizzle in melted milk chocolate thinned with a small amount of coconut oil (stracciatella technique).

**Result:** Flavor spot-on (Bounty-like), but texture powdery and crumbly due to excessive fat from creamed coconut. Not what was intended.

---

#### Attempt 2 — Revised Base (closer, but flavor off)

Abandoned the infusion approach. Used coconut milk and coconut cream directly in the base without straining.

**Ingredients:**
- **400g** coconut milk @ 18% fat
- **400g** coconut cream @ 22% fat
- **110g** sugar
- **100g** dextrose
- **100g** double cream
- **75g** SMP (skimmed milk powder)

**Steps:**
1. Combine all ingredients and mix well until dissolved.
2. Chill the base until cold.
3. Churn in an ice cream machine.

**Result:** Much better texture — scoops traditionally, smooth. Flavor tasted like "fake coconut" — suspected cause is the specific coconut milk brand used (18% fat). Still not achieving the true Bounty bar flavor. Nick noted more desiccated coconut is also needed for the right Bounty texture/chew.

---

**Notes:**
- Target flavor profile: Bounty bar (UK) / Almond Joy (US) — dense, chewy, desiccated coconut filling covered in milk chocolate.
- Fat content of coconut products varies widely (11%, 15%, 18%, 21%, 22%) and they are not interchangeable — fat level has a major impact on texture.
- Creamed coconut (~65% fat) is unsuitable as a flavor infusion ingredient; it overwhelms the fat balance of the base.
- Frozen grated coconut (the ideal ingredient) was unavailable locally — this whole video is a workaround exploration.
- The stracciatella finish (milk chocolate + coconut oil) in Attempt 1 looked and tasted good — worth keeping in a future version.
- Nick plans to continue testing different coconut milk/cream products until the right Bounty flavor is achieved. More desiccated coconut is also needed for texture.
- This video is explicitly a "discovery in progress" — neither recipe is considered a finished result.
---

### [EASY Coconut Frozen Yogurt](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=F4qe6zR9JkA)

Simple 4-ingredient coconut frozen yogurt — creamy, tangy, and strong coconut flavour with no stabilizers.

**Ingredients:**
- **296g** coconut Greek yogurt (2% fat or less; coconut flakes inside are a bonus)
- **327g** coconut cream (~just under a full standard can)
- **100g** allulose (can substitute regular sugar or xylitol — use less xylitol)
- **37g** coconut milk powder (used in place of skim milk powder for full coconut theme)

**Steps:**
1. Combine all ingredients in a saucepan. Warm gently to 40–50°C to fully dissolve the allulose and disperse the coconut milk powder — do not overheat.
2. Stir until smooth, then pour into the Ninja Creami Deluxe pint container.
3. Freeze for a minimum of 24 hours.
4. Spin on Ice Cream setting. The first spin may leave the centre unprocessed (expected given the thick, high-solids base) — run a second spin immediately after.

**Notes:**
- Recipe is sized for the Ninja Creami Deluxe (750ml pint). For the standard Creami, reduce all amounts by roughly 1/3.
- Nick explicitly flags this as an off-balance/"experimental" recipe — high fat and high solids from coconut cream + coconut milk powder together. It works well despite being outside normal calculator ranges.
- The base is very thick before freezing; not suitable for a traditional churn machine.
- A double spin was required to get smooth texture — factor this in.
- Texture and flavour result described as excellent: strong but not overpowering coconut, tangy yogurt note, great mouthfeel.
- Optional: fold in toasted coconut flakes as a mix-in after spinning to further elevate the coconut experience.
- The description notes any Greek yogurt flavour works as a 1-for-1 swap (replace coconut yogurt with any flavour + matching powder/cream) for a simple frozen yogurt template.
---

### [Eggnog that can ALSO be Ice Cream — Eggnog Ice Cream Recipe](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5VIvfFZVohI)

A science-based eggnog custard engineered to double as a churnable ice cream — uses 40DE glucose syrup to balance freezing point and solids without over-sweetening.

**Ingredients:**
- **300g** whole milk (3% fat)
- **250g** whipping cream (33% fat)
- **60g** skimmed milk powder
- **80g** sugar
- **60g** glucose syrup (40DE)
- **100g** egg yolks
- Vanilla extract (to taste, added off-heat)
- Nutmeg (to finish/garnish)

**Steps:**
1. Combine sugar, egg yolks, and glucose syrup (40DE) in a saucepan and begin mixing — the glucose is thick/sticky; warm it briefly in the microwave a few seconds to loosen if needed.
2. Add skimmed milk powder and mix in.
3. Add milk and cream, then heat over the hob to 65–70°C, stirring continuously. At this temperature the egg yolk proteins set and thicken the custard properly.
4. Remove from heat. Once the mix cools to around 50°C or below, stir in vanilla extract (adding it while hot burns off the alcohol that carries the flavor).
5. Chill the base thoroughly in the refrigerator.
6. To use as eggnog: serve chilled, dusted with nutmeg. Add booze on the side if desired — do not add alcohol to the base you intend to freeze.
7. To use as ice cream: decant the required amount into your ice cream machine and churn. Transfer to a tub and freeze.

**Notes:**
- The 40DE glucose syrup is the key functional ingredient — it provides solids and freezing point depression (FPD) without adding sweetness proportional to sucrose (40DE is only ~40–45% as sweet as sugar). Nick states there is no real substitute for it in this recipe.
- Calculator metrics for the base: ~14% fat, ~11% sugar, ~11.7% MSNF (milk solids-non-fat), ~43.3% total solids, ~56.7% water — all within standard ice cream ranges except sugar (slightly low, offset by the glucose syrup FPD).
- Standard eggnog fails as ice cream because booze in the base depresses the freezing point too far — either won't freeze or freezes very icy. Keep alcohol out of any portion destined for churning; serve it on the side instead.
- Always add vanilla extract at or below 50°C to preserve flavor; the alcohol in extract carries the vanilla compounds and evaporates quickly on the hob.
- This recipe makes more than one churn batch — portion off what you need for freezing and keep the rest refrigerated as drinkable eggnog.
- Nick references a boozy eggnog ice cream recipe from 2022 as an alternative if you want alcohol incorporated.
---

### [Festive Eggnog Ice Cream Recipe](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9Yye5d6cKbc)

Boozy custard-based eggnog ice cream churned in a traditional machine — rich, spiced, and spiked with Old J Spiced Rum.

**Ingredients:**

- **350g** whole milk
- **350g** double/heavy cream
- **50g** brown sugar
- **120g** white sugar
- **60g** skimmed milk powder
- **5** egg yolks
- **5g** ground nutmeg (fresh preferred, dried fine)
- **70g** Old J Spiced Rum (or alcohol of choice — Jack Daniel's, Captain Morgan's, Baileys, etc.)
- **5g** vanilla paste or extract

**Steps:**

1. Add milk (whole milk), brown sugar, white sugar, skimmed milk powder, and egg yolks directly into a saucepan. Whisk together well.
2. Heat on the stove, stirring continuously, until the mixture reaches 82°C. This pasteurises the eggs and creates the custard base without a separate tempering step.
3. Remove from heat and transfer to a container.
4. Stir in the double/heavy cream.
5. Add the rum (or alcohol of choice) and nutmeg. Stir to combine.
6. Refrigerate until completely chilled.
7. Pre-chill your ice cream machine bowl well (high alcohol content means the mix resists freezing).
8. Churn until done — took 13 minutes in Nick's 5030 machine.
9. Transfer to a tub and freeze.

**Notes:**

- Traditional churned recipe — not a Ninja Creami recipe.
- The 70g of rum significantly depresses the freezing point; sugar was deliberately reduced to compensate. If you reduce alcohol, you can increase sugar slightly.
- Nick used notably orange-yolked eggs, which give the ice cream a golden colour — not a problem, just visual.
- Alcohol choice is flexible: whiskey, rum, Baileys, brandy all work. Stronger spirits give a more pronounced effect on texture and freeze point.
- Nick warns against using store-bought eggnog as a milk substitute — he found it gave a subpar flavour.
- Vanilla paste or extract both work; paste gives flecks.

---

### [Festive Homemade Christmas Pudding Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=l6YRvuE7crc)

Churned ice cream base with cinnamon, nutmeg, and cherry brandy, with broken Christmas pudding folded in at soft-serve stage.

**Ingredients:**

- **475g** whole milk
- **350g** cream (whipping or double/heavy)
- **180g** sugar
- **15g** golden syrup (or corn syrup / glucose)
- **47g** skimmed milk powder
- **1.2g** xanthan gum (stabilizer)
- **5g** ground cinnamon
- **5g** ground nutmeg
- **30g** cherry brandy (24% ABV — or any preferred alcohol)
- **100g** Christmas pudding (leftover or freshly cooked; theirs contained brandy, cider, rum, and orange)

**Steps:**

1. Add milk to a pan and warm gently.
2. Mix stabilizer (xanthan gum) with a portion of the sugar to prevent clumping; add to the milk along with the remaining sugar.
3. Add golden syrup and stir to combine.
4. Separately mix cinnamon and nutmeg into the skimmed milk powder, then add to the milk and stir well.
5. Remove from heat and stir in the cherry brandy.
6. Transfer to a container, add the cream (this brings the temperature down quickly), then refrigerate for several hours until fully chilled.
7. Churn the base in an ice cream machine until soft-serve consistency.
8. Break Christmas pudding into small chunks; fold into the churned ice cream and churn for another minute or two to distribute.
9. Transfer to a container and freeze.

**Notes:**

- Cinnamon and nutmeg are ground spices and will not dissolve — they settle (typically to the top during chilling), so stir the base well before churning.
- Any alcohol can be substituted for the cherry brandy; ABV affects freezing point.
- Christmas pudding can be supermarket-bought or homemade. Small chunk size is recommended for even distribution.
- This is a traditional churned ice cream recipe, not a Ninja Creami recipe.

---

### [Fig & Honey Ice Cream - Thor inspired](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=UYNRtZtj3cE)
A fun Marvel/Thor-themed video producing a two-swirl churned ice cream: blue fig and honey, decorated with rose-gold white chocolate shards. No exact measurements given — the narration is entirely in-character Asgardian roleplay.

**Ingredients:**

*Fig ice cream base:*
- Fresh figs (quantity unspecified — cooked down to a jam)
- Sugar (quantity unspecified)
- Duck egg yolk (1 per base — described as "golden duck egg yolk")
- Whole milk (quantity unspecified — "finest milk")
- Double cream (quantity unspecified — "finest cream")

*Honey ice cream base:*
- Honey (quantity unspecified — described as fructose-rich, so use ~half the quantity you'd use for regular sugar)
- Whole milk (quantity unspecified)
- Double cream (quantity unspecified)
- Blue food coloring (for the "pastel dragon honey" color effect — turns green once mixed into the base)

*Rose-gold white chocolate shards:*
- White chocolate (quantity unspecified)
- Red food coloring
- Gold lustre/shimmer powder (for brushed decoration)

**Steps:**

1. Cook fresh figs in a pot over medium heat, stirring until broken down into a jam. Simmer for ~30 minutes until thick.
2. Remove from heat and strain through a sieve into a mixing container to remove seeds/skins. Retain all the liquid fig jam.
3. Add sugar and duck egg yolk to the strained fig jam. Mix well — the yolk adds richness and a golden color.
4. Add milk and double cream. Stir to combine. Refrigerate until thoroughly chilled.
5. For the honey base: combine honey, milk, and double cream. Add blue food coloring. Stir to combine — note it will turn green once fully mixed. Refrigerate until thoroughly chilled.
6. For the chocolate shards: melt white chocolate over a bain-marie (bowl over warm water). Add red food coloring and stir in. Spread thinly over a silicone baking sheet or non-stick surface.
7. Brush melted chocolate with gold lustre powder before it sets. Allow to harden (or refrigerate to speed up). Break/cut into shards once set.
8. Churn the fig base first in an ice cream machine. Transfer to the serving container and keep in the freezer while churning the honey base.
9. Once the honey base is churned, layer/swirl both ice creams together in the container.
10. Press chocolate shards into the top of the ice cream and scatter a few on the surface for decoration. Freeze until firm before serving.

**Notes:**
- No quantities are given in this video — the entire narration is in-character as a Thor/Asgard-themed skit. Use your standard ice cream ratios and adjust to taste.
- Honey is fructose-heavy and very sweet. Nick advises using roughly half the amount you would normally use for sugar, then adjusting upward if needed. Don't substitute all sugar with honey.
- The "blue" color in the fig base comes from the natural blue/purple pigment inside ripe fresh figs — it bleeds through when cooked.
- The honey base turns green (not blue) once mixed with milk/cream due to the blue food coloring interacting with the yellow cream.
- Duck egg yolks are richer and larger than hen eggs — they add extra emulsification and a deeper golden color to the fig base.
- This is a traditional churned ice cream (not a Ninja Creami recipe). The video is part of a Marvel-themed series alongside "Stark Raving Hazelnuts" and "Hunka Hulka Burning Fudge."
- White chocolate shards are primarily decorative — press them into the tub before final freeze so they stay put.
---

### [Fixing VIRAL Ninja Creami recipes using SCIENCE - Peach](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xL8K8vMLKog)

Taking the viral "1 ingredient canned peach ice cream" TikTok recipe and fixing it with proper ice cream science: adding cream for fat/solids, allulose for FPD and sweetness, inulin for bulk solids, and xanthan gum to bind free water.

**Ingredients:**

- **1 whole can** sliced peaches in juice (Del Monte brand used — sucralose-sweetened, 8g sugar/167ml serving)
- **100g** whipping cream (33–36% fat; double cream also works)
- **70g** allulose (or xylitol, but use less)
- **25g** inulin
- **1g** xanthan gum

**Steps:**

1. Open the can of peaches and pour the entire contents (fruit and syrup) into a blender.
2. Add 100g whipping cream, 70g allulose, 25g inulin, and 1g xanthan gum.
3. Blend for 1–2 minutes until completely smooth and the peaches are fully liquefied.
4. Check for graininess by running a spoon along the edge of the container — allulose should dissolve without heating in this context.
5. Pour into a Ninja Creami pint and freeze for at least 24 hours until fully hardened.
6. Process on Ice Cream (or Gelato — identical settings). Re-spin once if needed due to low solids content.

**Notes:**

- Yield is approximately 600g of base, filling the pint.
- Two spins may be required — solids are still on the lower end for optimal ice cream texture.
- Use xanthan gum (not guar gum) as guar gum performs poorly in acidic environments; peaches are mildly acidic.
- Del Monte sucralose-sweetened cans preferred (8g sugar/serving vs. 22g in sugar-sweetened varieties) to reduce added sugar load.
- The viral "can of peaches only" original produces enormous visible ice crystals due to insufficient solids — that version is essentially frozen fruit, not ice cream or sorbet.
- Inulin is chicory root fiber; also sold under that name. Any brand works — they are all the same ingredient.
- Nick notes this fat content is technically gelato-level, but without traditional sugar — allulose provides FPD (freezing point depression) instead.

---

### [Häagen Dazs Chocolate Midnight Cookies — Plant Based](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=X8whcn7hwzg)

Plant-based recreation of HD Chocolate Midnight Cookies using 10% plant-based half & half cream. Batch recipe (traditional churn, not single pint).

**Ingredients:**

*Chocolate Base:*
- **700g** plant-based half & half cream (10% fat)
- **190g** sugar
- **20g** dextrose
- **65g** cocoa powder
- **1g** stabilizer (optional)

*Chocolate Fudge:*
- **50g** sugar
- **50g** 80% chocolate
- **10g** cocoa powder
- **50g** plant-based milk
- **15g** corn syrup or golden syrup
- **15g** coconut oil
- **3g** vanilla extract

*Mix-in:*
- Oreo halves (cream removed)

**Steps:**
1. Combine plant-based cream, sugar, dextrose, and cocoa powder. Heat gently to dissolve.
2. If using stabilizer, heat to activation temp. Otherwise just warm to combine.
3. Chill base completely.
4. For fudge: combine all fudge ingredients in a pan, heat until melted and smooth. Chill.
5. Churn the base in ice cream machine.
6. Layer churned ice cream with fudge drizzle and Oreo halves into tubs.
7. Freeze overnight.

**Notes:**
- Nick uses Silk brand plant cream (coconut-based, 10% fat) in Canada; recommends Elmlea plant cream for UK.
- Coconut-based creams add an unwanted coconut undertone — Nick specifically dislikes this but works around it with strong cocoa.
- Stats: 8.8% fat, 19.9% sugar, 40% solids.
- Nick says no one could tell this from dairy — it's that good.
