### [Ice Cream Cookie Sandwiches (ASMR)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ytdo46ZSq1o)

Double chocolate and red velvet cookies sandwiching a boozy white chocolate ice cream, churned on a Musso 5030.

---

**Cookies (makes both double chocolate and red velvet — shared base, then divided):**

- **150g** butter, softened
- **200g** sugar
- **1** egg
- **1 tsp** vanilla extract
- **200g** flour
- **1 tsp** baking soda

Double Chocolate half:
- **25g** cocoa powder
- **100g** milk chocolate chips

Red Velvet half:
- **8g** cocoa powder
- Red food colouring
- White chocolate chips (amount to taste)

**White Chocolate Ice Cream:**

- **435ml** milk
- **450ml** cream (40% fat)
- **70g** sugar
- **60g** dextrose
- **40g** golden syrup
- **35g** SMP (skim milk powder)
- **50g** white chocolate
- **30ml** Mozart White Chocolate Liqueur

---

**Steps:**

**Cookies:**
1. Preheat oven to 175°C fan.
2. Cream butter and sugar until smooth. Beat in egg, then stir in vanilla.
3. Combine flour, baking soda, and salt; stir into creamed mixture until just blended.
4. Divide the dough in two. Add 25g cocoa + milk chocolate chips to one half (double chocolate). Add red food colouring + 8g cocoa + white chocolate chips to the other (red velvet).
5. Refrigerate both halves for 2–3 hours.
6. Portion into 2" rounds on a lined baking sheet.
7. Bake 12–15 minutes at 175°C fan. Cool completely (allow ~2 hours).

**Ice Cream:**
1. Warm the milk, sugars (sugar, dextrose, golden syrup), and SMP gently.
2. Add white chocolate and stir until melted. Remove from heat.
3. Add cream, then chill in fridge overnight (at least 4 hours).
4. Before churning, blend with an immersion blender if the white chocolate has semi-solidified.
5. Churn in machine (Musso 5030 shown). Freeze for ~2 hours until firm enough to scoop.

**Assembly:**
1. Scoop ice cream onto flat side of one cookie. Press second cookie on top.
2. Serve immediately or wrap and freeze.

---

**Notes:**

- Batch recipe — yields multiple sandwiches; no exact count given.
- White chocolate commonly semi-solidifies after overnight chilling — immersion blender or whisk before churning is essential.
- The Mozart White Chocolate Liqueur adds a noticeable boozy note; Nick describes flavour as "VERY nice white chocolate with alcohol bonus."
- Cookies are intentionally slightly gooey in the centre for the best sandwich texture.
- Machine used: Musso 5030 (Stella or Pola). This is a traditional batch ice cream churner, not a Ninja Creami.

---

### [Ben & Jerry's Fossil Fuel Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wojgoyZk9TY)
Recreating Ben & Jerry's discontinued (2005–2010) sweet cream ice cream with fudge dinosaur shapes, a chocolate fudge swirl, and chocolate cookie pieces.

**Ingredients:**

Ice Cream Base:
- **750ml** milk
- **450ml** double cream (heavy cream)
- **300g** sugar
- **7.5g** Silk Gel (mono- and diglycerides of fatty acids) OR **45g** skimmed milk powder
- Vanilla essence or extract (to taste)

Fudge Dinosaur Shapes:
- **60g** milk chocolate (Belgian milk chocolate callets used in video)

Fudge Swirl:
- **125g** Dairy Milk chocolate
- **125g** butter
- **1** egg
- **30g** sugar
- **5g** glucose (optional)

Cookie Pieces:
- Chocolate cookies — store-bought chewy cookies (or homemade if preferred)

**Steps:**

1. Combine all base ingredients (milk, cream, sugar, Silk Gel/skimmed milk powder) in a pan and heat gently until the stabiliser is fully dissolved. Add vanilla essence or extract.
2. Remove from heat, transfer to a container, and chill for a minimum of 4 hours.
3. Melt 60g milk chocolate in the microwave. Pour into a dinosaur silicone mould, ensuring all cavities are filled. Freeze for about 1 hour, then unmould the shapes and keep them in a container in the freezer until needed.
4. For the fudge swirl: melt the chocolate and butter together in the microwave. In a separate bowl, whisk the egg and sugar together. Combine the two mixtures and whisk well. Transfer to a container, ready to swirl in during assembly.
5. Churn the chilled base in an ice cream machine until done (approx. 12 minutes in a high-end machine; standard machines will take longer).
6. Transfer churned ice cream to a tub. Fold in cookie pieces (torn up), fudge swirl, and chocolate dinosaur shapes. Stir gently to distribute.
7. Freeze overnight (or at least a few hours) before serving.

**Notes:**
- The original B&J flavour was a plain sweet cream base — vanilla is an optional addition Nick adds for interest.
- Silk Gel (E471, mono- and diglycerides of fatty acids) is the stabiliser used; skimmed milk powder is given as a substitute.
- For the fudge dino shapes: if you want a softer, fudgier result rather than pure chocolate, add water at 15:1 ratio to the chocolate before melting, or add 0.5 tsp coconut oil before melting.
- Cookies were store-bought chewy chocolate cookies; use a homemade recipe if preferred.
- The glucose in the fudge swirl is optional.
- Machine used: Musso Stella 5030 (churns the full batch in ~12 minutes).
- This is a batch recipe (uses a traditional ice cream machine, not a Ninja Creami).
---

### [How to Make Ben & Jerry's Gimme S'more Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0GjUpocuBl8)

Homemade recreation of Ben & Jerry's Gimme S'more — toasted marshmallow custard base with a cookie swirl, graham cracker (digestive biscuit) swirl, and chocolate fudge flecks.

**Ingredients:**

Ice Cream Base:
- **90g** sugar
- **250ml** milk
- **300ml** double cream
- **3** large egg yolks
- **100g** white marshmallows (good quality)

Cookie Swirl:
- **75g** chocolate cookies (Nick used Maryland Cookies)
- **~2 tbsp** chocolate syrup (to suit)

Graham Cracker Swirl:
- **75g** graham crackers / digestive biscuits
- **1.5 tbsp** honey
- **1 tbsp** butter

Fudge:
- **1 can** condensed milk
- **100g** dark chocolate

**Steps:**

1. **Custard base:** Heat sugar and milk in a pan over low heat, stirring to dissolve sugar. Whisk egg yolks in a separate bowl. Slowly temper the hot milk into the yolks, adding a little at a time and whisking constantly to avoid scrambling. Once half the milk is incorporated, return the mixture to the pan and cook on low, stirring slowly, for 5–10 minutes until slightly thickened.
2. **Toast marshmallows:** Spread marshmallows on a baking tray and toast at 150–175°C for about 10 minutes. You want colour on the outside — start under the grill, then finish with a kitchen torch if needed. Keep marshmallows hot — do not let them cool or they become impossible to handle.
3. **Blend base:** Remove custard from heat. Immediately add the hot toasted marshmallows to a blender, pour in the custard, and blend on full power for 30–60 seconds until smooth and combined. The colour will change from the burnt marshmallow. Pour into a container, cover with cling film, and refrigerate for 24 hours.
4. **Graham cracker swirl:** Crush biscuits into a bowl. Melt butter and honey together on the hob, mix through crushed biscuits until a thick paste forms. Refrigerate until ready.
5. **Cookie swirl:** Crush cookies into a bowl and mix with chocolate syrup until you have a loose, scoopable mix. Refrigerate until ready.
6. **Fudge:** Melt dark chocolate and condensed milk together in a double boiler until thoroughly combined. Pour into a cling-film-lined container and let set at room temperature or in the fridge. Once hard, grate or chop into flecks. Store fudge flecks in the freezer so they stay separated.
7. **Churn:** Stir the chilled marshmallow custard well to loosen (it gels thick from the marshmallow gelatin), then combine with the double cream. Churn in an ice cream machine (took approx. 20 minutes).
8. **Layer and swirl:** Transfer churned ice cream to your container in layers, adding the cookie swirl, graham cracker swirl, and fudge flecks between each layer. Fold and swirl gently with a spatula. Freeze until firm.

**Notes:**

- Keep marshmallows hot when transferring to the blender — once they cool they stick to everything and become very hard to work with.
- Good quality marshmallows make a significant difference to the final flavour.
- Digestive biscuits are the UK substitute for graham crackers; adding honey to the swirl compensates for the honey/cinnamon notes in authentic graham crackers.
- Grating the fudge cold from the fridge and storing the flecks in the freezer prevents them from clumping back together before mix-in.
- Don't go easy with the add-ins — generous swirls are key to the s'more experience.
- The marshmallow gelatin causes the base to thicken noticeably after 24 hours in the fridge; stir well before combining with cream.

---

### [Ben & Jerry's Phish Food (Homemade)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=yYWdY5TKZtI)
Chocolate ice cream base with marshmallow cream swirl, salted caramel sauce, and molded chocolate fish pieces — a full recreation of the B&J classic.

**Ingredients:**

Ice cream base:
- **80g** sugar
- **2.5g** vanilla extract
- **30g** high-quality cocoa powder
- **30g** non-fat powdered milk

Chocolate fish (mix-ins):
- **100g** 80% dark chocolate
- **1 tbsp** water

Marshmallow cream:
- **2** large egg whites
- **100g** granulated sugar
- **1/4 tsp** cream of tartar
- **1/2 tsp** pure vanilla extract (optional)

Caramel sauce:
- Shop-bought salted caramel sauce (used: Jude's Salted Caramel)

**Steps:**

1. **Chocolate fish:** Combine chocolate and water in a bowl. Microwave in 30–45 second bursts, stirring until melted and smooth. Pour into small silicone molds (fish-shaped if available). Refrigerate until set, then pop out and store in fridge until assembly.
2. **Marshmallow cream (Swiss meringue):** Combine egg whites, sugar, and cream of tartar in a heatproof bowl over a pan of simmering water (double boiler). Stir constantly, heating gently until sugar fully dissolves and mixture reaches 71°C. Remove from heat and whisk with a hand or stand mixer until glossy, smooth, and stiff peaks form. Cover and refrigerate.
3. **Ice cream base:** Combine all base ingredients (sugar, vanilla, cocoa powder, milk) in a saucepan. Gently heat, stirring to dissolve and combine. Allow to cool, then refrigerate for at least 4 hours (24 hours ideal). Before churning, whisk in 2 tbsp non-fat milk powder to boost MSNF (milk solids-non-fat) and improve creaminess.
4. **Churn:** Pour chilled base into ice cream maker and churn 25–35 minutes until soft-serve consistency.
5. **Assembly:** Layer churned ice cream into tub. Dot in chocolate fish pieces, drizzle caramel sauce, and add spoonfuls of marshmallow cream. Gently swirl with a spoon to incorporate without fully mixing. Freeze until firm.

**Notes:**
- The water added to the chocolate prevents it from setting rock-hard in the freezer — this keeps the fish pieces pleasantly soft/fudgy.
- No fish mold? Use any small silicone mold (dinosaurs were used here as a substitute).
- Heating the base breaks down fat molecules; cooling encases them in milk protein, producing a creamier texture.
- Jude's Salted Caramel sauce is recommended; dolce de leche works but has a stronger, distinct flavor.
- The non-fat milk powder is added after chilling, whisked in just before churning — it adds MSNF for body and creaminess.
- This is a traditional churned ice cream recipe (ice cream maker required), not a Ninja Creami recipe.
---

### [S'mores Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3vd07LekYGc)

Churned s'mores ice cream with malt-flavoured base, homemade marshmallow fluff (torched in the tub), molded milk chocolate pieces, and buttery digestive biscuit crumb — a more intense version of Ben & Jerry's Gimme S'more.

**Ingredients — Ice Cream Base:**
- **500g** milk (1.8% fat)
- **350g** double cream (50% fat)
- **140g** sugar
- **50g** dextrose
- **60g** SMP (skimmed milk powder)
- **10g** malt extract (brewer's/beer-making grade)
- **5g** triple-fold vanilla extract (or 10g standard shop-bought)
- Proprietary stabilizer mix (mixed with some of the sugar before adding to milk)

**Ingredients — Digestive Biscuit Crumb (graham cracker sub):**
- Digestive biscuits (quantity to taste)
- Butter (melted, quantity to taste)

**Ingredients — Marshmallow Fluff:**
- **3** egg whites
- **150g** sugar
- **50g** golden syrup (or corn syrup — not high fructose)
- Pinch of salt
- **3–5g** vanilla extract

**Ingredients — Molded Chocolate Pieces:**
- Milk chocolate (quantity to taste)
- Small amount of deodorized coconut oil

**Steps:**
1. Warm the milk in a pan. Mix the stabilizer with a portion of the sugar first, then whisk into the warm milk to remove lumps. Add remaining sugar, dextrose, and malt extract; heat and stir until fully dissolved.
2. Add SMP and continue heating until fully homogenized.
3. Remove from heat, transfer to a container, stir in the cream and vanilla extract. Chill in the fridge overnight.
4. **Biscuit crumb:** Blitz digestive biscuits to a fine powder in a blender. Mix with melted butter, press onto a parchment-lined baking sheet and bake until set. Cool completely, then refrigerate. Cut into ~1 cm cubes before use.
5. **Marshmallow fluff:** Combine egg whites, sugar, and golden syrup in a heatproof bowl over a bain-marie (inch of simmering water). Heat to 75°C, then remove from the bain-marie. Whisk vigorously with a hand mixer until the mixture cools, thickens, and becomes glossy. Stir in vanilla extract. Set aside (keeps ~48 hours covered; watch for separation if refrigerated several days).
6. **Chocolate pieces:** Melt milk chocolate in the microwave. Stir in a small amount of deodorized coconut oil (softens set so pieces are less brittle in the ice cream). Pour into a mold (Nick used coffee-bean shaped). Refrigerate until set.
7. Pre-chill the ice cream machine bowl (~10 minutes). Churn the cold base until ribbons form on top.
8. In the final minutes of churning, add the chocolate pieces and biscuit crumb cubes; churn to incorporate.
9. Transfer to tub, layer marshmallow fluff over the top. Freeze until firm.
10. When serving, torch the marshmallow fluff on top until charred and caramelized before scooping.

**Notes:**
- Malt extract sourced from a homebrew/beer-making supplier — not widely available in supermarkets. Adds a warm, malty depth to the base.
- Digestive biscuits are the UK sub for graham crackers — not identical but the closest widely available alternative.
- Deodorized coconut oil removes the coconut flavour; adding it to melted chocolate keeps pieces slightly softer and easier to unmold. Nick noted he'd add a bit more next time for a ganache-like texture rather than fully hard pieces.
- Torching the marshmallow fluff directly in the tub (or on the serving bowl) does not melt the ice cream — only the surface is touched by the flame. This delivers the key s'mores "burnt marshmallow" note missing from the original B&J version.
- Marshmallow fluff keeps ~48 hours at room temperature (covered); may start to separate after a few days in the fridge depending on ingredients.
- This is a traditional churned ice cream recipe, not a Ninja Creami recipe.

---

### [How to Master Häagen-Dazs Belgian Chocolate Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=WMqQvMxHbzE)

A churned Belgian chocolate ice cream dupe of the Häagen-Dazs classic — rich, simple, and eggless.

**Ingredients:**

- **250ml** double cream (heavy cream)
- **125ml** milk
- **125g** sugar (granulated or caster)
- **100g** Belgian chocolate, 70% (chopped or grated fine)
- **30g** dark cocoa powder (organic recommended)
- **30g** skimmed milk powder

**Steps:**

1. Combine cream, milk, sugar, cocoa powder, and skimmed milk powder in a pot on the hob.
2. Heat, stirring, until sugar is fully dissolved.
3. Add the chopped/grated Belgian chocolate while still on the heat; whisk until fully melted and amalgamated.
4. Remove from heat and refrigerate for 24 hours.
5. After chilling, stir the mix (milk solids may have risen to the top — stir back in).
6. Churn in an ice cream machine until done (~30 minutes). Add any reserved chocolate pieces as a mix-in in the final couple of minutes of churning.
7. Transfer to a tub and freeze for ~4 hours before serving.

**Notes:**

- Use skimmed (no-fat) or at most low-fat milk powder — you want to increase solids, not fat content.
- Chocolate: 70% is the sweet spot. Above ~85% can turn bitter.
- An update from Nick recommends grating the chocolate (rather than chopping) with a fine/small grater for better integration into the mix.
- Any dark Belgian chocolate works — supermarket own-brand is fine, no need for premium couverture.
- This is a traditional churned recipe, not a Ninja Creami recipe.

---

### [How to make Cadbury Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-sjbNIYTPWM)

Custard-based chocolate ice cream loaded with Cadbury Dairy Milk, cocoa powder, Fingers, and a Flake — with an optional Mozart chocolate liqueur swirl.

**Ingredients:**

- **500ml** milk
- **600ml** double (heavy) cream
- **6** egg yolks
- **300g** sugar
- **120g** Cadbury Dairy Milk chocolate
- **2 tsp** Cadbury Cocoa Powder (heaped)
- **1** Cadbury Flake
- **1/2 pack** Cadbury Fingers
- **1–2 tbsp** Mozart Chocolate Cream Liqueur (optional)
- Dark chocolate sauce (for layering)

**Steps:**

1. Combine milk and sugar in a pan. Gently heat until sugar dissolves and milk just begins to steam — do not boil.
2. Add 6 egg yolks to a separate bowl. Temper by gradually adding ~5 tablespoons of the hot milk mixture while whisking vigorously, then pour the tempered eggs back into the pan.
3. Keep over low heat, stirring continuously. Add 120g Cadbury Dairy Milk chocolate (broken up) and 2 heaped tsp cocoa powder. Stir until chocolate melts and custard coats the back of a spoon. Pour into a large jug.
4. Stir in 600ml double cream and whisk to combine. This is your chocolate ice cream base.
5. Pour the base into a pre-chilled ice cream maker (pre-chill ~20 minutes if required) and churn until soft-serve consistency — approximately 20 minutes.
6. While churning, slice Cadbury Fingers and Flake into chunks.
7. Once churned, add half the chocolate chunks and the Mozart liqueur (if using) into the machine for a final quick churn to distribute evenly.
8. Spoon half the ice cream into your container, add the remaining chocolate chunks, then top with the rest of the ice cream.
9. Add a generous squirt of dark chocolate sauce and gently swirl with a spoon to create ribbons throughout. Freeze until firm.

**Notes:**

- Tempering tip: if unsure, keep adding an extra spoonful of hot milk to the eggs before returning them to the pan — this prevents scrambling.
- The Mozart Chocolate Cream Liqueur is entirely optional but adds a boozy depth; Cadbury originally made their own chocolate liqueur but discontinued it.
- Cadbury add-ins are flexible — use any favourite Cadbury chocolate (Roses, Buttons, Heroes, etc.) cut into chunks.
- This is a traditional churned custard recipe, not a Ninja Creami recipe.

---

### [How to make Dondurma Turkish Ice Cream at home FINAL](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ap5Wg4fNfro)

Luxury stretchy Dondurma using glucomannan (Konjac root flour) as a salep substitute — no salep, no mastic needed.

**Ingredients:**
- **450g** milk (2% fat)
- **350g** cream (33–36% fat)
- **120g** sugar
- **40g** dextrose (or 50g additional sugar)
- **40g** SMP (skim milk powder)
- **4g** glucomannan (Konjac root flour / Konjac flour)
- **1** vanilla bean (split and scraped)

**Steps:**
1. Combine sugar, dextrose, and glucomannan in a bowl and stir together (dry mixing first helps prevent clumping).
2. Add milk and cream to a pot. Pour in the dry mix and the skim milk powder. Stir to combine.
3. Add the vanilla bean pod and scraped seeds to the pot.
4. Heat on the hob, stirring continuously, until the mixture reaches 85–90°C (185–195°F). Hold and stir for a few minutes to fully activate the glucomannan starch.
5. Pour into a container, remove vanilla bean pod after infusing, and refrigerate overnight.
6. After chilling, the base will be thick and viscous. Stir well, then churn in an ice cream machine for ~10 minutes.
7. Scoop into a freezer container and freeze overnight.
8. Before serving, remove from freezer and let temper at room temperature for 5–10 minutes (target serving temp ~-9 to -10°C).
9. Wearing food-safe gloves, work the ice cream by hand (beating and stretching) for 1–2 minutes. The heat from your hands plus the warmed temperature allows the glucomannan starches to reform and develop maximum stretch.
10. Serve immediately.

**Notes:**
- Glucomannan is the active polysaccharide in traditional salep (Turkish orchid root flour) — Konjac root flour contains the same compound and is the recommended substitute.
- Higher heating temp (85–90°C) is required compared to typical stabilizers because glucomannan is a starch and needs more heat to fully hydrate and activate.
- The beat-and-stretch step at the end is essential — skipping it results in far less stretch.
- Ice cream should be served at ~-9 to -10°C, significantly warmer than straight-out-of-freezer temperature; this is authentic to street-vendor style Turkish dondurma.
- Mastic is an optional traditional flavoring for stretchiness; Nick finds it unnecessary with glucomannan.
- Vanilla is not a traditional Dondurma flavor (rose water is more authentic), but it works well here.
- This is a churned recipe (ice cream machine required), not a Ninja Creami recipe.
---

### [Keto Chocolate Caramel Ice Cream (Rolo-inspired)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IFnXQi_WndE)

Keto/sugar-free chocolate ice cream with a homemade allulose caramel swirl — churned in a traditional ice cream machine, layered into tubs.

**Ingredients — Ice Cream Base:**
- **390g** heavy cream (33% fat)
- **197g** water
- **107g** allulose
- **36g** inulin
- **25g** cocoa powder (Nick uses deZaan True Gold)
- **2g** tara gum

**Ingredients — Allulose Caramel:**
- **80g** allulose
- **28g** butter, room temperature
- **45g** heavy/whipping cream, room temperature
- **1/4 tsp** vanilla extract
- **1/2 tsp** flaky sea salt

**Steps:**
1. Pre-mix tara gum with the allulose to prevent clumping.
2. Add cream, water, and allulose/tara gum mixture to a pan. Heat, stirring, until ~50°C.
3. Add cocoa powder and stir until fully dissolved and homogenized.
4. Bring base to 80–85°C, then remove from heat.
5. Transfer to a container and chill in the fridge.
6. Make the caramel: add allulose and butter together into a pan over medium heat. Do not stir — let melt and start to brown on its own, swirling the pan as needed.
7. When a rich caramel color is reached, turn off heat. Carefully add room-temperature cream (mixture may spit). Stir until combined.
8. Let caramel cool off-heat until no longer bubbling, then stir in vanilla extract and a pinch of flaky sea salt. Refrigerate alongside the ice cream base.
9. Once base is fully chilled, stir to remove lumps and pour into a pre-chilled ice cream machine (pre-run for 10 minutes). Churn until soft-serve consistency.
10. Layer ice cream and caramel into tubs: one scoop of ice cream, one drizzle of caramel, repeat until tubs are full.
11. Freeze for at least 24–48 hours before serving.

**Notes:**
- This is a **traditional churned recipe** (not a Ninja Creami recipe) — yields multiple tubs.
- Allulose is critical for both the base and caramel. It has strong freezing-point depression — measure precisely; 20–30g too much will prevent the base from freezing.
- Avoid erythritol for caramel — it recrystallizes almost every time. Allulose or monk fruit work best.
- Flaky/high-quality sea salt is recommended over table salt — different character and flavor impact.
- Nick uses deZaan True Gold cocoa powder — milky chocolate profile (~30–35% cocoa solid equivalent), dissolves easily. Any quality cocoa works.
- Nick reports 9.5g net carbs per tub (500ml), 4.5g net carbs per serving (half tub).
- Base macros per description: 18.8% fat, 0 sugar, 3% MSNF, 43.2% solids.

---

### [Lemon Meringue Pie Ice Cream (Philly Style, No Eggs)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=zV9tAIwk6WU)

Eggless philly-style lemon ice cream base layered with crushed meringue and lemon curd.

**Ingredients:**

- **500g** double/heavy cream
- **250g** full-fat whole milk
- **170g** granulated sugar
- **30g** non-fat milk powder (2 tablespoons — acts as stabilizer)
- **2.5g** vanilla extract (½ tsp — reduced from full 1 tsp to not overpower lemon)
- **1** whole large lemon, zest/rind only

**Mix-ins / assembly:**
- Crushed French meringue (shop-bought used in video)
- Lemon curd (shop-bought used in video; homemade = lemon juice + eggs + butter + sugar)

**Steps:**

1. In a large mixing bowl, combine cream, milk, sugar, milk powder, vanilla extract, and lemon zest.
2. Whisk well until fully combined.
3. Pre-chill ice cream machine (condenser type) for ~10 minutes.
4. Pour mixture into ice cream maker and churn ~30 minutes until soft-serve consistency.
5. In a pre-chilled container, layer: lemon curd on the bottom, then a layer of ice cream, then crushed meringue, then more lemon curd — repeat until container is full.
6. Freeze to harden.

**Notes:**

- Philly style = no eggs; produces a creamy, luxurious texture without a custard base.
- Milk powder is used as a stabilizer to prevent the ice cream going rock solid in the freezer.
- Vanilla is intentionally halved to avoid competing with lemon flavor.
- Lemon zest in the base gives subtle, fresh lemon flavor; lemon curd in the layers provides the punch.
- Meringue must be very dry or it will dissolve into the ice cream. Shop-bought works well.
- Lemon curd can be made at home (lemon juice, eggs, butter, sugar) but shop-bought is fine.

---

### [McDonald's Shamrock Shake & McFlurry](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wWkmo72umjw)

Homemade copycat of the McDonald's Shamrock Shake milkshake and Shamrock Oreo McFlurry — both made from the same vanilla-mint ice cream base churned in an ice cream machine.

**Ingredients:**

- **500ml** double (heavy) cream
- **250ml** milk
- **150g** sugar
- **2–3 tbsp** fat-free (1%) milk powder (skim milk powder)
- **2 tbsp** vanilla extract
- **1/16 tsp** peppermint extract
- Green food colouring, to taste
- Crushed Oreos (for McFlurry mix-in and decoration)
- Whipped cream (from cream whipper, for Shamrock Shake topping)

**Steps:**

1. Combine milk and sugar in a bowl and whisk until sugar fully dissolves.
2. Add skim milk powder, stir in, then refrigerate for 20 minutes.
3. Remove from fridge, stir again, then add the cream and vanilla extract.
4. Add the peppermint extract (just 1/16 tsp — it's a very subtle hint, not mint ice cream).
5. Add green food colouring to desired shade and give a final stir.
6. Pour the base into an ice cream machine and churn 15–25 minutes (depending on machine size) until soft-serve consistency.
7. **For the McFlurry:** ~5 minutes before churning finishes, add approximately 75% of the crushed Oreos into the machine; reserve the rest for decoration. Once done, scoop into a glass/cup and top with remaining crushed Oreos.
8. **For the Shamrock Shake:** Transfer the churned ice cream to a container and freeze for several hours. When ready to serve, blend the frozen ice cream with a splash of milk until milkshake consistency, pour into a glass, and top with whipped cream from a cream whipper.

**Notes:**

- This is the same base for both products — make one batch and split it between the two.
- Key flavour insight: McDonald's Shamrock Shake is **vanilla ice cream with a touch of mint** (not mint ice cream). Using actual mint ice cream gives the wrong flavour profile.
- Peppermint extract is very potent — 1/16 tsp is intentional; don't increase it.
- If making the milkshake from frozen ice cream, add extra green food colouring and a touch more peppermint to the glass before blending — some colour and flavour intensity is lost when blending with milk.
- McFlurry requires soft-serve texture, so an ice cream machine is strongly recommended; a no-churn base works but won't replicate the soft-serve result.
- Recipe makes enough for both a milkshake and a McFlurry from a single batch.

### [Snowball 3 Ways — Advocaat Ice Cream (UK Christmas Drink)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=q0-Fxmpru7s)

Three serving variations of the classic British Christmas Snowball cocktail (advocaat + lemonade), with a homemade advocaat ice cream as the centrepiece — dedicated advocaat ice cream recipe video was promised as a follow-up.

**Ingredients (Variation 1 — Classic Snowball):**
- **50ml** Warnick's advocaat
- Lemonade, to top up
- Lime wedge, to garnish

**Ingredients (Variation 2 — Ice Cream Float Snowball):**
- **50ml** Warnick's advocaat
- Lemonade, to top up
- 1 scoop advocaat ice cream (eggless base with a large quantity of advocaat + meringue pieces mixed in — full recipe promised in separate video)

**Ingredients (Variation 3 — Advocaat Affogato Snowball):**
- 1–2 scoops advocaat ice cream (with meringue pieces)
- 1 shot freshly brewed espresso, poured over the top
- Advocaat white chocolate sauce (made separately), drizzled on top

**Steps:**
1. **Classic Snowball:** Pour 50ml advocaat into a glass, top with lemonade, stir briefly, garnish with a lime wedge.
2. **Ice Cream Float:** Make the classic snowball as above, then add a scoop of advocaat ice cream on top — the carbonation from the lemonade creates a float effect.
3. **Affogato Snowball:** Place 1–2 scoops of advocaat ice cream in a martini glass, pour a shot of hot espresso over the top, then drizzle advocaat white chocolate sauce over everything.

**Notes:**
- This video is a Christmas drinks showcase, not a Ninja Creami recipe video. The dedicated advocaat ice cream recipe (Ninja Creami) was filmed separately and released after Christmas.
- Advocaat brand used: Warnick's (Dutch egg liqueur, widely available in the UK).
- The ice cream used is described as an eggless base with a large amount of advocaat and meringue pieces folded in — full recipe not given in this video.
- The advocaat white chocolate sauce was made fresh approximately 20 minutes before filming; no recipe given.
- Nick notes the classic Snowball is 50ml advocaat topped with lemonade — variations sourced from Warnick's official website.
- Two further hot-chocolate-based advocaat variations were teased for a follow-up video.

### [How to make perfect Ben & Jerry's Karamel Sutra](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MDFSO3ZTOUQ)

Ben & Jerry's Karamel Sutra dupe — two churned ice cream halves (chocolate and caramel) with a sticky caramel core piped down the centre. Batch recipe (multiple tubs).

**Ingredients — Chocolate Base:**
- **550g** milk @ 2% fat
- **300g** cream @ 50% fat
- **160g** sugar
- **50g** dextrose or glucose
- **60g** skimmed milk powder
- **75g** cocoa powder @ 10–12% fat (Nick used a lower-fat cocoa from Design)
- **2g** stabilizer (optional)

**Ingredients — Caramel Base:**
- **500g** milk @ 2% fat
- **400g** cream @ 50% fat
- **140g** sugar
- **30g** dextrose
- **75g** Sosa Dulce de Leche Powder (or 50g canned dulce de leche as substitute)
- **2g** stabilizer

**Ingredients — Caramel Core:**
- **250g** sugar
- **100g** water
- **25g** butter
- **250g** cream (heavy or double)
- **50g** glucose (liquid — important for freeze stability)

**Steps:**

1. **Caramel base:** Add milk to pan. Mix sugar, stabilizer, and Sosa Dulce de Leche Powder together and whisk into the milk. Heat until fully dissolved. Remove from heat, pour into container, add cream, stir to combine. Chill in fridge 4–5 hours.
2. **Chocolate base:** Add milk to pan. Pre-mix sugar, stabilizer, skimmed milk powder, and cocoa powder together dry, then whisk into the milk — combining dry ingredients first helps the cocoa dissolve. Heat until completely dissolved. Remove from heat, pour into container, add cream, stir to combine. Chill in fridge 4–5 hours.
3. **Caramel core (wet caramel):** Add sugar and water to pan and bring to a deep amber colour — watch colour, not a thermometer. Turn off heat. Add butter carefully (will spit). Once butter is fully melted in, add liquid glucose and stir until melted. Finally add cream and stir until fully emulsified. Transfer to container and chill to fridge temperature.
4. **Churn chocolate base** first. When nearly done, take a few spoonfuls and put into one half of each tub (tub held on its side). Cover with cling film/plastic wrap, lid on, freeze on its side for ~45 minutes.
5. **Churn caramel base** while chocolate half is setting in freezer. Once churned, take tubs out of freezer and fill the other half of each tub with caramel ice cream. Tap down to fill voids, cover with cling film, lid on, return to freezer upright.
6. **Core and fill:** After a few hours (monitor — don't let it go rock solid), use a coring tool to bore a tunnel down the centre of each tub. Pipe or pour the chilled caramel core into the tunnel. Seal and return to freezer until fully set.

**Notes:**
- Sosa Dulce de Leche Powder is Nick's preferred caramel flavour ingredient — canned dulce de leche (50g) is the accessible substitute.
- Glucose in the caramel core is important — it lowers the freezing point so the core stays sticky and scoopable rather than setting hard.
- Nick accidentally let the ice cream freeze overnight before coring, making the step much harder; do the coring within a few hours while the ice cream is still workable.
- Semi-sweet 50–56% chocolate (chopped) is added as a mix-in to the chocolate half just before it finishes churning — amounts to taste.
- This is a batch recipe: the ingredient quantities produce multiple tubs. Scale down proportionally for a single tub.

### [How to make the most delicious dulce de leche gelato](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=D0-aAwonX7U)
Argentinian-style Dulce de Leche gelato adapted from Gellö Helados (Vera Cruz, Mexico), using two types of Argentine DDL for an authentic, less-sweet flavor profile.

**Ingredients:**
- **471g** water (no milk in this recipe)
- **60g** skimmed milk powder
- **28g** whole milk powder
- **60g** dextrose (powdered glucose if dextrose unavailable)
- **150g** whipping cream (33% fat)
- **126g** Havana dulce de leche (repostero/pastry-grade DDL)
- **90g** Vacalin dulce de leche
- Stabilizer blend: carrageenan + locust bean gum + guar gum (amounts not specified on screen)
- Emulsifier: sunflower lecithin (amount not specified on screen)

**Steps:**
1. Combine water, skimmed milk powder, whole milk powder, dextrose, and the stabilizer/emulsifier blend in a pot. Stir to combine.
2. Add whipping cream and both dulce de leche varieties.
3. Heat on the hob, bringing to 85°C and holding for a couple of minutes to fully hydrate the stabilizers and homogenize the mix.
4. Remove from heat, transfer to a container, and refrigerate overnight (minimum 4 hours).
5. Next day, stir the chilled base well, then churn in an ice cream machine until it reaches approximately -10 to -12°C.
6. Transfer to ice cream containers and freeze for 24 hours before serving.

**Notes:**
- Recipe is a slightly modified version of Gellö Helados' original; adapted for home ice cream machines.
- Two DDL types are used: a repostero/pastry-grade DDL (Havana) designed for ice cream use, and a standard table-grade DDL (Vacalin) — each has a different flavor strength.
- Argentine DDL is notably less sweet and more complex than North American/UK canned DDL (e.g. Eagle Brand); sourcing authentic Argentine DDL is strongly recommended. Nick sourced his from www.theargentino.com (ships worldwide from Patagonia).
- The invert sugars naturally present in dulce de leche give good scoopability straight from the freezer — no temper wait needed.
- Base is designed as a mix-in-friendly recipe; primed for nuts and chocolate add-ins.
- Must heat to 85°C due to carrageenan and locust bean gum requiring high-temperature hydration.
- Chill overnight for best results (4 hours minimum).

### [Alton Brown's Serious Vanilla Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ppesx3LOfC4)

Nick tests Alton Brown's egg-free vanilla ice cream that uses liquid pectin as the stabilizer, analyzes the numbers, and gives his honest verdict.

**Ingredients:**

- **100g** sugar (for dairy mixture)
- **50g** sugar (for pectin activation)
- **60g** water
- **15g** liquid pectin
- **480g** half and half (10% fat — North American; outside NA: blend single cream + water to reach 10% fat)
- **245g** heavy cream (34–36% fat)
- **40g** vanilla bean paste
- **0.1 tsp** sea salt (pinch; do not use table salt)

**Steps:**

1. Combine 50g sugar and 60g water in a small saucepan. Bring to a boil.
2. Add 15g liquid pectin. Return to a rolling boil for exactly 1 minute, then remove from heat.
3. Cool pectin mixture at room temperature for 25 minutes, until it reaches approximately 89°F (32°C). Gently swirl the pan occasionally to prevent a sticky layer forming on the sides.
4. Meanwhile, combine the remaining 100g sugar, the half and half, the heavy cream, and the sea salt in a separate saucepan. Heat to 185°F (85°C), then remove from heat.
5. Cover with a lid (or foil) and steep for 20 minutes. When removing the cover, avoid dripping condensation back into the mixture.
6. Whisk the cooled pectin mixture thoroughly, then pour it into the dairy mixture. Stir to combine.
7. Add 40g vanilla bean paste. Stir well.
8. Cool the combined mixture to room temperature using an ice bath, stirring continuously.
9. Transfer to an airtight container and chill in the fridge for 6–24 hours (Nick does a full 24 hours).
10. Churn in an ice cream maker per machine instructions.

**Notes:**

- Outside North America, "half and half" (10% fat) can be approximated by diluting single cream with water to reach the target fat percentage. Same approach applies to the heavy cream if using higher-fat double cream (50%): add water to bring it back to ~36% fat.
- Nick's critique: the recipe is low in total solids (~31–32%), which results in a serving temperature of around -10°C — too cold to scoop straight from the freezer. Needs 5–10 minutes tempering before serving.
- The missing fix: skim milk powder (SMP / low-fat milk powder / zero-fat milk powder) would raise solids, lower serving temperature, and improve texture. The lactose in SMP provides PAC (anti-freezing power) and brings serving temperature to a better range.
- The pectin activation step (boiling with 50g sugar + water) is essential — sugar is required to activate pectin; water prevents scorching. The 185°F dairy heating denatures proteins and integrates the pectin. The overnight chill does the real structural work; the 20-minute steep is largely incidental.
- Pectin is an unusual stabilizer for ice cream but works decently. Alton Brown uses it instead of eggs to achieve a smooth, creamy texture without a custard base.
- Nick rates it as a good, accessible recipe — easy to make, great flavor — but not yet "serious" without the solids boost from SMP.

---

### [Coffee Coffee Buzz Buzz Buzz (Insanely Caffeinated Coffee Fudge Ice Cream)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=s-vhOxo64yo)

Ninja Creami coffee ice cream inspired by the Ben & Jerry's flavor — ultra-caffeinated espresso base with a dense dark chocolate coffee fudge mix-in. Ninja Creami Deluxe 750ml batch.

**Ingredients — Ice Cream Base:**
- **190g** whole milk
- **255g** cream
- **120g** espresso (or strong coffee — see notes)
- **130g** sugar
- **10g** corn syrup (or glucose / golden syrup)
- **45g** skim milk powder
- **1.5g** stabilizer (guar gum or similar — used to prevent icing; described as slightly more than usual due to coffee's tendency to ice)

**Ingredients — Coffee Fudge:**
- **400g** sweetened condensed milk (1 full can)
- **200g** dark chocolate (Lindt used in video)
- **20g** instant coffee powder (or 10g coffee extract)

**Steps:**
1. Combine milk and cream in a jug.
2. In a separate container, mix sugar, skim milk powder, and stabilizer together dry.
3. Brew 120g espresso (or make very strong instant coffee — 4–5 teaspoons in 120ml water).
4. Add the dry sugar mix into the milk/cream jug. Add the corn syrup. Pour in the hot espresso. Stir until sugars dissolve.
5. Pour the base into the Creami pint. Skim off any large air bubbles from the surface. Freeze overnight (minimum 24h).
6. **Make the fudge:** Gently heat the sweetened condensed milk in a pan until warm enough to melt the chocolate. Add the dark chocolate and instant coffee powder. Stir until a smooth, glossy, homogeneous fudge forms — do not fully dissolve all coffee granules (intentional texture).
7. Pour fudge into a lined baking pan/sheet. Cool to room temperature, then freeze until firm (overnight alongside the base).
8. Spin the frozen base on the Ice Cream setting.
9. While the base is spinning, remove the fudge from the freezer/fridge and chop into small cubes.
10. Add fudge cubes to the spun ice cream and run the Mix-In setting.

**Notes:**
- Batch size is for the Ninja Creami Deluxe 750ml pint — scale down for the standard 24oz pint.
- Espresso alternatives: pod machine on espresso setting, moka pot, or any strong brew method. Avoid weak coffee — the high water content from coffee can cause iciness, hence the slightly elevated stabilizer amount.
- Liquid coffee cannot be used in the fudge — only powdered/instant coffee works there. Leaving some granules undissolved is intentional for texture.
- The fudge is dense and sticky; the Mix-In setting handled it without needing to hand-fold.
- High caffeine content — consume responsibly.

---

### [Authentic Japanese Matcha Gelato](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gOmZtb6VYAk)

Ultra-low-fat gelato (6% fat) made with ceremonial-grade matcha, kept cold-processed to preserve the vivid green color and bold umami flavor.

**Ingredients:**

- **30g** organic ceremonial-grade matcha powder (any grade works; ceremonial preferred)
- **500g** 2% milk (Nick noted the corrected batch uses more milk — see Notes)
- **125g** 33% whipping cream
- **30g** skim milk powder
- **120g** sugar (110g added to base; 10g reserved to mix with stabilizer)
- **40g** dextrose
- Guar gum (amount unspecified — mixed with the reserved 10g sugar for cold-process dispersion)

**Steps:**

1. Combine the 500g milk, 125g cream, 40g dextrose, 110g sugar, skim milk powder, and 30g matcha powder in a saucepan.
2. Mix the reserved 10g sugar with the guar gum, then add to the pan.
3. Gently warm on the hob to ~55–60°C (steam point only — do not overheat, to avoid cooked dairy flavor overpowering the matcha).
4. Remove from heat. Transfer to a container and let cool.
5. Once cool, strain through a fine sieve to remove any matcha lumps or impurities.
6. Refrigerate for at least a few hours; overnight or 24h is ideal to let the matcha flavor bloom into the dairy.
7. Churn in an ice cream machine until the desired consistency is reached.

**Notes:**

- Nick filmed two batches. The first used the recipe as listed above and produced almost no overrun — the high fiber content of matcha made the mix too viscous. He redid it with more milk to reduce viscosity and achieve better overrun. The description does not list the corrected amounts; use more milk (exact amount not specified on-screen) if overrun is poor.
- Dextrose is used instead of more sugar to reduce sweetness, letting the matcha flavor dominate, while also helping lower the freezing point.
- Guar gum is a cold-process stabilizer — no need to heat above 60°C.
- Matcha fiber (insoluble) boosts overrun potential but also increases viscosity; balance is key.
- The cream smooths out matcha's natural bitterness; if omitting cream, increase stabilizer and consider adding another body builder.
- Strain after chilling, not before — this avoids pushing large particles through the sieve.
- Serving tip: the flavor is very intense — a small quenelle portion is sufficient.
- This is a traditional churned gelato recipe, not a Ninja Creami recipe.
---

### [Ben & Jerry's Dirt Cake Topped](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tZPno_X1pLc)

Vanilla pudding ice cream with Oreo cookie chunks and crumb swirl, topped with a milk chocolate ganache and crushed cookie crumb — a homemade recreation of Ben & Jerry's Dirt Cake Topped.

**Ingredients:**

Base:
- **370g** milk (2%)
- **370g** cream (33% whipping cream)
- **26g** sugar
- **90g** invert sugar / corn syrup
- **10g** vanilla extract (optional — pudding mix provides enough vanilla flavour)
- **56g** skim milk powder
- **84g** Jell-O vanilla pudding mix

Oreo layer & swirl:
- **270g** pack Original Oreos (1 full pack)

Chocolate top:
- **100g** milk chocolate (Cacao Barry, ~41-45% cacao)
- **10g** refined coconut oil (deodorized — no coconut flavour)

**Steps:**

1. Combine milk, cream, sugar, invert sugar, vanilla extract, skim milk powder, and Jell-O vanilla pudding mix in a saucepan. Whisk until fully combined.
2. Heat to 80°C, then remove from heat. Transfer to a container and refrigerate until completely chilled.
3. Set aside 9 Oreo cookies. Roughly chop the remaining Oreos into large cookie chunks and store separately.
4. Split the 9 reserved Oreos, scrape out the cream filling, then crush the cookie wafers with a pestle and mortar into a coarse crumb (not fine powder). Store separately.
5. Pre-chill your ice cream machine bowl for 5–10 minutes. Pour in the chilled base and churn.
6. A couple of minutes before churning is done, add the large cookie chunks and let them incorporate.
7. Layer into tubs: one scoop of ice cream, a layer of cookie crumb powder, repeat until ice cream is used up. Reserve some cookie crumb for the top. Leave ~1 cm of headspace at the top.
8. Freeze tubs for approximately 3 hours until the surface is firm and smooth.
9. Melt 100g chocolate with 10g coconut oil in the microwave (~30 seconds). Stir until fully melted and emulsified.
10. Gently pour the melted chocolate over the top of each frozen tub. Immediately sprinkle the reserved cookie crumb on top.
11. Return to freezer to harden completely before serving.

**Notes:**

- This recipe makes multiple tubs (batch yield — description states 270g pack of Oreos across the batch).
- Jell-O vanilla pudding mix is the key flavour driver; vanilla extract is optional and can be omitted.
- Use deodorized (refined) coconut oil for the chocolate top — it has zero coconut flavour. Extra virgin olive oil also works and adds a subtle flavour note.
- For the cookie swirl, remove the cream filling from the reserved 9 Oreos before crushing — only the wafer crumb is used.
- Leave ~1 cm headspace in the tub before freezing so the chocolate top fits within the lid line.
- Nick uses Cacao Barry milk chocolate (~41% cacao) for the topping; any quality milk chocolate around 40–45% cacao works.
- Recipe metrics (base only): ~13% fat, ~17% sugar, ~10.9% MSNF (milk solids-non-fat), ~41.5% total solids, ~58.3% water.

---

### [Häagen-Dazs Belgian Chocolate Ice Cream (Custard Base)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cnXYz8YixeY)

Custard-base dupe of Häagen-Dazs Belgian Chocolate, churned in a traditional ice cream machine with a chocolate chunk add-in.

**Ingredients:**

- **250ml** milk @ 1.8% fat
- **200ml** cream @ 49% fat (double/heavy cream)
- **100g** sugar
- **40g** dextrose
- **2** egg yolks (UK medium)
- **10g** cocoa powder @ 24% fat (Callebaut used in video; Seven Hills recommended for closer HD match)
- **40g** 70% chocolate (melted into base; can reduce to 25–30g)
- Extra 70% chocolate for add-in (amount to taste), frozen ahead of time

**Steps:**

1. Add milk to a pan. Add sugar and dextrose; stir to combine.
2. Add egg yolks and begin heating over medium heat.
3. At around 50°C, whisk in the cocoa powder.
4. Continue heating to 77°C (ensures eggs are cooked; skip if using pasteurised eggs, though heating helps custard consistency).
5. Remove from heat. Add the 40g of 70% chocolate and stir well until fully melted.
6. Decant into a container, add the 200ml cream, and stir well.
7. Refrigerate for several hours until fully chilled.
8. Meanwhile, place the add-in chocolate in the freezer — frozen chocolate is much easier to chop finely.
9. Churn base in an ice cream machine for 15–30 minutes until soft-serve consistency.
10. While churning, chop the frozen add-in chocolate as finely as possible (a grater works well).
11. At the end of the churn, add the chopped chocolate into the machine and let it incorporate.
12. Transfer to a tub and freeze for several hours before scooping.

**Notes:**

- This is a traditional churned recipe (not a Ninja Creami recipe).
- Nick considers his earlier eggless version (https://youtu.be/WMqQvMxHbzE) a closer match to actual Häagen-Dazs — the custard base version is very good but not as accurate.
- The cocoa powder brand makes a significant difference: Seven Hills cocoa powder produced an almost identical flavour profile to Häagen-Dazs in the eggless version; Callebaut was used here and is noticeably different.
- Dextrose can be substituted with table sugar, though it will have a slightly different effect on scoop texture.
- Chop add-in chocolate as fine as possible for the best distribution; grating works well.

---

### [Jack Sparrow Rum Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hmX9T1e5-hM)

A Pirates of the Caribbean-inspired boozy ice cream featuring three rums at different ABVs: Lambs vanilla rum in the base, Sparrow's rum in a wet caramel sauce, and Pusser's Gunpowder Proof in dark chocolate truffles.

**Ingredients — Ice Cream Base:**
- **356g** heavy/double cream
- **451g** milk (2% or semi-skimmed)
- **84g** skimmed milk powder
- **80g** egg yolk (approx. 3 medium yolks)
- **180g** sugar
- **50g** Lambs Vanilla Rum (30% ABV / 60 proof)
- **2g** stabiliser blend

**Ingredients — Sparrow's Rum Caramel:**
- **225g** sugar
- **60g** water
- **71g** butter (room temperature)
- **125g** cream
- **80g** Sparrow's Rum (40% ABV / 80 proof)

**Ingredients — Gunpowder Rum Chocolate Truffles:**
- **100g** double cream
- **25g** salted butter, cubed
- **300g** milk chocolate, finely chopped
- **50ml** Pusser's Gunpowder Proof Rum (54.5% ABV / 109 proof)
- **2–3 tbsp** cocoa powder (for coating)

**Steps:**
1. **Ice cream base:** Combine milk, egg yolks, sugar, and skimmed milk powder in a saucepan. Heat to 70°C to cook the egg yolks. Turn off the heat, then stir in the Lambs Vanilla Rum. Transfer to a container and chill in the fridge.
2. **Truffles:** Gently warm the double cream in the microwave (or hob). Pour over the finely chopped milk chocolate and cubed butter; stir until fully melted. Stir in the Pusser's Gunpowder Rum. Pour into a lined 8x8 square tin and refrigerate until solid.
3. Once the truffle slab is chilled, scoop portions and roll between gloved hands into balls. Drop into cocoa powder and roll until fully coated. Store in the fridge until assembly.
4. **Caramel:** Combine sugar and water in a pan; dissolve the sugar and gently bring to heat. Cook to desired amber colour, then turn off the heat (it continues cooking). Add room-temperature butter and cream; mix until combined. Stir in the Sparrow's Rum (80g). Keep at room temperature until assembly.
5. **Churn:** Process the chilled base in an ice cream machine. Note: high alcohol content means it will take longer to churn — reducing the sugar compensates for this.
6. **Assembly:** Transfer churned ice cream to a tub, layering in caramel and halved truffles throughout. Seal and freeze for at least 6 hours.

**Notes:**
- Sugar in the base is intentionally reduced because the Lambs rum contributes sweetness and lowers the freezing point — this also prevents the base from being too soft after churning.
- Rum ABV has a significant impact: Lambs (30%) is mild in the base; Sparrow's (40%) intensifies the caramel; Pusser's Gunpowder (54.5%) makes the truffles fiercely potent.
- The ice cream alone is moderate in alcohol; the caramel and truffles make this extreme — serve them "on the side" for guests who want to control the intensity.
- Any rum can be substituted, but match the ABV carefully — swapping to a higher-proof spirit will keep the ice cream softer/less frozen.
- Three-part recipe: base + caramel + truffles are all made separately before final assembly.

---

### [Irresistible Häagen Dazs Chocolate Peanut Butter Recipe](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VykKwEV-82w)

Homemade dupe of the original Häagen-Dazs Chocolate Peanut Butter flavour — rich chocolate base with frozen peanut butter chunks and a xylitol-based peanut butter swirl that stays soft in the freezer.

**Ingredients:**

Base:
- **250ml** double cream (heavy cream)
- **125ml** milk
- **125g** sugar (granulated or caster)
- **100g** 70% Belgian chocolate
- **2 tbsp** dark cocoa powder
- **2 tbsp** milk powder

Peanut butter swirl:
- **50g** xylitol
- **50ml** water
- **30–40g** peanut butter (Skippy used)

Peanut butter chunks:
- Peanut butter (Skippy), dolloped as lumps — amount to taste

**Steps:**

1. Combine all base ingredients (cream, milk, sugar, chocolate, cocoa powder, milk powder) in a saucepan and gently heat, stirring until the chocolate melts and cocoa disperses fully.
2. Remove from heat. Chill in the fridge for at least 4 hours.
3. For the chunks: dollop spoonfuls of peanut butter onto a frozen plate and freeze until solid.
4. For the swirl: combine xylitol and water in a small pan. Reduce by half like a simple syrup (note: xylitol syrup won't thicken as much as cane sugar syrup). Stir in peanut butter until well combined. Store in a container; remove from fridge 20 minutes before assembly to soften.
5. Once the base is chilled, stir in the milk powder (added at this stage as a stabilizer for long-term freezer storage).
6. Layer the base into your tub, adding frozen peanut butter chunks and swirling in the peanut butter syrup as you go.
7. Freeze for at least 6–7 hours before serving.

**Notes:**

- The xylitol simple syrup is key — plain peanut butter goes rock hard in the freezer, but the xylitol syrup keeps the swirl pliable and scoopable.
- Frozen peanut butter chunks (Skippy) freeze hard but melt very quickly in the mouth — don't worry about the texture.
- Milk powder acts as a stabilizer and improves texture if storing long-term in the freezer.
- The chocolate base is essentially the same as Polar's Häagen-Dazs Belgian Chocolate recipe — use that as a reference.
- The recipe is a traditional full-fat base (double cream + sugar) — not a low-cal version.

### [Jaffa Cake Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=j68G6ZitR2g)

Chocolate ice cream base with a chocolate orange fudge swirl, chopped Jaffa Cakes, and a milk chocolate ganache top.

**Ingredients:**

Ice Cream:
- **450g** milk
- **300g** cream (49% fat)
- **70g** sugar
- **35g** dextrose
- **40g** SMP (skim milk powder)
- **20g** cocoa powder 21% fat (Callebaut)
- **30g** milk chocolate
- **2g** stabilizer

Chocolate Orange Fudge Swirl:
- **50g** sugar
- **50g** milk chocolate
- **15g** cocoa powder
- **50g** milk
- **15g** corn syrup
- **7g** coconut oil
- **5g** orange extract (Nielsen-Massey recommended)

Chocolate Ganache Top:
- **50g** milk chocolate
- **5g** coconut oil

Mix-In:
- Jaffa Cakes (several, chopped — quantity to taste)

**Steps:**

1. Combine milk, sugar, dextrose, and SMP in a pan. If using a stabilizer, mix it into the sugar first before adding to the pan.
2. Add cocoa powder (Callebaut 22–24% fat used here) and whisk well. The smaller-than-usual amount gives a milk chocolate profile rather than dark.
3. Heat until up to temperature, then turn off the heat and stir in the milk chocolate until fully melted and emulsified.
4. Transfer to a container, add the cream, stir to combine, and chill in the fridge.
5. Once chilled, churn in an ice cream machine.
6. While the base chills, make the fudge swirl: combine corn syrup, milk, and coconut oil in a pan. Add sugar. Warm until fully melted.
7. Add cocoa powder, mix well, and bring to a rolling boil for about 30 seconds.
8. Turn off the heat, add the milk chocolate, and stir until glossy and smooth.
9. Once the fudge has cooled slightly, stir in the orange extract. Cool to room temperature before refrigerating (it will stiffen slightly in the fridge but won't go hard).
10. Optional: chop the orange jelly pieces from actual Jaffa Cakes into small cubes and stir into the fudge just before assembly for extra texture.
11. Chop whole Jaffa Cakes into roughly 1cm pieces.
12. Layer churned ice cream and fudge swirl alternately into the tub, then top with chopped Jaffa Cake pieces. Put the lid on and freeze for 20–30 minutes.
13. While the tub sets, melt ganache: combine milk chocolate and coconut oil and melt in the microwave. Let cool slightly.
14. Remove tub from freezer, pour the milk chocolate ganache over the top (over the Jaffa Cake pieces), replace the lid, and freeze for several hours until firm.

**Notes:**

- Nielsen-Massey orange extract is specifically recommended — the fudge tastes identical to actual Jaffa Cake flavor when using it.
- The ganache top sets hard over the Jaffa Cake layer, creating a theatrical chocolate shell that cracks when scooped — inspired by Ben & Jerry's style chocolate tops.
- The small amount of cocoa powder is intentional: it produces a milk chocolate flavor in the base, not dark chocolate.
- If adding orange jelly pieces from real Jaffa Cakes into the fudge, do it just before loading the tub for added bite and texture in the swirl.
- This is a churned recipe (conventional ice cream machine), not a Ninja Creami recipe.

### [Nick's Darkest Chocolate Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=99Oh_jGLVp4)

A triple-source chocolate base (physical chocolate + high-quality cocoa + dark chocolate sauce) inspired by the original Jeni's Darkest Chocolate recipe before it was simplified for retail.

**Ingredients:**

- **250ml** whole milk
- **300ml** double cream
- **120g** sugar
- **15g** dextrose
- **50g** 70% dark chocolate (chips or chopped)
- **3 tsp** high-quality cocoa powder
- **1 tsp** 100% Dutch-process cocoa powder (optional — color only)
- **10g** skimmed milk powder
- **0.5 tsp** instant coffee / espresso powder
- **0.25 tsp** kosher salt
- Monin Dark Chocolate Sauce (or homemade — see Notes), to layer

**Steps:**

1. Add milk, cream, and sugar to a saucepan and gently heat.
2. Once warmed, add the chopped/chip chocolate and stir until fully melted and combined.
3. Add cocoa powder (both regular and 100% Dutch if using), instant coffee, and salt. Stir well.
4. Add skimmed milk powder and combine thoroughly. Turn off the heat.
5. Chill the mix in the fridge for a few hours.
6. Churn in your ice cream machine until done.
7. If your machine allows, add additional chopped 70% chocolate before the end of churning; otherwise fold it in after batching.
8. Layer in dark chocolate sauce (Monin or homemade) as you pack the container.
9. Freeze for at least 4–6 hours before scooping.

**Notes:**

- Nick renamed this from "Jeni's Darkest Chocolate" to his own version — the original Jeni's Splendid Ice Creams book recipe used cream cheese and physical chocolate; the current retail product uses only cocoa powder.
- The 100% Dutch-process cocoa powder is purely for deeper color, not flavor — skip if not available.
- The dark chocolate sauce is a key layered element; Nick uses Monin brand. A homemade version (from description): melt 200g 70% chocolate, heat 175ml milk + 2 tbsp double cream + 30g caster sugar, whisk together onto chocolate, bubble briefly, whisk in 30g diced butter, pass through sieve.
- Extra chopped 70% chocolate can be added as a mix-in at end of churn or folded in post-batch.

### [KETO Orange Chocolate Chip Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fg5Zav7Xjww)
Keto orange ice cream (high fat, low carb) with milk chocolate chips — cold process, no heating required.

**Ingredients:**
- **264g** heavy cream
- **119g** water
- **55g** allulose
- **54g** inulin
- **5g** orange extract (Nielsen Massey brand used; or zest of half an orange)
- **5g** guar gum
- **25–50g** milk chocolate (Valrhona used; kept frozen then chopped into chips)

**Steps:**
1. Combine dry ingredients (allulose, inulin, guar gum) and mix together to break up the gum.
2. Add dry mix into wet ingredients (cream + water) in the Ninja Creami pint.
3. Add orange extract (if using zest instead, refrigerate the base for a few hours first to let orange oils infuse before freezing).
4. Stir to combine — guar gum will begin thickening the base slightly. Total base is ~500g, filling the pint to the line.
5. Seal and freeze minimum 24 hours.
6. Before spinning, chop the frozen milk chocolate into small pieces.
7. Spin on Ice Cream. While machine runs, prepare chocolate chips.
8. After spin, add chocolate chips and use Mix-In to incorporate.

**Notes:**
- Cold process recipe — guar gum works without heating. Alternatively, heat all ingredients to 80–85°C (180–185°F) with a hot-process stabilizer.
- If using orange zest instead of extract, rest base in the fridge several hours before freezing to allow orange oils to infuse; texture will be slightly firmer.
- For stronger orange flavor, increase extract slightly but reduce allulose to compensate.
- Keep chocolate in the freezer before chopping — makes it easier to cut cleanly.
- Macros (per 100g, excluding chocolate chips): Fat 17g, Carbs 4.18g, Sugar 1.8g, Fiber 0.7g, Protein 1.4g.
- Residual sugars come from lactose in the dairy (not added sugar).

### [Kinder Bueno Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MQgLlh7SQu4)
Hazelnut ice cream base layered with homemade hazelnut butter, a white chocolate hazelnut cream topping, and a milk chocolate stracciatella swirl — a full from-scratch Kinder Bueno recreation. Batch recipe (makes ~2 litres).

**Ingredients:**

Ice Cream Base:
- **830g** milk
- **740g** whipping cream (33% fat)
- **220g** sugar
- **60g** corn syrup (or glucose syrup — not high-fructose corn syrup)
- **120g** skim milk powder (SMP)
- **3.5g** stabilizer (optional)
- **5–10g** hazelnut extract (5g if strong, 10g if weak — add after heating)

Hazelnut Butter:
- **100g** hazelnuts (roasted, skins removed)
- **175g** white chocolate (e.g. Callebaut), melted

Hazelnut Cream:
- **130g** white chocolate (melted)
- **5g** hazelnut extract
- **5g** corn syrup (or dextrose/corn sugar)
- **~10g** refined (deodorized) coconut oil

Milk Chocolate Stracciatella:
- **100g** milk chocolate
- **10g** refined coconut oil (or any unflavored oil)

**Steps:**

1. Preheat oven to 325°F (163°C). Spread hazelnuts on a small pan and roast 10–15 minutes until skins crack and crack. Transfer hot nuts to a lidded container, shake vigorously to remove skins. Allow to cool slightly; don't stress over a few stubborn skins.
2. Combine milk, cream, sugar, corn syrup, and skim milk powder in a saucepan. Mix stabilizer with a little sugar first (prevents clumping), then add to the pan. Heat over medium, stirring, until it reaches 75–80°C (stabilizer activation temperature).
3. Remove from heat. Transfer base to a container and refrigerate 3–4 hours. Add hazelnut extract only after the base has chilled (not during heating).
4. While the base chills, make the hazelnut butter: blend the warm skinned hazelnuts into a paste. Add the melted white chocolate (175g) and blend until fully smooth. Refrigerate.
5. Make the hazelnut cream: melt 130g white chocolate with ~10g refined coconut oil in the microwave in 10-second bursts, stirring between each, until smooth. Add 5g hazelnut extract and 5g corn syrup. Stir to combine. Refrigerate. (Coconut oil prevents the chocolate from seizing and helps it stay pourable.)
6. Make the milk chocolate stracciatella: melt 100g milk chocolate with 10g refined coconut oil in the microwave until smooth. Keep warm/fluid for churning.
7. Churn the chilled base in an ice cream machine. In the final 1–2 minutes of churning, pour in half (~50g) of the milk chocolate mixture — it will shatter into flakes throughout the ice cream.
8. Layer the churned ice cream into containers: alternate scoops of ice cream with spoonfuls of hazelnut butter throughout the tub until full.
9. Pour the hazelnut cream gently over the top in a thin layer. Drizzle the remaining milk chocolate stracciatella over that. Freeze 4–5 hours to harden.

**Notes:**
- This is a large batch recipe — divide everything by two for a smaller yield.
- Stabilizer is optional but recommended for storage longevity; it keeps texture quality for longer.
- Hazelnut extracts vary greatly in strength — start at 5g, go up to 10g if needed.
- Use refined (deodorized) coconut oil — not virgin — to avoid coconut flavor in the chocolate elements.
- The hazelnut cream and hazelnut butter need to come up to room temperature before layering so they're pourable.
- Nick notes the white chocolate + hazelnut extract combination closely replicates the Kinder Bueno core filling.
- If you only want to make part of this, even just the ice cream base with the hazelnut cream swirled on top is described as "ice cream heaven."
---

### [Kraft Cheese Gelato](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Rg-dhzZvggA)

Savory-sweet gelato made with Kraft cheese powder — an unusual flavor fusion popular in parts of Asia.

**Ingredients:**

- **436g** milk (2% fat)
- **110g** cream (33% fat / whipping cream)
- **75g** Kraft cheese powder
- **100g** sugar
- **30g** dextrose
- **1g** guar gum

**Steps:**

1. Combine milk and cream in a saucepan and gently warm on the hob.
2. Pre-mix 10g of the recipe sugar with the guar gum and set aside.
3. Add the remaining sugar, Kraft cheese powder, and dextrose to the warm milk/cream mixture. Whisk until fully dissolved.
4. Once everything is dissolved (around 52°C), turn off the heat. Add the guar gum + sugar blend, stir well for about 30 seconds.
5. Transfer to a container and refrigerate for 3–4 hours until thoroughly chilled.
6. Churn in an ice cream machine. Mix any foam back in before churning.
7. Transfer to a container and freeze.

**Notes:**

- Guar gum is a cold-mix stabilizer — no need to heat the base to a high temperature; ~52°C is sufficient to dissolve everything.
- Pre-mixing guar gum with a small amount of sugar before adding to the base helps prevent clumping.
- After chilling, the base may develop a thick foamy layer on top — stir back in before churning.
- The finished gelato is a light yellow color with a surprisingly creamy texture.
- Nick notes he personally dislikes Kraft cheese flavor, but the gelato genuinely tastes of Kraft cheese — suited for fans of the ingredient.
- Kraft cheese powder ingredients: dried whey, cheddar cheese (milk, salt, bacterial culture, modified milk ingredients, microbial enzymes, cream, calcium chloride, lipase, annatto), salt, butter, natural flavoring, paprika, turmeric, annatto extracts, citric acid, sodium phosphate. High in lactose-derived sugars (no added sugar).

### [Luxury Keto Protein Chocolate Peanut Butter Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=DKdKYNas7zE)

Keto, high-protein, zero-added-sugar chocolate ice cream churned in a traditional machine with layered peanut butter swirl.

**Ingredients:**

- **300g** whole milk
- **317g** whipping cream (33% fat; 35–36% is fine)
- **20g** cocoa powder (strong/dark recommended)
- **43g** chocolate protein powder (use whatever you have; chocolate-flavored preferred)
- **1g** guar gum
- **50–100g** peanut butter (for swirl mix-in)
- **1 tsp** refined coconut oil (for peanut butter swirl — refined so no coconut taste)

**Steps:**

1. Combine milk, cream, and cocoa powder in a saucepan. Heat gently to 55–60°C, stirring, until cocoa is fully homogenized into the liquid. Do not boil.
2. Remove from heat. Allow to cool slightly to around 40°C.
3. Mix protein powder and guar gum together in a separate bowl, then whisk into the warm base. Adding protein at this lower temperature prevents overcooking (overcooked protein becomes thick and gummy).
4. Transfer base to a container and refrigerate until fully chilled.
5. Pre-chill ice cream machine bowl for 5–10 minutes. Pour in the cold base and churn until soft-serve consistency.
6. While churning, gently melt peanut butter with coconut oil in a microwave until fully liquid and homogenized.
7. Layer soft-serve into your storage tub in stages, drizzling the liquid peanut butter swirl between layers.
8. Freeze for 3–4 hours (or overnight) to harden off.
9. Scoop and serve; drizzle any remaining warm peanut butter over the top.

**Notes:**

- This recipe uses a traditional churning ice cream machine, not the Ninja Creami. If using a Ninja Creami, pour the cooled base directly into the pint and freeze 24h before processing — but expect higher calorie density per serving due to zero overrun vs. 10–30% overrun from a churning machine.
- Protein powder brand is not specified — Nick uses his own daily protein shake powder. For a chocolate recipe, chocolate-flavored protein is preferred but plain works since cocoa is added separately.
- Many flavored/pre-mixed protein powders contain their own gums and stabilizers — be careful stacking additional stabilizers on top, as the base can become too thick and freeze rock hard.
- Macros (as churned, no overrun): 15.8% fat, 0.3% sugar, 6% MSNF (milk solids non-fat), 38.6% total solids, 61.5% water, ~3g carbs/100g, ~6g protein/100g.
- Peanut butter quantity is flexible (50–100g) depending on how much swirl you want.
- Use refined coconut oil for the swirl so it stays neutral-flavored.

---

### [Lemon Meringue Pie Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9jLluMgclC4)
Updated remake of Nick's first-ever YouTube recipe: lemon ice cream base with buttery baked graham crumb cubes and a vanilla Swiss meringue swirl.

**Ingredients:**

Base:
- **592g** whole milk (3.25%)
- **582g** heavy cream (33–36% fat; if using UK double cream, reduce to 500g)
- **230g** sugar
- **24g** invert sugar / corn syrup / glucose syrup
- **65g** skim milk powder (SMP)
- **6–10g** lemon extract (adjust to taste; Nick used ~10g)
- Zest of ½ lemon (steeped in base during cooking; Nick recommends leaving it in)
- Locust bean gum (amount not specified — mixed with the last 10g of sugar before adding)

Graham pie crust add-in:
- **200g** honey graham crumbs
- **100g** butter, melted

Swiss meringue swirl:
- **100g** egg whites (carton egg whites work fine)
- **100g** sugar (from the 230g total — 10g held back for this; Nick used 100g separately)
- **30g** corn syrup / glucose syrup
- **1.5g** cream of tartar (~¼ tsp; citric acid works as a substitute)
- 1–2 tsp vanilla extract (stirred in after whisking)

**Steps:**

1. Combine milk, cream, 220g sugar, corn syrup, and SMP in a saucepan. Mix the locust bean gum with the reserved 10g sugar, then whisk into the base. Add lemon zest.
2. Heat base to 85°C (185°F), hold for ~5 minutes, then remove from heat.
3. Cool base to ~60°C, then stir in lemon extract (add off-heat to preserve volatile aromatics).
4. Transfer base to fridge overnight to mature and allow lemon oils from the zest to develop.
5. Make the graham crust: combine graham crumbs with melted butter (microwave to melt). Press into a foil-lined pan, flatten down. Bake at 300°F (150°C) for 5–8 minutes. Cool completely to room temperature, then cube and refrigerate.
6. Make the Swiss meringue: combine egg whites, sugar, corn syrup, and cream of tartar in a heatproof bowl. Whisk together off heat, then place over a bain-marie (simmering water). Heat to 70–75°C, whisking continuously. Remove from heat and whisk with a hand mixer for 15–20 minutes until cool, pillowy, and marshmallow-like (~21°C). Stir in vanilla extract.
7. Churn the ice cream base in your machine.
8. Towards the end of the churn, add graham cracker cubes directly if your machine can handle it; otherwise fold in by hand after loading.
9. Layer into containers: ice cream, then vanilla Swiss meringue swirl, then more ice cream. Repeat until full. Top with remaining graham cracker cubes.
10. Freeze.

**Notes:**
- This is a batch recipe — yield is multiple pints (base alone ~1.5 kg before churn).
- Nick notes the base is ~14% fat — a rich, luxurious result.
- Two lemon sources (zest + extract) give a more natural, balanced lemon flavour than either alone. If using only zest, use a full lemon's worth and steep during cooking; if cold-process, infuse zest in the milk for at least 1 hour before mixing.
- Check lemons for wax coating (run fingernail over skin — wax comes off white); wash under warm water if present.
- Store-bought meringues turn to mush in ice cream — do not substitute. The Swiss meringue here stays stable due to the cooked egg whites and glucose syrup; the glucose gives it a marshmallow-like texture that holds in the frozen base.
- Lemon extract brand mentioned: Nielsen-Massey (available at Sainsbury's and Tesco in the UK).
- Full nutritional stats (fat%, sugar%, MSNF%, PAC, POD) available on Patreon / YouTube membership.
