### [Let's Make Ben & Jerry's Fudgy Flan](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=v5Sj3ZBNqsk)

Burnt caramel ice cream base with fudge brownie chunks and a vanilla pastry cream (Crème Pâtissière) swirl, replicating Ben & Jerry's Fudgy Flan.

**Ingredients — Ice Cream Base:**
- **330g** whipping cream (35%+)
- **454g** whole milk (3.25%)
- **30g** burnt caramel (made from ~50g sugar + a touch of water, cooked dark)
- **112g** sugar
- **1.5g** guar gum
- **4g** soy lecithin (or sunflower/liquid lecithin)
- **35g** skim milk powder (SMP)

**Ingredients — Pastry Cream (Crème Pâtissière) — large batch, halve if needed:**
- **500g** whole milk (heated to ~85°C)
- **100g** cream
- **175g** sugar
- **45g** all-purpose (plain) flour
- **2** whole eggs (~60g)
- **10g** honey (or glucose / invert sugar)
- **5g** butter
- **7.5g** vanilla extract
- **2.5g** vanilla bean powder (optional)
- Pinch of salt

**Ingredients — Mix-ins:**
- Fudge brownies, chopped (store-bought or homemade — Costco two-bite brownies recommended; sugar content keeps them from freezing rock-hard)

**Steps:**

1. **Make the burnt caramel.** Place ~50g sugar in a pan with a small splash of water (just enough to wet the sugar). Heat on medium-high without stirring, swirling, or touching it. Watch closely — once the syrup thickens and starts to brown it will darken fast. Cook to a deep, dark colour (go further than you think — the dairy will mute the flavour). You need 30g of finished caramel.
2. **Build the ice cream base.** Combine the milk, cream, SMP, sugar, guar gum, and lecithin in a bowl and stir to dissolve the dry ingredients. This is a semi-cold process — no need to heat the whole base since no egg yolks are used.
3. **Incorporate the caramel.** Do not pour hot caramel directly into cold dairy — it will seize solid. Instead, take a small amount of the base, microwave it until warm, then whisk the 30g hot caramel into that warm portion until liquid. Stir the tempered caramel-dairy back into the main base. If it seizes anyway, microwave the whole mix in 5-second bursts while stirring until smooth. Aim for a homogeneous caramel dairy mix; cool it back down to fridge temperature (2–3°C).
4. **Freeze the base.** Pour into the Creami pint, freeze for at least 24 hours.
5. **Make the pastry cream.** Heat the milk and cream in a microwave or saucepan to ~85°C. In a separate bowl, whisk together sugar, flour, and eggs until a smooth pale paste. Slowly stream the hot milk-cream into the egg mixture a little at a time, whisking constantly (don't dump it all at once or it won't combine easily). Transfer back to a pan, heat on medium-high, stirring constantly until it thickens at the simmer — the flour will cause it to thicken noticeably once it approaches boiling point. Remove from heat; stir in salt, honey, vanilla extract, butter, and vanilla bean powder if using. Press cling film directly onto the surface to prevent a skin, cool to room temperature, then refrigerate.
6. **Spin the ice cream.** Process the frozen pint on Ice Cream mode.
7. **Load the pint.** Scoop out a divot, layer in chopped brownie chunks and spoonfuls of cold pastry cream. Use the Mix-In mode or fold gently to distribute.

**Notes:**
- Nick explicitly recommends making this hot process instead — heating the full base, adding the caramel while both are warm, then cooling. The cold-process approach shown is difficult and demonstrated as a proof-of-concept.
- Burnt caramel loses a lot of flavour when diluted into dairy — cook it darker than feels comfortable to compensate.
- Brownies with high sugar content stay soft in the freezer; sugar-loaded store-bought brownies (e.g. Costco two-bite) work particularly well as mix-ins.
- The pastry cream batch yields ~800g — more than enough for one pint; freeze or refrigerate the remainder for other uses.
- Pastry cream will split if frozen in the base — only ever add it as a swirl/mix-in after spinning.
- Lecithin type is flexible — soy, sunflower, or liquid lecithin all work equally here.

---

### [Low-Sugar Homemade Ice Pops for Kids (Peppa Pig Strawberry Popsicles)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=11fvbXJKAUA)

Ultra low-sugar strawberry ice pops made with real fruit, a small amount of corn syrup for texture, and a milk/cream base — shaped in Peppa Pig popsicle moulds for kids.

**Ingredients:**
- **65g** fresh strawberries, quartered
- **60ml** water (1/4 cup — to prevent burning during cooking)
- **6g** corn syrup
- Milk and cream mixture (exact amounts visual-only in video; described as "not a lot of cream")
- Milk powder (amount visual-only in video)
- Allulose (amount visual-only in video)

**Steps:**
1. Add strawberries and water to a pan over medium-high heat.
2. Cook until strawberries soften and break down, stirring with a silicone utensil to help break them up.
3. Turn off the heat and add the corn syrup, then a portion of the milk/cream mixture; stir to homogenize the strawberry flavor into the dairy.
4. Add the remaining milk/cream mixture and stir well.
5. Add the milk powder and allulose; stir for 1–2 minutes until fully dissolved (residual heat is enough).
6. If large strawberry chunks remain, break them up with a blender or potato masher.
7. Pour mixture into popsicle moulds, filling to the top. Put lids on (or cover with cling wrap).
8. Freeze until fully set.

**Notes:**
- Makes 7 popsicles: some moulds are 30g, others 50g (Peppa Pig moulds from Amazon/TEMU).
- Nutrition per 100g: 126 kcal, 7g fat, 5g sat fat, 9g carbs, 8.75g sugar, 4.3g protein. Per 30g pop: ~38 kcal, under 3g sugar, under 3g fat.
- Corn syrup is used specifically for texture and chew in the popsicle; can be omitted for zero sugar, but texture will suffer.
- Exact amounts for milk, cream, milk powder, and allulose are shown visually in the video and not narrated — recipe reconstruction requires watching the video directly.
- Nick opens this as the first entry in a popsicle/ice pop series.

---

### [MUST TRY Ninja Creami Gingerbread Cookie Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2IsiNj2cpoA)

Chocolate-ginger ice cream base (1 kilo / 2 pints) with homemade chewy gingerbread cookies mixed in.

> **BATCH RECIPE** — makes 2 standard Ninja Creami tubs (1 kg total base).

---

#### Gingerbread Cookies

**Ingredients:**
- **375g** all-purpose flour
- **165g** dark brown sugar, packed
- **4.5g** baking soda
- **8g** ground cinnamon
- **5g** ground ginger
- **1g** ground cloves
- **3g** pink Himalayan salt
- **170g** unsalted butter, room temperature, cut into pieces
- **252g** dark molasses (fancy molasses / dark molasses)
- **30g** milk

**Steps:**
1. In a stand mixer bowl add flour, spices (cinnamon, ginger, cloves), baking soda, salt, and brown sugar. Fit the balloon whisk attachment and mix on low to combine dry ingredients and break up any lumps.
2. Add butter pieces and mix on low until mixture resembles fine sand or a mealy crumb texture.
3. With the mixer running, slowly pour in molasses and milk until a dough forms. Dough will be very sticky.
4. Divide dough into two portions. Press each flat (do not roll into a ball — that generates unnecessary heat), wrap in plastic wrap/cling film, and refrigerate for at least 24 hours (or freeze for 30–45 minutes if short on time).
5. Preheat oven to 180°C / 350°F. Prepare baking sheets lined with parchment paper.
6. Flour the work surface and rolling pin generously (dough is very sticky). Roll out one portion of chilled dough and cut into shapes. Use an offset spatula to gently slide shapes onto the parchment-lined tray rather than picking them up by hand.
7. Bake for exactly 10 minutes. Underbaking = too soft; overbaking = too hard and burnt. Target: chewy with enough structure to hold shape in ice cream.
8. Cool on the tray. Reserve the second half of dough for another use (freezes well).

---

#### Chocolate Ginger Ice Cream Base

**Ingredients (makes 2 pints / ~1 kg):**
- **370g** whole milk
- **40g** cocoa powder (Ghirardelli or Valrhona recommended)
- **165g** dark brown sugar
- **390g** 33% whipping cream
- **35g** skim milk powder
- **~2g** ground ginger (approx. 3/4 tsp)
- **1g** guar gum

**Steps:**
1. Combine milk, cocoa powder, and brown sugar in a bowl or jug. Stir to begin incorporating cocoa (it resists absorbing liquid cold — submerge it under the surface and stir continuously). Microwave for approximately 1 minute until warmed to ~50–55°C, then stir until fully dissolved and smooth.
2. In a separate container, combine skim milk powder, ground ginger, and guar gum. Stir them together (guar gum disperses easily in liquid but pre-mixing with the powder helps even distribution).
3. Add the dry powder mix to the warm chocolate base and stir to combine.
4. Pour in the cold whipping cream and stir well. The cold cream immediately cools the base.
5. Divide between two Ninja Creami tubs and freeze for at least 6–7 hours (or longer — Nick filmed 3 days later).

---

#### Spinning & Mix-In

**Steps:**
1. Spin on Ice Cream setting (or Gelato — Nick notes these settings perform identically and the distinction is marketing).
2. Break one gingerbread cookie into pieces and press into the centre of the spun pint.
3. Run Mix-In stage.
4. Optionally fold in additional broken cookie pieces after the Mix-In cycle.

---

**Notes:**
- The cookie recipe makes more than needed for 2 pints — freeze the remaining dough half for later use.
- More brown sugar in the cookie dough = softer cookies; less = crispier. Aim for chewy so pieces stay pleasant in the frozen base rather than turning rock-hard.
- Do not roll dough into a ball before chilling — pressing flat avoids generating extra warmth from your hands/rolling.
- Cocoa powder fully absorbs liquid at ~50–55°C; microwaving the milk-cocoa-sugar mix briefly shortcuts this process versus a long cold stir.
- Ice Cream and Gelato settings on the Ninja Creami are functionally the same — use either.
- Gingerbread premix packets from the supermarket can substitute for the homemade cookie dough if desired.

---

### [Ultra-Smooth Chocolate Soft Serve Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=zRo8UG3RnQ0)

Chocolate soft serve formulated for draw at -6°C: 10-12% fat, lower sugar than hard-pack, minimal guar for elasticity without gumminess.

**Ingredients:** (915 g total)
- **480 g** whole milk (3.25% fat)
- **200 g** heavy cream (33-36% fat)
- **40 g** skim milk powder
- **140 g** sugar (sucrose)
- **50 g** unsweetened cocoa powder (10% fat — use a strong-profile cocoa if possible)
- **~1 g** salt
- **~4 g** vanilla extract (1 tsp)
- **0.25 g** guar gum (0.2-0.3 g range)

**Steps:**
1. Weigh milk and cream together into a saucepan.
2. In a separate small container, combine all dry ingredients (sugar, skim milk powder, salt, cocoa powder, guar gum) and whisk together to break up clumps and distribute the guar evenly.
3. Add dry ingredients to the liquid and stir to combine, then heat on the stove to ~50-55°C — just enough to dissolve the cocoa powder. No need to go higher; guar is a cold-process stabilizer.
4. Remove from heat and let cool for a few minutes. Once below ~60°C, add vanilla extract (adding it too hot burns off the volatile aromatics).
5. Chill in the refrigerator overnight to fully hydrate the stabilizer and rest the serum phase.
6. Churn in a soft serve machine (Goumia/Gormia horizontal batch freezer used here) until the soft serve draws at the correct texture.

**Notes:**
- Soft serve recipes differ from hard-pack: target 10-12% fat and lower sugar to match soft serve draw temperature (~-6°C); too much sugar and the mix won't freeze properly in the machine.
- Horizontal batch freezer machines (like the Gormia) incorporate air significantly better than vertical-bowl home churners — a genuine advantage for soft serve.
- Guar at 0.25 g provides elasticity and shape-holding; do not exceed — more guar = gummy/stringy texture.
- Use a strong-profile (darker, more intense) cocoa powder for best flavour at this 50 g load.
- Vanilla extract goes in after the base cools below ~60°C to preserve volatile aromatics.
- Rest overnight in the fridge; the stabilizer needs time to fully hydrate before churning.

---

### [Making Amazing Alcohol Free Blueberry Extract](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bk_AFHlxlxI)

Technique video: how to make a glycerin-based (alcohol-free) blueberry extract using freeze-dried blueberries and vegetable glycerin thinned with water.

**Ingredients:**

- **250ml** vegetable glycerin (food grade; brands shown: Lauran from Michaels craft store, NOW Solutions from Amazon)
- **60ml** water
- **120g** freeze-dried blueberries (from Amazon; can substitute fresh with a dehydrator — reduce water if using fresh)

**Steps:**

1. Combine vegetable glycerin and water in a small saucepan. Stir well — the mixture will go slightly slimy at first; this is normal. Water is needed to thin the glycerin so it can permeate into the fruit flesh.
2. Add the freeze-dried blueberries to the pan (whole pack for maximum strength).
3. Heat on the stovetop until nearly boiling (80–90°C / 180–190°F), then reduce to the lowest heat setting.
4. Simmer on lowest heat for at least 1 hour. The liquid will turn very dark, almost black.
5. Transfer to an airtight container (Tupperware or bottle).
6. Store at room temperature for approximately 1 month to continue extracting. Alternatively, refrigerate — Nick refrigerated his batch for just over a month.
7. Strain through cheesecloth before use. Yields approximately 250ml of extract.

**Notes:**

- This technique works for any freeze-dried fruit — same ratios, same process, just strain at the end.
- Vegetable glycerin is too thick on its own to extract flavor; the water addition reduces viscosity and enables permeation. Use 100% food-grade VG; organic is preferable.
- Glycerin provides the same freezing point suppression benefit as alcohol, making this extract directly suitable for ice cream and gelato bases.
- If using fresh fruit instead of freeze-dried, reduce the added water since fresh fruit already contains water.
- Use glycerin extract in place of alcohol-based extracts 1:1 in ice cream recipes, coffees, and baking.

---

### [Making Amazing Chocolate Waffle Cones at Home](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xOh--hwq9DY)

Vegan chocolate waffle cone batter mixed with olive oil and refined coconut oil, pressed at 180°C for ~1 min 25 sec.

**Ingredients** (makes ~4–5 cones, batch size depends on 60–65g batter per cone):
- **80g** all-purpose flour
- **20g** cocoa powder (Ghirardelli recommended — use a good quality one)
- **50g** sugar
- **140g** water
- **20g** olive oil
- **20g** refined coconut oil (refined, not virgin — avoids coconut flavour)

**Steps:**
1. Whisk together flour, cocoa powder, and sugar to break up any lumps.
2. Combine water and olive oil. Melt coconut oil in the microwave for a few seconds, then stir into the water mixture.
3. Add wet ingredients to dry and whisk until smooth — consistency should resemble a thick ice cream base.
4. Refrigerate batter for at least 1 hour (up to 2 days) to fully hydrate the flour and thicken to a semi-viscous, scoopable consistency. Batter must not be runny.
5. Heat waffle iron to 180°C (350°F). Lightly oil plates with olive oil or spray oil.
6. Scoop 60–65g (approx. 1/4 cup) of batter onto an 8-inch waffle iron plate.
7. Cook for 1 minute 25 seconds — can extend to 1 minute 50 seconds, but do not exceed or it burns.
8. Remove immediately with a spatula and quickly roll into a cone shape using a roller/former. Hold until cool enough to retain shape.
9. Stand upright in a glass or cone stand; optionally refrigerate briefly (no more than 30 seconds) to set shape.
10. Optional: seal the tip with a mini marshmallow or a drop of melted chocolate once the cone is fully cooled.

**Notes:**
- Equipment: stovetop waffle cone iron (~$22 CAD on Amazon) works just as well as a dedicated waffle press (Vevor press linked in description). Both use the same batter and temperature.
- Cone former: the plastic former included with most irons works fine. Stainless steel versions exist but are expensive (~$150). A rolled paper cone or 3D-printed form also works.
- Batter thickness is critical — too thin and the cone is fragile and crumbles; thick batter (soft-serve consistency) produces a sturdy, crispy cone.
- To close the tip without a filler: fold the edge just short of the disc's perimeter so the last bit of the disc rolls in on itself when shaping.
- Refrigerating the batter 2 days in advance is fine and may improve texture.
- Refined coconut oil is specified to avoid coconut flavour; olive oil adds neutral richness.

---

### [Making Amazing VEGAN Häagen Dazs Strawberries & Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=yZYA9L8bTFg)

Vegan/plant-based dupe of Häagen Dazs Strawberries & Cream using Carnation vegan condensed milk and Elmlea plant-based cream.

**Ingredients:**
- **500g** ripe strawberries
- **100g** sugar
- Splash of lemon juice
- **370g** Carnation vegan condensed milk
- **500g** Elmlea Plant Based cream (2 × 250ml tubs)
- **50g** glucose
- **40g** vegan milk (low-flavour type — rice milk or oat milk)

**Steps:**
1. De-stalk and chop strawberries. Add to a pan with the sugar and a splash of lemon juice. The sugar draws out the moisture; lemon juice stabilises the mixture and helps the strawberries hold their structure.
2. Heat gently until it just starts to bubble and takes on a jam/conserve texture. Remove from heat and let cool.
3. Once cooled, blend a portion of the strawberry mixture until completely smooth and fine.
4. In the pan, combine the blended strawberry base with the Carnation vegan condensed milk (it is quite stiff — warm gently to combine).
5. Add in the Elmlea Plant Based cream (both 250ml tubs) and the remaining strawberry sauce and pieces. Warm gently, stirring until nicely combined. Do not need to reach any specific temperature — just warm enough to fully combine.
6. Transfer to a container. Stir in another ~70g of the remaining strawberry mix to boost colour. Stir well.
7. Chill in the fridge for 3–4 hours until completely cold.
8. Churn in your ice cream machine to desired consistency, then transfer to a tub and freeze.

**Notes:**
- The key ingredient is the Carnation vegan condensed milk — Nick describes it as "a revelation" for vegan ice cream.
- Elmlea Plant Based cream is praised as the best cream substitute available; it comes in 250ml tubs.
- Vegan recipes using off-the-shelf condensed milk and cream tend to freeze harder than dairy equivalents — if texture is too firm, increase sugar slightly or rest in the fridge for 10–15 minutes before serving.
- Glucose (50g) and vegan milk (40g) are listed in the description ingredients but not explicitly called out by name in the video steps — include as part of the base.
- The homemade version turns out pinker/deeper in colour than the real Häagen Dazs due to no artificial colouring — that is expected and desirable.
- Strawberries are ~95% water and will always have some iciness unless soaked in glycerin or alcohol before use.

### [Making Ample Hills - Hopelessly Devoted to Moo](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4dKqHEscREI)

Malted vanilla ice cream with chunks of Brooklyn Blackout chocolate cake and a chocolate pudding swirl — a recreation of the discontinued Ample Hills Creamery flavor.

**Ingredients — Brooklyn Blackout Cake:**
- **100g** unsalted butter, room temperature
- **270g** granulated sugar
- **2** large eggs, room temperature
- **1/4 tsp** vanilla extract
- **70g** unsweetened cocoa powder
- **3/4 tsp** baking powder
- **3/4 tsp** baking soda
- pinch of salt
- **215g** all-purpose flour
- **165ml** whole milk

**Ingredients — Chocolate Pudding Frosting:**
- **600ml** water
- **540g** granulated sugar
- **1 tbsp** corn syrup
- **110g** unsweetened cocoa powder
- **80g** cornstarch
- **78g** unsalted butter, cold, cut into 1-inch cubes
- **1/2 tsp** vanilla extract

**Ingredients — Malted Vanilla Ice Cream:**
- **300ml** milk
- **300ml** cream (49% fat)
- **90g** granulated sugar
- **50g** dextrose
- **40g** golden syrup
- **50g** Horlicks Malt Powder
- **1.1g** stabiliser
- **2 tsp** vanilla extract

**Steps:**
1. Preheat oven to 160°C (325°F). Butter and dust two 8-inch pans with cocoa powder.
2. Make the cake: cream butter and sugar, add eggs and vanilla, combine dry ingredients (cocoa, baking powder, baking soda, salt, flour), alternate adding dry mix and milk. Bake 25–30 minutes.
3. Make the pudding frosting: combine water, sugar, corn syrup, cocoa, and cornstarch in a saucepan; cook until thickened, then whisk in cold butter and vanilla. This doubles as the pudding swirl.
4. Make the ice cream base: combine milk, cream, sugar, dextrose, golden syrup, Horlicks, and stabiliser; churn in an ice cream machine.
5. Assemble: layer churned ice cream with crumbled blackout cake chunks and swirls of the chocolate pudding.

**Notes:**
- This is a multi-component batch recipe — significant prep time required. Nick notes you can skip baking and use a store-bought chocolate cake to simplify.
- The pudding frosting serves dual purpose: it frosts the cake AND is swirled into the ice cream.
- Horlicks Malt Powder is the key flavoring for the "malted vanilla" ice cream base.
- Cream is listed at 49% fat — a high-fat double/extra-thick cream.
- Stabiliser quantity is minimal (1.1g) — type not specified, likely a commercial blend.

---

### [Making Ben & Jerry's Cherry Garcia in a Ninja Creami](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=m_2Dj7o8rI8)

Three-part B&J Cherry Garcia clone: cherry ice cream base, chocolate fudge, and erythritol-soaked cherries as mix-ins — made in the Ninja Creami.

**Batch note:** Recipe as shown makes 1.5L (3 Ninja Creami pints). Ingredients below are for the full batch; divide by 3 for a single pint.

---

**Ingredients — Ice Cream Base (full batch, ~1.5L):**
- **375g** 2% milk
- **600g** cream, 33% fat (whipping cream)
- **110g** skim milk powder (SMP)
- **190g** sugar
- **30g** invert sugar (corn syrup)
- **200g** frozen cherries (blended into base)
- Ice cream stabilizer (off-the-shelf blend of stabilizers and emulsifiers, e.g. Special Ingredients brand) — mixed into sugar before adding liquids

**Ingredients — Erythritol-Soaked Cherries (mix-in):**
- **100g** frozen cherries (for mix-in, not blended into base)
- **150g** water
- **60g** erythritol/monk fruit blend (erythritol-only also works; xylitol works too)

**Ingredients — Chocolate Fudge:**
- **1 can** condensed milk (390g / 300ml)
- **350g** dark chocolate, 70% (e.g. Cacao Barry 70%) — do not use milk chocolate

---

**Steps:**

1. **Soak the cherries (mix-in):** Combine 100g frozen cherries, 150g water, and 60g erythritol/monk fruit blend in a small saucepan. Simmer on low for ~20 minutes. The heat allows the sugar syrup to infuse into the cherry flesh, preventing them from freezing rock-solid. Cool completely, then refrigerate (ideally overnight).

2. **Make the ice cream base:** Combine sugar and stabilizer in a large saucepan and mix together. Add skim milk powder and stir to distribute. Add corn syrup/invert sugar and the 200g frozen cherries. Pour in the milk and cream cold. Heat everything together from cold — do NOT add hot acidic cherry liquid to cold dairy, it will curdle. Bring up to 85°C (185°F) to activate stabilizer and fully break down the cherries. Do not exceed ~90°C. Once at temperature, turn off heat.

3. **Blend the base:** Transfer the hot mixture to a blender (carefully — it is super hot). Blend on low for ~20 seconds, then increase to medium/high in short bursts until smooth. This extracts all cherry flavor and produces a clean, smooth pink base. If no blender, mash cherries and pass through a fine-mesh sieve.

4. **Fill pints and chill:** Pour base into Ninja Creami pints (fills ~3). Refrigerate until fully chilled (several hours). Then freeze for at least 24 hours.

5. **Make the chocolate fudge:** Combine condensed milk and 350g dark chocolate in a saucepan. Warm gently over low heat, stirring, until chocolate is completely melted and mixture is smooth and silky. Do NOT overheat. Pour into a lined 12×8-inch baking tray. Leave at room temperature to set. Once set, cut into ~5mm pieces for mix-in. Stores in the freezer for months (over a year reported).

6. **Prepare cherries for mix-in:** Remove the soaked cherries from fridge. Drain and chop into quarters.

7. **Spin and add mix-ins:** Spin frozen pint on the Ice Cream setting. Use a spoon to make a channel down the center to the bottom. Add chopped fudge pieces and quartered soaked cherries into the channel. Run Mix-In program.

---

**Notes:**
- This is the North American version (fudge pieces), not the European version (chocolate pieces). Nick never tried the NA version before moving to Australia.
- Use dark chocolate (70%+) for the fudge — milk chocolate does not pair well with cherries.
- The re-spin decimates the mix-ins in the Ninja Creami; a traditional ice cream machine preserves them better. Cut fudge small (~5mm) as the machine cannot process large hard chunks.
- Soaking cherries in erythritol syrup infuses FPD (freezing point depression) into the fruit, preventing them from going icy/solid. This also avoids curdling the base from acidic cherry juice.
- Add cherry mixture cold to cold dairy, then heat together — prevents curdling from acid + heat differential.
- Erythritol/monk fruit combo recrystallizes within 24 hours in ice cream; use it only for the cherry soak, not the base. For the base, use regular sugar (or xylitol at reduced quantity; avoid allulose).
- Fudge keeps in the freezer for over a year; this batch makes a large amount — treat it as a long-term supply.
- The blended cherry base produces a naturally pink ice cream (the OG B&J color, not the modern bland brown).

---

### [Making Ben & Jerry's Dublin Mudslide at home](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=zyiWuqA3FDc)

Irish cream ice cream with coffee fudge swirls and double chocolate chip cookie pieces — a recreation of the retired (and revived) Ben & Jerry's flavour using Baileys Irish Cream Liqueur.

**Ingredients:**

Ice cream base:
- **340g** whole milk
- **455g** whipping cream (35% fat)
- **120g** sugar (sucrose)
- **45g** skimmed milk powder
- **50g** glucose syrup / golden syrup / corn syrup
- **100ml** Baileys Irish Cream Liqueur (note: 120ml used in video — described as too much)
- **1.3g** stabilizer (optional)

Coffee fudge swirl:
- **50g** sugar
- **50g** chocolate
- **15g** cocoa powder
- **50g** milk
- **45g** golden syrup (or corn syrup)
- **7g** coconut oil
- **10g** instant coffee
- pinch of salt

Mix-ins:
- store-bought double chocolate chip cookies (cut into ~1cm pieces), quantity to taste

**Steps:**

1. Add milk to a saucepan. Add sugar and glucose syrup, then warm to 50–55°C.
2. At that temperature, whisk in the skimmed milk powder until fully dissolved. Add stabilizer if using.
3. Turn off heat and slowly stir in the whipping cream, then add the Baileys Irish Cream Liqueur. Stir well.
4. Pour into a container and chill in the fridge for 3–4 hours.
5. For the coffee fudge: combine milk and golden syrup in a pan. Turn on heat, add sugar and let it dissolve before adding cocoa powder and instant coffee.
6. Bring to a high rolling boil (this starts converting sugars to invert sugar). Once boiling hard, turn off heat and stir in the chocolate until smooth.
7. Transfer fudge to a container and set aside to cool.
8. Churn the chilled ice cream base in an ice cream machine.
9. When churning is almost complete, add the cookie pieces and let them incorporate.
10. Transfer churned ice cream to a tub, layering in generous amounts of coffee fudge swirl as you fill.
11. Seal and freeze for a few hours before serving.

**Notes:**

- 100ml Baileys is the recommended amount; the 120ml used in the video was noted as too much.
- Golden syrup is preferred over corn syrup in the fudge — it adds extra depth of flavour.
- Use a good-quality instant coffee; quality varies significantly and affects the fudge flavour.
- Cookies are store-bought double chocolate chip; Nick notes he plans to make homemade versions in a future video.
- The high rolling boil of the fudge begins converting sucrose to invert sugar, improving texture and shelf life.
- This is a traditional churned recipe, not a Ninja Creami recipe.
---

### [Making Ben & Jerry's Marshmallow Sky ice cream at home](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PApVMCFSApc)

Homemade dupe of a B&J scoop-shop-only flavour: blue spirulina vanilla ice cream with chocolate chip cookie dough, double chocolate chip cookie dough, and marshmallow swirls.

**Ingredients:**

Ice Cream Base:
- **400ml** milk (2% fat)
- **300ml** cream (49% fat / double or heavy cream)
- **65g** sugar
- **65g** dextrose
- **25g** skimmed milk powder
- **75g** marshmallows (gelatin-based — important)
- **2-3 tsp** vanilla extract
- **1/2 tsp** blue spirulina mixed with **1 tsp** water (added cold)

Chocolate Chip Cookie Dough:
- **60g** unsalted butter, room temperature
- **45g** sugar
- **40g** brown sugar
- **1/2 tsp** salt
- **2 tbsp** heavy/double cream
- **2 tbsp** golden syrup (or corn syrup)
- **1 tsp** vanilla extract
- **70g** plain (all-purpose) flour, heat-treated
- **45g** semi-sweet chocolate, chopped

Double Chocolate Chip Cookie Dough:
- **60g** unsalted butter, room temperature
- **45g** sugar
- **40g** brown sugar
- **1/2 tsp** salt
- **2 tbsp** heavy/double cream
- **2 tbsp** golden syrup (or corn syrup)
- **1 tsp** vanilla extract
- **50g** plain (all-purpose) flour, heat-treated
- **20g** cocoa powder
- **45g** semi-sweet chocolate, chopped

Marshmallow Swirl:
- **2** egg whites
- **100g** sugar
- **1/4 tsp** cream of tartar

**Steps:**

1. Heat-treat the flour for both cookie doughs: spread on a tray and bake at 180°C for 10 minutes. Let cool 15 minutes, then sieve (use a metal sieve, not plastic).
2. **Chocolate Chip Cookie Dough:** Beat together butter, sugar, brown sugar, and salt. Add golden syrup, vanilla extract, and cream. Mix in the 70g heat-treated flour, then fold in chopped chocolate. Place in a container and freeze.
3. **Double Chocolate Chip Cookie Dough:** Make exactly as above, but use 50g flour + 20g cocoa powder instead of 70g flour. Fold in chopped chocolate. Freeze.
4. **Ice cream base:** Add milk, sugar, and dextrose to a pan over medium heat. Chop the marshmallows and melt them into the hot milk mixture. Remove from heat, pour into a container, and stir in the cream. Once cooled enough, stir in vanilla extract and skimmed milk powder. Mix spirulina powder with 1 tsp water, add to base and stir. Refrigerate until fully cold.
5. **Marshmallow swirl:** Combine egg whites, sugar, and cream of tartar in a heatproof bowl. Heat over a bain-marie, stirring constantly, until mixture reaches 70°C. Remove from heat and beat with a hand mixer on maximum speed for ~5 minutes until thick and glossy. Refrigerate until needed; bring out slightly before assembly to soften.
6. Chop frozen cookie doughs into small cubes; return to freezer.
7. Churn the cold ice cream base in an ice cream machine (Nick uses a Lello 5030). Once churned, layer ice cream, cookie dough cubes (both kinds), and marshmallow swirl into the tub. Freeze for several hours before serving.

**Notes:**

- This is a **traditional churned recipe** (not Ninja Creami). Uses an ice cream machine to churn before packing into a tub.
- Cookie doughs are adapted from Salt & Straw's recipe — noted to be "really good."
- B&J's original uses pectin instead of actual marshmallow to create the marshmallow texture. Nick uses real gelatin-based marshmallows melted into the base.
- The marshmallow swirl is technically a Swiss meringue — it behaves like a marshmallow swirl when frozen.
- Blue spirulina is critical to the visual identity of this flavour; must be added cold to preserve the blue colour.
- If dextrose is unavailable, substitute with an equal weight of sugar (use 130g total sugar instead of 65g sugar + 65g dextrose).
- Batch size is larger than a standard single pint — the churner used is a Lello 5030 (1.5L capacity).

---

### [Making Ben & Jerry's Thick Mint Ice Cream at Home](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6XgSZ0yY1SM)
Ben & Jerry's Topped "Thick Mint" dupe — mint ice cream with cookie swirl, mint chocolate cookie balls, mini cookies on top, and chocolate ganache lid.

**Ingredients:**

Ice Cream Base:
- **350ml** milk (1.8% fat)
- **300ml** cream (49% fat)
- **90g** sugar
- **40g** dextrose
- **30g** golden syrup / corn syrup
- **35g** skimmed milk powder
- **2 tsp** mint extract (good quality)

Cookies (adjusted recipe — BBC Good Food base):
- **150g** butter, softened
- **200g** sugar
- **1** egg
- **1 tsp** vanilla extract
- **200g** flour
- **50g** dutched cocoa powder
- **1 tsp** baking soda

Ganache Top:
- **50g** dark chocolate (50%)
- **1 tbsp** water

**Steps:**

1. **Cookies:** Cream butter and sugar, beat in egg and vanilla, mix in flour, cocoa powder, and baking soda. Bake at 175°C for 10–12 minutes (adjust to your oven). Cool on a wire rack.
2. **Mint cookie balls:** While a couple of cookies are still slightly warm, break them into a small bowl and mix with a little mint extract and a splash of heavy cream. Take approximately quarter-teaspoon portions, semi-shape on a plate, and freeze. Once frozen, roll smooth and return to freezer until needed.
3. **Mini cookies for top:** Use the smallest cookie cutter you have (~10–12mm diameter). Press down gently into cooled cookies to cut out mini rounds. Store in a small container in the freezer.
4. **Cookie swirl:** Dry 1–2 cookies in the fridge overnight. Break them into a fine cookie dust/powder to layer into the tub.
5. **Ice cream base:** Add all base ingredients except cream and mint extract to a pan. Gently heat to dissolve the sugars. Remove from heat, stir in cream, then mint extract. Chill in fridge for a couple of hours.
6. **Churn** the chilled base as normal in an ice cream machine.
7. **Layer up:** When churn is complete, load the tub — layer in cookie swirl dust and mint cookie balls. Do not overfill. Freeze for about an hour until the top is just starting to firm.
8. **Top:** Place mini cookies on the surface, then pour over the chocolate ganache. Return to freezer for a few more hours until fully set.
9. **Ganache:** Melt 50g of 50% dark chocolate with 1 tbsp water in the microwave, checking every 10 seconds. If it splits, recover using the warm water technique. Nick recommends sticking to 50% chocolate — higher % (e.g. 70%) is more likely to split as a water ganache.

**Notes:**
- This is a **batch recipe** — uses a traditional ice cream churn, not a Ninja Creami.
- The original cookie recipe (from a generic blog) went wrong; Nick switched to a BBC Good Food-based recipe, which worked well.
- Cookie swirl is just dry cookie powder layered into the churned ice cream, mimicking the real B&J method.
- Keep mint cookie balls in the freezer until the very last minute before assembly — they soften quickly.
- The 50% dark chocolate ganache works well as a water ganache; 70% is liable to split (overpowers the cocoa butter ratio).
- If ganache splits, recover by adding a little warm water gradually while stirring.
- Nick notes the mint balls are the star of the recipe — strong mint flavour; skip entirely if you dislike mint.
---

### [Making Ben & Jerry's: Salted Caramel Brownie Topped ice cream at home](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xaKN8JOv4p0)

Ben & Jerry's Salted Caramel Brownie Topped dupe: vanilla sweet cream base (from the B&J book) with brownie chunks, salted caramel swirl, and a crackle chocolate top.

**Ingredients — Ice Cream Base:**
- **500ml** double (heavy) cream or whipping cream
- **250ml** whole milk
- **2** large eggs
- **150g** sugar
- Vanilla extract (Nielsen-Massey brand used)

**Ingredients — Brownies:**
- **110g** butter
- **275g** dark chocolate
- **275g** sugar
- **3** large eggs
- **150g** plain flour
- Baking powder (pinch)
- Salt (pinch)
- **3 tbsp** high-quality cocoa powder

**Ingredients — Salted Caramel Sauce:**
- Jude's salted caramel sauce (store-bought, UK brand) or homemade (see: https://www.livewellbakeoften.com/homemade-salted-caramel-sauce/)

**Ingredients — Chocolate Crackle Top:**
- **100g** dark chocolate (~70%)
- **1 tsp** coconut oil

**Steps:**

1. **Chocolate shapes (optional mix-in):** Melt chocolate in a mold for decorative shapes. Use 50–70% dark chocolate. Set in freezer.
2. **Brownies:** Melt butter and dark chocolate together in the microwave. Add sugar and 3 eggs; stir well until fully incorporated. In a separate (large) bowl combine flour, baking powder, salt, and cocoa powder. Add the chocolate batter and stir thoroughly — avoid flour explosion. Pour into a lined 8x8 baking pan (or whatever size you have). Bake at 170°C for 30 minutes. Cool completely, then cut into chunks.
3. **Ice cream base:** Whisk 2 eggs in a bowl, then slowly add sugar while whisking until fully incorporated. Add milk, cream, and vanilla extract; whisk until combined. Churn in an ice cream maker for 20–30 minutes until soft-serve consistency.
4. **Load the tub:** Layer in brownie chunks and chocolate shapes, then swirl in salted caramel sauce. Freeze for ~30 minutes to set the layers.
5. **Crackle top:** Melt 100g dark chocolate with 1 tsp coconut oil in the microwave (~30 seconds, stir well). Working quickly, pour over the surface of the ice cream. Return immediately to freezer. Freeze for at least a couple of hours (ideally overnight — Nick froze for ~24 hours before serving).
6. **Serve:** Break through the crackle top layer with a spoon.

**Notes:**
- Base is Ben & Jerry's "Sweet Cream Base #1" from their official book — described as light in texture; Nick notes he personally prefers a richer, higher-body base.
- Recipe uses raw eggs — use pasteurized eggs if preferred (can be pasteurized at home).
- Caramel sauce: Jude's is a UK brand. The linked homemade recipe is recommended as an easy alternative.
- The crackle top works because coconut oil lowers the melting point of chocolate, causing it to set hard on cold ice cream.
- Nick's wife did the tub decoration.

---

### [Making Cinnamon Snickerdoodle ice cream by Salt & Straw](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3fZjgE0tN7Q)

Salt & Straw's cinnamon ice cream with homemade snickerdoodle cookies folded in, churned in a traditional ice cream maker.

---

**Ingredients — Snickerdoodle Cookies:**
- **100g** sugar
- **57g** butter
- **1** small egg
- **110g** flour
- **2.5g** salt
- **4g** cinnamon
- **5g** baking powder (UK: baking powder; US original: baking soda + cream of tartar)

**Ingredients — Cinnamon Ice Cream Base:**
- **100g** sugar
- **30g** skimmed milk powder
- **1g** xanthan gum
- **30g** light corn syrup / glucose syrup / golden syrup
- **322g** whole milk
- **326g** heavy cream
- **20g** ground cinnamon (Red Ape Cinnamon in the original)
- Pinch kosher salt
- Pinch ancho chile powder

---

**Steps:**

1. **Cookies — cream:** Beat butter and sugar together (stand mixer with paddle, or hand mixer) until light and airy.
2. Add egg and whisk until well combined.
3. Add flour, salt, and baking powder (see note). Mix until incorporated. If dough is too wet, add up to 30g more flour.
4. Refrigerate dough for a couple of hours.
5. Roll dough into small balls, coat in a cinnamon-sugar mix, place on a baking tray.
6. Bake at 190°C (375°F) for 20 minutes, rotating the tray halfway through. Cool completely and set aside.
7. **Ice cream base — dry mix:** Combine xanthan gum, sugar, and skimmed milk powder in a bowl.
8. Add corn syrup (or glucose syrup) and milk to a saucepan and warm gently.
9. Add the dry ingredient mix, stirring until sugar and milk powder are fully dissolved. Remove from heat.
10. Transfer to a container, add heavy cream, stir to combine. Chill in the fridge for 2–3 hours.
11. Remove base from fridge; stir in ground cinnamon and ancho chile powder. Return to fridge for 10–15 minutes to allow spices to hydrate. (Note: spices will clump rather than fully dissolve — consider blending instead.)
12. Pre-chill ice cream maker bowl for 10–15 minutes, then churn the base. Recipe is low in sugar so it churns quickly — approximately 8 minutes.
13. In the last couple of minutes of churning, add crumbled/chopped snickerdoodle cookies (roughly 0.5–1 cm pieces). If the machine is not strong enough to incorporate them, fold in by hand after transferring to the tub.
14. Transfer to tub, freeze overnight.

---

**Notes:**
- Recipe is from the Salt & Straw ice cream cookbook. Available via saltandstraw.com and major booksellers.
- UK/US leavening swap: US recipe uses baking soda + cream of tartar; in the UK, baking powder already contains the acid so cream of tartar is not needed.
- Red Ape Cinnamon is specified in the original but not available in the UK — any good-quality ground cinnamon works.
- The chile powder amount is not listed in the book; Salt & Straw confirmed it is "just a pinch" of ancho chile powder.
- Cinnamon tends to clump when stirred into the cold base; blending it in (rather than stirring) would give a smoother result.
- The base is relatively low in sugar, which causes it to churn and firm up faster than typical recipes (~8 min in a Cuisinart 5030).
- Batch recipe: uses a traditional churning ice cream maker, not a Ninja Creami.
---

### [Pumpkin Spice Gelato](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=liA8HlIivC4)
Low-fat pumpkin spice gelato using allulose and a homemade spice blend, spun on Lite Ice Cream.

**Ingredients:**

Pumpkin Spice Blend (make ahead):
- **3 tbsp** ground cinnamon
- **2 tsp** ground ginger
- **2 tsp** nutmeg
- **1.5 tsp** ground allspice
- **1.5 tsp** ground cloves

Gelato Base:
- **521g** 2% milk
- **100g** whipping cream (33–36% fat)
- **95g** allulose
- **31g** skim milk powder (SMP)
- **5–10g** pumpkin spice blend (to taste; Nick used ~5g / 1 tsp)
- **2g** cold-process stabilizer (guar gum or xanthan gum)
- **1.5g** lecithin (added as emulsifier to help cohesion of fats; ideally pre-mixed with allulose)

**Steps:**
1. Combine all spice blend ingredients in a jar, shake well to break up any lumps of ginger. Set aside.
2. Pre-mix stabilizer into the skim milk powder to prevent clumping when added to liquid.
3. Add milk, cream, allulose, SMP+stabilizer mix, and pumpkin spice blend to a saucepan.
4. Add lecithin (ideally pre-mixed with the allulose/sugar). Heat on the hob for ~5 minutes, stirring to dissolve all powders.
5. Pour into Ninja Creami pint, stir to distribute pumpkin spice (dry spices are hydrophobic and will settle).
6. Freeze for minimum 24 hours.
7. Spin on **Lite Ice Cream** (fat is very low, ~5.5g total; water content ~70.6%, higher than Nick's preferred 68% max).
8. Serve plain (Scoop A) or topped with pumpkin spice caramel (Scoop B — Patreon recipe).

**Notes:**
- This is a ~750g batch, slightly over a standard pint.
- Pumpkin spice can be bought pre-made (common in the US) or mixed from scratch as above.
- Stir the pint periodically while freezing (e.g., once an hour) to keep spices evenly distributed, as dry spices settle in liquid.
- Pre-mix lecithin with the allulose/sugar before adding to the base to avoid clumping.
- Nick chose Lite Ice Cream over Gelato mode due to the very low fat content.
- Nick notes: remove the cream and calorie savings come at the cost of a poor mouthfeel/texture.
---

### [Making Dondurma Turkish Stretchy Ice Cream Part 1](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=O7xOlzCmi28)

Traditional Turkish dondurma made with authentic salep orchid root powder and mastic resin, churned then stand-mixer beaten for its signature stretchiness.

**Ingredients:**
- **600g** goat's milk
- **200g** whipping cream
- **150g** sugar
- **15g** salep orchid root powder (true orchid root — NOT pre-made salep drink mix)
- **3g** mastic resin (ground)

**Steps:**
1. Grind mastic resin with a small amount of sugar (helps prevent sticking) and mix together with all remaining dry ingredients.
2. Combine goat's milk, cream, sugar, ground mastic, and salep powder in a bowl. Whisk for 2 minutes until sugar is fully dissolved — run a spoon along the bottom and sides to check for undissolved sugar.
3. Cover and refrigerate for 1 hour.
4. Churn in an ice cream machine until the mix thickens and the machine tone changes — stop slightly earlier than you would for a normal ice cream (it should hold its shape but not be fully frozen).
5. Transfer the partly-churned ice cream immediately into a pre-chilled stand mixer bowl (bowl and beater both pre-frozen). Beat for 2 minutes on the stand mixer.
6. Transfer to a container and freeze.
7. Before serving, remove from freezer and let temper for ~15 minutes until the ice cream just starts to become malleable (~-10°C). Work it by hand for about 5 minutes to develop stretchiness — this mimics the traditional pounding/stretching done with paddles by street vendors.

**Notes:**
- Salep must be true orchid root powder sourced from Turkey — not the pre-mixed salep drink sold in shops, which is a starch pudding base and will not produce stretchiness.
- Mastic resin is available in Mediterranean/Greek grocery stores. Grinding it with sugar prevents it from clumping and sticking.
- Stretchiness is activated at the right serving temperature (~-10°C); straight from the freezer it will be too firm.
- Flavor is mildly rooty with notes of cinnamon and a hint of vanilla from the salep. Additions like vanilla extract or pistachio are recommended.
- This is Part 1 (traditional ingredients). Part 2 covers a modernist version without salep or mastic.
- Equipment needed: hand whisk, stand mixer with pre-chilled metal bowl and beater, ice cream machine.

---

### [Making Häagen-Dazs Belgian Chocolate in 2025](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wfpRaBd_Qu0)

Egg-free Häagen-Dazs Belgian Chocolate copycat — rich cocoa base with chopped Belgian chocolate stirred in at the end of the churn. **Batch recipe (makes 2L/2kg).**

**Ingredients:**

- **840g** double cream (33–36% fat; heavy cream or whipping cream)
- **640g** whole milk
- **215g** sugar (195g granulated/caster + 20g vanilla sugar; or all regular sugar + 1 tsp vanilla extract)
- **107g** dextrose
- **100g** skimmed milk powder
- **95g** dark cocoa powder (milk-chocolate-profile cocoa strongly recommended — Nick uses a specific UK brand for the authentic Häagen-Dazs flavour; do NOT use a dark/bitter cocoa profile)
- **200g** 70% Belgian chocolate (Callebaut dark 70% recommended; used as mix-in, chopped — ~100g per litre of ice cream)
- **2g** stabilizer (optional)

**Steps:**

1. Combine cream, milk, sugar (including vanilla sugar), skimmed milk powder, and dextrose in a pot. If using a stabilizer, mix it into the sugar or dextrose first before adding.
2. Heat on the hob to 45–50°C, stirring to dissolve the dry ingredients.
3. Add the cocoa powder and whisk in. Some lumps are normal at this stage.
4. Continue heating to 85°C (with stabilizer) or 60–65°C (without stabilizer) until cocoa is fully dispersed and the mix is smooth.
5. Transfer to a container, let rest on the surface for ~10 minutes, then refrigerate overnight.
6. Next day, whisk the chilled base to reincorporate any settled cocoa solids.
7. Pour into a pre-chilled ice cream machine (run machine 10 minutes before churning). Churn according to machine instructions.
8. At the end of the churn, fold in roughly chopped Callebaut 70% Belgian chocolate (about 100g per litre).
9. Transfer to freezer containers and harden for a minimum of 5 hours before serving.

**Notes:**

- The cocoa powder is the most critical ingredient — Nick uses a UK milk-chocolate-profile cocoa (not available in North America; can be sourced from Amazon). A dark cocoa will still taste good but won't replicate the Häagen-Dazs profile exactly.
- Cocoa powder does not truly dissolve — it disperses into particles. Heating to sufficient temperature ensures full dispersion; stirring/whisking the chilled base before churning is important to reincorporate settled solids.
- Dextrose amount is important for texture and scoopability — do not substitute or adjust casually.
- This is a batch recipe (2L/2kg) — scale down proportionally for smaller containers.
- Callebaut 70% dark chocolate is the recommended brand for the mix-in pieces.

---

### [Making Häagen Dazs Caramel Cone Explosion ice cream at home](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=24oLvxorIEY)

Caramel ice cream with almond praline (brittle) and milk-chocolate-covered waffle cone pieces — homemade dupe of Häagen Dazs Caramel Cone Explosion (Canada/US).

**Ingredients:**

_Ice cream base:_
- **500g** whole milk
- **400g** cream
- **30g** sugar
- **5g** light brown sugar
- **50g** dextrose
- **55g** SMP (skim milk powder)
- **1.1g** stabilizer (optional)

_Caramel:_
- **100g** sugar
- **30g** water (approx. 2 tbsp)
- **50g** cream
- **50g** butter

_Almond praline / brittle:_
- **100g** sugar
- **30g** water
- **200g** blanched almonds (toasted)

_Chocolate-covered waffle cone pieces:_
- **100g** milk chocolate
- **10g** neutral / refined coconut oil (deodorized — no coconut flavor)
- Waffle cone pieces (quantity to taste)

**Steps:**

1. **Caramel:** Combine 100g sugar and 30g water in a saucepan. Bring to a boil — no stirring, only swirling. Once it turns a light amber and starts darkening, remove from heat. Carefully add 50g cream and 50g butter (mixture will bubble vigorously — cold butter causes more splatter). Stir until fully combined and smooth. Set aside.
2. **Ice cream base:** Warm 500g milk in a pan. Add sugars (with stabilizer mixed in) and dextrose; heat to 30–50°C. Add SMP and stir until fully dissolved. Pour in the prepared caramel; stir well and keep at 50–60°C until caramel is fully incorporated. Remove from heat, add 400g cream, stir, then refrigerate until fully chilled.
3. **Almond praline:** Spread blanched almonds on a baking tray; toast at 175°C for 15–20 minutes. Meanwhile, cook 100g sugar and 30g water in a pan until golden amber. Remove almonds from oven; roughly break them into smaller pieces (blender pulse, rolling pin, or chopping). Stir almond pieces into the hot caramel; cook briefly if deeper color is desired. Pour onto a silicone baking mat, spread flat, and cool completely. Once set, smash into small chunks.
4. **Chocolate-covered waffle cone pieces:** Melt 100g milk chocolate in the microwave. Stir in 10g deodorized coconut oil until smooth. Break waffle cones into pieces and either dip individually or toss all pieces in the chocolate, then spread on a tray/pan. Refrigerate until set, then transfer to freezer until needed.
5. **Churn:** Take chilled base from fridge, give it a final stir, and churn in ice cream machine. In the final minutes of churning, add almond praline chunks (if machine is strong enough; otherwise fold in by hand after churning). Add chocolate-covered waffle cone pieces for the last few seconds. Turn off machine, transfer to tub, freeze until firm.

**Notes:**

- This is a traditional churned ice cream recipe (not Ninja Creami). Yield is larger than a single pint.
- The stabilizer (1.1g) is optional — Nick lists it but does not specify the blend.
- Use deodorized / refined coconut oil for the chocolate coating — virgin coconut oil will impart a coconut flavor.
- Nick notes the caramel separates more easily with warm butter, though cold butter causes less violent bubbling — trade-off to consider.
- Caramel cone pieces are messy to coat individually; tossing all pieces in the chocolate at once and setting as a slab is the cleaner method.
- Keep finished waffle cone pieces in the freezer right up until mix-in time to prevent sogginess.
- If using a lower-power ice cream machine, fold in praline and cone pieces by hand after churning rather than adding during the last spin.

---

### [Making Häagen Dazs Irish Cream Brownie at home](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6dNRLkdAD8k)

Custard-based Irish cream ice cream with chocolate fudge swirl and brownie chunks — Häagen Dazs dupe. Batch recipe (~1.2L).

**Ingredients:**

Ice Cream Base:
- **340ml** milk
- **455ml** whipping cream (35% fat)
- **120g** sugar
- **35g** SMP (skim milk powder)
- **55g** golden syrup / corn syrup
- **3** large egg yolks
- **100ml** Baileys Irish Cream
- **0.85g** stabilizer

Fudge Swirl:
- **40g** brown sugar
- **70g** white sugar
- **25g** cocoa powder
- **57g** butter
- **60ml** milk
- **1.5 tbsp** corn syrup / golden syrup
- **0.5 tbsp** vanilla extract

Brownies:
- **110g** butter
- **150g** plain flour (all-purpose)
- **35g** cocoa powder
- **0.5 tsp** baking powder
- **0.5 tsp** salt
- **275g** Bournville chocolate
- **275g** sugar
- **3** eggs

**Steps:**

1. Mix milk, sugar (with stabilizer mixed in), egg yolks, and skim milk powder in a pan. Heat gently to 82°C to cook the eggs — no tempering needed with this method.
2. Remove from heat, transfer to a container. Add cream, vanilla bean paste, and Baileys. Stir briefly, then refrigerate until fully chilled.
3. For the fudge swirl: heat milk, sugars, and butter together until melted. Add cocoa powder, stir well, bring to a boil then remove from heat. Stir in vanilla extract. Store in a container in the fridge.
4. For the brownies: bake at 160°C for 22 minutes (see linked brownie video). Chop and keep in the freezer until assembly.
5. Once the base is fully chilled, churn in your ice cream machine (approx. 10–30 minutes depending on machine — a Lello 5030 took 12 minutes for the full 1.2L batch).
6. While churning, chop brownies and keep frozen.
7. Layer churned ice cream, fudge sauce, and brownie chunks into tubs. Freeze for a few hours before serving.

**Notes:**

- This is a batch recipe yielding ~1.2L — scale down as needed for smaller containers.
- Mixing stabilizer into the sugar before adding to the base ensures even dispersion without clumping.
- Adding Bailey's off-heat (not cooked) preserves the Irish cream flavour — the host specifically addresses the common complaint that you can't get enough flavour into the base.
- Brownies should stay frozen right up until the loading phase so they don't go soggy in the warm churned ice cream.
- Reference brownie video: https://youtu.be/uBvbcW-55TY

---

### [Key Lime Pie Ice Cream (Better Than Häagen-Dazs)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YVJ7syvqwmc)
Zesty key lime ice cream churned with buttery baked graham cracker crumble pieces and a Swiss meringue swirl — the meringue element Häagen-Dazs missed.

**Ingredients — Ice Cream Base** (yields ~1 litre):
- **415g** whole milk or 2% milk
- **370g** whipping cream (33%+ fat; UK: double cream)
- **130g** allulose (listed as "sugar" in description; spoken as allulose in video)
- **30g** corn syrup or glucose syrup (not golden syrup for base)
- **60g** skim milk powder (SMP)
- **2g** stabilizer (optional)
- **5–7g** key lime extract (LorAnn or similar — linked in description)

**Ingredients — Graham Cracker Crumble** (for ~1 litre):
- **100g** graham cracker crumbs
- **50g** butter, melted
- **20g** corn syrup, glucose syrup, or golden syrup

**Ingredients — Swiss Meringue Swirl**:
- **150g** egg whites (storebought acceptable)
- **150g** sugar (1:1 ratio by weight to egg whites)
- **1/3 tsp** cream of tartar (stabilizer for egg whites)
- Splash of vanilla extract (added after whipping, once cooled)

**Steps:**
1. Combine milk, cream, allulose, corn syrup, and SMP in a saucepan. Mix the stabilizer into the SMP first, then stir into the liquid.
2. If using stabilizer, heat to 80–85°C to activate. Without stabilizer, heat to 50–60°C until fully dissolved and homogenised.
3. Let the base cool slightly (about 10°C below peak temp), then add key lime extract. Extract can separate on top if added too hot — it will incorporate once fully cooled.
4. Chill base in fridge overnight to let the lime flavour develop.
5. For the graham cracker crumble: combine crumbs, melted butter, and corn/glucose/golden syrup. Press flat on a parchment-lined baking tray. Bake at 190°C (375°F) for 5–8 minutes until set — do not burn. Cool to room temperature, then refrigerate until firm. Chop into ~1cm cubes. The syrup is essential — without it the crumble falls apart in the ice cream.
6. For the Swiss meringue: combine egg whites, sugar, and cream of tartar in a heatproof metal bowl. Set over a pot of simmering water (bain-marie). Stir constantly with a spatula until sugar fully dissolves and no grittiness remains — scrape the bowl sides to prevent crunchy sugar bits. Remove from heat.
7. Whisk with a stand mixer (strongly recommended over hand-whisking) until stiff peaks form and the mixture cools to near room temperature. Fold in vanilla extract once cooled.
8. Churn the chilled base in an ice cream machine. Towards the end of the churn, add the graham cracker crumble pieces.
9. Layer into the container: alternate scoops of churned ice cream with spoonfuls of Swiss meringue swirl throughout. Fill the whole tub this way.
10. Freeze for several hours until hardened before scooping.

**Notes:**
- Nick filmed a double-batch; the ingredient amounts above are the single-batch quantities he states for ~1 litre.
- Allulose is the actual sweetener used (spoken clearly in the VTT). The description labels it "Sugar" generically.
- Corn syrup or glucose syrup preferred in the base; golden syrup explicitly not recommended for the base (but works well in the crumble where it brings out caramel notes).
- Swiss meringue (egg whites warmed with sugar over bain-marie, then whipped) gives a marshmallow-fluff texture that holds in frozen ice cream — the same technique used in Ben & Jerry's Fish Food.
- Storebought carton egg whites work fine for Swiss meringue despite common claims otherwise — just use cream of tartar as a stabiliser.
- Key lime extract can be strong; start at 5g and adjust. LorAnn-style oils are very concentrated.
- Add extract once base cools slightly below peak temp to prevent oil separation on the surface (will reincorporate as it cools further).
- Chill base overnight for best lime flavour development.
- The corn/glucose syrup in the crumble creates a chewier, cohesive texture that holds together in the ice cream; more butter causes leeching rather than binding.
---

### [Making Häagen Dazs Midnight Cookies & Cream / Triple Chocolate Fudge Cookie / Chocolate Midnight Cookies](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Sc-1dZwI5zs)

Haagen-Dazs recreation of a discontinued custard-based chocolate cookies & cream with a homemade chocolate fudge swirl and cream-removed Oreo pieces, churned in a Musso 4080.

**Ingredients — Base:**
- **300ml** milk
- **300ml** double (heavy) cream (49% fat)
- **100g** white sugar
- **30g** dark muscovado sugar
- **2 Tbsp** skimmed milk powder
- **2** egg yolks
- **20g** cocoa powder
- **1 tsp** vanilla extract

**Ingredients — Chocolate Fudge Swirl:**
- **50g** sugar
- **50g** 70% dark chocolate
- **15g** cocoa powder (reduce to 10g — see Notes)
- **50ml** milk
- **3 Tbsp** corn syrup
- **1/2 Tbsp** coconut oil (deodorised)
- **1 tsp** vanilla extract
- Pinch of kosher salt

**Ingredients — Mix-In:**
- Oreo cookies (cream filling removed, cookies chopped)

**Steps:**
1. Combine all base ingredients except the cocoa in a pan. Add egg yolks directly — no tempering needed.
2. Slowly bring to 80–85°C, stirring continuously. This pasteurises the eggs in-pan without traditional tempering.
3. Add the cocoa powder and combine well.
4. Transfer to a container, cover, and refrigerate overnight.
5. For the fudge: combine milk and cocoa in a pan over gentle heat, then add corn syrup and coconut oil. Mixture may look slightly separated — keep going.
6. Bring fudge to a rolling boil, then remove from heat. Add the chopped chocolate and stir well to combine.
7. Transfer fudge to a container and cool to room temperature.
8. While the base is churning in the machine, split Oreos, scrape out and discard the cream, and chop the cookie wafers.
9. Once churned, layer ice cream into a tub, folding in cookie pieces and fudge swirl as you go.
10. Freeze for a few hours until set, then serve.

**Notes:**
- Fudge cocoa: 15g was slightly too intense compared to the original Haagen-Dazs. Drop to 10g for a closer match.
- The fudge recipe is a reverse-engineered approximation — no official Haagen-Dazs numbers exist. Results are very good but not identical.
- Oreo cream is removed because the original Haagen-Dazs uses plain chocolate cookie wafers with no cream filling.
- Machine used: Musso 4080.

---

### [Making INTENSE Chocolate Extract at Home](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LctwRr6RKkg)
DIY chocolate extract by steeping cacao nibs in alcohol — a sugar-free, intensely flavored ingredient for ice cream, baking, and more.

**Ingredients:**
- Organic cacao nibs — enough to fill ~3/5 of a 118ml (4oz) jar
- Vodka, rye whiskey, or rum — 40% ABV minimum, to fill the jar (approx. 70–80ml)

**Steps:**
1. Fill a small jar (118ml / 4oz) roughly 3/5 full with organic cacao nibs.
2. Top off with 40% ABV alcohol (vodka or rye whiskey recommended; rum must be at least 40% ABV).
3. Seal the jar and give it a quick shake.
4. Leave on the counter or in a cupboard for 3–4 weeks minimum; 2 months yields a seriously intense extract.
5. Strain out the cacao nibs and decant the liquid. Keep a fresh batch going continuously.

**Notes:**
- Use organic cacao nibs — non-organic/ultra-processed nibs can produce an artificial, bitter aftertaste.
- Minimum steep: 3–4 weeks. Optimal: 2 months for full intensity.
- Shake the jar daily during steeping for better flavor extraction.
- Cacao nibs will eventually go off if left in the liquid long-term; strain and decant once steeped.
- Yields approx. 50–75ml of finished extract per 118ml jar.
- Use in ice cream bases, hot chocolate, baking, cocktails, or savory dishes.
- This is a **component/technique video** — no specific gram weights given; fill ratios are visual (3/5 nibs, remainder alcohol).
---

### [Jeni's Fluffernutter Pie Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=d4ZGPeULuAA)

A duo-tub recreation of Jeni's Fluffernutter Pie: salty peanut butter ice cream and vanilla marshmallow ice cream, both loaded with a baked oat crumble. Makes ~1.3L total (two halves).

**Ingredients:**

Peanut Butter Ice Cream:
- **300ml** milk (1.8% fat)
- **150ml** cream (49% fat)
- **110g** sugar
- **1g** stabilizer — commercial blend (Locust Bean Gum, Fatty Acids, Guar Gum, Sodium Alginate, Agar Agar) — optional
- **1/2 tsp** kosher salt
- **65g** peanut butter (Skippy smooth)

Marshmallow Ice Cream:
- **300ml** milk (1.8% fat)
- **200ml** cream (49% fat)
- **75g** sugar
- **75g** large marshmallows (~3 large; must contain gelatin and ~30–35g sugar)

Oat Crumble (shared between both halves):
- **80g** quick-cook oats
- **40g** all-purpose flour
- **80g** dark brown sugar
- **0.5 tsp** salt
- **75g** butter

**Steps:**

1. **Peanut butter base:** Combine milk, cream, sugar, and stabilizer in a saucepan. Heat to 75–80°C, stirring to dissolve. Remove from heat, stir in peanut butter (Skippy) and salt until smooth. Transfer to a container, cool to room temperature, then refrigerate until ready to churn.
2. **Marshmallow base:** Combine milk, cream, and sugar in a saucepan. Add marshmallows chopped (use scissors or a knife). Heat gently — marshmallows will resist melting, then dissolve suddenly; stir until fully incorporated. The gelatin in the marshmallows acts as stabilizer. Remove from heat, transfer to a container, stir in cream, refrigerate until ready to churn.
3. **Oat crumble:** Combine oats, flour, dark brown sugar, salt, and melted butter in a bowl. Mix thoroughly until no dry lumps remain. Spread flat on a baking tray. Bake at ~180°C for 15–20 minutes until golden (remove any over-browned edges). Cool completely, then cut/break into small pieces. Store in freezer until needed.
4. **Churn peanut butter base** in ice cream machine (15–30 min depending on machine). Add crumble pieces at the end of the churn. Fill one half of a storage tub (or a separate container), press cling film onto the surface, lid on, and place in freezer on its side for 20–30 min to firm up while you clean the machine.
5. **Churn marshmallow base** the same way, adding crumble at the end. Pack into the second half of the tub alongside the first flavor. Tap down firmly to eliminate voids, lid on, and freeze for 3–4 hours before serving.

**Notes:**

- This is a **batch recipe** yielding ~1.3L — two ice cream flavors packed as a duo tub.
- Use marshmallows that have a strong vanilla flavour; bland/flavorless marshmallows will produce a flat-tasting base. Adding a splash of extra vanilla extract is recommended.
- The marshmallow gelatin replaces the need for a commercial stabilizer in that half. The peanut butter half uses 1g commercial stabilizer (optional but recommended).
- Butter is used in the crumble instead of sunflower oil (either works — you just need a fat to bind the dry ingredients).
- Smooth peanut butter preferred; if using chunky, stir the base during churning as peanut chunks may sink to the bottom.
- Crumble can be mixed in during the last minutes of churn (if your machine is robust) or folded in by hand in the tub.

### [Making Orange Ice Cream from Bad to Good](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Rr-5QuzHejc)
A subscriber's failed orange ice cream is diagnosed and rebuilt with a rind-infused base and proper invert sugar technique.

**Subscriber's Original Recipe (the "bad" version):**
- **500g** milk
- **400g** cream
- **160g** sugar (sucrose)
- **65g** SMP (skim milk powder)
- **200g** orange juice (from ~5 medium-large oranges, reduced)
- **20g** sugar (added to reduction)
- **1g** stabilizer (includes emulsifier)
- **1** medium egg (or Silk Gel — mono and diglycerides of fatty acids — as emulsifier substitute)

**Nick's Improved Recipe:**
- **3** large oranges (juiced — yields ~200g strained juice; roll first to loosen flesh)
- **160g** sugar (sucrose), added to juice
- **20g** sugar (sucrose), reserved for second stage
- **65g** SMP (skim milk powder)
- Rind of **1** large orange (for infusion)
- (Cream and milk quantities carried over from original base; balanced using an ice cream calculator)

**Steps:**
1. Juice 3 large oranges (roll on the counter first to maximise yield). Strain through a sieve into a pan — discard pulp bits.
2. Add 160g sugar to the strained juice. Heat and reduce by half (from ~200g down to ~100g by weight). This creates an invert sugar. Watch constantly — it boils over fast. Takes up to an hour at a heavy simmer.
3. Transfer the 100g reduction to a clean pan. Add the reserved 20g sugar. Heat to a suitable temperature.
4. Add SMP and dissolve fully (stir well; different powders take different times).
5. Peel the rind from 1 large orange (whole strips, not grated). Add to the hot base. Leave the heat low for a couple of minutes to infuse the essential oils into the base. The flavor lives in the rind oils, not the juice.
6. Turn off heat. Transfer to a container (rind left in overnight to steep further). Refrigerate overnight.
7. Remove rind before churning. Churn in an ice cream machine, then freeze.

**Notes:**
- The original recipe failed because it used only juice — citrus flavor comes primarily from essential oils in the rind, not the juice. Juice alone produces almost no orange flavor in ice cream.
- The reduction step creates an invert sugar (sucrose hydrolysed by citric acid in the OJ into glucose + fructose). Glucose and fructose have higher FPD (freezing point depression) than sucrose — this softens scoopability. The original recipe froze too hard/soft for the machine to handle due to imbalance.
- The original also had a gummy/slimy texture — caused by recipe imbalance, not excess stabilizer.
- Silk Gel (mono and diglycerides of fatty acids, E471) was used in the original test batch as an egg substitute/emulsifier. Nick notes eggs are easier to source and do the same job (lecithin in yolk emulsifies fats and water).
- If making again, Nick would also grate some zest directly into the base for even stronger flavor.
- Technique applies to any citrus: swap orange for lemon, lime, etc. — same method.
- Pairs well as a palate cleanser alongside rich chocolate desserts.
---

### [Oreo Gelato (No-Cook / Cold Process)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qhlhEbkB-rM)
Cold process cookies and cream gelato — no heating required, ready to churn in under 10 minutes.

**Ingredients:**
- **600g** 2% milk
- **150g** whipping cream (33%)
- **165g** sugar
- **40g** dextrose
- **65g** skimmed milk powder
- **1.7–2.5g** xanthan gum (Nick used ~2.5g)
- **8 Oreos** (cookie only, no cream filling) — crushed to powder, mixed into base during churn
- Broken Oreo pieces — to taste, added as mix-ins

**Steps:**
1. Pre-mix the dry ingredients first: combine sugar, dextrose, and xanthan gum together in a small bowl. Mixing the stabilizer with the sugars prevents clumping and ensures even distribution.
2. Add skimmed milk powder to your main mixing bowl, then pour in the 600g milk and 150g cream.
3. Add the sugar/dextrose/xanthan gum mixture to the bowl.
4. Whisk thoroughly for 5–6 minutes until everything is fully dissolved — no graininess at the bottom. Test by spooning from the bottom of the bowl.
5. Refrigerate the base while preparing the Oreos (just a few minutes is fine).
6. Separate the Oreo cookies from their filling (discard or snack on the cream). Place the cookie wafers in a zip-lock bag, expel the air, and crush into a fine powder.
7. Optionally, reassemble some Oreos with double filling (two cream halves together) to use as whole mix-in pieces.
8. Churn the base in a pre-chilled ice cream machine. When the base starts to come together, add the Oreo powder. Continue churning for another 5–6 minutes until done.
9. Transfer to a tub. Press broken Oreo pieces into the gelato as mix-ins.
10. Serve immediately or freeze. Note: gelato is ideally served at -10°C to -12°C — standard home freezers (-18°C) will set it firmer, so allow a short temper before scooping.

**Notes:**
- Cold process works here because all ingredients (xanthan gum, skimmed milk powder, sugar, dextrose) hydrate and dissolve without heat.
- Crushing Oreo wafers to powder turns the gelato a grey-brown colour — not the most attractive but delivers strong cookie flavour throughout.
- Use only the Oreo wafer (no filling) for the powder component; the cream is too sweet and fatty to incorporate evenly.
- The "double-stuffed" trick: press two cream halves together on one wafer before crushing — makes chunkier, richer pieces if using broken Oreos as mix-ins.
- Nick notes scales are essential for xanthan gum — never measure stabilizers by spoon volume alone.

### [Making Salt & Straw - The Great Candycopia ice cream at home](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IInNcZLNGx8)

Bourbon butterscotch ice cream churned with Snickers, Heath Bars, Whoppers, and Reese's Peanut Butter Cups — a Halloween special based on the Salt & Straw recipe book.

**Ingredients:**

_Base (traditional churn — makes ~1 litre):_
- **90g** sugar
- **45g** SMP (skim milk powder)
- **1.5g** xanthan gum
- **45g** light corn syrup (or golden syrup)
- **470ml** milk at 1.8% fat
- **470ml** heavy cream at 35% fat

_Butterscotch sauce:_
- **50g** light brown sugar
- **15g** granulated sugar
- **15g** corn syrup
- **30g** bourbon
- **60g** heavy cream
- **5g** butter
- Splash of vanilla extract
- Pinch of salt

_Add-ins:_
- **2x** Snickers bars, chopped
- **2x** Heath bars, chopped
- **250g** bag of Whoppers
- **6x** Reese's Peanut Butter Cups, chopped

**Steps:**

1. In a pan, combine corn syrup and milk and stir well. Add sugar (pre-mixed with xanthan gum) and skim milk powder. Stir until sugar is dissolved.
2. Turn off heat and stir in the heavy cream. Chill the base for 4+ hours.
3. Make the butterscotch: combine brown sugar, granulated sugar, corn syrup, and bourbon in a pan. Stir until sugars look slightly wet, then heat without stirring until it reaches a gentle smoke point. Remove from heat.
4. Add heavy cream, butter, vanilla, and salt to the butterscotch. Stir well and hold at room temperature until the base is chilled.
5. Once the base is chilled, stir in the butterscotch sauce thoroughly (takes a few minutes of stirring to fully incorporate).
6. Churn in an ice cream machine until soft-serve consistency.
7. While churning, chop all add-ins into small pieces.
8. Remove churned ice cream from machine and fold in all add-ins. Freeze for a few hours before serving.

**Notes:**

- Recipe is from the official Salt & Straw recipe book (available at the links in the description).
- Nick notes: if making again, he'd go easier on the add-ins and would add the butterscotch to the base while still warm (easier to incorporate).
- Whoppers and Heath Bars are North American products and can be hard to source in the UK/Europe — plan ahead.
- This is a traditional churned recipe, not a Ninja Creami recipe.

### [Salt & Straw Maple Bacon Streusel Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fZezHjIDT8o)

Salt & Straw's maple ice cream with candied bacon and oat streusel — a sweet-and-savory combo made famous by Voodoo Donuts.

**Ingredients — Streusel:**
- **80g** quick-cook oats
- **40g** all-purpose flour, sifted
- **80g** dark brown sugar
- **0.5 tsp** salt
- **75g** cold butter

**Ingredients — Candied Bacon:**
- **50g** maple syrup
- **50g** brown sugar
- Bacon (quantity to taste)

**Ingredients — Ice Cream Base:**
- **315ml** whole milk (1.8% fat)
- **315ml** heavy cream (35% fat)
- **60g** sugar
- **30g** light corn syrup (or golden syrup)
- **100g** maple syrup (65% sugar)
- **30g** SMP (skim milk powder)
- **1g** xanthan gum (or stabiliser of choice)

**Steps:**

1. **Candied bacon:** Coat bacon strips on both sides in the maple syrup and brown sugar. Place on a foil-lined baking tray, add extra sugar on top if desired. Bake at 180°C for 20–25 minutes. Remove and cool quickly in the freezer. Once cold, chop into small pieces.
2. **Streusel:** Combine oats, sifted flour, dark brown sugar, salt, and cold butter. Mix until crumbly. Bake at 175°C for 12–15 minutes until golden. Remove from oven and let cool. Add the chopped candied bacon to the cooled streusel. Place the combined mix in the freezer until fully chilled.
3. **Ice cream base:** Add corn syrup and milk to a saucepan, stir to combine. Mix the 1g stabiliser into the 60g sugar, then add to the pan. Add maple syrup and skim milk powder. Heat to 65°C (or to the temperature required by your stabiliser). Remove from heat and stir in the heavy cream. Store covered in the fridge overnight.
4. **Churn:** Churn the chilled base in an ice cream machine for 15–30 minutes depending on your machine.
5. **Pack:** Layer churned ice cream and streusel-bacon mixture into a tub. Freeze until set.

**Notes:**
- Salt & Straw use guar gum and carrageenan in their commercial tubs but specify xanthan gum in their home recipes. Any stabiliser works — keep the total amount the same (1g).
- Pre-mix the stabiliser into the dry sugar before adding to the pan to ensure even distribution and prevent clumping.
- Line the baking tray with foil when candying the bacon — burnt caramelised sugar is very difficult to clean.
- Cool the bacon in the freezer immediately after baking so it firms up and is easier to chop.
- Taste profile: subtle maple ice cream base, bacon flavour comes through as a finish, streusel adds a pleasant textural contrast. Nick notes this is a personal-taste flavour — not universally loved (he personally wasn't convinced) but technically well-executed.
- Streusel recipe is the same proven recipe used in the Jeni's Fluffernutter video.

### [Making Salt & Straw Peppermint Patty Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=giXf2hPTdq0)

Salt & Straw's cocoa peppermint base churned with a semi-liquid sweetened condensed milk peppermint fondant core piped into the center.

**Ingredients:**

*Salt & Straw Standard Ice Cream Base:*
- **110g** granulated sugar
- **30g** skim milk powder
- **1.2g** xanthan gum
- **30g** light corn syrup
- **332g** whole milk
- **332g** heavy cream

*Cocoa Addition (mixed into base):*
- **55g** granulated sugar
- **30g** unsweetened cocoa powder (not drinking chocolate)
- **45g** water
- **5g** peppermint extract (or 1 drop peppermint oil; Nick uses more than 5g personally)

*Peppermint Patty Core (batch — description lists half amounts for single pint):*
- **240g** sweetened condensed milk (approx. 2/3 of a standard can)
- **200g** powdered/icing sugar
- **30g** water (reduce to ~15g for a firmer core; add up to 45g for a looser, more pourable core)
- Peppermint extract — to taste
- **~0.6g** fine salt (pinch, to balance flavor)

**Steps:**

1. Whisk together sugar, skim milk powder, and xanthan gum in a small pot to combine dry ingredients.
2. In a separate saucepan, combine corn syrup and whole milk. Add the dry mixture and stir. Heat gently until sugar dissolves — do not superheat. Remove from heat.
3. Stir in heavy cream. Transfer to a container and refrigerate at least 6 hours, preferably 24 hours (better flavor).
4. For the cocoa addition: combine 55g sugar and 45g water in a small saucepan. Bring to a rapid boil, then immediately remove from heat. Quickly stir in 30g cocoa powder. Cool to room temperature (about 30 minutes, or over a cold water bath) before adding peppermint extract. Do not add extract while hot — volatile flavor compounds will burn off.
5. Once both components are fully cold, whisk the cocoa-mint mixture into the chilled base until fully incorporated (about 1 minute). If it seizes cold from the fridge, loosen by briefly stirring — avoid refrigerating the cocoa mix, as it becomes too stiff to blend.
6. Churn combined base as normal in an ice cream machine. Fill churning tubs without overfilling — leave room for the core.
7. Freeze churned base for 1–2 hours until firm enough to hold a carved core channel.
8. For the peppermint core: combine condensed milk and water in a small saucepan. Heat gently to 40–50°C — just enough to thin the condensed milk. Do not boil. Remove from heat.
9. Stir in 200g powdered sugar and peppermint extract until smooth. Add salt. The mixture should be pipeable or pourable. Adjust water for desired texture in the freezer.
10. Carve or scoop a channel down the center of the partially frozen ice cream. Pipe or pour the peppermint core into the channel. Return to freezer until fully set.

**Notes:**

- This recipe is a batch — the description's peppermint core amounts (120g condensed milk, 100g powdered sugar, 15g water, 5–10g extract, ~0.3g salt) are half the video batch quantities; use those for a single pint.
- The Salt & Straw method involves making a universal base and adding flavor components per recipe — the cocoa-peppermint addition is not part of the base itself.
- Do not refrigerate the cocoa addition overnight as the book suggests — it becomes too stiff to incorporate into the cold base cold. Keep it at room temperature until needed.
- Core consistency is adjustable: 30g water = semi-firm, scoopable core; 15g water = very firm; 45g water = soft, pourable, won't freeze solid.
- Peppermint oil (1 drop) is the recipe's spec, but peppermint extract requires more (5g+). Nick uses more than specified.
- The core must be added to a partially frozen (1–2 hr) base — too soft and the channel fills back in before the core sets.
