### [Salt & Straw Salted Malted Chocolate Chip Cookie Dough](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=zRbNJEJ5hrg)

Exact recreation of Salt & Straw's salted malted chocolate chip cookie dough ice cream — churned base layered with malted cookie dough chunks and malted chocolate fudge.

**Ingredients — Ice Cream Base:**
- **100g** sugar
- **2 Tbsp** milk powder
- **½ tsp** salt
- **¼ tsp** xanthan gum
- **320ml** whole milk
- **2 Tbsp** light corn syrup (glucose in UK)
- **1 tsp** molasses (black treacle in UK)
- **320ml** heavy (double) cream
- **2 tsp** vanilla extract

**Ingredients — Malted Cookie Dough:**
- **60g** unsalted butter, room temperature
- **2 Tbsp** sugar
- **55g** brown sugar or muscovado sugar
- **1 tsp** salt
- **2 Tbsp** heavy (double) cream
- **1 tsp** molasses
- **1 Tbsp** corn syrup (golden syrup in UK)
- **1 tsp** vanilla extract
- **60g** all-purpose flour (plain in UK), heat-treated (baked at 180°C / 350°F for 10 minutes)
- **2 Tbsp** malt powder
- **45g** semi-sweet chocolate chips

**Ingredients — Malted Fudge:**
- **1 Tbsp** cold water
- **2 tsp** malt powder
- **110g** corn syrup
- **120ml** heavy cream
- **1 Tbsp** unsalted butter
- **⅛ tsp** kosher salt
- **1 Tbsp** cocoa powder
- **85g** 80% dark chocolate, chopped

**Steps:**
1. **Ice cream base:** Whisk together sugar, milk powder, salt, and xanthan gum in a bowl until well combined (distributes the xanthan gum evenly).
2. In a pot over medium heat, combine the milk, corn syrup, and molasses; whisk until combined. Add the dry mixture and whisk until lump-free. Cook 2–3 minutes until sugar dissolves, then remove from heat.
3. Pour into a large container, add cream and vanilla. Cover and refrigerate at least 4 hours before churning. Churn 15–30 minutes depending on your machine.
4. **Malted cookie dough:** Cream together butter, both sugars, and salt with a spoon until combined. Add cream, molasses, corn syrup, and vanilla; mix. Add heat-treated flour and malt powder; mix until no lumps. Fold in chocolate chips.
5. Spread dough onto a parchment-lined tin and freeze for at least 1 hour until solid. Remove and chop into small cubes; return to freezer until needed.
6. **Malted fudge:** In a small pot, combine water and malt powder. Add corn syrup, heavy cream, butter, and salt. Heat until butter is fully melted and mixture just comes to a boil, then remove from heat. Add cocoa powder and chopped chocolate; whisk until smooth and chocolate is fully melted. Transfer to an airtight container and store in fridge.
7. **Assemble:** Layer churned ice cream, malted fudge, and cookie dough chunks into the storage tub — don't skimp on the mix-ins. Cover and freeze at least 3 hours before serving.

**Notes:**
- Recipe is a direct copy from Salt & Straw (published in their cookbook/online).
- Heat-treating the flour (180°C for 10 min) kills any raw flour bacteria — required for safe no-bake cookie dough.
- Malt powder appears in both the cookie dough and the fudge — key flavor element throughout.
- Churn time varies: Nick got 15–20 min, but can be up to 30 min depending on the machine.
- Freeze assembled tub at least 3 hours before eating for best texture.

---

### [Salted Malted Chocolate Chip Cookie Dough Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ggTCquqtfEM)

Salt & Straw's salted malted chocolate chip cookie dough ice cream — molasses-vanilla base with malted cookie dough pieces and a malted chocolate fudge swirl.

**Ingredients — Ice Cream Base:**
- **620g** whole milk
- **495g** heavy cream (33–36% fat)
- **200g** sugar
- **92g** molasses
- **12g** glucose syrup (or corn syrup or golden syrup)
- **68g** skim milk powder
- **1.5g** guar gum (or xanthan gum)
- **12g** vanilla extract

**Ingredients — Malted Cookie Dough:**
- **60g** all-purpose flour (heat-treated: bake at 175°C / 350°F for 10 min, then cool completely)
- **60g** unsalted butter, room temperature
- **30g** sugar
- **55g** brown sugar
- **5g** salt
- **30g** heavy cream
- **5g** molasses
- **15g** corn syrup (or glucose or golden syrup)
- **5g** vanilla extract
- **30g** malt powder (Horlicks, dry malt extract, or plain Ovaltine — not chocolate)
- **50g** semi-sweet chocolate chips

**Ingredients — Malted Chocolate Fudge:**
- **15g** cold water
- **10g** malt powder (Horlicks; Nick uses ~3x the recipe amount for stronger malt flavor)
- **110g** corn syrup (or golden syrup or glucose)
- **120g** heavy cream
- **15g** unsalted butter
- **1g** salt
- **20g** cocoa powder
- **85g** chopped chocolate (or chocolate chips)

**Steps:**

1. **Ice cream base:** Combine skim milk powder, guar gum, and sugar in a container and stir together. Add whole milk, cream, molasses, glucose syrup, and vanilla extract. Whisk for 1–2 minutes until sugar is dissolved and everything is fully combined. Refrigerate overnight.

2. **Cookie dough — heat-treat flour:** Spread 60g flour on a tray and bake at 175°C / 350°F for 10 minutes to kill pathogens. Cool completely before using.

3. **Cookie dough:** Cream together butter, sugar, brown sugar, and salt until lumps are broken up. Add cream (30g), vanilla (5g), molasses (5g), and corn syrup (15g); stir to combine. Add cooled heat-treated flour and malt powder; stir together. Fold in chocolate chips. Spread onto foil-lined tray and refrigerate overnight. Before use, chop into small cubes.

4. **Malted fudge:** Stir malt powder into cold water to hydrate. Add corn syrup, cream, butter, salt, and cocoa powder to a small saucepan. Heat over medium, stirring, until mixture comes to a gentle boil and butter has melted. Remove from heat, add chopped chocolate, and stir until completely melted and smooth. Whisk to ensure cocoa powder is fully incorporated. Pour into a container and refrigerate overnight (keeps at least 2 weeks refrigerated).

5. **Churn:** Give the chilled base a final stir to homogenize. Churn in a pre-chilled ice cream machine. Near the end of the churn, add cookie dough pieces and let the machine incorporate them. If the machine isn't powerful enough, fold in by hand.

6. **Pack tubs:** Layer ice cream into containers a few spoonfuls at a time, followed by spoonfuls of malted fudge. Add extra cookie dough pieces as desired. Freeze a few hours to harden.

**Notes:**
- Batch: recipe as written yields ~1.5 kg base; Nick got 2L finished ice cream (Musso 4080 churner). Scale down proportionally for home use.
- Ninja Creami adaptation: make the base, spin it up, then stir in cookie dough pieces by hand, followed by the malted fudge swirl. Warm the fudge slightly before swirling — it is thick when cold.
- The fudge from the original Salt & Straw recipe has a slight iciness when frozen; Nick notes he would add more anti-freeze agent if making it his own way.
- Malt powder options: Horlicks (original, not chocolate), dry malt extract, or plain Ovaltine. Adjust quantity to taste — more malt = more depth.
- Adjust fudge consistency: add more cream for thinner, reduce cream for thicker.
- Do not skimp on chocolate chips in the cookie dough — they provide important textural contrast.
- Base is a molasses-vanilla style — the molasses adds depth and color, not a pronounced treacle flavor.
- Original recipe source: https://tasty.co/recipe/salted-malted-chocolate-chip-cookie-dough-ice-cream-by-salt-and-straw

---

### [Making Salt & Straw's Honey Lavender Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2sjo6_BRGU4)

A faithful recreation of Salt & Straw's signature honey lavender ice cream using their standard base, stabilized with xanthan gum only.

**Ingredients:**

*Lavender honey syrup:*
- **86g** wildflower honey
- **20g** dried food-grade lavender buds
- **180g** water
- **10 drops** natural purple food coloring (optional)

*Ice cream base:*
- **100g** granulated sugar
- **30g** skimmed milk powder
- **1.4g** xanthan gum
- **30g** corn syrup (not high-fructose)
- **315g** whole milk (~2.4% fat)
- **315g** heavy cream (~37% fat)

**Steps:**

1. Combine honey and water in a saucepan. Bring to a full boil, stirring occasionally.
2. Turn off heat. Add dried lavender buds and steep for a minimum of 4 hours, ideally overnight.
3. Strain the lavender syrup through cheesecloth. Refrigerate until needed.
4. For the ice cream base: combine corn syrup and milk in a pan. Warm gently — no boiling, no steaming.
5. Pre-mix xanthan gum into the sugar (prevents clumping). Add the sugar/xanthan blend and milk powder to the warm milk. Stir until fully combined.
6. Remove from heat. Add cream. Refrigerate until cold.
7. Combine chilled lavender syrup with the ice cream base. Add purple food coloring if using. Stir to combine.
8. Churn in an ice cream machine until scoopable consistency (~11-12 minutes, machine-dependent). Do not stop early — churn until you can scoop it from the machine.
9. Transfer to tub and freeze.

**Notes:**

- This is the Salt & Straw standard base — xanthan gum is the only stabilizer used, which is intentional and true to the original recipe.
- Pre-mixing xanthan gum into the dry sugar before adding to liquid is key to avoiding lumps.
- The lavender syrup can be double-strained for a cleaner result, but a single strain through cheesecloth is sufficient.
- Purple food coloring is optional but strongly recommended — the visual cue sets flavor expectation and enhances the eating experience.
- Dried food-grade lavender buds can be expensive depending on location; only make this if you enjoy floral flavors.
- Tyler Malek (Salt & Straw) stops the churn slightly early in his videos — Nick recommends churning longer until the ice cream is scoopable directly from the machine.

---

### [Making Serendipity Friends Ice Cream - Central Perk Coffee Almond Fudge](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BsLh_8bE27o)

Custard-based coffee ice cream with Dairymilk fudge swirl and dark chocolate covered almonds, inspired by Serendipity's Friends Reunion tie-in flavour.

**Ingredients — Coffee Ice Cream:**
- **300ml** whole milk
- **300ml** double (heavy) cream
- **150g** granulated sugar
- **3** egg yolks
- **3–4 tsp** instant coffee
- **1 tsp** vanilla extract
- **1/4 tsp** salt

**Ingredients — Dairymilk Fudge:**
- **110g** Cadbury Dairymilk chocolate
- **110g** butter
- **1** egg
- **25g** sugar

**Mix-ins:**
- **50–75g** dark chocolate covered almonds

**Steps:**
1. Combine milk, cream, sugar, egg yolks, and salt in a saucepan. Heat gently to 82–85°C, stirring continuously — no traditional tempering needed, just heat everything together.
2. Add instant coffee powder and stir to dissolve fully.
3. Remove from heat and stir in vanilla extract.
4. Pour into a container and chill in the fridge for 4 hours.
5. Churn the chilled base in your ice cream machine.
6. Make the fudge: melt Dairymilk chocolate and butter together in the microwave. Whisk egg and sugar together, then combine with the chocolate-butter mixture. Refrigerate until needed. Warm slightly before using — it sets firm from the fridge and won't pour cold.
7. Chop the dark chocolate covered almonds to your preference (mix of large and small pieces works well).
8. Layer the churned ice cream into a container, swirling in the fudge and almonds generously. Don't skimp.
9. Freeze for at least a few hours (24 hours recommended) before scooping.

**Notes:**
- The fudge recipe is specifically developed for Cadbury Dairymilk — other chocolate brands may require adjustments to get the right consistency.
- Fudge sets firm in the fridge; microwave briefly to loosen it to a pourable consistency before layering.
- Instant coffee can be swapped for decaf if preferred.
- Scoops well after a full freeze; strong coffee aroma and flavour. Creamy texture despite the egg-based custard method.
- This is a traditional churned ice cream recipe (not a no-churn or Ninja Creami recipe).

---

### [Stark Raving Hazelnuts Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=C7--9U5Inz4)

Marvel-inspired luxury hazelnut ice cream with whiskey caramel sauce and 24ct gold leaf — a custard base churned in a traditional ice cream machine, not a Ninja Creami. Batch size: ~1.5L.

**Ingredients:**

Ice Cream Base (22.3% fat, MSNF 8%, Sugar 16.1%, Total Solids 46%):

- **500ml** milk (1.8% fat)
- **600ml** double cream (heavy cream, 49% fat)
- **6** egg yolks
- **220g** dark muscovado sugar
- **40g** SMP (skimmed milk powder, added after chilling)
- **1** Tahitian vanilla bean + seeds (large)
- **1.5 tbsp** Frangelico (hazelnut liqueur — optional)
- **200g** chopped Piedmont hazelnuts

Whisky Caramel Sauce:

- **200g** granulated sugar
- **60ml** water
- **120ml** double cream (heavy cream)
- **2 tbsp** Laphroaig whisky (10-year)
- pinch of salt

Decoration:

- **1 sheet** 24ct gold leaf per serving (80x80mm sheets)

**Steps:**

1. Combine milk, double cream, muscovado sugar, and egg yolks in a suitable pan. Gently heat to 77°C — do not boil.
2. Add vanilla seeds and the pod. Add Frangelico. Cover and refrigerate to chill the base.
3. Remove vanilla pod from chilled base, then stir in skimmed milk powder. Let hydrate for 20 minutes.
4. Pour base into ice cream machine and churn until done.
5. Layer churned ice cream with chopped Piedmont hazelnuts and whiskey caramel swirl, then freeze for at least 4 hours before serving.
6. For the whisky caramel: heat water and sugar together without stirring until desired amber colour is reached. Remove from heat, add double cream mixture, then add a pinch of salt and the Laphroaig whisky. Transfer to a heatproof container.
7. To serve: scoop, drape whisky caramel over the top, and finish with one sheet of gold leaf per portion.

**Notes:**

- This is a traditional churned custard recipe (ice cream machine required), not a no-churn or Ninja Creami recipe.
- Piedmont (Piemonte) hazelnuts are considered the premium choice; the description notes these specifically over generic hazelnuts.
- Tahitian vanilla is preferred here — noted as widely considered the best vanilla variety.
- Frangelico can be omitted without significantly affecting the base texture.
- Laphroaig 10-year Scotch whisky is the specified spirit for the caramel — its smoky/peaty character is intentional.
- Gold leaf options mentioned: gold spray, gold glitter, gold powder, or leaf — gold leaf recommended for best visual effect.
- Total cost estimate: ~£92.81 per 1.5L batch (assuming no ingredients on hand, including full bottles of booze).
- Batch is large enough to justify buying full bottles of alcohol.
---

### [Making Vanilla Ice Cream at home - Only 4 Ingredients](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=41K6_jrhXzI)

A no-fuss 4-ingredient churned vanilla ice cream using condensed milk, cream, and your choice of vanilla.

**Ingredients:**
- **350g** milk 2%
- **350g** cream 33%
- **400g** sweetened condensed milk (1 can / 300ml / 10 oz)
- **15g** vanilla bean paste (or 15g vanilla extract, or 1 vanilla bean)

**Steps:**
1. If using a vanilla bean, split it and heat gently with the milk on the lowest heat for 30–60 minutes to infuse. Skip this step for extract or paste.
2. Combine milk, cream, condensed milk, and vanilla in a bowl. Whisk for a couple of minutes until fully combined.
3. Refrigerate the mix while the ice cream machine comes down to temperature (~10 minutes).
4. Churn in an ice cream machine for ~20 minutes until soft-serve consistency.
5. Transfer to a container and freeze until firm.

**Notes:**
- Yields approximately 1.4L from ~1.1L of ingredients — about 20–25% overrun, which Nick considers ideal for quality ice cream.
- Vanilla bean paste keeps the caviar (seeds) visible in the final product; all three vanilla forms work equally well.
- The recipe is a base template — swap vanilla for lemon, orange, coffee, chocolate, or hazelnut extract to create variations.
- No heating required when using extract or paste — just mix and churn.

---

### [Mango Ice Cream: Version 1](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=dsyaO9-If6s)
Creamy mango ice cream made with canned mango flesh — great texture, subtle colour and flavour (Version 2 to follow with lower fat for stronger mango character).

**Ingredients:**
- **300g** canned mango flesh (from a 398ml can — use entire contents)
- **350g** milk @ 2%
- **300g** cream @ 33%
- **97g** sugar (sucrose)
- **55g** dextrose (51g added with dry ingredients + 4g pre-mixed with xanthan gum)
- **95g** skim milk powder
- **1.5g** xanthan gum

**Steps:**
1. Combine milk and cream in a saucepan. Add sugar, dextrose (51g), and skim milk powder.
2. Warm gently — no more than ~50°C — whisking until all sugars and milk powder are fully dissolved. Remove from heat.
3. Pour the dairy/sugar mixture into a blender. Add the entire contents of the canned mango (flesh + juice).
4. Blend for 5–10 seconds to start incorporating.
5. Mix the xanthan gum with the remaining 4g of dextrose, then add to the blender. Replace lid.
6. Blend for 2 full minutes until the xanthan gum is completely incorporated and the base is smooth.
7. Strain through a sieve into a clean bowl to remove any lumps.
8. Chill in the fridge until cold (base was noted as being straight out of the fridge before straining).
9. Pour into pint container and freeze for at least 24 hours.
10. Process in ice cream machine on the Ice Cream program.

**Notes:**
- Nick notes the result has a lovely smooth, creamy texture and scoops well, but the mango colour is a subtle yellow rather than bold orange — no artificial colouring used.
- Mango flavour is present but considered a little subtle; Version 2 will use less fat to intensify colour and flavour.
- Pre-mixing xanthan gum with a small amount of dextrose before adding to the blender prevents clumping and helps dispersion.
- Canned mango is used here as fresh mango has a short season; the 398ml can yielded exactly 300g of flesh.
- Nick describes this as technically closer to a Gelato due to the recipe ratios.
---

### [Mascarpone Cherry Gelato](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=UgE2Hgwgx1Q)
Homemade recreation of a Waitrose mascarpone amarena cherry gelato — lower sugar, with a Morello cherry swirl instead of the overly sweet store-bought amarena sauce.

**Batch:** Yes — yields multiple tubs (~2kg base)

**Ingredients:**

Gelato base:
- **1227g** milk (1.8% fat)
- **200g** mascarpone cream cheese
- **358g** sugar
- **110g** liquid glucose
- **80g** skimmed milk powder
- **5g** stabilizer

Cherry swirl:
- **400g** Morello cherry compote/sauce (jarred, off the shelf)
- **30g** liquid glucose

**Steps:**

1. Make the cherry swirl first: empty the jar of Morello cherry compote into a pan, add 30g liquid glucose, and warm over heat. Stir until combined and emulsified. Remove from heat and strain through a sieve — liquid only, discard the cherry flesh. Set aside to cool.
2. For the gelato base: add milk to a pan. Add sugar, skimmed milk powder, and stabilizer.
3. Heat the milk mixture. Once it reaches 60–70°C (the activation temperature for the stabilizer), add the mascarpone cheese and stir to incorporate.
4. Once fully combined and smooth, remove from heat. Transfer to a container and refrigerate overnight (or at minimum 4–5 hours) to chill completely.
5. Remove chilled base from fridge and stir well to loosen — mascarpone can settle; it will re-emulsify with mixing.
6. Churn in an ice cream machine until frozen (approx. 18 minutes for this batch size).
7. Load tubs by layering: spoon of churned gelato base, spoon of cherry sauce, repeat until tub is full.
8. Freeze until hardened off.

**Notes:**

- High sugar content (the store original was 20% sugar) means the mix takes longer to churn and will be quite soft straight from the freezer — this is by design for gelato.
- Morello cherries are more tart than amarena cherries; they pair better with the rich mascarpone base and balance the sweetness.
- Cherry sauce choice is flexible: use sweet amarena sauce for a sweeter ripple, Morello for a more balanced/tart result.
- A stabilizer is strongly recommended for large batches — it dramatically improves shelf life and texture stability over time.
- Use a large enough pan; the full batch volume is tight.
- The video was filmed well in advance of publishing; Nick confirmed the finished tubs held up very well in the freezer long-term due to the stabilizer.
---

### [Japanese Apple Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VrBgrat2kxA)

Poll-winner recreation of an apple-miso ice cream from a street cart in Shibuya, Tokyo — warm umami notes with subtle apple flavour and visible apple flesh flecks.

**Ingredients:**

Apple compote:
- **250g** apple (ideally Fuji; Pink Lady works well)
- **100g** sugar
- **50g** water
- **15g** white miso paste (recipe states ~13g used on camera)

Ice cream base:
- **500g** milk
- **300g** cream
- **70g** sugar (additional)
- **20g** glucose liquid
- **65g** skimmed milk powder

**Steps:**

1. Peel and chop apple into small cubes. Combine with 100g sugar and 50g water in a pan.
2. Cook on medium heat for 30–45 minutes, stirring, until apple breaks down soft and tender.
3. Stir in white miso paste until fully incorporated. Remove from heat and cool completely (a few hours).
4. Transfer cooled apple-miso mixture to a blender. Blitz thoroughly until smooth but with some apple fibre remaining — do not over-process to total smoothness.
5. Combine milk, cream, apple puree, remaining 70g sugar, glucose, and skimmed milk powder. Mix until fully combined and emulsified.
6. If the base splits at this stage: mix a small teaspoon of sugar with a quarter teaspoon of sunflower lecithin, add back into the split base off the heat, and gently whisk until it comes back together. (This rescue works for eggless bases only.)
7. Refrigerate base overnight — apple fibres will swell, giving an unusual thick consistency; this is expected.
8. Give a final stir, then churn in ice cream machine.
9. Transfer to containers and freeze until fully hardened.

**Notes:**

- Fuji apples are preferred (sweeter, authentic to the Tokyo original); Pink Lady or similar sweet apple is a good substitute.
- The base will look unusual and lumpy both before churning and during the churn — this is normal; the end result scoops cleanly with visible apple flecks.
- White miso acts as a flavour enhancer with warm, umami/sesame-like notes rather than a savoury flavour.
- Base splitting is caused by the high fruit-acid content mixing with dairy; the lecithin rescue method works reliably for eggless bases.
- Traditional churned recipe — not a Ninja Creami no-churn recipe.

---

### [Must-Try Ninja Creami Lemon Gelato Recipe](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3aWgxIEZTMA)
Simple 2-ingredient gelato base (half & half + condensed milk) with alcohol-based extract for flavour and freezing-point control.

**Ingredients:**
- **550g** half & half (10% fat — Dairyland brand used)
- **350g** sweetened condensed milk (approx. one 300ml can)
- **10g** alcohol-based lemon extract (or hazelnut, vanilla, mint, coffee — must be alcohol-based)
- **30g** glucose syrup / invert sugar / corn syrup *(optional — improves texture and smoothness)*

**Mix-in (lemon version):**
- White chocolate (Valrhona Ivory), chopped small — added via Mix-In program

**Steps:**
1. Combine half & half and condensed milk in the pint container. No heating required.
2. Add 10g lemon extract (alcohol-based). Whisk briefly to distribute.
3. If using glucose/invert sugar, add 30g and stir in.
4. Freeze for at least 24 hours (video uses overnight).
5. Spin on Gelato program straight from freezer.
6. Add chopped white chocolate on top and run Mix-In program.
7. Serve immediately or store — the alcohol in the extract keeps it from going rock solid on refreezing.

**Notes:**
- The recipe makes two pints if dividing into two flavours (lemon + hazelnut) — base fills two Creami containers.
- Alcohol-based extract is essential for freezing-point depression; glycerin-based extracts also work but require ~50% more by weight.
- Without glucose/invert sugar: serving temp ~-10°C, fat ~9.4%, sugar ~16.7%, solids ~43.6%, water ~65%, POD 170.
- With glucose/invert sugar: serving temp ~-12°C, fat ~8.5%, sugar ~18.5%, solids ~35.9%, water ~63.8%, POD 196.
- Condensed milk already provides very high sugar — no additional sweetener needed.
- Lemon zest can substitute for lemon extract but won't contribute alcohol for FPD (freezing-point depression).
- Result is low-overrun, dense, rich gelato-style ice cream.
---

### [Ninja Creami - How To Make Mint Ice Cream At Home!](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8jKOYuN8LSA)

Condensed milk mint choc chip ice cream using fresh peppermint leaves steeped in the dairy base — makes two pints (one plain, one green-colored).

**Ingredients:**

- **300g** 2% milk
- **300g** 33% whipping cream
- **300g** condensed milk (standard supermarket can)
- Fresh peppermint leaves (handful, stalks removed)
- Semi-sweet chocolate chips (high quality, to taste — added as mix-in)
- Green food coloring (optional, for one pint)

**Steps:**

1. Add milk and cream to a saucepan. Strip peppermint leaves from stalks and add to the dairy.
2. Warm to ~50°C to steep — the heat encourages the essential oils to leach out. Leave for at least 1 hour (2 hours for stronger flavor); heat can be off after initial warming.
3. Add the condensed milk and stir to combine.
4. Strain out the mint leaves. Divide base between two Ninja Creami pints. Add green food coloring to one if desired.
5. Freeze overnight (minimum).
6. Spin on Ice Cream mode.
7. After spinning, make a divot down the center of the pint and add chocolate chips. Run Mix-In function.
8. Serve immediately — texture softens quickly.

**Notes:**

- Real peppermint leaves give a distinctly different flavor vs. mint extract — stronger and more natural.
- Do not chop the leaves into the base; remove them entirely before freezing.
- Condensed milk provides both sweetness and MSNF (milk solids-non-fat), so no additional sugar is needed.
- The high sugar/fat content means this re-freezes well and can be eaten straight from the tub without respinning — a deliberate design choice from Nick.
- Batch: recipe yields 2 pints (base split equally).
- The green-colored pint was reserved for a separate Starbucks peppermint mocha frappuccino recipe.

---

### [Hazelnut Affogato with SKOR Bar](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KVNlfrSj6wQ)
Hazelnut half-and-half gelato with chopped SKOR pieces, topped with a hazelnut espresso shot and caramel sauce — served affogato style.

**Ingredients:**

*Hazelnut Gelato Base (made separately — see linked half-and-half gelato video):*
- Half-and-half (equal parts)
- Sweetened condensed milk
- Hazelnut extract (alcohol-based)
- Invert sugar / corn syrup (small amount)

*Mix-In:*
- **SKOR mini bars** — a few, chopped small (Heath Bar is a valid substitute)

*Affogato Assembly:*
- **1 shot** hazelnut espresso (brand: Rosso Cafe)
- **Caramel syrup/sauce** — drizzle to taste

**Steps:**

1. Prepare the hazelnut half-and-half gelato base in advance and freeze for at least 24 hours (see linked video for full base recipe).
2. Chop SKOR mini bars into small pieces.
3. Spin the frozen gelato pint in the Ninja Creami on Gelato.
4. Use a spoon to cut a channel down the middle of the processed pint all the way to the bottom.
5. Fill the channel with chopped SKOR pieces and run Mix-In.
6. Pull a shot of hazelnut espresso.
7. Scoop gelato into a serving bowl or glass.
8. Pour the hazelnut espresso shot directly over the top of the gelato.
9. Finish with a drizzle of caramel syrup.

**Notes:**

- This is an assembly recipe — the gelato base is made in a prior video (hazelnut half-and-half gelato). The base is half-and-half + condensed milk + hazelnut alcohol extract + invert sugar/corn syrup.
- SKOR bar is similar to a Dime bar; it contains toffee with almond pieces and a chocolate coating. Heath Bar is the direct North American equivalent.
- The hazelnut espresso amplifies the hazelnut flavor in the gelato without overpowering it — the coffee bitterness comes through the cream.
- Caramel syrup on top highlights the SKOR bar's toffee-caramel notes.
- A "balanced" base means leftovers can go back in the freezer successfully.
- Nick's tasting note: "I could just eat this for hours" — one of his highest-rated recipes.

---

### [No Added Sugar Luxury Triple Chocolate Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=322XErJ4F6I)

Rich, high-solids triple chocolate ice cream — cocoa powder, dark chocolate in the base, plus fudge brownie and fudge swirl add-ins — no added sugar, designed for a traditional ice cream machine (also works in Ninja Creami).

**Ingredients:**

- **600g** whipping cream (33–36% fat)
- **600g** whole milk (3.5% fat)
- **150g** allulose
- **100g** cocoa powder, 20–24% fat (Fry's brand used; "premium cocoa/cacao")
- **50g** dark chocolate, no added sugar (65% cacao, e.g. Lindt), chopped
- **45g** skim milk powder
- **5g** liquid stevia (approx. 1 tsp — to boost perceived sweetness, as allulose is less sweet than sugar)
- **~0.75g** stabilizer blend (Nick's own blend; he suggests asking in comments — not disclosed)

**Add-ins:**

- Fudge brownies (Costco Two-Bite Brownies used — dense, chewy; supermarket brownies work but are lighter)
- Condensed milk fudge pieces (chopped; Nick's own recipe — link in description)
- Soft fudge swirl (Nick's own recipe — link in description)

**Steps:**

1. Add milk to a saucepan. Add allulose and stir to combine.
2. In a separate container, mix skim milk powder with the stabilizer blend; whisk into the cold milk mixture to avoid clumps.
3. Add cocoa powder and liquid stevia directly into the cold liquid. Stir together.
4. Heat the mixture, stirring, to 85°C. Hold at 85°C for 5 minutes (achieves ~2–3% evaporation; don't stress about exact evaporation).
5. Add the chopped dark chocolate to the hot base; stir until fully melted (takes ~30–60 seconds).
6. Add the cold cream (straight from fridge) to the hot base and stir together — this rapidly drops the temperature (to ~41°C immediately).
7. Transfer to a container. Cool in fridge overnight (or use an ice bath first if preferred).
8. Churn in a pre-chilled ice cream machine per machine instructions.
9. Layer base and add-ins (brownie pieces, fudge chunks, fudge swirl) into tubs as you pack them. Freeze until completely hard.

**Notes:**

- This is a high-solids recipe — it scoops firmer than usual even when PAC is balanced to -4°C. Let it temper for 5 minutes before scooping.
- The added dark chocolate increases total solids/density, which improves melt resistance in the bowl.
- Cocoa powder goes directly into cold liquid — Nick does this intentionally despite viewer complaints.
- Stabilizer is Nick's undisclosed proprietary blend (~0.75g total — very low because the base is already high fat).
- Add-in recipes (hard fudge and soft fudge) are linked separately in the video description.
- Also works in the Ninja Creami (note: this was made in a traditional churning machine).

---

### [No Added Sugar Salted Caramel Gelato](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=vsiAqzxcliA)

Silky salted caramel gelato using caramelized allulose and stevia — no added sugar, diabetic-friendly, Ninja Creami Deluxe.

**Ingredients** (750g batch for Ninja Creami Deluxe):

- **632.6g** light cream (18% fat — UK: single cream)
- **80.1g** allulose (powdered)
- **32.3g** skim milk powder
- **3.0g** salt
- **2.0g** stevia drops

**Steps:**

1. Weigh 80g allulose into a small saucepan. Heat on medium-high, swirling the pan manually. The allulose will turn translucent at the edges, then bubble up intensely — bubbles subside in about 10 seconds. Watch closely: allulose caramelizes much faster than sucrose. Pull from heat when you reach the desired amber-brown color (a color swatch is shown on screen in the video). Do not burn — over-caramelization makes it bitter.
2. In a separate pan, combine the light cream and skim milk powder. Heat to around 60–70°C — it needs to be hot before the caramel is added.
3. Pour the hot caramelized allulose into the warm cream mixture. Scrape out every bit from the pan (any left behind throws off the recipe). Whisk or stir together until all allulose crystals are fully dissolved. Return to heat briefly if needed.
4. Add the salt (3g) and stevia drops (2g). Stir to combine.
5. Pour into the Ninja Creami pint container. Bubbles will form on top from agitation — let the mix settle a few minutes, then skim off excess bubbles so the fill level is accurate. Do not freeze with a thick bubble layer on top (it will freeze into an unpleasant layer and overfill the container).
6. Freeze overnight (minimum).
7. Spin on **Ice Cream** mode (Nick notes Ice Cream and Gelato settings perform identically on this machine).

**Notes:**

- Allulose caramelizes significantly faster than sucrose — stay at the pan and don't walk away once it starts to color.
- Powdered allulose caramelizes less evenly than granulated; both work but require close attention.
- Stevia drops serve purely as a sweetness booster (no effect on texture/freezing point). Omit if you prefer less sweet.
- Salt strongly affects freezing point — if increasing salt beyond 3g, reduce allulose to compensate and maintain freeze.
- Caramel flavor from allulose will be more subtle than sucrose caramel; taking it too dark results in bitterness rather than stronger caramel.
- Bubbles in the poured mix are from agitation and the hot liquid addition — they settle on their own within a few minutes.

---

### [High Protein Keto Vanilla Cookies & Cream Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5HVFqfie3XQ)

High-protein, no-added-sugar keto vanilla base with chocolate mint keto cookies mixed in.

**Ingredients:**
- **290g** high-protein milk (Nutrell 2%, ~7.2% protein by weight — higher than Fairlife)
- **290g** whipping cream (33–36% fat)
- **80g** xylitol
- **85g** vanilla protein powder (Pure Protein Vanilla brand used; 25g protein/serving — check for added sugars)
- **8g** vanilla extract (alcohol-based or glycerin-based both work)
- Keto-friendly cookies for mix-in (high-protein, ultra-low-sugar — chocolate mint variety used; ~24g per 2-cookie serving, 6g fat, 2g sugar, 4g allulose, 4g protein)

**Steps:**
1. Combine milk, cream, and xylitol in a saucepan. Warm gently on the hob for ~5 minutes until xylitol dissolves — do not overheat.
2. Remove from heat. Whisk in vanilla protein powder until fully combined. Do not cook the protein (overheating causes texture issues).
3. Add vanilla extract and stir through.
4. Pour base into Ninja Creami Deluxe pint. Freeze until solid (base was not heated hot, so freezes faster than a cooked base — still allow at least 24h).
5. Spin on Ice Cream.
6. Make a divot in the centre of the spun pint. Add crushed keto cookies. Run Mix-In.

**Notes:**
- Recipe makes one Ninja Creami Deluxe-sized batch.
- Nick prefers xylitol over erythritol — he finds erythritol always recrystallises over time regardless of quantity. If using erythritol, keep quantities low and stabilise.
- Allulose is a viable xylitol substitute but has a weaker freezing-point-depression effect — use slightly more.
- Vanilla extract (alcohol or glycerin-based) serves dual duty: boosts flavour and lowers freezing point. Vanilla bean powder adds flavour only, no freezing-point effect.
- Protein powders can cause high viscosity in churn machines due to thickeners/gums in the powder — not an issue in the Ninja Creami since the base is frozen solid then processed, not churned.
- Use any keto-friendly cookie for the mix-in; keep them low-sugar to stay on theme.
- Taste result: dense, scoops nicely, strong vanilla protein powder flavour with natural vanilla finish from the extract. Chocolate mint cookies complement the vanilla base well.

---

### [PERFECT Ninja Creami Mango Sorbet](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SDXxHKvncoo)
Simple 4-ingredient mango sorbet using fresh ripe mangos, designed to work in both the Ninja Creami and traditional ice cream machines.

**Ingredients:**
- **314g** ripe mango flesh
- **420g** cold water
- **210g** sugar (200g added to blender, 10g reserved for stabilizer)
- **3.5g** guar gum
- **52g** corn syrup (or glucose syrup / golden syrup / dextrose)

**Steps:**
1. Add mango flesh and cold water to a blender.
2. Add 200g of sugar to the blender.
3. In a small bowl, mix the reserved 10g of sugar with 3.5g of guar gum until fully combined (no clumps). This prevents the stabilizer from clumping when it hits the liquid.
4. Add the sugar/guar gum mixture to the blender.
5. Add 52g of corn syrup to the blender.
6. Blend on low speed for ~30 seconds until ultra-smooth.
7. Pour into Ninja Creami pint(s) and freeze for minimum 24 hours.
8. Spin on Sorbet program.

**Notes:**
- Corn syrup/glucose syrup lowers the serving temperature from -10°C to -16°C, making it scoopable straight from the freezer without waiting.
- Always pre-mix stabilizer with a dry powdered solid (sugar, cocoa, milk powder, etc.) before adding to liquid — prevents clumping and ensures full dispersion.
- The Ninja Creami's high shear produces a smoother, denser sorbet than a traditional churn machine; both taste identical but the Creami texture wins.
- Recipe yields more than one Creami pint — Nick kept the excess for another video / used in a traditional ice cream machine for comparison.
- Do not just blend plain frozen fruit and expect good results — the sugar balance is essential to prevent an icy block.
---

### [Häagen Dazs Strawberry Cheesecake Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gtnwc1K06o8)

High-fat strawberry cheesecake ice cream with cream cheese base, strawberry sauce swirl, and baked graham cracker crumb pieces. Batch recipe (traditional churn).

**Ingredients:**

*Strawberry Sauce:*
- **500g** ripe strawberries
- **100g** sugar
- **15g** lemon juice

*Cheesecake Base:*
- **250g** cream (33% fat)
- **125g** cream cheese (27% fat)
- **500g** milk (2% fat)
- **160g** sugar
- **40g** dextrose
- **65g** skimmed milk powder

*Cheesecake Crumb:*
- **85g** butter
- **140g** biscuit crumb (graham cracker or digestive)
- **15g** sugar

**Steps:**
1. Cook strawberries with sugar and lemon juice until broken down. Strain through fine mesh sieve. Refrigerate overnight.
2. Gently heat milk, sugar, dextrose, and SMP. Add cream cheese and cream, stir until smooth. Heat to 80°C if using stabilizer. Chill overnight.
3. Mix butter into biscuit crumbs and sugar. Bake at 360°F for 10 min. Chill 24h in fridge.
4. Next day: churn the cheesecake base.
5. Layer churned ice cream with strawberry sauce and crumb pieces into tubs. Stir gently.
6. Freeze 6-8 hours.

**Notes:**
- High fat is intentional — cream cheese flavor needs it to come through properly.
- The strawberry sauce must be strained to remove seeds and pulp.
- All three components are made a day ahead — the recipe requires overnight rests.

---

### [Perfect Sugar-Free Hibiscus Sorbet in the Ninja Creami](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_mjseQKvbe4)

A simple 4-ingredient sugar-free hibiscus sorbet steeped for 1 hour, strained, and spun in the Ninja Creami.

**Batch:** 1.5L — fills 2x Ninja Creami Deluxe pints, 3x standard Ninja Creami pints, or 1x 1.5L ice cream machine.

**Ingredients:**

- **1,114g** water
- **240g** xylitol (allulose also works)
- **144g** inulin
- **3 tbsp** dried hibiscus flowers
- **5g** xylitol (reserved for stabilizer mix)
- **2g** guar gum

**Steps:**

1. Add 1,114g water, 240g xylitol, and 144g inulin to a pan. Warm gently over low heat, whisking until fully dissolved (around 5 minutes). Do not boil.
2. Turn off heat. Add 3 tablespoons of dried hibiscus flowers and stir in. Leave to steep for 1 hour at room temperature.
3. After steeping, combine 5g xylitol and 2g guar gum in a small bowl, mix together, then stir into the warm hibiscus mixture. The residual heat is sufficient to dissolve both.
4. Strain the mixture through a fine-mesh sieve into Ninja Creami pints, discarding the spent hibiscus flowers.
5. Freeze overnight (minimum 24 hours).
6. Spin on Sorbet mode. No re-spin needed — texture should be smooth with a flat top.

**Notes:**

- Sorbet requires 25-35% sugar-substitute content by weight for proper texture; without this, the result is dry and powdery — do not reduce xylitol significantly.
- Nick's calculated stats for this recipe: serving temperature -15C, zero sugar, 25.4% solids (slightly low), 74% water (slightly high), POD 169, PAC 368.
- Guar gum is used instead of CMC/xanthan because there is no need to heat the base to 85C.
- The steeped hibiscus produces a vivid deep purple-red color.
- Nick notes that a well-balanced recipe should produce a flat top after freezing; a domed/bumped top indicates the mix needs adjustment.

---

### [Rebel Keto Coffee Chip Ice Cream - SUGAR FREE and KETO](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JjJizfl8L7A)

A high-fat, sugar-free keto coffee chip ice cream inspired by Rebel Ice Cream, churned to ~42% overrun for a dense, luxury texture across ~2.7 pints.

**Ingredients:**

Base:
- **400g** heavy cream (33% fat)
- **100g** water
- **130g** allulose
- **30g** inulin powder
- **20g** SMP (skim milk powder)
- **90g** egg yolks (approx. 3–4 yolks)
- **15g** instant coffee powder
- **2g** tara gum (or 2g standard stabilizer mix)

Straciatella chocolate chip:
- **100g** dark chocolate (72% — mixed with some milk chocolate to soften bitterness; Nick used Balcolade milk chocolate from Bulk Barn alongside a 72% bar from Safeways)
- **10g** olive oil (deodorized coconut oil also works — use the flavourless kind)

**Steps:**

1. Combine cream, water, coffee powder, allulose, inulin, skim milk powder, and egg yolks in a saucepan. Stir to dissolve the dry ingredients and break up the yolks — no special tempering needed, egg yolks go in cold.
2. Add 2g tara gum (or stabilizer mix) and stir in. Heat on the hob, stirring continuously, until steaming — target 65–80°C. Hold at steaming point for a couple of minutes to fully activate the stabilizer.
3. Remove from heat, pour into a container, and refrigerate until thoroughly chilled (overnight or several hours).
4. Chill the ice cream machine bowl for ~10 minutes. Whisk the cold base to agitate, then churn. Monitor temperature — when the mix reaches around -10 to -11°C, prepare the chocolate drizzle.
5. Chop the dark and milk chocolate into rough pieces, place in a microwave-safe bowl with the olive oil. Melt in 10–15 second bursts in the microwave, stirring between each burst. Remove while a few lumps remain — residual heat finishes the melt. Stir until smooth and pourable.
6. When the churned ice cream reaches approximately -12°C (machine is nearly done), drizzle the melted chocolate in a thin stream into the running machine — the cold environment instantly sets it into thin shards (straciatella technique).
7. Continue churning until done. Pack into containers. Target overrun: ~42%. Total yield: ~2.7 pints / ~1280g churned weight.

**Notes:**

- Batch recipe: yields approximately 2.7 pints (~897g unchurned base + chocolate). Scale down to a single pint if needed.
- Total net carbs: ~18.6g per pint at 42% overrun. Unchurned base is ~50.3g net carbs across the full batch.
- Nick deliberately targets ~30–42% overrun (not the 95–100% overrun of commercial keto brands like Rebel) — the result is far denser and more luxurious, comparable to Häagen-Dazs texture.
- Tara gum (Peruvian carob gum) is the stabilizer used in Rebel's original formula; 2g of a normal stabilizer blend is offered as a substitute.
- For the chocolate, Nick mixes a 72% dark bar with an unknown-% milk chocolate (Balcolade from Bulk Barn) to soften the bitterness. Any blend of dark + milk chocolate works.
- Use deodorized (flavour-neutral) coconut oil if substituting olive oil — the aromatic kind will add coconut flavour to the chocolate chips.
- When melting chocolate in the microwave, pull it out while a few lumps remain and stir to finish — prevents overheating and seizing.
- Drizzle chocolate at -10 to -12°C machine temperature for best straciatella shards.

### [Rosemary Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3VmJ6zjqsRQ)
Plant-based rosemary-infused ice cream made with cashew milk, plant cream, and Carnation plant-based condensed milk.

**Ingredients:**
- **700g** cashew milk (store-bought)
- **500g** plant cream (~31% fat, e.g. Elmlea plant-based)
- **370g** plant-based condensed milk (Carnation plant-based)
- **100g** sugar
- **50g** dextrose
- **1.5g** stabilizer (optional but recommended)
- Handful of fresh rosemary, leaves stripped from stalks and roughly bashed/chopped

**Steps:**
1. Strip rosemary leaves from stalks, discarding woody parts. Bash and roughly chop the leaves to release the oils.
2. Combine cashew milk, sugar, dextrose, and stabilizer in a pot. Heat while stirring to dissolve.
3. At around 50°C add the rosemary and steep on low heat for about 5 minutes. Stir to ensure stabilizer reaches 65–75°C, then remove from heat.
4. Add the plant cream to the mixture and whisk to combine.
5. Transfer to a container and refrigerate for 24 hours to steep and chill.
6. After 24 hours, strain out all rosemary pieces.
7. Pour the base into an ice cream machine and churn (approx. 20 minutes).
8. Transfer to pint tubs and freeze until fully set.

**Notes:**
- Nick notes the finished batch froze rock solid and went slightly icy — likely due to insufficient total sugar/PAC rather than too much. Consider increasing dextrose or adding more condensed milk in future versions.
- Also came out slightly gummy — attributed to stacked stabilizers: stabilizer in the plant cream, stabilizers in the plant-based condensed milk, plus the added 1.5g stabilizer. If skipping the added stabilizer, note that both the cream and condensed milk already contribute stabilizers.
- Strong rosemary aroma; the flavor is assertive. Nick describes it as an acquired taste — similar to the honey lavender he made previously.
- Different rosemary varieties may yield different intensity; use what is available.
- This is a churned (traditional ice cream machine) recipe, not a no-churn or Ninja Creami recipe.
---

### [Rum Raisin Ice Cream CLASSIC](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=u3sngyfXi0I)

Classic churned rum raisin ice cream with rum-soaked raisins stirred in at the end of churn.

**Ingredients:**
- **550g** milk (1.8% fat)
- **400g** cream (50% fat)
- **130g** sugar
- **55g** dextrose
- **60g** skimmed milk powder
- **50ml** strong rum
- **2g** stabilizer (optional)
- Raisins (quantity to taste — Nick used Cape raisins from South Africa)

**Steps:**
1. Place raisins and rum in a pan, warm gently and simmer for 5–10 minutes to open the pores of the raisins and allow the rum to permeate. Transfer to a container and leave overnight.
2. Combine milk, cream, sugar, and stabilizer pre-mixed with dextrose in a pan. Heat slowly, bringing up to 60–70°C.
3. At temperature, whisk in the skimmed milk powder until fully incorporated.
4. Remove from heat, pour base into a container, allow to cool slightly, then refrigerate until completely chilled.
5. The following day, strain the raisins from any remaining rum. Stir that remaining rum into the cold ice cream base (do not add rum to a hot base — this preserves the alcohol).
6. Churn the base in an ice cream machine. At the end of the churn, add the soaked raisins (as many or as few as desired).
7. Transfer to tubs and freeze until hardened.

**Notes:**
- Add rum to the chilled base, not the hot base — adding it hot drives off the alcohol.
- The alcohol in the rum keeps the ice cream from freezing completely solid, so be mindful of total sugar when balancing the recipe.
- Raisins soaked in alcohol stay completely soft in the frozen ice cream, giving a burst of rum flavour with each bite.
- Optional: add a small amount of vanilla to the base to bring forward complementary flavours.
- Nick used Cape raisins (from South Africa) — large, juicy, and excellent texture contrast in the finished ice cream.
- This is a batch recipe yielding multiple tubs; scale ingredients proportionally for a single pint.

---

### [Stunning Hazelnut Gelato in the Ninja Creami - ULTRA smooth](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MRJ7khMUhdk)

Rich, nutty hazelnut gelato using extract instead of nut butter, with homemade hazelnut praline served alongside.

**Ingredients:**

Gelato base (Ninja Creami Deluxe — 750g batch):
- **567g** cream, 10% fat (half and half in Canada/US)
- **46g** skim milk powder
- **124g** granulated sugar
- **5g** Lauran super strength creamy hazelnut extract
- **4g** guar gum (3g if using cup measurements / less confident in weighing)
- **5g** sunflower lecithin (optional but recommended — improves perceived creaminess)

Hazelnut praline:
- **100g** hazelnuts, roasted
- **100g** granulated sugar (75g also works)

**Steps:**

1. Weigh all gelato base ingredients into a bowl. Add cream first, then skim milk powder, sugar, guar gum, and lecithin. Break up any lecithin lumps with the back of a spoon.
2. Add 5g hazelnut extract. Whisk everything together thoroughly.
3. Pour into Ninja Creami Deluxe container. Freeze overnight (minimum) until frozen solid.
4. For the praline: spread sugar in a flat layer in a pan. Heat on medium until caramelized — aim for a reasonably dark amber, not so dark it turns bitter.
5. Spread roasted hazelnuts on a tray/pan. Pour the hot caramel over the hazelnuts, capturing all the nuts. Let harden completely at room temperature.
6. Once hardened, blend the praline in a blender for at least 5 minutes until semi-smooth paste. Work in stages if using a small blender — let it cool between runs to avoid overheating the motor.
7. Transfer praline paste to a container and refrigerate overnight. Reheat gently when ready to serve.
8. Spin frozen gelato base in the Ninja Creami on Gelato mode.
9. Serve gelato with praline paste drizzled or spooned alongside.

**Notes:**

- Sized for the Ninja Creami Deluxe (750g / ~24oz). For the standard Ninja Creami, remove ~30% of the mix.
- Hazelnut extract brand used: **Lauran super strength creamy hazelnut** — available on Amazon UK, Amazon France, and in the US/Canada. Other brands also available on Amazon UK.
- Real hazelnut butter is extremely difficult to make smooth at home (requires commercial equipment) — extract is the practical solution for home use.
- Guar gum target is 4g for authentic slightly-chewy gelato texture; drop to 3g if unsure or doing cup measurements.
- Lecithin is optional — Nick notes his recipes are balanced without it, but it increases perceived creaminess.
- If praline splits during blending (fat separates from solids): dissolve 3g lecithin in a small amount of warm water (~half to 3/4 tsp), add the emulsion to the blender, and re-blend. Result will be smooth.
- Praline works with any flavorful nut — hazelnuts and almonds are most common.
- Single spin result described as: "perfect texture, ultra smooth, slightly chewy — how gelato is meant to be."

---

### [Stunning Ninja Creami Espresso con Panna Gelato - DUAL LAYER](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Qm7MhtFsL9Q)

Italian-inspired dual-layer Ninja Creami Deluxe recipe: espresso gelato base topped with stabilized vanilla whipped cream, frozen together as a two-layer pint. Makes 2 pints.

**Ingredients — Espresso Gelato Base (2 pints):**
- **490g** milk (2% or whole)
- **245g** whipping cream (double/heavy cream)
- **115g** allulose
- **50g** skim milk powder
- **100g** espresso (brewed — moka pot, machine, or pods)
- **12g** stabilizer blend (Nick's own blend, heat-activated; or 3g guar gum / xanthan gum if using a cold-process stabilizer)
- **1 tbsp** instant coffee powder (optional, for extra intensity — add while base is still hot)

**Ingredients — Stabilized Whipped Cream Top Layer (enough for 2 pints + extra):**
- **500g** whipping cream (double/heavy cream)
- Vanilla sugar (sugar stored with used vanilla pods — or substitute vanilla extract)
- **~2g** guar gum (or xanthan gum; must be cold-process if using xanthan)

**Steps:**
1. Combine milk, cream, allulose, and espresso in a saucepan. Mix stabilizer blend with skim milk powder first (to prevent clumping), then add to the liquid.
2. Heat to 85°C / 180°F, stirring until fully hydrated and cooked. Remove from heat.
3. While still hot, stir in 1 tbsp instant coffee powder if using, until dissolved.
4. Split base evenly between two Ninja Creami Deluxe pints. Freeze until the top is firm but the middle is still partially liquid (a few hours — enough to support the cream layer).
5. Whip 500g cream with vanilla sugar and guar gum to roughly 50% air incorporation — target a thick, pourable "melted chocolate" consistency, not stiff peaks. Stop before over-whipping.
6. Gently spoon whipped cream on top of the semi-frozen gelato in each pint, filling right to the max-fill line. Do not let the cream sink into the base.
7. Freeze overnight (full 24h) until completely solid.
8. Spin on **Lite Ice Cream** setting (TOP only, Deluxe). The faster spin re-creams the whipped cream layer without destroying the gelato underneath.

**Notes:**
- This is a batch recipe yielding 2 Deluxe pints; base ingredients are shared across both.
- Lite Ice Cream spins faster than standard Ice Cream — critical for processing the high-fat cream top layer back to a creamy texture.
- Use instant coffee powder rather than adding more espresso to boost coffee flavor; espresso is mostly water and will dilute/destabilize the base.
- Nick uses a heat-activated custom stabilizer blend at 12g per double batch; for common cold-process stabilizers (guar or xanthan gum) use ~3g total.
- Pre-mix stabilizer into skim milk powder before adding to liquid to prevent clumping.
- Vanilla sugar (sugar infused with spent vanilla pods for ~1 month) is Nick's flavoring for the cream layer; plain vanilla extract works fine as a substitute.
- The two-layer result: spun cream on top, coffee gelato beneath — both distinct textures in one pint.
---

### [Super EASY Ninja Creami Coffee Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=1-LM4HRpu-k)
American-style coffee ice cream made with 18% coffee cream (UK single cream), condensed milk, golden syrup, and alcohol-based coffee extract — no stabilizers, no churning.

**Ingredients:**
- **635g** 18% fat coffee cream (UK: single cream; North America: coffee cream)
- **350g** sweetened condensed milk (1 standard 300ml can)
- **10g** golden syrup (or corn syrup / glucose syrup)
- **4g** Nielsen-Massey coffee extract (alcohol-based — essential)

**Steps:**
1. Combine cream, condensed milk, golden syrup, and coffee extract in a bowl. Whisk briefly to combine — no need to worry about air bubbles; this cream won't whip.
2. Split the mixture evenly between two Ninja Creami pints.
3. Lid on, freeze overnight (minimum — full freeze required).
4. Spin on Ice Cream.
5. Serve as-is or stir in a spoonful of dulce de leche and top with grated Valrhona Dulcey (blonde caramel chocolate).

**Notes:**
- Makes 2 pints (batch recipe — each pint gets half the mix).
- Extract must be alcohol/ethanol-based — this is what balances freezing point and texture. Water-based flavourings will not work the same way.
- Golden/corn/glucose syrup is optional but improves mouthfeel and freezing point balance; omit if unavailable.
- If adding dulce de leche or any liquid sauce, stir it in after spinning rather than using the Mix-In program — the program distributes sauces too thinly.
- A well-balanced recipe: once spun, it can be refrozen and eaten later without respinning.
- Nick uses Nielsen-Massey coffee extract; any quality alcohol-based coffee extract works.
---

### [Tempting Treat: Chocolate Ice Cream with Valrhona Cocoa Powder](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0C_vgeKGd1A)

Episode 4 of the Chocolate Series — a pure Valrhona cocoa powder base with no added chocolate, churned in a traditional ice cream machine.

**Ingredients:**
- **583g** milk (1.8% fat)
- **344g** double cream (48% fat)
- **80g** sugar (sucrose)
- **80g** dextrose
- **57g** Valrhona cocoa powder
- **60g** SMP (skimmed milk powder)
- **2g** commercial stabilizer mix with emulsifiers (optional)

**Steps:**
1. Combine milk, SMP, sugars, and stabilizer in a pan. Warm over heat, dissolving the sugars fully.
2. Add Valrhona cocoa powder and let it bloom in the warm base for 5–6 minutes, whisking to disperse.
3. Remove from heat. Transfer to a container, stir in the cream, then refrigerate until fully chilled (at least 4 hours).
4. Give the chilled base a thorough mix to ensure it is well incorporated.
5. Churn in an ice cream machine until done.
6. Transfer to a freezer-safe container and freeze until firm.

**Notes:**
- The stabilizer mix contains emulsifiers to help disperse cocoa fat. Egg yolks can substitute if no commercial stabilizer is available.
- Valrhona is a Dutch-processed cocoa (contains potassium carbonate as acidity regulator) — produces a very dark, intensely flavoured, fudgy result that Nick rates above cheaper cocoa powders.
- Optional add-in variation shown: hard fudge pieces + liquid chocolate fudge swirl folded in after churning ("chocolate double fudge").
- Valrhona 1 kg bags sourced from a specialist supplier at roughly £6/bag at time of filming.
- Part of a broader Chocolate Series comparing cocoa powders and chocolate additions — see linked EP1 for the comparison episode.

### [The BEST Raspberry Sorbet recipe](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8BHJUvsw4dY)
Smooth, creamy raspberry sorbet stabilized with pectin — intense fruit flavour, no ice crystals.

**Ingredients:**
- **300g** water
- **500g** raspberries
- **220g** sugar
- **85g** dextrose
- **5g** pectin powder

**Steps:**
1. Mix the pectin powder together with the sugar (dry) before adding to liquid — pectin will not dissolve if added to water alone.
2. Combine the pectin/sugar mixture with the water and dextrose in a saucepan.
3. Bring to a boil, stirring, until the pectin is fully dissolved. The mix will thicken slightly.
4. Remove from heat and cool for 20–30 minutes.
5. Add the cooled syrup and the raw raspberries to a blender. Blend thoroughly for about a minute.
6. Strain through a fine mesh sieve to remove seeds.
7. Chill the strained mix in the fridge for 3–4 hours until completely cold.
8. Churn in an ice cream machine. The sorbet will lighten in colour as it churns. Remove after about 15–20 minutes (machine-dependent) once lightened and stiffened.
9. Transfer to a container and freeze.

**Notes:**
- Critical technique: always pre-mix pectin with sugar before adding to water — it clumps irreversibly if added directly to liquid.
- Raspberries are not cooked, which preserves pure, intense flavour and aroma.
- Sorbet is ready to pull from the machine once it visibly lightens in colour and stiffens up.
- Recipe is for a traditional churned ice cream machine, not a Ninja Creami — adapt freeze-and-process method accordingly.
---

### [The Best Ben & Jerry's Caramel Brownie Recipe You Need](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=uBvbcW-55TY)

Homemade dupe of Ben & Jerry's France-only "Caramel Brownie Party" — caramel ice cream base churned with homemade brownies, caramel swirl, and Oreo cookie pieces.

**Ingredients — Ice Cream Base:**
- **335ml** whole milk
- **340ml** cream
- **100g** sugar
- **50g** dextrose
- **1–2** egg yolks
- **60g** caramel sauce (from batch below)
- **42g** SMP (skim milk powder)
- **1.5g** stabiliser

**Ingredients — Caramel Sauce (makes a batch; 60g used in base):**
- **300g** sugar
- **110g** butter
- **110ml** cream
- **3/4–1 tsp** salt

**Ingredients — Brownies:**
- **110g** butter
- **150g** plain flour (all-purpose)
- **35g** cocoa powder
- **1/2 tsp** baking powder
- **1/2 tsp** salt
- **275g** Bournville dark chocolate
- **275g** sugar
- **3** eggs

**Ingredients — Mix-Ins:**
- Oreo cookies (broken into pieces), quantity to taste

**Steps:**
1. **Brownies:** Melt chocolate and butter together in a microwave. Add eggs and sugar; beat well. Fold in dry ingredients (flour, cocoa, baking powder, salt). Pour into a pan and bake at 160°C for 22 minutes. Cool completely, then cut into chunks.
2. **Caramel sauce:** Heat sugar in a pan until fully melted (stir or swirl as preferred). Once caramelised, add butter and combine well. Add cream and stir until smooth. Transfer to a container and reserve.
3. **Ice cream base:** Combine milk, SMP, sugar, dextrose, and egg yolks in a saucepan. Heat while dissolving sugars, then add stabiliser. Continue heating to 80°C. Add 60g of the caramel sauce and mix in. Remove from heat, add cream (this drops the temperature quickly). Transfer to a container and refrigerate until fully cold (up to 4 hours).
4. **Churn** the cold base in an ice cream machine for 15–30 minutes depending on machine.
5. **Layer/fold in** brownie chunks, broken Oreo pieces, and extra caramel sauce swirl during layering into the storage container.
6. Freeze until firm before serving.

**Notes:**
- Ice cream base is adapted from Patrik Svensson's Häagen Dazs salted caramel recipe (icecreamcalc.com), adjusted to Nick's preference.
- Caramel sauce makes more than needed for the base — the remainder is used as a swirl mixed in during layering.
- Any favourite brownie recipe can be substituted for the Bournville-based one above.
- Oreos used for the cookie pieces; any similar sandwich cookie works.
- This recipe is subscriber-suggested (IDKFPV). The flavour was a France-only B&J release at time of filming.

---

### [The Best Pineapple Ice Cream to make at home](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=j8w_7cgVPqI)

Classic churned pineapple ice cream using a cooked pineapple jam technique to eliminate icy fruit chunks.

**Ingredients:**

Pineapple jam:
- **250g** ripe pineapple, cut into ~1cm cubes
- **150g** sugar
- **30–50g** cold water
- **3g** pectin powder
- **5g** sugar (to mix with pectin before adding)

Ice cream base:
- **500g** double cream (heavy cream)
- **400g** milk (1.8–2% fat)
- **70g** sugar
- **80g** skimmed milk powder

**Steps:**

1. Combine pineapple chunks, 150g sugar, and 30–50g cold water in a pan. Heat to a soft boil and cook for 20–25 minutes, stirring occasionally, until most free water has evaporated.
2. Mix 3g pectin powder with 5g sugar to prevent clumping, then stir into the boiling pineapple mixture. Continue cooking a further 10 minutes. The key sign it's ready: bubbles rise up the pan on their own without stirring — this means free water is stabilised by the sugars and pectin.
3. Remove from heat, cool slightly, transfer to a sealed container and leave to cool completely (Nick left it 24 hours).
4. When pineapple jam is fully chilled, add milk to a pan with 70g sugar and heat until sugar dissolves. To avoid curdling the milk from the pineapple's acidity, add the jam in two stages: first drain some of the sugar syrup from the jam into the warm milk and stir through, then add the remaining pineapple pieces and syrup. Stir well and remove from heat.
5. Transfer to a large container, add the cream, and refrigerate overnight until completely chilled. The pineapple will settle to the bottom — whisk gently to redistribute before churning.
6. Churn in an ice cream machine until done (Nick's machine took 10–12 minutes). Scoop into containers and freeze for 4–5 hours to harden.

**Notes:**

- Use fully ripe pineapple — unripe pineapple will produce hard, icy chunks. The thumb-press test: the skin should yield slightly when pressed.
- The boil-up stage (bubbles rising on their own after stirring) is the critical sign that free water is bound — don't skip it or the pineapple pieces will turn icy in the finished ice cream.
- For a more scoopable result straight from the freezer, substitute some of the table sugar (sucrose) for dextrose or other invert sugars to increase freezing point depression.
- Nick recommends adding a stabilizer blend to keep the ice cream ice-free throughout its freezer life.
- This is not a Ninja Creami recipe — it uses a conventional ice cream machine with a churn step.

---

### [The Best Vegan Black Forest Ice Cream Recipe](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-y5XWXW7oxI)
Vegan chocolate ice cream base churned with homemade cherry agar jellies and chopped vegan biscuits, inspired by Australian Cadbury Dairy Milk Black Forest chocolate.

**Ingredients:**

Ice cream base:
- **250g** Oatly oat cream (single cream alternative)
- **250g** Elmlea Plant Based Cream
- **250g** Alpro Soy Based Cream
- **50g** coconut oil
- **100g** UHT soy milk
- **130g** sugar
- **60g** dextrose
- **40g** cocoa powder (Twinings/Whittard's brand mentioned)
- **2g** xanthan gum

Cherry jellies:
- **125g** cherry juice (from canned pitted black cherries) — or 125ml water + cherry extract + red food colouring
- **100g** sugar (may need a little more to taste)
- **3g** agar agar powder

Mix-ins:
- Vegan biscuits (shortbread-style, e.g. Sainsbury's Free From range), chopped — quantity to taste

**Steps:**

1. Pour UHT soy milk into a saucepan. Add sugar, dextrose, coconut oil, cocoa powder, and xanthan gum. Warm and combine until smooth and thick.
2. Pour base into the ice cream machine container and chill in the fridge for a few hours.
3. Meanwhile, drain the liquid from a can of pitted black cherries into a saucepan. Taste — add cherry extract and more sugar if desired.
4. Add agar agar powder to the cherry juice. Warm and stir until fully dissolved and emulsified.
5. Pour into small molds (a coffee bean mold works well for size). Leave to begin setting, then transfer to the fridge until fully gelled.
6. When the base has chilled and thickened, whisk it well to loosen before churning.
7. Churn the base in an ice cream machine as normal.
8. Once churned, layer the ice cream into tubs with the popped cherry jelly pieces and chopped vegan biscuits.
9. Freeze for 3–4 hours until hardened.

**Notes:**
- Nick notes this recipe has a high ingredient cost due to using all three plant-based creams simultaneously; in practice you can use just one (Elmlea preferred) as your standard vegan base and still get the same result.
- The base becomes very thick after chilling — whisk vigorously to loosen it before churning.
- Canned black cherry juice provides strong, natural cherry flavour; cherry extract can supplement if needed.
- Agar agar is fully vegan and sets firm enough to pop out of molds cleanly.
- Use any vegan shortbread-style biscuit; brand is unspecified (packaging discarded before filming).
- This is a traditional churned ice cream recipe, not a Ninja Creami recipe.
