### [The Secret Recipe for Ben & Jerry's Coffee Coffee Buzz Buzz Buzz](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wNNmVsDADlc)

Ben & Jerry's Coffee Coffee Buzz Buzz Buzz dupe — strong coffee ice cream base with espresso chocolate fudge chunks, made in a traditional churner.

**Ingredients — Ice Cream Base:**
- **500g** milk
- **300g** cream
- **140g** sugar
- **50g** dextrose (or increase sugar to 180g total if omitting)
- **60g** SMP (skim milk powder)
- **2** egg yolks
- **2g** stabiliser (optional)
- **50g** ground coffee beans OR **50g** whole coffee beans OR **40–50g** instant coffee powder

**Ingredients — Espresso Chocolate Fudge:**
- **400g** condensed milk (sweetened condensed milk)
- **200g** dark chocolate
- **20g** instant coffee powder OR **5g** coffee extract

**Steps:**

1. Make the fudge first: gently warm condensed milk in a pan, add dark chocolate and stir until completely melted. Add instant coffee powder and stir to combine thoroughly.
2. Pour fudge into a lined container and refrigerate for a few hours to harden. Once set, cut into small pieces (~0.5cm squares) while still cold/frozen — it gets very sticky at room temperature.
3. Make the coffee-infused milk: warm milk on the hob and add ground/whole/instant coffee. Keep on low heat for at least 1 hour to steep and infuse.
4. Strain the coffee from the milk (if using ground or whole beans). Return infused milk to the pan and add sugar, dextrose, egg yolks, and skim milk powder. If using stabiliser, pre-mix it into the sugars before adding.
5. Heat mixture until combined. Remove from heat, pour into a container, add cream (cools the base quickly), and refrigerate until fully chilled.
6. Churn the chilled base in an ice cream machine. In the final minutes of churning, add the chopped espresso fudge pieces and let them incorporate.
7. Transfer to a tub and freeze for at least 4 hours before serving.

**Notes:**
- Batch recipe — makes multiple servings, not a single pint.
- Coffee method is flexible: ground beans give fastest infusion, whole beans take longer (smaller surface area) but produce the same final strength, instant coffee is the quickest option.
- Fudge is best chopped straight from the freezer — at room temperature it becomes very sticky and messy.
- Stabiliser is optional; pre-mixing it with the sugars helps even dispersion.
- Adding cream after the heat step (rather than heating it) brings the base temperature down quickly before chilling.

---

### [The Secret to Perfect Homemade Mango Gelato](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V-dPxbFgWyk)

A low-fat mango gelato remake with improved color, flavor, and overrun using higher mango content and reduced fat (6%).

**Ingredients:**

- **400g** mango flesh (sliced/frozen mangoes)
- **215g** milk
- **170g** cream (33% whipping cream) + **50g** cream added just before churning
- **80g** sugar
- **71g** dextrose
- **65g** skim milk powder (SMP)
- **4g** stabilizer blend (Special Ingredients brand recommended)

**Steps:**

1. Combine all dry ingredients (sugar, dextrose, SMP, stabilizer blend) together in a bowl and mix well so the stabilizer is fully dispersed — this prevents clumping when added to liquid.
2. Gently heat milk and cream (170g) in a pan. Pour in the dry ingredient mixture and whisk together.
3. Heat to 80–85°C, then stir off the heat for about 5 minutes to ensure the stabilizer is fully hydrated.
4. Blend mango flesh in a blender on low until smooth, then add the hot dairy base and blend again to combine.
5. Strain the mixture through a fine mesh sieve into a clean bowl to remove any lumps.
6. If the base is too viscous/thick, add the reserved 50g (up to 75ml) of cream to loosen it to a pourable consistency.
7. Pour into a container and freeze.

**Notes:**

- This is a remake of a previous mango gelato — the key improvement is dropping fat to ~6% and using a higher mango ratio, which delivers stronger color, better mango flavor, and improved overrun.
- Pre-mixing the stabilizer with the other dry powders before adding to liquid is critical — adding stabilizer directly to liquid causes clumping.
- The high fiber content from 400g of mango flesh contributes to good overrun and a slightly whipped, light texture.
- The extra 50g cream added after blending is a viscosity adjustment step, not part of the original base calculation — add it if the blended base is too thick to pour.
- Stabilizer brand used: Special Ingredients (no affiliation declared); requires heating to 80–85°C to activate.

---

### [The ULTIMATE Chocolate Ice Cream recipe works in Ninja Creami too](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=u5hYG3yjZ0I)

Full-fat traditional churn base using premium dutched cocoa + milk chocolate, golden syrup for caramel depth, and a stabilizer blend — works in Ninja Creami with no adjustments.

**Ingredients:**

- **450g** 2% milk
- **550g** whipping cream (33–35% fat)
- **140g** sugar
- **44g** dextrose
- **10g** golden syrup (corn syrup works as substitute)
- **40g** skim milk powder (0.1% fat — not whole milk powder)
- **50g** cocoa powder (Ghirardelli dutched recommended; also: Cacao Barry, Valrhona, Guittard Rouge)
- **40g** milk chocolate (Valrhona Taïnori 33% cocoa solids used; or any high-quality milk chocolate)
- **2g** stabilizer blend (Nick's own "422" blend with emulsifier)
- **3g** lecithin — sunflower or soy (optional but recommended for best result)
- **2g** vanilla extract

**Steps:**

1. Combine milk, cream, and golden syrup in a saucepan.
2. Mix skim milk powder and sugar together, add to the dairy.
3. Combine stabilizer, dextrose, and lecithin (if using) together, then stir into the dairy mix.
4. Add cocoa powder and stir to incorporate (will look mottled — that's fine).
5. Add milk chocolate pieces (no need to chop; they melt as it heats).
6. Heat on the hob, stirring, to 85°C (185°F). Do not boil. Target 3–5% evaporation by weight — weigh before and after heating.
7. Remove from heat. Allow to cool to ~60°C (~140°F), then stir in vanilla extract (adding it while still too hot drives off the alcohol and the flavor).
8. Cool with lid on for 30 minutes at room temp, then refrigerate minimum 12 hours (overnight recommended — aging develops flavor).
9. Churn in ice cream machine. For Ninja Creami: freeze standard pint, use Ice Cream program.

**Notes:**

- This is a full-fat recipe — not low-cal. Nick explicitly states it's not low-fat.
- Golden syrup is the key flavor differentiator — its caramel notes build the "ultimate" profile. Available at Walmart, Safeway, any UK supermarket.
- Ghirardelli dutched cocoa gives a milk chocolate profile; combined with actual milk chocolate you get layered chocolate depth — two different milk chocolate flavor profiles in one base.
- High-quality cocoa is non-negotiable: Cacao Barry, Valrhona, Guittard Rouge all excellent. Cheap cocoa is the #1 reason commercial chocolate ice cream disappoints.
- Lecithin (sunflower or soy) acts as an emulsifier — same function as egg yolk lecithin. Chocolate already contains some lecithin, so it's optional, but adds texture quality.
- Vanilla goes in off the heat (~60°C) — above 80°C alcohol evaporates and takes the flavor with it.
- Aging (minimum 12h fridge) is called "super important" — flavors combine and intensify.
- Chocolate ice cream goes hard due to extra fats and the cocoa powder absorbing water; stabilizers and specific sugars (dextrose) counter this.
- Batch size: full churn batch — scale down to pint for Ninja Creami.

---

### [The Ultimate Plant Based Lemon Meringue Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XaCA_6dwpdM)
A fully plant-based lemon meringue pie ice cream with an oat milk/vegan cream base, aquafaba marshmallow fluff meringue, and a baked graham cracker/digestive biscuit crust.

**Ingredients:**

Ice Cream Base:
- **250g** oat milk
- **450g** plant-based whipping cream (33% fat or above — Nick uses Silk dairy-free whipping cream)
- **140g** sugar
- **30g** dextrose
- **2g** stabilizer blend
- **2** large lemons (zest of both, juice optional)
- Yellow food colouring (optional — a few drops to correct the off-white colour from the oat milk/cream)

Graham Cracker / Biscuit Base:
- **200g** graham crackers (vegan-friendly; substitute digestive biscuits in the UK — both ~400g package)
- **70g** plant-based butter (melted)

Aquafaba Meringue (Vegan Marshmallow Fluff):
- **120g** aquafaba (chickpea liquid — canned chickpea brine, chilled)
- **90g** sugar (added gradually once aquafaba reaches soft peaks)
- **50g** golden syrup / corn syrup (key for silky, sticky fluff texture)
- **2g** cream of tartar
- Vanilla extract (to taste — be generous, ~1 tsp or more, to mask any chickpea flavour)

**Steps:**

1. Combine oat milk, plant-based cream, sugar, dextrose, and lemon zest from both lemons in a saucepan. Mix the stabilizer into the dry sugars first before adding to the liquid (to avoid clumping).
2. Heat on the hob, stirring continuously, until the mix reaches 70–75°C. Steam rising from the surface is a reliable visual cue if you don't have a thermometer — hold it there for a couple of minutes, agitating to prevent sticking.
3. Transfer to a bowl, cover with a lid, and refrigerate overnight to age and steep the lemon flavour.
4. For the biscuit base: crush graham crackers / digestive biscuits to fine crumbs. Melt the plant-based butter in the microwave (30–40 seconds). Combine butter with crumbs until the mixture resembles damp sand that just holds its shape when pressed. Use just enough butter — too much and it will separate in the oven.
5. Press the crumb mixture firmly into a lined baking pan (use the back of a spoon, a glass, or a rolling pin). Bake at 325°F (163°C) for about 10 minutes. Allow to cool, then break/cut into small cubes for mix-in.
6. For the aquafaba meringue: chill the chickpea liquid beforehand. Add aquafaba, cream of tartar, and vanilla extract to a stand mixer or use a hand mixer. Whip on high until soft peaks form. Then add golden syrup and gradually stream in sugar, continuing to whip until stiff, glossy, sticky peaks form. Use a generous amount of vanilla extract to mask the chickpea flavour.
7. Optionally add a few drops of yellow food colouring to the churned/chilled ice cream base to give a clean lemon-yellow appearance (plant-based cream produces an off-white/beige colour).
8. Churn the aged base in an ice cream machine (Nick uses a Lello/Musso-style machine, pre-chilled). Churn approximately 15 minutes until desired consistency.
9. Assembly: layer into pint tubs — add biscuit crumb cubes, spoon over marshmallow fluff meringue, and top with ice cream (or fold in layers). Freeze until solid.

**Notes:**
- This is a batch recipe — Nick mentions making multiple tubs; the ingredient quantities are larger than a single Ninja Creami pint.
- Stabilizer is especially important in plant-based ice creams due to the high free-water content in oat milk and plant-based creams (no casein to bind water). Nick uses a Special Ingredients stabilizer blend and ups the amount slightly compared to dairy recipes because there is no skim milk powder for body.
- Golden syrup / corn syrup in the meringue is not standard but Nick considers it essential — without it the fluff lacks the silky, sticky marshmallow texture and turns into a plain Swiss meringue.
- Aquafaba quality varies significantly by brand; canned chickpea liquid that looks clear and "egg-white-like" whips much better than a murky, thick one.
- Lemon zest is added directly to the warm mix and the base is aged overnight with the zest in it, which extracts the lemon oils fully into the cream.
- Optional: a small amount of lemon extract can be added for an additional flavour boost without adding more zest.
- Plant-based ice cream will not be as white as a dairy base; food colouring is cosmetic only but Nick prefers a yellow tint to signal lemon flavour visually.
- If not going fully plant-based, Swiss meringue (egg whites + sugar cooked to 60°C then whipped) can substitute for the aquafaba fluff.

---

### [Malted Mini Egg Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AdISavTeLbU)

A malted ice cream base churned with micro mini eggs — inspired by a Calgary ice cream shop (Marble Slab / Made by Marcus style seasonal flavour).

**Batch:** 1500g — makes 2x Ninja Creami Deluxe pints or 3x standard Ninja Creami pints.

**Ingredients:**
- **564g** whole milk
- **66g** whipping cream
- **170g** sugar
- **60g** dextrose
- **67g** skim milk powder
- **2g** tara gum
- **4g** sunflower lecithin (emulsifier)
- **50g** dry malt extract (medium roast — powder or liquid)
- **3 packs** micro mini eggs (Cadbury-style; broken up slightly)

**Steps:**
1. Combine milk and whipping cream in a saucepan.
2. Mix together sugar, dextrose, skim milk powder, tara gum, and lecithin in a small bowl; whisk to combine and break up clumps.
3. Add the dry ingredient mix to the milk/cream and stir to incorporate.
4. Add the dry malt extract slowly (it's a fine powder — add carefully to avoid dust).
5. Heat the base to 85°C (185°F) and hold for 5 minutes, stirring throughout.
6. Strain through a fine mesh sieve to remove any undissolved lecithin lumps.
7. Cool, then refrigerate for 24 hours to let the malt flavour develop.
8. Whisk the chilled base to re-homogenise, then churn in your ice cream machine.
9. In the final stages of churning, add the micro mini eggs (a mix of whole and lightly crushed pieces).
10. Transfer to tubs and freeze overnight to harden.

**Notes:**
- Nick suggests doubling the malt extract (100g) for a stronger malt flavour — 50g gives a medium intensity.
- If using liquid malt extract, use the same weight (50g or 100g); it adds a negligible amount of extra water.
- Micro mini eggs are strongly preferred over standard size — they don't freeze rock-solid and won't break your teeth; their candy shell colour also leeches into the base for a decorative effect.
- Use medium or dark roast dry malt extract for best flavour depth; light roast lacks intensity.
- Tara gum requires reaching 85°C to hydrate and activate properly.
- Strain the base before chilling — lecithin can leave visible lumps.
- Malt extract is available at homebrew/beer-making shops or online (Amazon).
---

### [Tiramisu Ice Cream in the Ninja Creami?](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NVqzrxzi0A4)

A double-layered tiramisu pint: coffee-soaked ladyfinger base, thin dark chocolate divider, and a cooked cream cheese gelato top — spun on Lite Ice Cream (Deluxe TOP mode).

**Batch:** Recipe makes 2 pints (quantities below are for both tubs combined).

---

**Ingredients — Coffee-Soaked Ladyfinger Base (per 2 pints):**
- ~430–450ml espresso (4 lungo shots; Nick ended up needing 4 after starting with 3)
- 5 tbsp Allulose (erythritol-and-monk-fruit blend; used to depress freezing point of coffee liquid — approx. 20% by weight)
- 12 ladyfinger biscuits (savoiardi; Safeway / Amazon / most European supermarkets)

**Ingredients — Chocolate Divider Layer:**
- 50g dark chocolate (60% cocoa)
- 1 tsp deodorized (refined) coconut oil

**Ingredients — Cream Cheese Gelato Top (per 2 pints):**
- 244g full-fat cream cheese (8g fat per 30g; mascarpone is ideal but expensive — standard cream cheese used here)
- 540g milk (2% used; any milk works)
- 79g xylitol (rounded to 80g)
- 35g skimmed milk powder
- 3–4g hot-process stabilizer (proprietary blend; if using xanthan gum, reduce significantly — xanthan goes gummy fast)
- 1 tsp vanilla bean paste

---

**Steps:**

1. **Coffee soak:** Brew 4 lungo espresso shots (~430–450ml total). Stir in 5 tbsp allulose until dissolved. Dip ladyfingers one at a time, ensuring they are thoroughly soaked. Layer them in the bottom of each pint tub. Pour a little extra coffee liquid over the top to fill gaps. Freeze for a few hours until cold and set (not fully solid).

2. **Chocolate layer:** Melt 50g dark chocolate with 1 tsp refined coconut oil in the microwave. Remove frozen ladyfinger base from freezer and pour a very thin layer of melted chocolate over the top. Return to freezer — chocolate sets almost instantly on the cold base.

3. **Cream cheese gelato base (cooked):** Add milk to a saucepan. Cut cream cheese into smaller pieces and add. Heat on the hob, stirring, until the mixture reaches 85°C (185°F) — required for the hot-process stabilizer to activate. It will be thick and lumpy at first; keep stirring. Once at temperature, add xylitol, skimmed milk powder, stabilizer, and vanilla bean paste. Stir until smooth. Refrigerate for 1–2 hours to chill completely.

4. **Assemble:** Pour the chilled cream cheese base on top of the set chocolate layer in each tub. Freeze for 24h minimum (36h recommended given the cooked base).

5. **Spin:** Spin on **Lite Ice Cream**, **full tub** mode. The top ice cream layer processes well; the bottom coffee layer may remain harder depending on how much the allulose depressed the freezing point.

6. **Optional chocolate top:** After the ice cream base has frozen hard in the tub (before spinning), add another very thin layer of melted chocolate on top — it shatters into flecks through the ice cream during spinning.

---

**Notes:**
- This is a Ninja Creami **Deluxe** recipe by design — the TOP/FULL/BOTTOM partial-spin feature is used to process only the ice cream layer while leaving the coffee base untouched. Can be made in a standard machine but without that layering control.
- The allulose at ~20% by weight was not quite enough to keep the ladyfinger layer soft at -18°C freezer temp — Nick notes you may need more, or use a higher-PAC sweetener, to keep the bottom layer scoopable rather than frozen solid.
- Nick used a branded Allulose/monk fruit blend ("Vop" / "Arial" — brand name unclear from audio); any allulose or erythritol-based sweetener works for FPD (freezing point depression) in the coffee soak.
- Mascarpone is the traditional choice for the cream layer; cream cheese is a direct, cheaper substitute.
- Xanthan gum can sub for the hot-process stabilizer but use much less — it goes very gummy at higher amounts.
- Result: top ice cream layer was delicious (creamy, cheesecake-like, chocolate flecks); bottom coffee/ladyfinger scoop was good but froze harder than intended. Nick rates concept a success with the noted freezing point adjustment needed.
- Spin mode used: **Lite Ice Cream, full tub**.

---

### [Ruby Chocolate Ice Cream with Fudge Pieces and Strawberry Swirl](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4gs4oOrHYbA)

Valentine's Day ruby chocolate ice cream base with Chambord, homemade dark chocolate fudge pieces, and a sieved strawberry-Chambord swirl, churned in an ICE100 Cuisinart machine.

**Ingredients:**

Fudge:
- **400ml** condensed milk
- **340g** dark chocolate

Strawberry Swirl:
- **100–150g** seedless strawberry jam
- **1 Tbsp** Chambord

Ice Cream Base:
- **250ml** milk
- **500ml** double (heavy) cream
- **140g** sugar
- **110g** ruby chocolate
- **2–3 Tbsp** skimmed milk powder
- **1 Tbsp** Chambord

**Steps:**

Fudge:
1. Melt condensed milk and dark chocolate together.
2. Pour into a mould and refrigerate until set.
3. Keep a piece in the freezer when ready to grate (grating frozen makes it easier).

Strawberry Swirl:
1. Sieve the strawberry jam to remove seeds.
2. Stir in the Chambord.
3. Refrigerate until ready to use.

Ice Cream Base:
1. Combine milk, sugar, and ruby chocolate in a pan; gently heat to just steaming.
2. Remove from heat, cover, and refrigerate for a minimum of 4 hours.
3. When ready to churn, add the skimmed milk powder and leave for 20 minutes.
4. Whisk thoroughly, then add the cream and Chambord.
5. Churn in Cuisinart ICE100 for 20–25 minutes until done.
6. Layer into the container with grated fudge pieces and the strawberry-Chambord swirl.

**Notes:**

- Ruby chocolate gives a naturally fruity, slightly tart flavour — pairs well with strawberry and raspberry notes from the Chambord.
- Chambord is used in both the swirl and the base to amplify berry intensity.
- Grating the fudge from frozen produces fine pieces that distribute evenly.
- Skimmed milk powder is added after chilling and rested 20 minutes before churning — improves body without cooking the powder in.
- Machine used: Cuisinart ICE100 (traditional churn-freeze machine, not Ninja Creami).

---

### [ULTIMATE Butter Pecan Ice Cream — Musso & Creami](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o2ArE5TzUuY)

Custard-based butter pecan ice cream made with browned butter, dark brown sugar, and vanilla bean paste, churned in a Musso machine — with a bonus Ninja Creami variation using pecan pie as a mix-in.

**Batch size:** ~1.6–1.7L after churn overrun (enough for 2 Ninja Creami Deluxe pints or 2⅔ standard pints). A portion (~400g) is reserved and frozen in a standard Creami tub for the pecan pie variation.

**Ingredients:**

Ice cream base:
- **500g** milk (2% or full fat)
- **544g** whipping cream (33%; 36% also fine)
- **168g** dark brown sugar (or white sugar + molasses combined)
- **56g** dextrose (or corn syrup)
- **67g** skim milk powder
- **10g** browned butter (reserved from the pecan step below)
- **125g** egg yolks (~6 eggs)
- **25–30g** vanilla bean paste (or vanilla extract)

Butter-toasted pecans (add-in):
- **150g** pecans (broken in half)
- **50g** salted butter (good quality, grass-fed preferred)

Optional Ninja Creami variation:
- 1 small pecan pie (store-bought or homemade), cut into pieces — pressed into a divot in the frozen Creami tub before the add-in spin

**Steps:**

1. **Brown the butter:** Cut 50g butter into small cubes and melt in a pan over medium heat. Swirl constantly for 5–8 minutes until foam subsides, the butter turns transparent, and dark brown milk solids appear with a toasted hazelnut aroma. Remove from heat immediately. Reserve 10g of the browned butter in a small container for the ice cream base.
2. **Toast the pecans:** In a separate dry pan over high heat, toast 150g of pecans (broken in half) for a couple of minutes until fragrant. Remove from heat and add to the pan with the remaining browned butter. Stir to coat. Store at room temperature for up to 24 hours to let flavors develop.
3. **Make the ice cream base:** Combine milk, cream, dark brown sugar, dextrose, skim milk powder, and the reserved 10g browned butter in the pan used for pecans. Whisk together. Add egg yolks and whisk until fully incorporated. Stir in vanilla bean paste.
4. **Cook the custard:** Heat over medium, stirring constantly, until the base reaches 70–75°C. Do not exceed this temperature.
5. **Chill:** Cool the base and refrigerate for at least 12 hours, ideally 24 hours, to allow flavors from the brown butter, vanilla bean paste, and dark brown sugar/molasses to develop fully.
6. **Churn:** Process in a Musso (or other ice cream machine) until churned. Near the end of the churn, briefly warm the pecans if the butter has hardened (15 seconds in microwave), stir to re-coat, and add all pecans plus remaining browned butter solids into the churning machine. Reserve a few pecans for decoration.
7. **Freeze:** Dish out into containers and freeze.
8. **Ninja Creami variation:** Pour ~400g of the unchurned base into a standard Creami tub and freeze (24+ hours). Spin on Ice Cream. Make a divot in the center, press in cut pieces of pecan pie, cover back over, and run the add-in program.

**Notes:**

- The "unknown" butter pecan flavor comes from the combination of dark brown sugar (molasses notes), browned butter (Maillard/hazelnut notes), and egg yolks deepening the vanilla base — not butter extract.
- Browned butter (French: *beurre noisette*) must be watched closely — it goes from perfect to burnt quickly. Pull it the moment foam subsides and toasted spots appear.
- Break pecans in half rather than leaving whole; open cut ends absorb the butter better and the flavor penetrates deeper.
- No stabilizer used in this recipe; Nick notes a stabilizer would make it "100% perfect" by countering the egg whites and improving structure.
- The pecan pie Creami variation is outstanding — the Creami add-in spin shatters the frozen pie filling into chewy caramelized nuggets throughout the ice cream.
- Dark brown sugar gives the finished ice cream a light caramel color.

---

### [Ultimate Nutella Ice Cream Recipe](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_HPWVlTdEKQ)

Super-premium churned Nutella ice cream with brownie bites and a marshmallow (Swiss meringue) swirl — inspired by Ben & Jerry's Phish Food style.

**Ingredients:**

Ice Cream Base:
- **300ml** double cream
- **250ml** whole milk
- **65g** Nutella
- **75g** granulated sugar
- **50g** high-quality cocoa powder
- **1/2 tsp** vanilla extract
- Pinch of salt
- **75g** milk powder (added after chilling, before churning)

Brownie Bites:
- **100g** frozen brownies (crushed/powdered)
- **2 tbsp** Nutella
- Chocolate mould

Marshmallow Swirl (Swiss meringue):
- **2** large egg whites
- **100g** granulated sugar
- **1/4 tsp** cream of tartar
- **1/2 tsp** vanilla extract (optional)

**Steps:**

1. Combine milk, cream, and sugar in a saucepan over gentle heat. Add Nutella and stir until fully dissolved and smooth.
2. Add cocoa powder and stir to incorporate. Turn off heat and cool for 5–6 minutes.
3. Add vanilla extract and a pinch of salt; stir well.
4. Transfer to a fridge-safe container, cover with cling film, and refrigerate for at least 4 hours.
5. After chilling, stir in 75g milk powder. Return to fridge for 20 minutes to allow the powder to dissolve fully.
6. Pour base into ice cream maker (pre-cool bowl if required) and churn for 20–25 minutes until thick and creamy.
7. **Brownie bites:** Crush/powder 100g frozen brownies. Mix with 2 tbsp Nutella to form a thick paste. Press into a chocolate mould and freeze solid.
8. **Marshmallow swirl:** Combine egg whites, sugar, and cream of tartar in a heatproof bowl/pan. Heat to 71°C, stirring. Remove from heat and whisk vigorously for 5–6 minutes until thick and glossy. Add vanilla if using.
9. **Assembly:** Layer churned ice cream, marshmallow swirl, and brownie bites in a container, repeating layers until full.
10. Freeze overnight before serving.

**Notes:**

- Sugar is reduced in the base because Nutella already contributes significant sugar.
- Milk powder is added cold (not heated with the base) to avoid any scorching; the 20-minute fridge rest lets it dissolve.
- Brownie bites must be frozen solid before mixing with Nutella so they can be powdered; store-bought brownies can substitute homemade.
- The marshmallow swirl is a Swiss meringue — a thermometer is recommended to hit 71°C accurately. Whisking off the heat for 5–6 minutes develops the thick, stable swirl texture (same technique Ben & Jerry's uses for marshmallow ribbons).
- Freeze result overnight; let sit out a couple of minutes before scooping to avoid melting too fast.
- This is a **churned** recipe using a traditional ice cream maker, not a Ninja Creami recipe.

---

### [Ultra Low Sugar Strawberry Sorbet in Ninja Creami](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9Ydlvz5OS3s)

Strawberry sorbet with xylitol instead of sugar, corn syrup or invert sugar for texture, taragum as stabilizer, and an optional reduced balsamic glaze.

**Ingredients — Sorbet (1 pint):**
- **135g** fresh strawberries
- **264g** water
- **68g** xylitol
- **30g** corn syrup (or invert sugar)
- **0.5g** taragum (stabilizer)
- **0.5g** sea salt

**Ingredients — Balsamic Glaze (optional topping):**
- **~100ml** balsamic vinegar (reduce by half on hob)

**Steps:**
1. Quarter the strawberries and add to a pan with the water, xylitol, corn syrup/invert sugar, sea salt, and taragum.
2. Heat on the hob over steady medium heat, stirring to dissolve the xylitol. Cook the strawberries down for 10–15 minutes until soft enough to break apart with a whisk or fork.
3. Remove from heat and let cool for 5–10 minutes. Do not blend while still hot.
4. Once cooled enough, blend (pulse first, then run on low for 20–30 seconds). Be careful with residual heat — lid can burst from pressure if mixture is still hot.
5. Pour into Ninja Creami pint tub. Freeze minimum 24 hours (36h recommended for cooked bases).
6. For the balsamic glaze: simmer ~100ml balsamic vinegar on the hob and reduce by half until syrupy. Store separately.
7. Spin on Sorbet. Dish up and drizzle reduced balsamic glaze over the top. If eating from the tub, poke holes with a chopstick and pour glaze in — use sparingly, it is very concentrated.

**Notes:**
- The mix may visibly layer/separate in the freezer as solids settle. Stir back together before re-freezing — this is normal with high-fruit sorbets.
- Xylitol has a very strong freezing point depression effect; 68g is near the upper limit for a pint — do not increase beyond this or it will not freeze. A gram scale is essential.
- Corn syrup or invert sugar significantly improves texture; the sorbet will still work without it but will be noticeably inferior.
- Taragum requires heat to hydrate — do not skip the cooking step.
- Strawberries contain ~5% natural sugar, so zero-sugar is not achievable; xylitol keeps added sugar minimal.
- The balsamic glaze is very acidic and concentrated after reduction — it cuts through the sweetness of the sorbet.
- Nick advises against using erythritol ("arrol") in sorbets due to its freezing behaviour (he planned a dedicated video on this).

---

### [Upgrade Your Desserts with Homemade Vanilla Paste](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OSFnAc0NhN8)

Technique/component video — how to make a rich, long-lasting vanilla bean paste from scratch using a sugar syrup base.

**Ingredients:**

- **70g** vanilla beans (Grade B or better — ~17 beans; must still be pliable, not bone-dry)
- **280g** water
- **160g** sugar
- **50g** corn syrup

**Steps:**

1. Sterilize your storage jar or bottle thoroughly.
2. Combine water, sugar, and corn syrup in a saucepan. Place on medium-low heat and stir to dissolve.
3. Trim the hard ends off the vanilla beans, then split each bean lengthwise with a small knife.
4. Chop the split beans into ~5mm pieces (seeds and pod together — don't discard the seeds).
5. Remove the syrup from heat briefly, add the chopped vanilla beans, stir, then return to medium-low heat.
6. Simmer for ~45 minutes, keeping a gentle bubble, until the syrup has reduced and thickened.
7. Allow to cool sufficiently before blending (hot liquid expands and can overflow a blender).
8. Blend or process the entire mixture (beans + syrup) until smooth. A large food processor works best; a smoothie blender also works — blend for a good while.
9. Strain through a sieve into the sterilized jar, pushing through as much liquid as possible from the bean solids.
10. Store in the refrigerator — the paste will thicken further when cold.

**Notes:**

- The corn syrup prevents recrystallization of the sugar syrup; honey can substitute (it's a natural invert sugar) but adds its own flavor.
- Grade A beans are ideal but expensive; Grade B works well. Avoid completely dried-out beans with no flexibility — minimal flavor remains.
- Do not discard the strained bean solids — dry them in the oven and use in other recipes.
- Vanilla bean paste keeps for a long time refrigerated; vanilla flavor compounds are stable.
- Vanilla acts as a flavor booster (like salt) — use 1/4 tsp in hot chocolate or cocoa, or add to savory dishes.
- This recipe makes a large batch (~500g paste); scale down freely (half, quarter, etc.).
- Use a large food processor, not a small countertop mixer — heat retained in the mix can cause overflow in a small machine.

### [Valrhona Chocolate Ice Cream 2.0](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Oug3TRwgBBE)

Luxury, rich, decadent all-Valrhona chocolate ice cream with a caramel and hazelnut praline swirl — made using Valrhona cocoa powder, Caramelia chocolate, and Valrhona praline.

**Ingredients:**

- **375g** milk
- **300g** cream
- **110g** sugar (sucrose)
- **50g** dextrose
- **45g** Valrhona cocoa powder
- **45g** skimmed milk powder
- **1.5g** stabilizer blend (optional)
- **50g** Valrhona Caramelia chocolate (chopped, mix-in)
- **100g** Valrhona praline (swirl)

**Steps:**

1. Combine milk, sugar, and dextrose in a saucepan over heat. Stir until completely dissolved.
2. Once the mixture reaches 50–60°C, add the cocoa powder and skimmed milk powder. Whisk until fully homogenized. Add stabilizer blend if using.
3. Remove from heat, transfer to a container, and stir in the cream. Chill in the fridge until completely cold.
4. Give the base a final stir, then churn in an ice cream machine.
5. In the last minute of churning, add the chopped Caramelia chocolate pieces.
6. Layer churned ice cream into containers, swirling the Valrhona praline throughout as you load each layer.
7. Freeze for several hours (or overnight) until fully set.

**Notes:**

- This is a traditional churned recipe, not a Ninja Creami no-churn recipe.
- Valrhona praline is a hazelnut-almond spread — described as "better than Nutella." The recipe was inspired by a Valrhona Instagram post.
- Valrhona Caramelia is a caramel milk chocolate; chopping it small ensures even distribution during the final churn stage.
- Nick notes the ice cream held well in the freezer for close to two months before serving — the stabilizer blend helps with long-term storage.
- Stabilizer blend is listed as optional but recommended if storing for more than a few days.
- Ingredients were funded via Ko-Fi supporter contributions (luxury ingredient batch).

---

### [Vanilla Ice Cream - Boring or Perfect?](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bmqquJnhuls)

Classic custard-based vanilla ice cream made with a churn method, using a dual-sugar base and real vanilla pods.

**Ingredients:**

- **500g** double/whipping cream
- **400g** milk
- **120g** sugar
- **40g** dextrose
- **20g** light brown sugar
- **50g** skimmed milk powder
- **3** medium egg yolks
- **1.5g** stabilizer (pre-mixed into dextrose)
- **1** large plump vanilla pod (or 2 smaller/thinner ones)

**Steps:**

1. Add milk to a pan over gentle heat. Add sugar, dextrose (with stabilizer pre-mixed in), and light brown sugar. Stir to combine.
2. Add egg yolks to the cold/barely warm mixture — this is the safest method to avoid scrambling.
3. Bring temperature up gradually, stirring constantly. Target ~70°C / when the base just starts to steam slightly with no bubbles.
4. Once at temperature, turn off the heat and let the base rest for a few minutes.
5. While resting, split the vanilla pod(s) lengthwise, scrape out the seeds, and add both seeds and pods to the hot custard.
6. Add skimmed milk powder and stir until fully combined.
7. Let the pods infuse for a couple of minutes, then remove the pods. Transfer base to a container and chill in the fridge until completely cold.
8. Once chilled, give the base a final whisk to ensure it is fully emulsified. Pour into an ice cream machine and churn as normal.
9. Once churned, transfer to a tub and freeze for several hours (overnight recommended) before serving.

**Notes:**

- Light brown sugar enhances the caramel notes of vanilla flavor; don't overdo it or the profile shifts toward caramel. It also darkens the color slightly.
- Use 1 large plump pod for best results; 2 thinner pods are an acceptable substitute.
- Adding egg yolks at the start (cold milk) is Nick's preferred method — safer than tempering, avoids scrambling.
- No thermometer? Watch for the first wisps of steam rising from the surface with no bubbles — that's ~70°C.
- The vanilla pods themselves infuse the base even after seeds are removed — leave them in during the chill.
- This base is versatile: add mix-ins or flavors just as commercial producers do.

### [Vegan Häagen Dazs Belgian Chocolate Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ar1GkLDvjVs)

Plant-based Belgian chocolate ice cream using Elmlea cream and Carnation plant-based condensed milk. Traditional churn recipe.

**Ingredients:**
- **300g** almond milk
- **500g** Elmlea plant-based cream
- **50g** dextrose
- **60g** 55% chocolate
- **50g** cocoa powder (good quality)
- **370g** plant-based condensed milk (Carnation — NOT coconut)
- **1.5g** stabilizer blend (optional)

**Steps:**
1. Combine almond milk, dextrose, and cocoa powder. Heat gently to dissolve cocoa.
2. Add chocolate, melt until smooth.
3. Stir in plant-based condensed milk and Elmlea cream.
4. If using stabilizer, heat to activation temp.
5. Chill completely (overnight preferred).
6. Churn in ice cream machine.
7. Transfer to tubs and freeze.

**Notes:**
- DO NOT use coconut condensed milk — Nick is emphatic it ruins the flavour.
- The Elmlea + Carnation plant-based condensed milk combo is what makes this work — described as "a revelation."
- No one would know this is vegan from taste alone.

### [Warning: Addictive Australian Milo Ice Cream](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=j-8ZS-sbufk)
Cooked malt-chocolate base using Milo powder — rich, creamy, and deeply flavoured through extended heating to concentrate and evaporate excess moisture.

**Ingredients:**
- **450g** milk (2% fat)
- **550g** whipping cream (33% fat)
- **170g** sugar
- **90g** Milo powder
- **30g** SMP (skim milk powder)
- **2g** stabiliser

**Steps:**
1. Combine sugar and stabiliser in one container; mix together. Combine Milo and SMP in a second container; mix together.
2. Add milk and cream to a pan and begin warming.
3. Add the sugar/stabiliser mix to the dairy, then add the Milo/SMP mix. Whisk everything together.
4. Heat to 75–80°C and hold at that temperature for 20–30 minutes, stirring. This evaporates excess water (Milo runs thin) and fully hydrates the stabiliser. The extended hold also produces a slightly creamier result.
5. Transfer base to freezer containers. A skin will form on top as it chills — this mixes back in.
6. Chill for at least 4 hours until cold throughout, then freeze overnight (24h minimum).
7. Churn in ice cream machine. Note: the high sugar content slows churning — allow extra time.

**Notes:**
- Milo is a malted drink powder (similar to Horlicks/Ovaltine) originating in Australia, widely available across Asia and North America. Its flavour is mild — 90g is needed to achieve a pronounced taste in ice cream.
- Milo is lower in milk powder than expected, hence the added SMP to boost MSNF and body.
- The 20–30 min hold at 80°C is key: it evaporates excess moisture and fully hydrates locust bean gum (if used in the stabiliser blend), which requires ~30 min at 80–85°C to hydrate completely.
- High sugar content (170g) means the base will take longer to churn than usual.
- Optional add-in shown in video: graham cracker/biscuit crumbs layered into the tub before freezing (leftover from a strawberry cheesecake batch).
- Suggested add-in variation: brownie chunks + caramel sauce (inspired by Gelato Messina's commercial Milo flavour).
---

### [White Chocolate Gelato (Gelato Messina) with Fudge Pretzels & Fudge Swirl](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GFR1lMHRaEQ)

Gelato Messina-sourced white chocolate gelato churned in an ice cream machine, layered with white chocolate fudge swirl and fudge-covered pretzels.

**Ingredients — Gelato Base:**
- **670g** milk
- **100g** sugar
- **15g** skimmed milk powder
- **30g** dextrose (or glucose)
- **5g** stabilizer
- **180g** white chocolate, chopped

**Ingredients — White Chocolate Fudge Swirl:**
- egg (quantity not specified)
- sugar (quantity not specified)
- white chocolate (quantity not specified)
- butter (quantity not specified)

**Ingredients — Mix-In:**
- fudge-covered pretzels (quantity to taste)

**Steps:**
1. Pour milk into a pan and slowly heat to 40°C.
2. Add the dry ingredients (sugar, dextrose, skimmed milk powder, stabilizer) and stir to combine.
3. Continue heating to approximately 65°C (mix will start to steam lightly). Hold at 65°C for 30 minutes, stirring every 5 minutes to prevent sticking.
4. Remove from heat and place pan in an ice bath. While cooling, add the chopped white chocolate and stir until melted and fully incorporated.
5. Continue cooling in the ice bath, stirring every 10–15 minutes, until the base reaches 4°C.
6. Transfer to the fridge until ready to churn.
7. Before churning, give the base a thorough whisk (immersion blender or hand whisk).
8. Churn in an ice cream machine.
9. Make the fudge: in one bowl whisk egg and sugar together; in a second bowl gently melt white chocolate and butter in the microwave (carefully — white chocolate burns easily). Combine both bowls and whisk smooth. Set aside.
10. Once churning is done, layer into the tub: spoon fudge swirl into the bottom, add broken fudge-covered pretzels, alternate gelato and mix-ins layer by layer.
11. Freeze until firm.

**Notes:**
- Recipe is adapted from the Gelato Messina recipe book (available at gelatomessina.com or Google Books).
- This is a traditional churned gelato — not a Ninja Creami recipe.
- Gelato style means less fat than ice cream, which lets the white chocolate flavour come through more intensely.
- Best eaten the same day; will keep a couple of days in the freezer before texture degrades.
- White chocolate is very sensitive to heat — melt gently in short microwave bursts.
- Fudge swirl uses white chocolate rather than dark for consistency with the base flavour.

### [Why Blueberry Ice Cream should be everyone's favorite](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=z1T75sEi0Ys)
Cooked blueberry ice cream with a marshmallow fluff swirl, made with frozen blueberries reduced on the hob for concentrated flavor.

**Ingredients:**
- **100g** frozen blueberries
- **300g** 2% milk
- **420g** 33% cream
- **55g** skim milk powder
- **35g** corn syrup
- **130g** sugar
- **1.5g** ice cream stabilizer (Special Ingredients brand, off-the-shelf)
- Store-bought marshmallow fluff (quantity to taste, layered as mix-in)

**Steps:**
1. Simmer the frozen blueberries on the hob, crushing them down with a silicone spoon, until the excess water boils off and the mixture reduces to a reasonably viscous syrup.
2. Combine milk, cream, skim milk powder, corn syrup, and sugar in a pot. Pre-mix the stabilizer into the sugar before adding.
3. Heat the base on the hob, stirring continuously (the corn syrup will clump at the bottom initially — keep stirring).
4. Once the base is coming up to temperature, add the blueberry reduction and stir to combine.
5. Continue heating to 85°C, then remove from heat.
6. Chill the base completely in the fridge, then whisk to homogenize.
7. Churn in a pre-chilled ice cream machine for 15–20 minutes until ready.
8. Layer into the container: scoop of ice cream, spoonful of marshmallow fluff, repeat until full. Freeze overnight (minimum).

**Notes:**
- Frozen blueberries work perfectly — the reduction step boils off the excess freeze-water and concentrates flavor.
- The stabilizer is pre-mixed into the sugar to ensure even dispersion before adding to the liquid base.
- Store-bought marshmallow fluff is very thick and forms more of a core than a true swirl — warm it slightly if you want to swirl it through properly.
- The blueberries produce a fully natural purple color — no artificial colors needed.
- Result: smooth, easily scoopable straight from the freezer, no discernible ice crystals.

### [Why You Need to Try Ample Hills' Coffee Toffee Coffee](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7-yCOiki3do)

A deep espresso ice cream inspired by Ample Hills Creamery (US), layered with homemade coffee toffee pieces coated in coffee-spiked milk chocolate.

**Ingredients:**

_Base:_
- **270ml** milk @ 1.8%
- **300ml** cream @ 49%
- **75g** sugar
- **40g** dextrose
- **30g** corn syrup / golden syrup
- **35g** skimmed milk powder
- **0.75g** stabiliser (LBG + guar gum combination)
- **7g** dark brown sugar
- **10g** espresso powder

_Coffee Toffee:_
- **200g** sugar
- **250g** butter
- **5g** espresso powder

_Chocolate topping (for the toffee):_
- **100g** milk chocolate
- **1 tsp** coffee extract

**Steps:**

1. Combine all base ingredients except cream in a pan. Warm gently, adding the 10g espresso powder. Heat to 65°C (or to whatever temperature your stabiliser requires).
2. Remove from heat, add the cream, then refrigerate until completely chilled.
3. Make the toffee: melt 250g butter and 200g sugar together in a pan. Add the 5g espresso powder (it will sit on top until the butter and sugar fully melt — this is normal). Cook to 125–130°C.
4. Pour toffee into a decent-sized pan (8x8 or slightly larger) lined with baking parchment. Leave to cool completely.
5. Melt the 100g milk chocolate, stir in 1 tsp coffee extract, and spread over the cooled toffee. Refrigerate until set, then transfer to the freezer.
6. Churn the chilled base as usual. While churning, remove the frozen chocolate-toffee slab and smash or chop into small pieces. Return pieces to the freezer immediately so they don't soften.
7. Layer churned ice cream and toffee pieces into a tub. Freeze for at least 3 hours before serving.

**Notes:**

- This is a traditional churned ice cream recipe — not a Ninja Creami no-churn recipe.
- Stabiliser is optional; Nick uses an LBG + guar gum combination. Can be left out entirely.
- Keep toffee pieces frozen right up until layering — they soften quickly at room temperature.
- A slightly larger than 8x8 pan works better for spreading the toffee thin enough for easy breaking.
- Inspired by the "Whirly Bird" (Witty Chicken) flavour from Ample Hills Creamery.

### [Will it Ice Cream? — Cup of Coffee in an Ice Cream Maker](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=dX69YdFLevY)

"Will it Ice Cream?" novelty experiment: a regular cup of coffee (with cream) churned in a cheap ice cream machine — no recipe, no freezing, just a fun test.

**Ingredients:**

- **1 cup** brewed coffee (made with Nescafe Azera x Grind collaboration instant)
- **Splash** of cream (added to the coffee)

**Steps:**

1. Brew a cup of coffee using Nescafe Azera (Grind collaboration). Add a splash of cream.
2. Let the coffee cool down completely (about 1 hour).
3. Pour the cold coffee into the ice cream machine bowl and churn for ~30 minutes.
4. Check result.

**Notes:**

- The answer to "Will it Ice Cream?" is **no** — a plain cup of coffee with cream does not turn into ice cream by churning alone.
- Nick explicitly states there is no recipe here; this is a pure curiosity experiment.
- Machine used: cheapest ice cream machine found on Amazon (traditional churn-style, not a Ninja Creami).
- The base was not frozen beforehand — just cooled to room temperature, then churned directly.
- Cream was added specifically to try to get a creamier result, though Nick noted it's not his usual approach.

### [Making the BEST Lemon Sorbet at home — Food Science](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=U3s9T38bC0o)

Precision-balanced lemon sorbet using an oleo saccharum technique for maximum citrus oil extraction. Uses xanthan gum (not guar — guar fails in acidic bases). Batch recipe (~1750g, enough for ~2.5 Creami pints).

**Ingredients:**

- **1098.5g** water
- **436.1g** sugar
- **74.9g** lemon juice (fresh, ~2 large lemons)
- **71.3g** glucose syrup (or light corn syrup)
- **43.6g** erythritol
- **20g** lemon zest (~2 large lemons)
- **5.7g** xanthan gum
- **1.5–2g** citric acid

**Steps:**

1. Zest 2 large lemons with a microplane, avoiding the white pith (bitter).
2. *(Optional — oleo saccharum)* Mix zest with some of the sugar in a small container. Leave 30 min+ (or overnight/up to a week) to extract citrus oils via osmosis.
3. Combine erythritol, citric acid, and xanthan gum. Whisk dry powders together to prevent xanthan clumps.
4. Add the dry mix to the water in a pan. Whisk together.
5. Add the remaining sugar. Heat to ~50°C (syrup phase) — dissolves sugars and activates xanthan. Do not overheat.
6. Stir in the glucose syrup until dissolved.
7. Add the lemon juice and the oleo saccharum (zest + sugar).
8. Stir until thick and semi-viscous (like an ice cream base).
9. *(Optional)* Strain to remove zest, or leave it in for visual effect — won't affect texture. Xanthan viscosity keeps zest suspended.
10. Pour ~680g into a Ninja Creami pint. Freeze 24 hours (can go straight to freezer since base wasn't superheated).
11. Spin on Sorbet setting.

**Notes:**

- **No guar gum** — guar does not work in acidic bases. Xanthan is the correct stabilizer for sorbets.
- Glucose syrup and light corn syrup are interchangeable.
- The method matters more than exact ingredients for sorbet — creating the semi-viscous syrup phase is the critical step.
- Nick suggests leaving the base overnight before freezing to let flavors build.
- Pairs well with torched Swiss meringue on top.
- Also tested in a traditional churn machine in the same video.
